# Architecture

The rules of this code, derived from the architecture paper _Architettura a domini_ (Alessandro Giansanti, in Italian, not public) and from the architecture decisions of q-. Read this before writing code; read [`glossary.md`](glossary.md) for the words.

## Version and how to read

- **Paper version**: vault commit `a14d825` (2026-10-14), last decision **D114**. If the paper changes, this document is updated in a dedicated pass; until then this version holds.
- **q- decisions**: Alessandro's notes on the architecture (5–6 Nov 2026) and on the project setup (6 Nov 2026), in Italian.
- **References**: `D<n>` is a numbered decision of the paper, `§<n>` a paragraph of its foundations; `[Q]` is a decision of q-, `[Q, paper gap]` a q- choice where the paper is silent. `[Q, plan]` is a point this document left open, closed with the [development plan](plan.md) (6 Nov 2026). A rule with both applies the paper to q-.
- **Rule ids** (`F3`, `DOM-7`…) are stable: cite them in reviews and commits.
- **When this document does not answer**, stop and ask. Never invent an architecture rule in the code. A gap of the paper is reported to the paper, not filled here.
- **Levels** (D114): the paper fixes _semantics_ (what an author or a user can observe); the reference interpreter fixes _interpreter rules_ (any rule would do, but only one), each with its test case; everything else (execution, internal shapes, technology) is _implementation freedom_ and is not written down. In q- the reference interpreter is the in-memory oracle, written with the domains (V10): this document states its rules, the oracle implements them.

## 1. The frame: q- inside the paper

The paper assumes a trusted server. q- has none; it recovers the server like this.

- **F1** Every node is the server of itself. The phone runs the whole stack of the paper: UI → user → persona → business → state ports. No node trusts another. Wherever the paper says "trusted server side" (D99, D100), read "my node". [Q]
- **F2** A remote peer is always a client. Everything inside a letter (persona and use case invoked, declared time, content) is a claim. What my node verifies or measures (signature, arrival mailbox, collect time) is trusted. D99 [Q]
- **F3** The signature of the letter is the credential. `post` opens the envelope and verifies the signature; the inbound interface turns the verified signer into the `Caller`. D30 [Q]
- **F4** A letter is a request, not a shared fact. The sender records its own fact ("I wrote M") with the effect "deliver M on bond L"; the receiver treats the letter as an invocation on its node and, if it accepts, records its own fact ("I received M from X"). No synchronous reply: a receipt is a new letter that invokes a use case on the sender's node. The outbound letter is an effect (D73, D108); the answer comes back in as an actor with its own identity (D91, D92). [Q] A request may instead be answered by a response, the return of the request (F7); which of the two a feature uses is the feature's (glossary). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **F5** Sending is an effect (DOM-13, DOM-14); collecting from a mailbox is a read on its stream from a position (DOM-17). D108, D64 [Q] A request is sent so, an effect of the asker's business; a response is not: the kernel deposits it after the served leaf's event, with no fact and no effect, and an opened response goes to the port of requests, to the request it names, never to a persona (F7, EXT-6; the two wirings in GR-6). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
- **F6** The software informs, it does not guarantee. Nothing happens in silence: annotations are part of the facts, dead letters are kept per effect, and both are shown to the local user. A rejection records no fact: a rejected letter goes to the persona's trace, which is eventual and without guarantees, and the UI shows it from there. Hiding (perimeter constraints, PER-14) applies to remote peers, never to the user of the node. D54, D106 [Q] The trace of a tree's ports since part 6.2 (PER-14, "The trace of a tree, at runtime"). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
- **F7** A request and its response (step 12; the concepts are Alessandro's, 19 Nov 2026, in the glossary). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - A **request** is a letter that invokes a use case of a face bonded to the sender, the kernel's or an extension's (EXT-5). It is an effect of the asker's business (F5). Its **identity**, the key sent outside, is composed by the kernel from whose effect it is and the entry's id: `{ of: 'an_extension', extension, effect, id }` (the extension's name the kernel adds, EXT-12) or `{ of: 'the_kernel', effect, id }`, encoded with no ambiguity and hashed (BLAKE2b, as the id of an `anonymous` letter, PER-4). It is one key for the port of requests (EXT-2), for the letter (DOM-14) and for the operation at the peer (DOM-11), so that the server's duplicate branch meets a request sent again as the same operation; the relay never reads it: each deposit of the letter is an operation of its own, which the relay cannot link to it (step 10) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]. The entry's id alone is unique only per effect (the key is the pair, DOM-13: Shed keys two waits by one loan), so two effects keyed by one value, of one extension or of two, would meet as one request: the second given the first's response at the port, or `identity_already_used` at the peer. [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] Rejected: the entry's id alone (above); the encoding in clear (the peer, which reads it in the letter, would read the effect and the entry of the asker's business that ask, none of its own; and every record keyed by it would take a key of any length) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]; the delivery composing it (an extension could name another's request and read its response, against EXT-14). The effect's world is the peer's use case; `post` and the relay are its transport (ADP-4, GR-6).
  - A **response** is the return stream of that call (D72.3, D73.2: every event of a server belongs to a stream its client opened): a letter back that names the request and carries the event of the served leaf's flow as its projection gives it (PER-6), in the words of the version the request named (PER-17): one of the leaf's own events, a closing of its nodes (`not_found`, `refused`, PER-14) or a base event (PER-5). The server keeps nothing of it, no fact and no effect: reliability is end to end (ADP-4), the asker sends the request again with the same identity (D73.6, D79), and the server answers it from the recorded fact (DOM-11, V15), so with the same response; a read leaf answers with its snapshot of then. A response asks for no response: a node never answers one, not even a malformed one, which it annotates (F6). A response goes to the bond the `Caller`'s key belongs to after the request, on the deposit address and the relays its peer declared (`bonds`): a request whose sender has no bond gets none.
  - The asker receives the response as the **return of its request** (DOM-13; the return carries what the executor's own call observed, D92.1, D108.3): the executor delivers the entry to the kernel's port of requests (EXT-2), which sends the letter, sends it again with the same identity until a response comes (the schedule is `post`'s, step 10) or the request is called off, and answers the delivery with the response's event, or with `no_response` once the time the entry declares is over: the effect adapter maps a call with no answer in its time onto the return's input (D108.2), and it is no outcome of the server's contract. Both are definitive with a meaning (DOM-15), as a request called off is (below, EXT-12), and reach the effect's return use case, whose input is their closed set; the paper would give the time to a wait of the business (V18). Only a response from the face the request went to, naming a request still waiting, is given: a second one, a late one or one to no request of the node's is annotated in the kernel's trace (EXT-6), never a fact of the feature (F2, F6). A delivery that cannot go on (the bond is gone, a call undeclared, or its input too large for a letter, EXT-2) is exhausted: a dead letter, shown (DOM-15). The port's answer is a closed set (EXT-2). **A request called off** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (an offer goes both ways, EXT-7): when the user deactivates an extension, or withdraws its offer from a bond, the kernel calls off its requests in flight, every one or those on that bond: `post` sends them again no more, and the port answers their deliveries `refused`, `not_active` or `not_offered_to_the_bond`, as it refuses a new request then (EXT-2), an answer it keeps under the identity as any other. A response that comes after is a late one, annotated (above). Rejected: `post` sending it again to its time, its delivery answered at the end (who withdraws and keeps asking, rejected at the gate, EXT-7).
  - The port **keeps the answer** it gave with the request's identity (the response's event, `no_response`, or the refusal of a request called off; not the bond gone, read again from `bonds` at every call, the same every time since a removed bond stays removed, step 11 [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]), as long as it keeps the identity (forever in the prototype, as DOM-11 keeps identities), and answers a call again under that identity with it, never by sending again: so a delivery met again after a fall, the request in flight or an entry already served above the executor's position (DOM-14), reads the same answer, and its return is the exact duplicate (DOM-11). A call the port refuses sends nothing and keeps nothing (EXT-2). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] Rejected: the request sent again to the peer (a read's snapshot of now, or `no_response` with the peer now off: another fingerprint, `identity_already_used`, a dead letter shown for a ping that had its pong); the executor keeping in its state the `any` entries handled above its position, as it keeps `held` (a second record of what the return's duplicate branch keeps, and no cover for the request in flight itself, whose answer must stay the same after a fall between the port's answer and the return).
  - **The port, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `adapters-common` (`extensions/the_port_of_requests.ts`): `the_port_of_requests(wiring)({ name, touches })`, one per composed extension, `(request) => Promise<answer>`, the boundary's call and closed answer (EXT-2). Its wiring is functions, so that the oracle and the phone wire it alike: the clock; the post's machine, for its hash (PST-3); the post's ports `send_a_request` and `the_answer_to_a_request` and the log of the node's facts; the bond a call names, as `bonds` gives it (`ABondToAskOn`: kept, with my face's id and key and theirs with their mailbox; or gone, removed or never kept); what `extensions` tells, `is_active(name)` and `is_offered(name, bond)` (EXT-7; the domain is the next block's); the kernel's trace (EXT-6). In order: `touches` without the port, `refused`, `not_declared`, traced (`a_call_not_declared`, EXT-4); an input over what a letter carries (the post's `content`, PST-12), `refused`, `too_large_for_a_letter` [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026], checked before the identity, which it does not need, by the shared path, the kernel's port's check too (below) [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]; not active, `not_active`; not offered to the bond, `not_offered_to_the_bond`: none sends or keeps anything; then the identity, `the_identity_of_a_request(machine)({ of: 'an_extension', extension: <its name>, effect, id })` (PST-2); then the answer kept under it, if the port sent it (a delivery met again after a fall: followed, never sent again); else the bond, gone `the_bond_is_gone`; else `send_a_request` under the operation `{ id: <the identity>, scope: 'the_port_of_requests' }`, its correlation the identity, its fingerprint over its path and its input (LIB-10), from my face to theirs, what it invokes with the extension's name added (EXT-5); then the answer kept: `waiting` followed on the log from the position its read gave, read again after each fact (the log resumes inclusively, DOM-17: one iteration, never started again from one position), `answered` → `responded` with the event, `no_response` → `no_response`, `called_off` → `refused` with its why (PST-14) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]. The kept answer is read before the bond, so that a bond removed while its request waits still gives the request's answer (PST-8: its letter is gone already), and a call again under one identity reads one answer whatever the bond became. **And before the refusals `not_active` and `not_offered_to_the_bond`** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4, a fix found by the oracle's checks): a delivery met again after a fall, its request answered or waiting, reads the answer kept even if the offer was withdrawn or the extension deactivated meanwhile, since the request is out already (F7: its return is the exact duplicate, DOM-11; while it waits, the kernel's look calls it off, and the answer kept is then the refusal, PST-14 [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]); only a new request is refused, which sends nothing and keeps nothing (EXT-2, EXT-7). `not_declared` stays first: it judges the call, not the request. Rejected: the refusals before the kept answer, as written first (an answered request read as a dead letter of its extension, a false history, F6). A request `post` does not send otherwise is the kernel's fault, thrown: the hosting traces it (`a_call_not_answered`) and the call stays unanswered until the extension's next load (EXT-15). **The answers kept for ever** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]: the port keeps its answers by identity for ever (PST-8, as DOM-11 keeps identities), one record per request in the node's storage, a cost that grows with the requests; a retention comes when its case comes, measured at step 27, with DOM-11's. Rejected: the bond read before the kept answer (a request answered whose bond is removed after would read `the_bond_is_gone` when called again: two answers under one identity, against F7); `send_a_request` called again under the identity, its duplicate branch trusted to answer (another fingerprint if the bond's mailbox changed meanwhile: `identity_already_used`); a stream of `the_answer_to_a_request` (the post's read has none, PST-8: the node's log is there already); an input too large thrown, as written first (the call unanswered until the extension's next load and the executor's position held behind it, the refusal thrown EXT-2 rejects: rejected by Alessandro, 21 Nov 2026). Tests: `packages/memory/test/extensions/the_port_of_requests.test.ts`, over the post's oracle (Anna's phone of the post's stories, Bruno's node played by the harness). **The kernel's own requests** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.3; EXT-8): `the_kernels_port_of_requests(wiring)`, beside it, for the kernel's effects (the office's question about the extensions in common): the same path, shared (`a_request_through_post`: the identity, the answer kept and followed, the bond, the sending, the answer followed until it closes), with the identity `{ of: 'the_kernel', effect, id }` and what it sends invoking `{ of: 'the_kernel', persona, use_case, version }`; no refusal, since the kernel's requests are declared by the kernel itself, the kernel is never inactive and an offer is an extension's (F8, EXT-7), so its answer is its own closed type, `responded` with the event, `no_response` or `the_bond_is_gone`, and its wiring has no `extensions`; the bond by the address book's id. **An input over what a letter carries** [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026] (PST-12): one rule for both ports, the check in the shared path, first, before the identity: nothing sent, nothing kept; the kernel's answer gains `{ answer: 'too_large_for_a_letter' }`, traced by the port (`a_request_not_sent`, with the effect, the entry's id, what it invokes, `now`; its wiring takes the kernel's trace for it), and the office's question about the extensions in common records it as `no_response` (EXT-8), so that the user learns why in the trace (F6); the introduction exhausts it, a dead letter (BND-10, step 17). Rejected: the throw kept because the kernel's inputs are small by construction (true today, but the same flaw with two treatments; a throw holds the executor behind it, which Alessandro rejected for the extensions on 21 Nov 2026). The trace's name is C's [Q, C, 23 Nov 2026]: `a_request_not_sent`; rejected: `a_request_too_large` (the case, not the reason, in the name: the next reason would need another), the extensions' `a_call_not_answered` (it is answered, and it is no extension's call), and the trace on the shared path for both ports (an extension's refusal is told to the extension, which shows its dead letter, EXT-2: twice told). The extension's port keeps its order, its refusals a gate of the shared path after the answer kept. Rejected: the extension's port given a name for the kernel (a request of the kernel through checks of activation and offer that are not its own, and an answer typed with refusals it never gives); two copies of the path. Tests: `packages/memory/test/extensions/the_kernels_port_of_requests.test.ts`.
  - **The calling off, as written** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (Alessandro's decision, 21 Nov 2026; F7, EXT-7, PST-14), in the port's module: `the_calling_off(wiring)()`, one look, which gives the identities it called off. Its wiring (`TheCallingOffWiring`): the clock; the post's `the_requests_waiting` and `call_off_a_request`; the bond a request went on, by the face of the node's that signs it and their key (`the_bond_of`, none for a bond removed); `is_active` and `is_offered`, as the port reads them (EXT-7). For each request that waits of an extension: not active, `not_active`; else on a bond kept and not offered to it, `not_offered_to_the_bond`; called off under the operation `{ id: <the identity>, scope: 'the_calling_off' }`, its correlation the identity, its fingerprint over its path and its input (LIB-10); one that closed meanwhile is refused by the post, nothing to call off. The kernel's requests are never called off, nor a request whose bond is gone (its letter is out already, PST-8). The port's delivery waiting on it reads the refusal on the log as it reads a response (`called_off` → `refused`). Who looks: the kernel, after each fact of the office and when the node starts (the extensions following the office, EXT-15, step 17), so that a fall between a gesture and its look is made good at the next start. Rejected: a reaction of `reaction/` from the office's facts to the post (one fact gives one command, the translation reads no state, and the post knows no bond: the bond of a request is the kernel's, by my face and their key); the port's own following loop calling off the request it follows (no loop runs for an extension deactivated and unloaded, nor after a fall, while `post` goes on sending); the bond's id kept by the post (PST-5). Tests: `packages/memory/test/extensions/the_port_of_requests.test.ts`.
  - The asker's screen waits with the leaf that asked (DOM-3: it `follows` the return's facts, at most a time, then `the_wait_is_over`, D74.2), which records nothing: "no response" on a screen is that wait, `no_response` in the history the effect's. Two times, both the feature's.
  - In stage 1 a leaf served to a remote face answers in one event: a command leaf with no `follows` (DOM-3), a read leaf with no `stream`, its snapshot in one page (PER-18); the composition checks it (EXT-4). A served leaf that waits or streams (a response per event, numbered) comes with its case.
  - A delivery of a request lasts until its response or its time, so the effect's other entries must not wait behind it: a request's effect is `order: 'any'` (SC-56), and the deliveries of such an effect are in flight together (DOM-14 delivers one at a time) [Q, A, 20 Nov 2026]. Rejected: one at a time, each call bounded by its time (Carla's ping would wait up to 60 seconds behind Bruno's); a key of its own on `effect` (`deliveries: 'together'`: a second key for what `any` already says, that the entries have no order between them).
  - Scenario (ping pong, step 14, whose names these only illustrate): Anna pings Bruno on their bond: `ping_pong` records `a_ping_has_been_sent` with the entry `ask_for_a_pong` (P1, 60 seconds), whose return is `receive_the_pong`. The executor delivers P1 to the kernel, which deposits the request P1 on Bruno's D. Bruno's phone collects it, `post` opens it (`Caller` = Anna's face key), the kernel routes it to `ping_pong`, offered to the bond (EXT-6), and the leaf `ping` on `known` records `a_ping_has_been_answered` and projects `pong`; the kernel deposits the response to P1 on Anna's D. Anna's kernel takes it (P1 went to Bruno's face and is still waiting) and answers the delivery with `pong`: `receive_the_pong` records `a_pong_has_been_received`, which Anna's screen follows. The response lost: the kernel sends P1 again, Bruno's duplicate branch gives the recorded fact, `pong` again. Bruno's phone off for longer than 60 seconds: `no_response`, which the return records (`the_ping_had_no_response`); his pong, coming after, is annotated.
- **F8** A node **stays silent**: it sends nothing back to a request for an extension it has not installed, has not active, or does not offer to the bond of the `Caller` (EXT-6, EXT-7) (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026: against spam, for the extensions to come). From outside the three cases look the same: the asker reads `no_response` at its time, or its screen's wait over (F7). Stronger than a perimeter's `not_found` (PER-14), which is a response: it is checked before any check that answers (EXT-6), so that not even `version_not_supported` tells an extension that is there but not offered. The kernel's own use cases always answer a bonded face: every node has them, and their surface is public to its bonds (D78.2). Toward a face with no bond the node is silent, the kernel included: a request whose sender has no bond gets no response (F7), a refused introduction neither (PER-25); declared, a divergence from the paper (V16) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]. Inside the node nothing happens in silence: the silence goes to the kernel's trace (EXT-6), shown to the user (F6). A flaw turned feature: a node may answer what Cumino leaves silent (the code is free), so an asker learns only what comes back, and reads nothing into a silence. Another [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: a silence does not look like a node off or a letter lost, since the kernel always answers a bond: an asker that sends with it a request to the kernel (the extensions in common, EXT-8) tells the two apart by the kernel's answer, the witness, as STK-6's seventh scenario proves (step 17: a node off told from a silence by the kernel's question); the relay witnesses nothing, since a collect records nothing and the relay keeps no log [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]. It learns no more than EXT-8 tells it, that the extension is not there for it, never which of the three cases. Rejected: the guarantee kept for a node off too (the kernel would have to stay silent toward a bond, against its public surface, D78.2). A retry after a withdrawal or a deactivation meets silence too (Alessandro, 20 Nov 2026): the recorded operation is in the extension's partition, which the kernel never reads (EXT-14), of an extension not loaded while inactive (EXT-15). So the asker records `no_response` where the server recorded its answer: two histories, each true of what its node saw, a declared feature (the software informs, F6). Rejected: the retry answered from the recorded fact (the kernel would read the extension's data, or load it inactive, and the answer would tell the extension is there). Divergence V16. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]

## 2. Packages and the graph

| package             | contains                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `domains`           | the pure domains of the kernel, one folder each: `library/`, `seals`, `post`, `mailbox`, `desk`, `bonds`, `extensions`, `auth`, `persona`, `user`, `ui`                                                                                                                                                    |
| `adapters-common`   | adapters that run in the browser and in Node; since part 12.4 the kernel's side of the extensions (`extensions/`: their composition, their hosting, the port of requests, the kernel's trace, a frame's fence; GR-10); since part 13.2 the kernel's entry (`entry/`) and auth's adapters (`auth/`), PER-25 |
| `adapters-web`      | browser adapters                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `adapters-node`     | Node adapters                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `phone`             | configurator: the PWA; since step 25 its composition root over STK-1's wiring (PWA-1, PWA-2), its extensions' realms in frames and the app's policy (PWA-3, PWA-4)                                                                                                                                         |
| `relay`             | configurator: the relay server                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `memory`            | configurator: phone and relay in one process, the oracles; since part 12.4 the extensions' partitions and an extension in process (EXT-13, EXT-14); since step 17 a phone's composition root and the stack (STK-1..STK-6)                                                                                  |
| `generator`         | the generator of the domains (DOM-25): a tool of the author, run by `pnpm generate`, never by a node                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `boundary`          | the kernel's contract with its extensions (EXT-9): the messages of the boundary, the contexts and identities the kernel gives, the calls, the manifest's form, the extension points, the boundary version; schemas and types, no domain                                                                    |
| `extensions/<name>` | an extension (EXT-9): its tree, its adapters, its configurator, its frames; in stage 1 `extensions/ping_pong`                                                                                                                                                                                              |

`generator` imports of `domains` the library only, and `typescript`; no package imports it, only the repository's configuration does (`.dependency-cruiser.mjs` takes from it the rules of the map). [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026] Its shell imports `prettier` too, and formats every file it writes with the repository's configuration, so that a generated file is what `pnpm format` leaves and `pnpm generate:check` compares texts exactly; rejected: the generator writing Prettier's line breaks itself (a second formatter, which a release of Prettier would contradict), Prettier run after the generator by the script (the check could not compare without writing). The cruiser keeps its arrows: `generator-imports-the-library-only`, `generator-core-no-node`, `nothing-imports-the-generator`. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]

`conversations` left the kernel (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026: messaging is an extension, § 4) and `extensions` entered it (EXT-7); the code followed in part 12.4: the folder `conversations`, empty, and its export left `packages/domains`, and `.dependency-cruiser.mjs` keeps the arrows of the table below (`extensions` → `library`, `persona` → `extensions`), the folder `extensions` coming with its domain (an arrow to a folder not yet there forbids and allows nothing). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] Since its domain came (part 12.4), `extensions` → `bonds` too, for the address book's identities only (GR-3: a bond is named by its id, of which there is no view of one's own), as `post` → `seals`: the map opens it (`sight.ts`, `extensions: { bonds: ['id'] }`), the arrow follows the map. Rejected: an id of its own for the bond in `extensions` (a second brand of one key, which the state adapter would translate, against GR-3's rule on identities). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4)

Arrows between domains, from consumer to consumed (enforced by [`.dependency-cruiser.mjs`](../.dependency-cruiser.mjs)):

| domain       | may import                               |
| ------------ | ---------------------------------------- |
| `library`    | nothing                                  |
| `seals`      | `library`                                |
| `post`       | `library`, `seals`                       |
| `mailbox`    | `library`                                |
| `desk`       | `library`, `mailbox`                     |
| `bonds`      | `library`                                |
| `reaction`   | `library`, `seals`, `bonds` (BND-12)     |
| `extensions` | `library`, `bonds` (GR-3)                |
| `auth`       | `library`                                |
| `persona`    | `library`, `auth`, `bonds`, `extensions` |
| `user`       | `library`, `persona`                     |
| `ui`         | `library`, `user`                        |

- **GR-1** The graph is acyclic. A consumer uses another domain's exported use cases and predicates, never its internals or its ports. D36, D43
  - Business domains import no other domain (GR-3, V11). Who reads whom among them is not declared by hand: it emerges from the imports of the in-memory oracle (TST-2). Reads may form cycles; cycles of reactions are checked when reactions come (DOM-16, step 8 of the plan). [Q]
  - A persona sees of a business domain only the door the map opens to it (`business/tools/_door_persona.ts`, DOM-25), its use cases and its identities and nothing else: its commands and queries, which the business alone sends and reads (DOM-2), cannot even be named (`Property 'command' does not exist`); and nothing of another persona (Shed, `sight.ts`; `.dependency-cruiser.mjs`, rules `shed-sight-of-persona`, `shed-authored-through-its-scope`). Since 15 Nov 2026 the door exposes the domain's facts too (DOM-16): they are its published contract (D101), and a fact imported gives no access to the log, which only the wiring gives; a persona's projections are still bound to the facts a read use case names, by type. The paper also gives the persona the business's predicates, for its perimeter constraints and selections (D36, D44, D50.4): the rule opens to them with the first such constraint. A persona mounting a leaf of another (D78.6) is decided with its case. [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
- **GR-2** Arrows are strict, not transitive: `user` does not import `bonds`, `ui` does not import `persona`. This is stricter than the paper and follows from it: the UI goes through the user domain (D76), an actor reaches the business only through its persona (D88, D24). [Q]
- **GR-3** A business domain imports no other domain, except its identities (`_id.ts`, DOM-24): the id of a tool is the key of the link, of which there is no view of one's own (D48, D96.2). It models its own view of the concepts of the others, in its own language, with its own values, predicates and queries: a concept may have another name or another shape there, or a value the other domain does not have (`loans` may call a tool's category its _type_). The link is the state adapter's: its fragments and sources implement the downstream's predicates and queries over the upstream's state (ADP-8). `extensions` has its own predicates ("the bond exists", to withdraw the offer from it), implemented over the stores of `bonds` (EXT-7); `conversations` did, before it left the kernel. A write in a domain after a fact of another is a reaction (DOM-16), never a direct call. D35, D68, D88, V11 [Q]
  - Atomicity: a predicate over another domain's state, inside a command condition, is read atomically with the write only when one state adapter serves both domains: the IndexedDB transaction of an `extensions` command (withdraw the offer from a bond) covers the `bonds` stores it reads. Otherwise the adapter reads first and the command is not atomic, a declared feature (F6), not a defense. D71 (its scope), D59 [Q]
  - A value two views share (a category, a `type` in `loans`) is not imported: the downstream declares its own, and the oracle links them with an exhaustive map (`satisfies Record<category of tools, type of loans>`), so that a new value upstream fails to compile until the link decides what it becomes downstream. [Q]
  - The fragments that cross domains are tested on the oracle and on each adapter (TST-1, TST-2): recommended; a prototype may defer the tests, and says so in its log. [Q]
- **GR-4** `auth` does not import `bonds`. Auth reads its data through its own outbound read ports, in its own language; the configurator implements them with an adapter over the read use cases of `bonds`. The logical edge `auth → bonds` remains (acyclic): the actor's data live in a business domain (V8). D29, D43, D41 (This corrects the arrow `auth → bonds` of the bootstrap.) GR-3 extends the same choice to the business domains. The same for `extensions`: auth reads the offers through its ports (PER-9, EXT-6), the logical edge `auth → extensions` remains; access data in a business domain, a divergence (V8). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **GR-5** `post → seals` is a domain import: `post` speaks the protocol, `seals` the cryptography; the primitives are behind a port of `seals`. D36, D37, §2
- **GR-6** `post` imports neither `mailbox` nor `persona`. The mailbox port of `post` is implemented by an HTTP adapter (to the relay) or by the imported `mailbox` domain (in memory). The inbound path (opened letter → interface → persona) and the effect executor (outbox → `post`) are wired by the configurator. D5, D30, D65, D108 [Q] Since step 12, two more [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: the kernel's entry hands the event of a leaf served to a request to `post`, which deposits it as the response on the asker's deposit address, with no effect (F7); an opened response goes to the kernel's port of requests, which gives it to the request it names (EXT-6), never to a persona. The port of requests itself reaches `post` as the executor's deliveries do; an extension's delivery reaches it only through the boundary (EXT-2).
- **GR-7** `ui → user` is an import: the UI drives the user domain. `user → persona` is an import of the persona's _contract_ surface; the _execution_ surface is wired by the configurator. `user` uses only the persona `self`, never the personas of remote peers. D76, D38, D40, D75
  - Enforced by type and by lint: the port of `user` toward the persona is typed on the contract of `self`, and an ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule on `domains/src/user/**` forbids the names of the other personas. [Q, plan] **The lint, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.3): in `eslint.config.js`, on `packages/domains/src/user/**/*.ts`, `no-restricted-imports` with one pattern on the import's path, `persona/known`, `persona/unknown` or `persona/anonymous` (relative or through the package, `@q-mino/domains/persona/…`, a type import too), so that any file of `user`, a generated one included, that imports a remote peer's persona fails, with GR-7 in its message. Checked by hand: a file of `user` importing `known` and `unknown` refused, one importing `self` let through. The type on `self`'s contract comes with `user`'s port (step 15). Rejected: a rule on the names imported (a persona's leaves have no name that tells their persona: `the_extensions_in_common` names no hat); the cruiser alone (GR-2's arrows are by domain, and `user → persona` is allowed: which persona below it, the lint's).
  - **An extension's actions and leaves** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (EXT-16, GR-10, D37): the leaves an extension adds to `self` are in no contract `user` imports (another release unit, composed at runtime, V17; `domains` never imports `boundary`, GR-10). `user` and `ui` know an action, its place and the leaf it invokes as their own data (the extension's name, the leaf's path, its label's text, the place's data), read from `extensions` (the manifests accepted, the offers, EXT-7), and invoke the leaf through a port of `user` that takes them as data, its events back as data, the closings and base events in the kernel's words (PER-5, PER-14); the kernel's adapter translates it into `boundary`'s invocation through the kernel's `self` (EXT-2, EXT-6: a translating adapter between release units, D37). The type on the contract of `self` covers the kernel's surface only. A frame is no client of `user`: it speaks to the kernel by the boundary (EXT-2). Rejected: the extensions' leaves typed in `self`'s contract (the kernel would import every extension's types, against GR-10); `ui` invoking the boundary itself (an activity outside `user`, D76). **Amended** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 15.1, USR-2): `user` names an action by its extension, its name and its place; the leaf it invokes is the composed manifest's, which the office of the extensions does not keep (EXT-7), read by `user`'s port toward `self`, which invokes it through the hosting.
- **GR-8** A domain is a folder of `domains` whose `index.ts` is its only exported surface. Domains compile without DOM and Node and import only `library`, other domains along the arrows, and `zod`. Identity, clock and randomness enter as parameters. D39, D20, D48 [Q]
- **GR-9** The steps of a process across domains (DOM-23) are reactions in a module above the domains: no domain imports it, the configurator wires it. A saga goes both ways (A → B, then B → A), so its steps inside the domains would make a cycle. D88, D68 [Q, plan]
  - The cycle of the business stays, in the flows, not in the imports: in Shed `tools` → `loans` (a retired tool's reservations cancelled, step 8) and `loans` → `tools` (a damaged return opens a repair, step 9) are two reactions, each reading one domain's log; no office keeps the other's forms, the standing order speaks both languages (GR-3). Naming another domain's fact by a string would hide the arrow, not remove it. A business domain dedicated to processes (DOM-23) holds a workflow's state when no single life cycle owns it: weighed at step 9 (A, 15 Nov 2026). [Q, 15 Nov 2026]
  - In Shed the module is the kind folder `reaction/`, beside `business/`, `persona/`, `auth/`, and its folders are **procedures**: the business occurrences that move more than one office, as the chapters of a manual (`reaction/retirement/`; at step 9 `@repair/`, both ways of the saga in one chapter). The grouping is free (D88.6): a reaction's log is given by its fact's path, not by its folder. A reaction imports of a business domain only its door (`shed-sight-of-reaction`). [Q, 15 Nov 2026]
  - A one-way reaction, not a step of a process, may live in the domain whose command it invokes, reading the producer's stream, only along a read arrow of this graph (or one added by an architecture decision). D89, D36, D65 [Q, plan] With GR-3 there are no read arrows between business domains: to revisit with the reactions (step 8).
  - The module's place in the tree is set when the first step is written: the ticket, step 8 of the [development plan](plan.md). In q- the top `reaction/`, as in Shed, since part 11.2 (BND-12). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **GR-10** The packages of the extensions (EXT-9) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: an extension imports its library, `zod` and `boundary`, nothing else of the repository: no domain of the kernel, no adapter, no configurator, no other extension. `boundary` imports `zod` only, and is imported by the extensions, by the kernel's adapters and configurators that host or compose them, and by the repository's configuration (`generator.config.ts`); never by `domains`, whose domains know the extensions only as their own data (EXT-7). No package of the kernel imports an extension but the configurators that compose it: `phone` takes its built bundle (isolated, EXT-13), `memory` its configurator (in process, the oracle). D37; D39 as V4 reads it
  - Inside an extension's tree `boundary` is imported only by the generated files of a mounted point (`_node.ts`, the context's schemas, EXT-11), never by an authored file, which imports its scope (DOM-25); outside the tree (`adapter/`, `configurator.ts`, `manifest.ts`) as by any file of the package. [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
  - The boundary is between release units (D37: a domain imports another only in one language and one release unit, else through a translating adapter): `boundary` is the contract, and each side translates at its adapters, the kernel's from its own domains' values (`bonds`' identity of a bond), the extension's into its business's meanings (EXT-12). The messages carry what the contract types, never translated on the way (ADP-4).
  - The cruiser keeps it with a rule per extension package, beside the arrows in `.dependency-cruiser.mjs` (`extension-<name>-imports`), and one for `boundary`; inside an extension's tree its map's rules hold, from the generator's configuration (`map_rules`, EXT-11). The domains' rules (`domains-npm-allowlist` and the others) stay for `packages/domains`. Written by the code step (12.3). **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.3), in `.dependency-cruiser.mjs`, from the generator's configuration (every tree whose root is under `packages/extensions/`): `extension-<name>-imports` (of the repository its library, `packages/domains/src/library/`, and `boundary` only); `extension-<name>-boundary-at-the-mounted-points` (in the folders its map names, `boundary` only from the `_node.ts` of a folder of its `mounted`); `boundary-imports-zod-only`; `the-kernel-composes-the-extensions` (no package imports an extension but `phone`, `memory` and the extension itself; the repository's configuration is no package); `the-generator-knows-no-boundary` (it writes the module the configuration names, EXT-11). `domains` importing `boundary` was refused already (`domains-no-other-package`). Checked by hand on the test extension's tree: an authored file importing `boundary`, and one importing a domain of the kernel, each refused by its rule. `boundary-imports-zod-only` fires since the final review's third session [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]: the cruiser's `exclude` hid the npm modules from every rule (§ 8, the cruiser's rules on npm imports); the test of the cruiser proves it on a fixture, `boundary` importing an npm package that is not `zod`. Rejected: one rule for every extension with a group (`^packages/extensions/([^/]+)/`), the rules named as GR-10 names them, per package; the rule of the mounted points kept by hand (a second list of the folders beside `mounted`).
  - **The packages, since part 12.3** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: `packages/boundary` (`@q-mino/boundary`, version 1.0.0, the boundary version, EXT-9) holds, for now, the contexts the kernel's node gives at a mounted folder, each an object schema named as the manifest's `sees` will name it: `the_bond` (`{ bond }`, an opaque identity of `boundary`'s own brand, every leaf on `known`), `the_peer_key` (`{ peer_key }`), `the_name_of_the_bond` (`{ bond_name }`); the manifest, the messages, the ports and the places come with part 12.4. The workspace lists `packages/extensions/*`; `packages/extensions/greetings` is the tree of the test extension of step 12 (its personas `self` and `known` mounted, values alone: its business and leaves are 12.4's), in `generator.config.ts` with `mounted` written by hand until it has a manifest, not compared with main's (a test tree, as Shed).
  - **`boundary`, since part 12.4** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: the whole contract, a file per theme under `src/`, exported from `index.ts`: `values.ts` (a JSON value, semver, the names and paths the manifest and the messages carry), `version.ts` (its `name` and `version`, its package's), `contexts.ts` (the contexts, and `seen_on_known`, what `sees.known` may name), `places.ts` (EXT-16), `ports.ts` (EXT-2, EXT-14, `PortsOf`), `manifest.ts` (the declaration and the data form, EXT-10), `mounted.ts` (`mounted_of`, EXT-11), `the_kernels_node.ts` (EXT-5), `messages.ts` (EXT-2). Its version is read from its `package.json`, imported as JSON (`with { type: 'json' }`, which TypeScript, vitest, Vite and Node read alike), its `tsconfig.json` rooted at the package with `package.json` included; parsed as semver when it loads. Rejected: a version file written by a script (a second place); the file read at runtime (`boundary` runs in the browser and imports zod only). Its tests, in `packages/boundary/test/` with a `tsconfig.json` of their own, so that `tsc` checks their type tests (`expectTypeOf`). `packages/extensions/greetings` has its `manifest.ts` (its name and `touches`; its actions with its leaves), and `generator.config.ts` reads its `mounted` from it (`mounted_of`, EXT-11).
  - **ESLint on the extensions' trees** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4; CLAUDE.md, "Casts, any and the tools' directives"): the rules of Shed's tree hold on every extension's tree, in `eslint.config.js` (`tree_rules`): no cast but `as const`, no directive, a branch's condition and a query's selection named, one scope per file (DOM-25); on the folders each map names, from the generator's configuration as the cruiser reads them, so not on `adapter/`, `configurator.ts`, `manifest.ts`, the tests, nor an `@` folder of service. Checked by hand: a cast in `greetings/persona/known/` refused. Rejected: a glob of the kind folders (`packages/extensions/*/{business,persona,reaction}/`, a second list beside the maps). q-'s tree (`packages/domains/src`) is not under them yet, though CLAUDE.md says ESLint keeps it: it passes the derogation rules, and one branch condition composed inline (`bonds/address_book/command.ts`) fails DOM-25's; reported, not changed here (a domain's code, outside the part).

## 3. Library: zen0 (`domains/src/library/`)

The paper foresees one library that implements, once for every domain, what is the same everywhere. q- is its pilot, and names it `zen0` (read _Zenzero_), as `q-` is read _Cumino_. [Q]

- **LIB-1** The library contains: the routing of commands (duplicate branch, conditions, the fact with its envelope; state and predicates evaluated by the domain's adapters, LIB-14); the interface component (contract version check, then hat check); the persona component (strict schema, fixed order of entry checks, fingerprint of the entry operation); the durable consumers (reactions, effect executor); the application of fact translations. A domain uses them and never rewrites them. D102
  - Revised in principle on 12 Nov 2026: the library knows no kind of domain and no functional area (DOM-25). Business, persona, auth, interface and hat are the architecture's, kept by its conventions and tools. Of the interface and persona components the paper puts in the library (D102), the library keeps only mechanisms with no kind (a use case over another's, with its own parts; the id of who asks; nodes that accumulate reads and conditions, D105), or nothing; which ones, and where the rest lives (a module apart, q-'s own code), is decided with their cases: step 6 of Shed, then step 6 of the plan (trees and personas, re-planned on 17 Nov 2026). [Q, 12 Nov 2026] On 12 Nov 2026 (2): the library reasons by tree (folders, nodes and their context, components found by their builders, paths, ports) and knows no domain, which is the architecture's (DOM-25). [Q]
- **LIB-2** No word of q- in the library. It imports no domain. It ships its own test cases, run on test domains written for the purpose: it is conformant if it passes them. D102 [Q]
- **LIB-3** It grows by need. First the nucleus (below); every other module at its trigger. The smallest rule that closes a point; no generalization or optimization without a case that asks for it. D82, D114 [Q]
- **LIB-4** Every rule that fixes semantics comes with its test cases. Every library rule goes into a registry with its constraint and its case. Test between the two: would the other choice change what an author or a user sees? If yes, it is semantics and belongs in the paper (until then, in this document). D102, D114
- **LIB-5** The reference interpreter is not in the library, nor is anything tied to it: it is the in-memory oracle, an artifact written with the domains (V10, TST-2). A domain may write in `dsl` a pure implementation of its commands over its own values (relevant state + command → fact or rejection): a building block of the oracle (DOM-5); a tool, not a requirement. D50, D102 [Q]
- **LIB-6** A predicate declares its name and its typed parameters, as a schema (DOM-25). What it reads and its value are the domain's adapters' (helped, if useful, by a pure function in `dsl`): no declared read, so the library derives no read set (V10). D96 [Q]
- **LIB-7** The condition language is closed: AND, OR, NOT and a leaf. The third value ("cannot be evaluated") exists only in applicability results; selections are two-valued (D51). A new construct must be domain-independent, come with test scenarios and bump the library version. D49, D110
- **LIB-8** Adapter capabilities form a closed catalogue of language forms, never domain predicates. The mandatory capabilities are never declared (AND/OR/NOT, version, duplicate branch, total order, at-least-once fact delivery per resource); optional ones are declared per port. D110
- **LIB-9** Every order is total. In q- a query declares the identity of its entries, a field whose value is an identity (DOM-24), unique per entry, which the business's commands keep unique (`NOT(a_loan_has_this_id)`): the library cannot check the uniqueness, the author declares it. The library closes the order with it, ascending, as the paper's core appends a tie-break on the domain id (D109). [Q, 11 Nov 2026: until then the author closed the order with a unique field] Value order (interpreter rule, registry of the conformance note): text by Unicode code point (no collation), an absent value greater than any value (last ascending, first descending), false before true. The reference interpreter (the oracle, V10), the adapters and the UI must agree. An instant needs no rule of its own: in UTC to the millisecond, its text orders as its time (A, 23 Nov 2026, "The day of an instant"). D109 Since part 5.1 the read port closes the order (DOM-18, "The read port's snapshot"), and the oracle's value order has its cases (`adapter/memory/order.test.ts`: no query of Shed orders by a field that may be absent, or by a boolean). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
- **LIB-10** The library owns the stable functions: the fingerprint and the delivery identity of a reaction (fact, reaction, part). A stability corpus is re-read at every release; an undeclared change is a fault. D100, D102 Since part 3.2 the identity an operation opens (`identity_of`, DOM-12) is one: the SHA-256 of the JSON of its name, the operation's scope and its id (`['identity_of', scope, id]`), in base64url, written in the library with no `crypto` (GR-8), its corpus in `test/library/stable.test.ts`; the name keeps two stable functions from meeting on one value. Rejected: the scope and the id told as they are (shorter, but every identity a reader sees would tell the `Caller`'s id and hat). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] Since part 5.3 the cursor (DOM-20, SC-22) is one too (`cursor_of`, DOM-18, "The read port's pages"): a client carries a page's cursor across a release, and a process with a cursor keeps its own in its state, which the library writes in a step's part since part 7.4, the instance's order key (DOM-20, "The steps of a process, at runtime"); its corpus through the read port, in `test/library/read_port.test.ts`, since no domain builds a cursor (DOM-20), so the library exports none. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] Since part 7.3 a reaction's operation is one too (`reaction_operation`, DOM-16): its scope the reaction's path ("system · reaction R", D91.3), its id the digest of `['reaction_operation', reaction, scope, id, part]`, the fact's operation's scope and id and the part's position in the fact told apart, so that two facts of two scopes with one id meet on no operation; its corpus in `test/library/stable.test.ts`, computed with another SHA-256 (Node's). A change would leave every fact already delivered to meet another operation: the duplicate branch would not find it, and the stay Marta's damaged return opened (`identity_of` over the reaction's operation, DOM-12) would be another. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
- **LIB-11** The fingerprint covers only what the retrier keeps and resends: identity, node, input restricted to the declared fields, computed before the schema's defaults are applied. Not the contract version, nor values added later. D100
  - **The fingerprint's form** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 4.2; D100, D79.4): `fingerprint_of({ path, input })`, a stable function (LIB-10), the SHA-256 of the JSON of `['fingerprint_of', path, text]` in base64url, as `identity_of`, where `path` is the node invoked (a leaf's, or a use case's where no tree is above it) and `text` the input as sent in its canonical JSON (RFC 8785, the JSON Canonicalization Scheme: an object's members by their names' UTF-16 code units, a field `undefined` absent, a number as ECMAScript writes it, a text as `JSON.stringify` escapes it). So two requests equal as structures, whatever the order of their fields or the form of their numbers, are one (D100: "equality of the values, not of their serialization"); a value JSON cannot write as it is (a number not finite, a bigint, a function, `undefined` in a list, an object of a class: a date, a map) stops it, so that two different values never meet on one text. The input is the one the schema accepted, before its defaults: strict (DOM-21), so holding only the fields it declares, the restriction D100 asks; a retry sends what it sent, so a default left out stays out (the visitor's `page_size`), and a release that changes a default still meets the retry as a duplicate. Not the operation: its identity and scope are the key the fingerprint is compared under (D79.4: identity, scope and fingerprint; the paper puts the `Caller` and the scope in the lookup, not in the fingerprint), so the fingerprint tells apart two requests under one operation, and nothing more. Its corpus and properties in `test/library/stable.test.ts`: a change is a migration of every fact kept within a retry's window (D100, TST-7). The entry computes it, the port compares it (D108.3: persona, reaction, executor alike); until the trees (step 6), the scenarios' harness enters a business use case as an entry would. Rejected: the operation in the digest (it adds nothing to a comparison made under it, and the paper keeps the scope out); the input as parsed (a default or a transformation of the release in it, so a retry across a release that changes a default would be `identity_already_used`); `JSON.stringify` as it is (the order of the fields counts, so a client that writes them in another order on its retry would meet `identity_already_used`); the input restricted by walking its schema (every object of a tree is strict, DOM-21, so it restricts nothing: with its case, LIB-3).
- **LIB-12** The queue policy of the user domain (pending activities, retries) belongs to the library. D76 In q- it is the effect executor (USR-4). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **LIB-13** License: the repository's (AGPL-3.0 with a commercial license); Apache-2.0 when it becomes a package of its own, `zen0`. [Q]
- **LIB-14** Scope: the library is logic, never state. It routes; it gives the format of commands, branches, predicates and facts, and the operators of the conditions; it derives types; it interfaces the domains with interfaces and adapters, and helps implement the adapters. It does not define entities, keys, generic CRUD or mutation primitives (create, modify, delete), nor how a fact's values reach the state: that is the domain's adapters'. [Q] (V9)
  - It guarantees the typing end to end, and gives typed tools to implement what surrounds a domain: command adapters (an adapter can return only a branch its command declares, with its typed fact, or the rejection with the failed predicates), reads (results typed by their schemas), reactions and every other consumer of facts, inside the domains too (querying an event, matching it against an imported schema on the path of its envelope, exhaustive matching over facts as over an ADT). It knows a component's path from the registry (DOM-24). [Q]
  - For the command port it gives the condition of each branch as typed data, the tree bound by the creator with its operators and components, with tools to traverse and fold it; from the commands it derives, by path, the predicates and facts an adapter must cover (its own and those of other domains it reads, GR-3), exhaustively; it types the outcome: a declared branch with its fact, or the rejection with the failed predicates. How the adapter evaluates and writes, in one or more steps, is its own. [Q]

**Stage 1** (nucleus, D82; triggers from the paper's cost note):

1. Nucleus: fact with event envelope and operation id; default duplicate branch; stream contract of each use case; schemas, strict; entry checks in their fixed order; routing of commands (conditions, LIB-7; total order); persona tree (shallow is fine); explicit `Caller`. The cost is lowered by implementation, never by removing pieces.
2. Outbox, effect executor and dead letters, with the stream read beneath them (D65, D108): triggered by the first external effect, "deliver M on bond L".
3. Client-generated ids (D62): triggered from the start, there is no server.
4. Contract versions side by side (D75): when peers do not update together; version in the message and "version not supported" are nucleus.
5. Reactions (D88, D89): with the first fact of one domain that causes a write in another. In q-, the first step of a process: the ticket, `bonds` → `seals` (DOM-23).
6. Processes and scheduling (D92, D93, D97): with the first real coordination or periodic job.
7. Projections; fact translations (D101): with the first change of a fact schema.
8. Optional adapter capabilities: when a domain's code requests them.

## 4. Domains

### Structure

- **DOM-1** Every domain has the same four parts: language, use cases (its primary ports), a pure core (entities, invariants, command builders, predicates), secondary ports. The core does no I/O. D35, D20, D48
- **DOM-2** Two kinds of use case, command and read, and two kinds of port on the state, command port and read port. No port to publish events: whoever wants facts reads the stream. D41, D55
  - A domain's use cases are its only entry: personas, reactions and the effect executor reach a business domain through them, never through its ports; a domain's command is sent only by that domain, in its own store (DOM-8). A business use case does not know who asks (D85): what depends on the caller (the persona's node, the values injected from the `Caller`, the projection of the events) is the persona's (step 6 of Shed). D23, D24, D36, D88 [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
  - In the trivial case a command use case declares only its command, in the module `usecase` (`command_use_case({ command: command.borrow_a_tool })`), and the library derives the rest: its input is the command's, its events are the command's outcome (DOM-7), the first event and a terminal one (D72.3). So it is a declared boundary, the domain's API, not boilerplate; it is not hand-written as a pass-through. It takes parts of its own (an input and a creator, events after the outcome and their terminals) only when it composes something (a transition, D111; a cursor, D112; listening to the stream, D58), with the same builder. A command with no use case is reachable by no one alone; it may still be composed into another's transition (D111, DOM-19). The library builds the use case ports over the state adapter (`command_use_case_ports`); the command ports stay inside it. The paper has the business use case build the command through the core and send it (D36); in q- the command's creator is that builder, so the trivial use case has nothing to write (an implementation choice, not the paper's rule). [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
  - A read use case declares its query (`read_use_case({ query: query.the_catalogue })`), one per query, and is always a stream: with no position, its first event is the snapshot (the entries and the position P, D66), then the events of the stream from P (DOM-17); from a position, the stream alone, a resume. The consumer takes what it needs and stops (D72.2): the snapshot alone is a read, up to `caught_up` a review, on and on a live view; the interface or the transport may close it at any time, a resume makes that harmless (D73.1, D72.8); a limit of a persona on reads (a visitor keeps no stream open) comes with its case. The two forms of the paper's read (D55, D72.4-5) are one use case: the paper composes them in the consumer, "a read that returns P, then a stream from P, with no third kind of use case" (D72), and is silent on who composes; q- has the library compose them. "The last n" is not a stream operator: it is the page of the query (D50.1), with its case (DOM-18). D55, D64, D72 [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
  - Whether a query streams is the adapter's, declared query by query (LIB-8), since whether a stream scales depends on the technology and the data, not on the business: `native` (its own stream: the oracle, a replication), `composed` (the library composes it from the adapter's read and its fact log: for each fact after P it reads the query again and compares the entries by identity, as the oracle does, DOM-17; a whole read per fact of the domain, chosen where that is cheap), or none, and the query is only read. The fact log is a port of the adapter: the facts it records with their writes (DOM-8), from a position, inclusive, then each new one; it is also what reactions will read (step 8). A composed stream is less exact than the oracle's: an entry is as it is when read again, at least up to the fact's position, and an entry may enter or leave on a fact before the one that caused it; the state it rebuilds is the same (the snapshot at P with the events from P equals the snapshot at Q, the test of D69.3). The port's type follows the declaration: with a stream, the snapshot and the events; with none, the snapshot alone. Since part 5.2 a composed stream does not resume: from a position it gives `position_unavailable`, and the consumer reads again (DOM-17, "The read port's stream"). A consumer of a live view asks for a port with `streamed: true` (`zz.Streamed`), so that wiring a live view to an adapter that does not stream that query does not compile. [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
- **DOM-3** A use case receives one message and opens an event stream; it declares the closed set of events it can send and which ones end the stream. D55, D72
  - A command leaf may stay open after its outcome until a final event (D58.2), declared with its events: which of them end its flow (`ends`) and what it `follows` (the facts it waits for, their projection into its events, how long at most). An event of its outcome not in `ends` keeps the flow open; the library reads the fact log from the position of the outcome's fact on (D72.8), keeps the facts of the chain, by correlation (DOM-10), whose path the leaf follows, projects them, and stops at the first event in `ends`; at the end of the wait, the base event `the_wait_is_over`, at the outcome's position, which closes the flow, records nothing and is no outcome. The facts followed may be of any domain the folder sees (GR-1): the final event is recognized by correlation and by a domain fact together (§ "Il caso d'uso resta attivo"). Scenario: Marta returns the drill damaged; her screen stays open until `tools` takes it into the workshop (`a_tool_has_been_sent_to_repair`, or `a_tool_has_been_sent_to_repair_again`, `the_tool_was_retired_before_the_repair`, D93), recorded by `reaction/damage/` with her return's correlation; whole, the return ends at once. The workshop's end (Friday) is not her flow. Rejected: a fact of `loans` for the repair, which would reopen the repair's one way (DOM-23, 16 Nov 2026 (4)) for a screen; a read of the tool's state, which would mix her repair with one Luca opens at the same time; the terminal marked by each projection, since the closed set and its terminals must read without running. The paper says the node of the persona declares the wait; in Shed the leaf does, since it declares what it follows. Whether a command's flow may hold another domain's facts is a gap of the paper (inbox, `architettura-domini`). [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (5)]
  - **A leaf's wait, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 6.3; D58.2, D72.8, DOM-10, DOM-22): an outcome whose projected event is not among the leaf's `ends` keeps its flow open: the library reads the fact log from the position of the outcome's fact on, that fact left out (its event is the outcome's), keeps the facts whose correlation is the outcome's fact's, the chain it began, and of each every part under a path the leaf follows, of any domain, read at the current shape by the store's versions (the one place), projected by the name in its path, in the order of the log and of the parts, up to the first event among its `ends`. The paths of the facts followed are found by the versions the tree is given (`Versions.path`, by object identity in the current registries), so that a fact of the same name in another domain is not followed; a fact of no domain given is a fault at wiring (CFG-2). How long at most is measured by a port the configurator gives the tree's ports beside the fact log (`zz.Elapsed`, a promise that resolves once so many milliseconds have elapsed: the domains have no clock that wakes, GR-8; the harness ends the waits when the scenario says, TST-3); a tree with a leaf that follows and no such port stops at wiring. At the end of the wait, or when the log keeps the outcome's position no longer (`position_unavailable`, nothing more can be followed), `the_wait_is_over` at the outcome's position, which records nothing. The chain is the recorded fact's, not the request's: Marta's phone retries her return under its operation with a new correlation and meets the recorded fact, then the workshop's, of the chain the first request began (D72.8: a client may follow again from that position). A rejection records no fact, so nothing follows it: a leaf whose rejection projects to an event that is not among its `ends` is the leaf's fault, thrown (the closed set and its terminals read without running, so its declaration is wrong); `identity_already_used`, `invalid_request` and the nodes' closings end the flow, as base events. Scenarios on the oracle (SC-27): Marta returns the drill whole and her flow ends at once; damaged, it stays open, a repair Luca sends of the sander is of another chain, and the workshop's fact recorded with her return's correlation (as `reaction/damage/` will, part 7.3) gives `the_tool_is_in_the_workshop` and ends it; nothing within a minute, `the_wait_is_over` at her return's position; her retry. Rejected: the request's correlation (a retry that sends a new one would follow nothing); the paths matched by the name alone (two domains may name a fact alike); a timer of the library over `now` (nothing wakes it: a clock that is read is not a clock that rings); the wait ended in silence when the log forgets the position (the client would wait for an event that never comes); a rejection not in `ends` left open on nothing (a flow that waits for a chain no fact began).
- **DOM-4** A use case runs at most one command, in any domain; reads are free. Two commands mean a process. D58

### Modules of a domain

- **DOM-25** The generator and the library work on a tree of folders, free but for two conventions: the tree is the folders the map names (`business/`, `persona/`, …, the keys of `sight.ts`, below) and every folder below them, a folder the map does not name (`adapter/`) being outside the tree, which the generator does not walk, and so a folder prefixed with `@` inside it, a folder of service (`business/tools/@fixtures/`); a folder named by a number is a past major of its domain (`business/loans/1/`, DOM-22, PER-17), in the tree and outside the path [A, 16 Nov 2026 (7)]; and in the tree a file prefixed with `_` is the generator's, any other `.ts` file the author's (`fact.ts`), tests (`*.test.ts`) aside. [16 Nov 2026, A: until then the folders of the tree were prefixed with `@` (`@business/@tools/`), a folder with no `@` outside it; once the map named the top of the tree and the files imported only their here, the prefix said nothing more, and `@` passed to the folders of service] A component is an export of an authored file built by a builder of the library (`id`, `fact`, `predicate`, `selection`, `command`, `query`, `command_use_case`, `AND`, …); an exported zod schema is a value (DOM-21); the rest (the functions of `dsl`, helpers) the library ignores. The generator imports the authored files, which are pure (GR-8), and finds the components by their builder. A component's path is its folders from the root of the tree, the kind of its construct, which its builder gives, and its export's name (`business/tools/fact/a_tool_has_been_added`, DOM-24): the file is not in it, so the author names the files as they like; a file builds with the scope of one kind (below), so it holds the components of that kind, with their compositions. [15 Nov 2026 (2): until then a folder with little in it could hold everything in one file] The kinds are `id` (DOM-24), `value`, `fact`, `predicate` (the vocabulary of the conditions), `command`, `selection` (the vocabulary of the selections, with its subject, DOM-18), `calculation` (the calculations over a subject, DOM-18, SC-21), `query`, `usecase`, and those that come with their case (`node`, `lookup`: PER-6, PER-9; `effect`: DOM-13; `reaction`: DOM-16). A name is unique per folder and kind (`retire_a_tool` is a command and a use case); the generator stops on a component reachable but not exported, on one exported under two names, and on two of one kind with one name. [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (2): until then the file named the module and the kind (`@fact.ts`), a module could be a folder (`@selection/`), a folder holding modules was a domain, and `@` marked the author's files, not the tree's folders] Every name (schemas, values, commands, facts, predicates, use cases) is snake_case and as long as its meaning needs, a whole business phrase if needed, with no economy on length (`retire_a_tool`, `a_tool_has_been_retired`, `a_tool_has_been_retired_again`); the input of a command or a query, and of a use case when it has one of its own, is declared inline, in its builder, and the generator writes it under the name `<command>_input` (`<query>_input`, `<use case>_input`), with its type; every signature is a zod schema (DOM-21). [Q, 12 Nov 2026: until then the author wrote `<command>_input` apart]
  - A folder is a segment of the path and a node of the tree: the library reasons by tree (folders, nodes and their context, components, paths, ports) and knows no domain, no kind of domain (business, persona, auth, D35) and no functional area (interface, hat) (LIB-1). The domain is the architecture's: a folder in the folder of its kind, `business/tools/`, `persona/keeper/` (the paper's kinds; the others come with their case), the boundary of consistency (D23: one atomic write, one domain, DOM-8, D59), of isolation (GR-3) and of entry (DOM-2); its rules follow its kind. What a folder sees of the others is the architecture's, given to the generator as a map (`zz.Sight`; Shed's is `sight.ts`): for the folders under a first segment, the kinds of construct they see of each folder under another (`'business': { 'business': ['id'] }`), what is not listed not seen. The library applies it knowing folders and kinds of construct, never kinds of domain: it writes the doors and the scopes from it (below), and `.dependency-cruiser.mjs` reads the same map; lint and later the generator keep the rest. [16 Nov 2026, A: until then the rules of who sees whom lived only in `.dependency-cruiser.mjs`, one rule per kind of domain] The path mirrors the folders with no exception, the kind's folder too (`business/tools/fact/a_tool_has_been_added`); the files and the extension are not in it. Unprefixed files (a README, a test) are not the generator's. [Q, 12 Nov 2026: until then a marker folder `_domain` inside the domain's folder, at a fixed depth; 12 Nov 2026 (2): the library reasons by tree, the domain is the architecture's]
  - Domains are flat, never nested. The paper has no nested domains, and its model covers the two senses of the word: a part that writes atomically with the domain is a folder or a file of it, a subtree of its tree (D105), since one atomic write touches one domain (DOM-8, D42, D59); a part with a boundary of its own (its language, state, invariants) is an upstream domain that only one other may import, a rule of visibility, declared as an arrow when a case comes (D36, D114). A child domain writing in its parent's transaction with rules of its own would break "one write, one domain". The test: must it write atomically with the parent? If yes, a folder of it; if no, a private upstream domain, whose folder may sit in the folder of the domain that imports it: a segment of its path, packaging, not containment. [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
  - Each persona is a domain of kind persona, named by its hat (`persona/keeper/`): the hat is the domain's segment of the path of its use cases (`persona/keeper/usecase/retire_a_tool`, PER-7, D78.1), which the interface reads by the architecture's convention, not the library's, its language is its own (PER-1), and its surface has its own contract version (PER-17). For q- this makes `persona` four domains (`self`, `known`, `unknown`, `anonymous`), `user` importing `self` only, and groups `src/` by kind (`desk` too, PER-19): decided at step 2 of the plan, before its code. [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
  - In a builder, inline goes only what the builder already names: the shape of an input (of a command, a query, a use case), of an event's payload, of what a node takes, an object of named fields, each a named schema, with the modifiers of presence (`.optional()`), read as a signature. A value with constraints of its own (`z.string().min(1)`, `.min(1)` on an array) is never inline: it is a value, named, in `value.ts`, with its JSDoc (`categories` of `visitor`). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (A)]
  - Functions of one argument: a builder takes one object whose parts have names (`command({ input, outcomes, creator })`, `query({ input, fields, identity, selection, order, creator })`), the creator last, after what it binds; a creator takes one argument per level (`(context) => (input) => …`). [Q]
  - A branch's condition is a predicate or a named condition, and so is a query's selection: never `AND`, `OR`, `NOT` composed inline (`[the_tool_is_new, a_tool_has_been_added]`, `selection: the_tool_is_shown_in_the_catalogue`), so that the branch reads as a sentence and the condition has its JSDoc. Enforced by ESLint (`no-restricted-syntax`). [Q]
  - **ESLint on q-'s tree** [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026] (CLAUDE.md, "Casts, any and the tools' directives"): the rules of Shed's tree (a branch's condition and a query's selection named, one scope per file, no cast but `as const`, no directive) hold on q-'s tree too, in `eslint.config.js`: the folders its map names (`packages/domains/src/sight.ts`, MBX-4), their `@` folders of service aside. Until the final review's third session they held on Shed's tree only, and q-'s escaped them (the review's finding on DOM-25).
  - The business language names no primitive, entity or key (DOM-5, V9). Of the library it names only the builders of commands and queries, the parts of a query (`fields`, `selection`, `order`, the paper's words, D50.1) and the operators of the conditions (`AND`, `OR`, `NOT`), in capitals so that they stand apart from the business phrases. The algebra of an adapter keeps its keys lowercase (`and`, `or`, `not`): there they translate the operators into a technology, among no business phrases. [Q]
  - `dsl` is the domain's pure functions over its values (DOM-5): no concept of the library, no pairing the library relies on. [Q]
  - Authored and generated files: the author writes `x.ts` (`fact.ts`), the generator writes beside it `_x.ts` (`_fact.ts`), which the rest of the code imports; every authored file has its twin, a re-export at least (`_dsl.ts`), so that the code imports generated files only; only its twin imports an authored file (`.dependency-cruiser.mjs`, rules `shed-authored-only-through-generated*`), and the editor does not offer authored files to auto-import (`.vscode/settings.json`). [Q, 12 Nov 2026: until then `_x.ts` the author's, `x.ts` the generator's, `dsl.ts` with no twin; 12 Nov 2026 (2): `@x.ts` the author's until the `@` moved to the folders] The generated files (DOM-24): every schema under its name, with its output type under the same name (`export type tool = z.output<typeof tool>`), so that one import gives both; for identities, facts, predicates and selection predicates the schema is branded with its path; with the author's JSDoc and a `@see {@link …}` to the authored member, so that one click leads from a use to its source; the path registry `_registry.ts`. No generated builders per component or parse functions: `parse` and `safeParse` of the generated schema. [Q] An authored file takes every builder, and every component of its own folder, from one generated file, the scope of its kind. In each folder of the tree the generator writes `_scope.ts`, the point: every builder bound to the context the nodes above give there (`zz.builders_at<context, closes>()`, `RootContext` with none), which no authored file imports; and beside it one scope per kind of the folder (`_scope_<kind>.ts`: `id`, `value`, `dsl`, `fact`, `predicate`, `selection`, `effect`, `lookup`, `command`, `query`, `usecase`, `node`, `reaction`), which exports, by name, the builders of its kind (with `AND`, `OR`, `NOT` where conditions are composed: `predicate`, `selection`, `command`, `query`; with `pick` where a command names its effects) and, as namespaces, the twins of the folder in the layers below its kind: `id` < `value` < `dsl` < `fact`, `predicate`, `selection`, `effect`, `lookup` < `command`, `query` < `usecase` (in a persona `value` < `dsl` < `node` < `usecase`).
