An authored file imports one scope, the one of the kind it builds (`import { command, AND, fact, predicate, value } from './_scope_command.ts'`; `command({ … })`, `fact.a_tool_has_been_retired`: a builder by its name, a component by kind and name, as in its path; `pick(effect, 'wait_until_the_due_date')` for a branch's effects by name, a name the namespace does not export not compiling), and none of its folder's twins nor the point (`.dependency-cruiser.mjs`, `shed-authored-only-through-scope`; ESLint, one scope); its own components it names directly; another folder it reaches through the same scope, which carries its ancestors (`_persona.dsl.the_account_of_the_caller`) and the doors the map opens to it (`tools.id.tool_id`, below); of the tree it imports nothing else. The layers keep every import one way: a scope gives nothing of its layer or above, so a use that would close a cycle does not compile. A here that would hold nothing is not written; the scopes nest under `_scope.ts` in the editor (`.vscode/settings.json`). The library exports no builder of its own, so that the scope is the one door to the builders, and the place where a builder could be added (an extension). Those whose creators read the context (`command`, `query`, `command_use_case_over`, `read_use_case_over`) are bound to the point's context, the node to the context of the point above it (§ the node, below); only the generator knows the tree, so the context is never the author's choice. [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (4), A: until then a creator inferred its context from its annotation, unchecked; 15 Nov 2026, A: until then only the builders that read the context came from `_here.ts`, the others (`id`, `fact`, `predicate`, `selection`, `command_use_case`, `read_use_case`, `lookup`, `AND`, `OR`, `NOT`) from `wlib.ts`, and `here` was a named export; 15 Nov 2026 (2), A: until then one `_here.ts` gave every builder as its default export (`command`), the components came from their twins one import each, and the node had `_above.ts`; 16 Nov 2026, A: until then the scopes were called heres (`_here.ts`, `_here_command.ts`), a name born with `here.command`, which said nothing once the builders were imported by name]
  - A folder's doors are generated, one per audience of the map: `_door_<audience>.ts` (`business/tools/_door_persona.ts`), what the map lets the folders under that first segment see of it, a namespace per kind of construct (`usecase`, `id`, `fact`). The here of a folder that sees it re-exports it under the folder's name (`tools`), so that a component of another folder reads by folder, kind and name, as its path (`tools.id.tool_id`, `loans.usecase.borrow_a_tool`), its type too (`accounts.id.account_id`), and moving a component between the files of the folder changes no import outside it. What a door exposes is the architecture's, by the map: in Shed a business domain opens to the personas its use cases, its identities and its facts (DOM-2, GR-1; the facts since 15 Nov 2026, DOM-16), to another business domain its identities (GR-3), to a reaction its use cases and its facts (GR-9), to an auth domain nothing; so that a kind not seen cannot even be named (`Property 'usecase' does not exist`), a rule kept by construction. Inside the folder the files never import its doors, which import its twins: a runtime import of one would be a cycle, since a command holds its predicates as values, branded. A door that would expose two components under one name fails. [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (2): until then only a module folder (`@selection/`) had a door, with a namespace per segment; 16 Nov 2026, A: until then one door per folder, `_index.ts`, flat under the components' names (`tools.retire_a_tool`), the same for every audience, imported directly]
  - A file sees what is above it, as a lexical scope, through its scope: the components of its folder, and every ancestor under its name prefixed with `_`, so that it meets no name of the folder's own (one `_` whatever the distance: the name says which ancestor, and the generator stops on two ancestors with one name), through the ancestor's door to the folders below (`_door_below.ts`: every twin of the ancestor, each a namespace; `_persona.dsl.the_account_of_the_caller`); no folder imports those below it, so no import goes round. Across sibling subtrees only the doors its scope carries, as the map opens them (GR-1). [16 Nov 2026, A: until then a file imported its ancestors' twins directly] A file in the folder of a kind sits above every domain of that kind: above the personas it is the paper's persona base (D78.7), above two business domains a shared kernel, which GR-3 excludes, so `business/` holds no file. [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - The generator will check the architecture's rules over the components, which it sees used, not only imported: a command of `business/loans` with a predicate of `business/tools`, a leaf that invokes what is no use case, a node that reads what is no lookup of auth. The rules are the architecture's, given as data, and the generator's is the mechanism, of no kind (LIB-1); `.dependency-cruiser.mjs` keeps the imports of what is no component (a `dsl` function, a type). With the generator (plan); until then the doors and `.dependency-cruiser.mjs`. [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - **Where the generator lives, and its commands** [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026]: a package of its own, `packages/generator` (§ 2), a tool of the author as the cruiser is: a pure core, from a tree as read to the files to write and the failures, compiled without Node as the domains are; and a Node shell, which walks the folders the map names, imports the authored files to find the components by their builder (A's direction, 12 Nov 2026), so that the builders run before it does, and reads in their source only what the runtime does not keep (the JSDoc, the scope a file imports). It writes by the layers of the scopes: an authored file is imported only once the files its scope imports are written. It runs on Node's type stripping, with no build, as the cruiser's configuration already does. Its configuration, `generator.config.ts` at the repository's root, lists the trees, each with its root, its map, the module its generated files import the library from (Shed's `wlib.ts` until the library is whole, below), and whether CI compares its registries with `main`'s (DOM-24): Shed's not, since Shed changes as design, not as releases (its README). `pnpm generate` writes; `pnpm generate:check` writes nothing and fails on a difference or on a check, first in `pnpm check` and in CI, so that a stale generated file is named before the type errors it causes: the pair repeats `format` and `format:check`. Rejected: the core in `library/` (a writer of source text, reachable from every domain through the library's one door and shipped with the PWA); a script at the root (no package, no tests of its own, no arrow in the graph); the components found in the source alone (the generator imports the authored files, A; the source gives what the runtime does not keep).
  - **The generator's messages** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]: one line per failure, as tsc and ESLint write theirs (`file:line  rule  sentence`), the sentence in the tree's words, naming the component by its path and the rule by its id (`business/tools/fact.ts:12  DOM-24  business/tools/fact/a_tool_has_been_added is exported twice, as a_tool_has_been_added and a_tool_was_added: one object, one path`).
  - **How the generator reads a tree, and the scopes it writes** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: the kind of an authored file is the kind of the scope it imports (`./_scope_fact.ts`); a file that imports none reaches nothing of the tree, is imported first, and its kind is what it exports (a component's kind, a value for a schema, `dsl` otherwise). Every authored file has its twin, its members in the order of the source (the module's namespace is alphabetical, so the source gives the order and the JSDoc); a scope's namespaces are its folder's twins by their file's name (`tool_selection`), in the order of the layers, then by name, then its ancestors outer first, then the doors by the map's order and by name. The layers of the scopes (DOM-25): `id` < `value` < `dsl` < the vocabulary (`fact`, `predicate`, `effect`, `selection`, `calculation`, `lookup`) < `node` < `command`, `query` < `usecase`; no scope gives a node's twin, which is the point's (`_scope.ts` reads its context); a reaction takes its folder's values, `dsl`, facts and use cases (DOM-16). A scope carries its ancestors' doors to the folders below and the doors the map opens, but an identity's and a version's, which see nothing, and a reaction's, which takes a door only where the map opens facts or use cases (DOM-16, GR-9): so `loans`' reactions see no door of `tools`, which opens identities only. A past major's scope gives `earlier`, `delegate`, the next major's twin of its kind as `next` (one file of the kind, or the generator stops), and of the domain what the domain's scope gives but its functions of `dsl` and its ancestors (DOM-22: they may change under a folder once released). A scope is written for every kind a folder holds, imported or not. The point's type arguments are what the nodes at or above it declare (`declared`: closes if one looks up or has a perimeter or a limit, accumulates if one binds or requires), a default left out, so that the point reads as written by hand (`builders_at<keeper_context, keeper_closes>()`); every node's twin gives its `_context`, `_closes` and `_accumulated`. The shell writes the scopes first, then imports each authored file once every authored file its runtime imports reach through generated files is imported and its twin written (a type-only import is erased), so that a tree with no generated file is written whole; then the scopes again, with what the nodes declare. Its tests on a tree of authored files alone in `packages/generator/test/`.
  - **The doors, the registries, the majors, and the map's rules** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: the generator writes every `_*.ts` of a tree, and deletes one it no longer writes (a `_*.ts` whose first line is `// generated`; `pnpm generate:check` names it). A door to an audience (`_door_persona.ts`) is written for a domain's folder and every folder below it, a past major's aside (nothing sees it), where the map lists the folder's first segment for that audience: a namespace per kind the map lists, in the map's order, from the folder's one file of the kind (two files of a kind a door opens stop the generator, until a case asks how a door joins them), then the door of each folder below it under its name (`loans.an_ongoing_loan.usecase`); a door that would expose nothing is not written, and no scope carries it. `_door_below.ts` is written in a folder with a folder below it that is not a past major's: every twin of the folder, by its file's name, in the order of the layers (a past major's scope takes no ancestor, so `persona/member/` has none). A registry (`_registry.ts`) is written for every folder with a path: its twins' members in the order of the layers, then by the file's name, each in the order of the source, under its path (a condition under its file's kind, a selection and a calculation under their subject, an input under its component's kind, a value under `value`; `dsl` has none); a domain's folder and every folder below it carry the registries of the folders below them, but a past major's (D105: one store, one map), and a folder above the domains carries none (the persona base's is its own); a past major's is its whole map, its twins' paths, the version apart, the delegated inputs among them (DOM-24). `_majors.ts` is written beside a version (`version.ts`): the current's registry under the major the version declares, read at runtime, and each past major's folder, the newest first; one text for a business domain and a persona, which the generator does not tell apart. The doors need no runtime: the shell writes them with the scopes, before the imports; the registries and the majors once every file is imported. The rules of the map for `.dependency-cruiser.mjs` are a function of the core (`map_rules`), which the configuration imports with `generator.config.ts`, one set per tree, named by its root's last folder (`shed-sight-of-persona`): the rules written by hand until then, the same patterns, `authored-only-through-*` renamed `shed-authored-only-through-*`. Rejected: the doors and the registries written after the imports, like the twins (a tree with no generated file could not import a file whose scope carries a door); a name per tree in the configuration (the root's folder names it already); every `_*.ts` it does not write left alone (an authored file deleted would leave its twin and its scope importing what is gone, and the check could not name them before the type errors they cause).
  - **The builders a scope gives** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]: every builder of its kind (DOM-25, "the builders of its kind"), in every folder, and the library's functions its constructs name: `command`, `cursor_step`, `AND`, `OR`, `NOT` with `pick`, `composes`, `each`, `identity_of` for a command; the four builders of a use case (`command_use_case`, `read_use_case`, `command_use_case_over`, `read_use_case_over`) for a use case; `node` with `AND`, `OR`, `NOT` for a node (a business node composes what it `requires`); `reaction` with `nothing` for a reaction. Shed's scopes, written by hand, gave each folder the builders it used (a persona's use cases the two `_over`, a business's the other two; `each` and `identity_of` in `tools` only). Rejected: the builders a folder uses, read from its sources (a scope written from what imports it, and a builder never offered before it is used); a set per kind of domain (the generator knows none, LIB-1). Which builders a folder may use is a rule the generator's checks keep (DOM-25), not the scope.
  - **What a reaction's scope and a past major's scope see** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]: the generator's rules above, one for every folder of a kind. A reaction's scope gives its folder's `dsl`, a business domain's reactions as `reaction/damage/` (its translation is a function, and may use the folder's). A past major's scope gives what its domain's scope gives, but the functions of `dsl` and the ancestors, which may change under a folder once released: of `loans/1/` the domain's values, of `persona/member/1/` the door of `accounts`, as the current major. Seeing more opens no arrow: the cruiser's rule for a past major's scope already lets it take any twin of its domain; and what it sees beyond its functions the comparison of the registries watches (DOM-24: a past major keeps what it was released with). Rejected: a past major seeing of its domain the identities alone, and of the doors only those its components name, which no rule of the generator can read (a rule per folder, or the uses read from the sources, as for the builders, above). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026: Shed's five scopes written by hand, which saw less, were held as written (below) until A decided]
  - **Files held as written** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]: a tree's configuration lists the generated files kept as written while a decision on them is pending (`held`, by their path from the tree's root: Shed's five scopes held for A until 19 Nov 2026, above; none since); the generator neither writes nor compares them, says so at every run (`generate: 5 files held as written`), and fails on one it would write the same or does not write at all, so that the list does not outlive the decision. So `pnpm generate:check` is first in `pnpm check` while A decides. Rejected: the five scopes restored by hand before each commit (parts 3.3–3.4: the check could not be in `pnpm check`); the check kept out of `pnpm check` until A decides (every other stale file unnamed meanwhile); the generator's rules bent to write them as they are (what a folder sees is the map's, A's).
  - **The generator's checks** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: over the tree as read, once every authored file is imported, the core stops on: an object the tree names twice, two exports or an export and an input declared inline (one object, one path, DOM-24; the shell numbers the runtime's objects); two of one kind with one name in a folder, a component named as another's input among them (`add_a_thing_input`); a component reachable and not exported, which the shell finds walking each exported component's parts, zod's structure among them, up to the next component, and through a condition composed with AND, OR, NOT, a tree of its operands named by its keys whose leaves have paths (`AND({ the_requested_tool_is_not_on_loan: NOT(predicate.the_tool_is_on_loan) })` passes, a predicate built and not exported does not); two ancestors with one name; a past major's folder in a domain that declares no version, or not below its current major (DOM-22); a folder with a node and a file of the vocabulary that builds (`id`, `fact`, `predicate`, `selection`, `calculation`, `effect`, `lookup`), whose scope takes the point's builders while the node's scope gives it, a cycle of the layers the cruiser would name only as a cycle; a folder below a domain whose parent holds no file, which neither the doors nor the registries would carry, until a case asks how an empty folder carries one. The scopes, the doors and the registries stop on two members, namespaces or imports under one name (a door's kind and a folder below named as it, `usecase/`; a file named as a door it sees; a file and a folder below with one name, in a registry), which would otherwise fail as duplicates in the types. A component of a past major forgotten (DOM-22) needs what the major was released with, which is not in the tree: `forgotten`, a function of the core, compares a major's registry with the paths it was released with (while the major is current, the domain's: a path gone without a major; once past, its folder's), and the comparison with `main` gives it the released paths (since part 3.6, below; Shed is not compared, its README). The messages follow the provisional form above. Rejected: what a component reaches read in the sources (the runtime holds what a builder composes, the source only what is written, and an alias hides it); a past major's folder checked against the next major (a component added at the new major would be asked of the old one); a past major's component bound to the next one's name (a leaf renamed at a major, kept under its old path over the new one, is DOM-24's backward compatibility).
  - **The registries by major, and their comparison with `main`'s** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (DOM-24, PER-17, TST-7): beside a domain's version the generator writes `_registry.json`, its current major's registry as JSON Schema, and in each past major's folder that major's (`business/loans/1/_registry.json`): the domain, the major, the version for the current, and every path with its kind and its shapes, each with the direction it is read in. The shapes are the library's JSON Schema export (`json_schema_of`, DOM-21), on the input side (what a schema parses, a default making a field optional), the brand left out (it is in the types alone); what JSON Schema cannot tell is marked, never "anything": a field that must be absent accepts nothing (`not: {}`), a refinement is `x-refined`, a form with no JSON word (a date, a function) `x-unrepresentable`, a form zod's reader reads back as another shape (a URL's protocol, a record's finite keys, an overwrite, a pipe, a Unicode pattern) `x-lossy` with what it loses (EXT-6). A component's shapes by part: its own schema (an identity, a value, a fact, a predicate, a named condition…), its input (the `<name>_input` path), a leaf's events, entry and stream's events; a delegated component of a past major has the next one's, as the port resolves it; an input declared as a function of the context is built with a context it may not read (it reads it when it parses, in its refinements), else marked. The directions: what the tree reads (an input, a fact, an effect: `read`) may only widen at a minor; what its clients read (a leaf's events, entry, stream's events: `given`) may only narrow; a shape carried by others (an identity, a value, a predicate, a selection, a calculation, a named condition: `carried`) is judged inside the shapes that carry it, inline in their JSON Schema, and changes at a minor whatever its direction. The comparison (`compared`, in the core, over the tree and the registries a release left) reads `main`'s `_registry.json` from git (`origin/main`, else `main`; CI must fetch it once a tree is compared) for a tree whose configuration says `compared_with_main`, at every run of the generator, and stops on: a path released and gone (`forgotten`, part 3.5); a version gone back; at the same version or a patch, a shape changed (a minor at least); at a minor, a shape changed against its direction (a major); in a past major's folder, a shape against its direction (a delegation whose next major moved the wrong way) or a path the major was not released with. Once past, a major keeps what is stored or published (DOM-22): its facts and effects, its leaves (the paths with a shape given to clients) and their inputs, so the major released as current is compared with its folder on those paths alone, its identities, values, commands and the business's use cases changing with the current major. The inclusion is conservative (`within`, over the forms the trees' zod schemas give: objects strict or not, strings with lengths, a pattern and a format, numbers with bounds, literals and enumerations, arrays and tuples, unions, discriminated or not, nullable, a field absent): what it cannot decide is a change, a refinement only narrowing what the rest of its schema accepts; the body of a refinement, a function, is not seen, a declared limit (F6). Every tree writes its snapshots, Shed's too (TST-7: the snapshot is diffed by `pnpm generate:check`), compared or not; a folder with no version has none (nothing moves by a minor there). Rejected: `main`'s registries read by running `main`'s code (another version's code run, a checkout of it; the committed snapshot TST-7 asks for is enough); zod's structure compared in place of JSON Schema (main's objects exist only by running main); a direction per value by its uses (the shapes that carry it are compared already, inline, and the generator would trace every use); equality alone (every widening a major).
  - **A path added, and a patch** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]: at the same version or a patch, a path added stops the comparison as a shape changed does: a minor at least (`is new, and things stays at 2.0.0: a path is added at a minor`). An addition is a minor (semver), and the gate stops a client at a higher minor (PER-17): a new leaf at the same version would meet an instance without it as `not_a_leaf`, a new fact a reader at that version that knows no such path. A patch changes no shape and adds no path. Rejected: additions free at any version (DOM-24 names a path gone or a shape changed, not one added).
  - **The builders at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: each builder gives back its parts with its kind. A component that is a schema (`id`, `fact`, `predicate`, `selection`, `calculation`, `effect`) is a copy of the schema it is given, so that it is an object of its own (one object, one path: an identity over a value is not the value), with the parts it declares (a fact's `resources`, an effect's `order`, a calculation's value); a command, a step and a query are their creator, with their input as declared and their parts; the others their parts under their symbol; a component whose declaration the library runs in a later step holds it whole (`declared`: a node, a leaf, a reaction). Every one carries a mark the generator reads to find the components (`kind_of`): its kind, and for a selection predicate and a calculation its subject; a condition composed with AND, OR, NOT is marked a condition, its kind its file's (`business/tools/command/the_tool_is_new`); a component of a past major (`earlier`, `delegate`) tells the kind of the next major's; an exported zod schema with no mark is a value. What only the types know is never there at runtime, and is optional in its type (the schema of an input declared as a function of the context, the leaves of a condition, what a node gives and how it closes, how a leaf closes and waits, a step's branches as a command's), so that the compiler checks every builder's value against its type, with no cast. NOT copies its condition's schema, which may be composed: so a composed condition holds its operands under its operator (`and`, `or`, `not`), each optional in the type, and NOT's value tells its own alone. The context, what closes and what accumulates are the types': `builders_at` gives the same builders at every point.
  - **Two runtime checks where the compiler cannot follow** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]: `pick` builds its object from the names, then checks it member by member, by identity with the namespace's, before the compiler takes it as the members named (a type guard over exactly what it checks); `earlier`, one builder over three forms (A, 16 Nov 2026 (7)), gives `next` to its form by what it is at runtime (a leaf has a use case, a read leaf an entry), and its type is the three forms' overloads: each form's body is checked against its type, the choice between them only by that test. Rejected: a cast, the same derogation said inside the body (`CLAUDE.md`, casts and `any`); three builders for `earlier` (`earlier`, `earlier_leaf`…: A's shape changed for the compiler's sake); `pick` returning the whole namespace (every effect of the folder queued by every branch).
  - **The ports, typed by their declaration** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]: the type of `command_use_case_ports` (a record of ports mapped over the use cases, each a conditional type of its use case) is one the compiler cannot follow into a body over every use case; its declaration is an overload, the type its callers see, and its body is typed by what it meets at runtime, every value parsed by its schema (the input, each branch's parameters, fact and entries), the third place after `pick` and `earlier`'s overloads. The read ports, the trees and the consumers will meet the same. Since part 4.4 `replay` is the fourth: its declaration takes the adapter's writes by path (`FactWrites`), its body calls each with the payload the versions parsed. Since part 5.1 `read_use_case_ports` is the fifth (DOM-18, "The read port's snapshot"); since part 6.1 `tree_ports` the sixth and `lookup_ports` the seventh (PER-6, "The ports of a tree, at runtime"). A section hard to type, confined, exposing precise types, reliable since every value is parsed (`CLAUDE.md`, casts and `any`, A, 19 Nov 2026): the overload declares its callers' type once, at its head; a confined cast would be the same derogation, said inside the body. Rejected: a port built by a generic function per use case (the same conditional type, one level down).
  - **The nodes of a business tree, at the command port** (SC-13, SC-31) [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: the command port finds the nodes of a use case's path in its registry, the folders at and above the use case's, outer first, and runs what a business node declares: its target, from the flat input, by its schema (`binds`), given down in the context, never rewritten (D105.2); its requirement, bound by its creator (`requires`), before every branch's condition. A node that looks up, takes or gives belongs to a persona's tree: a fault at the business port. Since SC-31 `correct_the_tool_of_a_loan` (SC-13) is below `an_ongoing_loan`, so this enters with part 4.1, from 6.2, which keeps the persona's side.
  - The test systems (Shed, `domains/test/`) declare the library they want in a `wlib.ts` ("wished library"): types and `declare` only, no bodies. It becomes the requirement of the library steps of the plan. [Q]
    - From step 3 of the plan the library's types are in `domains/src/library/`, and `wlib.ts` lists what has no body yet: it re-exports the library beside the declarations still without one, and each step moves its own out with their bodies, so that its diff shows them leave. Shed goes on importing `wlib.ts`, and the generator writes Shed's files over it, until the library is whole (step 7): then Shed imports the library and `wlib.ts` is deleted. Rejected: Shed's imports rewritten at each step (75 files, each step's diff hidden among them); the types moved area by area (they cross the areas: the registry is a union of every kind, the state adapter holds the step's, a command's outcome the nodes' conditions); bodiless declarations in the library (a function that compiles and is not there). [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026] Done at part 7.5: Shed imports the library (`domains/src/library/index.ts`, by a relative path, as `wlib.ts` did), the generator's configuration names it (`library`), Shed is regenerated, and `wlib.ts` is deleted; the five scopes held for A import no library and stay as written. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
    - **The library's name in Shed** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.5): Shed's files and the files the generator writes import the library as `zz` (`import * as zz from '…/src/library/index.ts'`), zen0's short name, by which this document cites its types and functions (`zz.Sight`, `zz.builders_at`); the generator writes it and reserves it among the names a tree may not take. `wlib.ts`, the declarations of steps 2–6, keeps its name where the history of the steps cites it. The registries and majors the generator writes are data, not components: typed by `satisfies zz.Registry` and `satisfies zz.Majors` over an import of types alone, their literal types kept, their checks at wiring (CFG-2). Rejected: `wlib` (C's first shape: the name of the wished library of step 2, gone with `wlib.ts`, and no word of the glossary); `zen0` as the namespace (A: the short name); the library's members imported by name (a scope would list every builder it gives, and a name of the library could meet a name of the tree); `zz.majors(...)` and `zz.registry(...)` in the generated files (A: a builder that builds no component, an identity, or checks thrown at the import, out of the wiring's one message).

### Commands, facts, outcomes

- **DOM-5** A command is data: an ordered list of branches. A branch has a condition (vocabulary predicates with AND/OR/NOT), the fact it records (a business phrase) with its payload, and outbox follow-ups. At most one branch applies. D16, D47, D49, D94
  - The fact describes what happened, and the change of state with it: the command names conditions and facts, never primitives, instances or the state. The state stays primary, materialized by the interpreter and by every adapter, and written atomically with the fact (DOM-8, D69.2). [Q] (V9)
  - How a fact's values reach the state (which instance, which resources for the envelope, D107) is the concern of the domain's adapters, not of the business language nor of the library: a domain declares no entities, keys or primitives, and the shape of the state emerges from the business descriptions, its values and the names of its elements (LIB-14). [Q]
  - Consequences: a payload carries at least the values its change writes (D56: no thinner than that), and the fact log is enough to rebuild the state, so an event-sourced store can be added beside the materialized one (D69.2); a change of the shape of what a fact writes is a new version of the fact (DOM-22). [Q]
  - A command's branches are a record, each under a business name that says the case and what follows (`the_tool_is_available_so_it_is_lent`, `the_member_already_has_the_tool_so_nothing_changes`); they are tried in the order they are written, and the creator resolves them under the same names. [Q]
  - AND and OR take named components, each name a business phrase (`a_tool_has_the_requested_id: a_tool_has_this_id`); NOT takes one condition. The leaves of a composed condition are of one kind, predicates or the selection predicates of one subject (DOM-18), checked by type where it is composed: a mixed condition is valid nowhere, so it fails there, not where it is used; `command` and `query` check the kind they take, for a bare leaf. A composed condition is the zod schema of its parameters: one field per component, holding that component's parameters. The creator binds a branch's condition as an object of that schema, component by component: no parameter is shared by name, the same predicate may appear twice with different values, and a rejection can name the failed components. When values repeat, the author may write a function that builds the object. A predicate names each parameter after its value (`tool_id`, never `id`), so that an adapter that links domains (GR-3) reads it without guessing. [Q]
  - The interpretation, `dsl` (by convention `dsl.ts`, functions the library ignores, DOM-25), holds the domain's pure functions over its values (the value of a predicate, derived values, queries on the data), added at need and usable by the adapters, the interface and the client. At most it holds a pure implementation of the domain's own commands over its own values; the oracle that links the domains is an in-memory adapter built from these functions (TST-2). [Q] A component asks a question of a value only through a function of `dsl`, named as a sentence (`the_due_date_of_a_loan_started_on(context.now)`), never with an expression of the language (`===`, `?.`, a comparison): the expression stays inside the function. So the component reads as sentences, and the shape of a value, chosen for what matters to the domain and never for the cost of changing it, is known to its `dsl` alone; the oracle asks the same functions (TST-2). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3), A]
  - A business domain's own tree (SC-31, D105, D51 precision D105). A folder of a business domain may hold a node, as a persona's does, met as the library walks a use case's path down; what it accumulates is the domain's, true for whoever asks (D105.1): the guard "an ongoing loan" in `loans` (`business/loans/an_ongoing_loan/`). The node is the same builder as a persona's, whose parts a business node does not use (LIB-1), and reads as sentences (A, 18 Nov 2026): it `binds` the target, a schema, part of the request, which it gives down in the context (`context.loan_id`), never rewritten (D105.2), so that no command below declares it in its input nor binds it to another value (checked by type, at the use case, and on a node below); it `requires` a condition in the vocabulary of the domain's predicates (checked by type), whose parameters its `creator` binds from the target and the context. It is the one part of a node taken from the request: it still sees no leaf's input. The use case below is invoked at the leaf's path with the target beside its command's input, flat (`{ loan_id, tool_id, member_id, due_day }`); the library gives the target to the nodes and the rest to the command, and a point carries what the nodes above it add (`<node>_accumulated` in `_node.ts`, beside `_context` and `_closes`; `builders_at<context, closes, accumulated>`). The core composes the path's conditions into every branch of the command, after the duplicate branch, so that a retry after a success is still the duplicate (D105 precision on who composes); the adapter sees a self-sufficient command and no tree, its contract unchanged. Every leaf of the language has its value, path and branch alike, with no order nor short-circuit (D51 precision D105); a rejection names the failed predicates of the path once, with no branch, and never the node (`zz.PathFailure`, D105.4), so a persona's projection may read them (`extend_your_loan` tells a booking by someone else apart). A condition common to the leaves of a folder is its node's; one of a single leaf, its command's (D105.7): a command valid from several states with a branch for each stays above the state (`return_a_tool`, with "already returned"; the eve and the overdue), and a node serves at least two leaves (`extend_the_loan`, new, and `correct_the_tool_of_a_loan`, moved from SC-13, which now rejects a correction of a loan already back where it read "already corrected": the duplicate branch still answers a retry). A business folder below a domain's is of the domain, one store (DOM-8): its paths are in the domain's registry, its use cases among the domain's ports, and the doors of the domain carry it by its name (`loans.an_ongoing_loan.usecase.extend_the_loan`); it sees its ancestor through the door to the folders below (`_door_below.ts`), as `persona/member/` does. Rejected (A): the target in each leaf's input, read by the node (every leaf declaring it again, and a node reading the request's input). Left with their cases: a query below a business node (its condition selects, D105.3, in the vocabulary of selection predicates), a command below a node composed into another's transition (D111). [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026; A for the shape]
- **DOM-6** Every applied branch records a fact, even with an empty mutation: "nothing to do" is a branch, not a rejection. A rejection records nothing and names the failed predicates. D95, D54, D17
  - **Which predicates a rejection names** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (an interpreter rule, V10; D17, D51): the adapter gives the value of every leaf of every branch, read in one step (`Execution`); the library judges each branch's condition, bound by the creator as data (LIB-14), and names, by its components' names and its path, every leaf that fails where it stands: a leaf fails when its value is not the one its place asks for, so a leaf under NOT fails when it holds; AND names its components that fail, OR every component when none holds, NOT its operand's leaves that hold where it asks them not to; a condition that holds names nothing. The path's predicates are named once, with no branch, before the branches' (D105.4). Scenario: Gianni asks for the drill Marta has (SC-4): `the_tool_can_be_lent.the_requested_tool_is_not_on_loan`, the predicate `the_tool_is_on_loan` (it holds, under NOT), and `the_tool_is_on_loan_to_this_member` of the other branch; Luca adds the drill again (SC-1): `a_tool_has_this_id`, under the NOT of `the_tool_is_new`. Rejected: every leaf whose value is false (the drill's NOT would name nothing, and a leaf that holds would be named when NOT asks it to fail); the first failing component only (a short-circuit, which D51 excludes: the failures would depend on the order of evaluation). A composed transition's are named under its name and its branch's (DOM-19, part 4.3).
  - **A fact's name tells a happening** [Q, A, 18 Nov 2026 (8)]: a fact records that something happened, so its name says what happened, never a state (`a_tool_has_been_lent`, `the_repair_notice_round_has_ended`, not `the_round_is_complete`). The fact of a "nothing to do" branch is the applied fact's name with `again` (`a_tool_has_been_lent_again`, `a_tool_has_been_retired_again`): it records that the request was handled once more and changed nothing, which keeps a retry of the same operation at most once (the duplicate branch finds it, DOM-9). The branch's name keeps the case (`the_member_already_has_the_tool_so_nothing_changes`). DOM-6 stays as it is: A's doubt of 14 Nov 2026, "not a fact, a smell", was about the names. Scenario: Marta asks for the drill she holds (L11): [`a_tool_has_been_lent_again`]; the answer is lost, she returns the drill, her phone sends L11 again: the duplicate branch, `you_already_have_the_tool`, not a loan she did not ask for. Rejected: a branch applied with no fact (the retry would lend the drill); a rejection (an error for what the member wanted, and the same retry); names of the request (`a_return_has_been_asked_for_a_loan_already_returned`: long, and a noun invented per request).
  - **One fact per meaning, or one fact with a closed case** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (DOM-22, "the shape of the facts follows the business language"; the principle of alternative definitions: the library offers both forms, and a domain of q- takes the one its office speaks): **one fact per meaning when the office speaks of them as different happenings**, "it answered", "it did not answer" (ping pong, step 14: `a_pong_has_been_received`, `a_ping_has_had_no_response`); **one fact with a closed case when the happening is one and the case one of its details**, "the activity has ended, so" (the user's activity, step 15: `an_activity_has_ended { how_it_ended }`). The criterion is the office's, as a fact's name is (it tells a happening): how the office tells what happened, not what the node does after it. Rejected: one fact per meaning only when it changes what follows (it would bring ping pong to one fact, against a name that tells a happening); one form for every domain (the principle keeps both, the one that suits and a fallback).
- **DOM-7** The outcome is typed: the fact (branch, resolved values, outbox entries) or the rejection. The domain returns it raw; the persona projects it. A failure with no business meaning aborts the command and records nothing. D14, D17, D25
- **DOM-8** A fact is written atomically with the state, in its own domain's store. One atomic write touches one domain, even when all domains share one IndexedDB database. D42, D59 [Q]
- **DOM-9** The command port guarantees: same result as the oracle, when there is one (V10), all or nothing, isolation that covers what the command reads (the adapter's: there are no declared reads, V10). A serial writer (one command at a time, its reads inside its own sequence, one transaction per command) gets isolation and duplicates by construction; it still owes atomicity on failure and must never let a read see half a command. Two tabs of the PWA are two writers. D21, D22, D96
  - q- has **one writer per node**: the Web Locks API gives the node to one tab; the other tabs say that q- is open elsewhere and offer "use here", which takes the lock; the tab that loses it stops. The IndexedDB adapter is a serial writer, one transaction per command, and the durable consumers (effect executor, reactions, collection from the mailboxes) run only in the writer tab. [Q, plan]
  - **The duplicate branch is in the adapter's step** (SC-11): the plan of a command or a step carries its operation (`PlannedCommand`, `PlannedStep`), and the adapter, in the one consistent step that reads the conditions and writes, looks up first the fact the operation recorded and gives it back as stored, at its position (`AlreadyRecorded`); the library compares the fingerprint and names the branch. So the stored fact carries, opaque to the adapter as a part's version, the name of the branch applied and the fingerprint (`StoredEnvelope`), which never leave the node (PER-18). The step is isolated: commands at the same instant give the outcomes of some serial order (TST-5); a step the technology aborts for a conflict (a serializable transaction, the unique key of the facts' operation) the adapter runs again, whole, since the plan is data and nothing left the step. The oracle is a serial writer by construction: nothing awaits inside an execution (an interpreter rule). Scenario: on Thursday at 10:00 Marta (L11) and Gianni (L12) ask for the drill at the same instant; one has it (`you_have_the_tool`), the other reads `the_tool_is_not_available`, both predicates of `borrow_a_tool` failed, whichever order; Marta's phone, with no answer, sends L11 again while the first is in flight, and reads `you_have_the_tool` from the first's fact. With the lookup outside the step the retry would pass it before the first commits and meet the branches with L11 recorded: a second fact, `a_tool_has_been_lent_again`, for one operation, whose identity is its fact's (DOM-10). Rejected: the library looking up the log before the execution (the race above); the adapter comparing the fingerprints (an outcome more, and the comparison is the library's mechanism, LIB-1); the branch found from the fact's path (`retire_a_tool` records `a_tool_has_been_retired` in two branches); the library running the plan again on a conflict outcome (a technology's error is its adapter's). A branch renamed by a major, met by a duplicate recorded before it, comes with its case. [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026] Since part 4.3 the stored fact keeps the branch each part it composed applied too (`StoredEnvelope.composed`, DOM-19). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] Beside the step, the adapter gives the same lookup alone (`recorded`, `zz.RecordedBy`), a read outside any step, which a command leaf asks before its schema (PER-5, "A retry after the input's window over `now` moved"); the step still looks the operation up first, for two tries racing: the oracle reads the cell's operations, the SQL sketch selects the fact in a transaction of its own. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
- **DOM-10** The event envelope carries: path, schema version, payload, outcome, event id, operation id and fingerprint, correlation, attribution, the frame stack (each frame with its own time), the resources of the fact; a composite fact carries name, version and resources per part. D56, D99, D100, D107
  - The correlation (`zz.correlation`) names the chain of facts born of one intention: created at the origin, by the client if it gives one (D72.7), else by the entry, it reaches the command port beside the operation (`CorrelationContext`, which no creator sees) and goes into the envelope; a reaction invokes with its fact's, the effect executor returns with its entry's, the fact's that queued it (§ "La busta e la pila"). Not part of the operation's identity (DOM-11). In Shed one log holds every domain's facts, so a leaf follows another domain's from its outcome's position; with a log per domain the mapping of positions is open (a gap of the paper, D64.3). [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (5)]
  - The resources of a fact are the identities in its payload (DOM-24), each by its path and value (`loans/id/loan_id`, `L7`): the library derives them from the fact's schema, finding the identities in the registry; nobody writes them (V9). [Q, 11 Nov 2026] A fact may declare narrower ones (SC-55, below). Since part 4.1 [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: the library walks the payload with its schema, in the order of its fields: an identity holds its value, an object its fields', an optional, nullable or default schema its inner one's when the value is there, an array and a tuple their elements', a union the first option the value parses with; each resource once. The configurator gives a domain's command ports, beside its `registry`, the registries of the other domains whose identities its facts hold (`identities`; `loans`' hold `tools`' `tool_id`, GR-3), as it gives the read ports (DOM-18, "An entry's identity of another domain, and its path"): the oracle gives `loans` the registry of `tools` there; an identity no registry given names stops the port, never a resource left out in silence. Rejected: the identities found among the registries of the store's versions (an identity of a domain whose data the store does not keep, an account's, would have no path); the other domain's registry merged into the domain's (the shape until A's choice of 19 Nov 2026: one need with two shapes, the read ports' and the command ports', and a `registry` that no longer says whose port it is).
  - **A fact's narrower resources** (SC-55) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (6); A for the shape]: every identity of the payload stays the default; a fact may declare narrower ones, the instances it changes and its target (D94.6), naming the fields of its payload that carry them (`resources: ['reservation_id']`, as a leaf's `operation.identity` names a field of its input, SC-34). A field brings every identity it holds, at any depth (a whole loan, its tool and member too); a field that holds none, or an empty list, does not compile (`zz.ResourceFields`). The library writes them in the envelope, per part (D107, D113.3), as before: whoever reads them reads the envelope and changes nothing, the reactions' hold (SC-42), the executor's and an entry's resources (SC-53, D108), the touch of a read's entry (DOM-17). Resources never leave the node (PER-18) and are written with the part: a declaration changes no stored or published shape, so no version, and a fact recorded before it keeps the resources it was written with. Scenario: SC-53's again. On Tuesday the drill goes to the workshop (RP1), and the round notifies Gianni (R8) and Paolo (R9); the notice declares its target alone, the reservation (it changes nothing, D95, and the stay is the round's, whose own part keeps RP1). Gianni's notice, exhausted, holds R8 alone, and Paolo's notice of RP1 goes on Tuesday. On Saturday (RP2) Gianni's notice of RP2 waits behind his of RP1, Paolo's goes; on Monday the operator redelivers Gianni's letter, and his notice of RP2 follows. The order per resource still holds where it matters: a member never reads the second stay's notice before the first's. Rejected: a function of the payload (`resources: (notice) => [notice.reservation_id]`), opaque, since the library needs the identity's path and not its value alone, and the generator could not read it; a marker in the schema (`zz.a_resource(id.reservation_id)`), the declaration hidden among the fields and a fact without markers ambiguous between none and the default; dotted paths to an identity inside a field (a field brings its own, enough until a case asks); dropping the stay from the notice's payload (the fact carries what its readers need, V9). Whether the fields declared are the instances the branch changes and its target is the author's: the library cannot know the target. Since part 4.3 the envelope is a scenario on the oracle (`scenarios/sc-55.test.ts`): only the step of the round of the notices records the notice in Shed (part 7.4), so the scenarios' harness enters it alone, through a use case it declares at a path of its own (`alone` in `scenarios/oracle.ts`), a test's entry, never Shed's (DOM-2). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
  - A fact's identity is the operation that recorded it, its scope and id: one operation in its scope records one fact (DOM-11), so no event id of its own is kept. A position identifies a fact only in one adapter's log, which a migration may renumber; the operation travels with the fact. A reaction derives its operation's id from the fact's operation, its own path and the part (DOM-16, LIB-10), so that a fact redelivered after a crash is the duplicate branch, and the facts a reaction records carry an operation of their own, from which the next step derives. Scenario: Luca retires the ladder; the reaction's consumer falls before saving its position and delivers the fact again; one work sheet. [Q, 16 Nov 2026]
  - A fact is a list of parts, one per branch applied, in order (D111): a command records one, a step two, the step's part, then the composed transition's; every consumer reads them alike, with no second shape (A, 16 Nov 2026 (2)). A part carries its path, payload and resources, and is named by its position in the fact, which enters the operation of a reaction to it (DOM-16, LIB-10). A fact touches a stream's entry when one of its parts does. Scenario: the second step of the ladder's work sheet records one fact, [the cancellation has advanced (the ladder, its cursor at R5), a reservation has been cancelled (R5)]; Gianni's "my reservations" sees R5 leave; a reaction to the first part derives its operation from the fact's, its path and part 0. The schema version per part comes with its case. [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (2)] The version of a part is its domain's when it was written (DOM-22, `StoredPart.version`); the parsed part has none, a reader seeing the current shape only (D101, PER-18). [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (7)]

### Duplicates and identities

- **DOM-11** The duplicate branch is built into the command port and runs first: the same operation id in the same scope returns the recorded fact, at the same position; a different fingerprint is rejected with "identity already used". The scope is `Caller` + hat. A domain may replace this branch with its own. D79, D61, D100
  - For a letter: operation id = the letter's identity; scope = the sender's `Caller` + persona; fingerprint over the invoked use case and the letter's declared fields as sent, without its schema version (LIB-11). Another peer cannot reuse an identity. For `anonymous` every letter is a new operation (PER-4). D91 [Q] For a request to an extension's leaf the letter's identity is the request's (F7), and the scope the extension is given is the hat and the bond, never the `Caller`'s key (EXT-2). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
  - How long identities are kept is a deployment choice, at least as long as the sender can retry; beyond it natural idempotency covers. D61, D66, D79
  - q- keeps them **forever** in the prototype: an accepted letter is kept anyway as a message under its identity, so the record adds one row per message. Revisited with measurements. The window it must cover: the relay's 7 days, plus the sender's time offline, plus a mailbox read again from its start (G1). [Q, plan]
  - The entry composes the scope from the hat, the persona's segment of the path, and the `Caller`'s id (`member/A-7`), opaque to the library; the client creates the operation id, and a retry keeps it (DOM-12, D75.8). The library computes the fingerprint where the request enters a tree, at the leaf, over its path and its input as sent, after the schema and before defaults (D100), and carries it with the operation to the command port, which compares it (D79.5): a mechanism of no kind (LIB-1). Its form is LIB-11's (`fingerprint_of`, part 4.2). "Identity already used" is a third outcome of the command port (`CommandOutcome`), beside applied and rejected: no projection sees it, and it crosses a use case over another as it is; an exact duplicate is the recorded fact, projected as any. [Q, 13 Nov 2026]
  - An actor from outside names its requests in what it sends (D91.2): the leaf names the field of its input that holds the operation's id, and the entry gives the scope alone (SC-34, PER-4). [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (3); A for the shape]
  - **Where the duplicate branch runs** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026 (the go); Q, C, 19 Nov 2026 (the build)]: one comparison (the fingerprint the caller carries against the recorded fact's, `duplicate`), in three places. The command leaf, after the gate and its major's leaf, before its schema, nodes and limits (PER-5, "A retry after the input's window over `now` moved", V15). The business's port, a command's and a step's, first, before it parses its input: the same lookup the leaf asks (`$Recorded`, through the adapter's `recorded`, a read outside the step, DOM-9), so that a caller that is not a leaf, a reaction delivered again (DOM-16) or the executor's return (DOM-14), reads its fact after its input's window over `now` moved, and does not go to a dead letter as `invalid_request`. The adapter's step, which reads the operation in the transaction that writes, for two tries racing (SC-11, DOM-9). A request through a leaf is looked up twice, at the leaf and at the port: two reads, the second meeting a retry recorded in between, kept for a port the same whoever calls it. Measured on IndexedDB (WEB-17) and kept as built [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026], to be measured again at step 27 on Chrome for Android.
- **DOM-12** A retry keeps the operation id, never rebuilds the command. The user domain creates the id before the first attempt. Resource ids are generated on the node and also serve as keys. D60, D73, D62
  - An instance whose opener cannot name it takes an id the domain derives from the operation that opens it (D75.8: the domain chooses a resource's id per operation): `zz.identity_of(id.repair_id)(context.operation)`, a stable function of the library (LIB-10). One operation records one fact (DOM-11), so it opens one instance, and a retry or a redelivery, the duplicate branch, gives back the same. Scenario: Marta returns the drill damaged and a reaction sends it to repair, its translation reading only the fact; Luca sends a drill found broken on the shelf; either way `tools` names the stay in repair, which the warnings of step 9 (part 2) are keyed by. Rejected: an id made by the reaction, a construct of its own with no case (its translation is pure, from the fact). [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (4)]

### Effects and reactions

- **DOM-13** An outbox entry declares: effect name, payload, entry id (also the event id and the key sent outside), optional delay, optional return use case whose input is the closed set of meaningful outcomes. D108, D12
  - An effect is a component (`effect({ payload })`, path `business/loans/effect/<name>`), named by what is asked of the world. A branch queues its entries as an optional third part, by name (`[condition, fact, { wait_until_the_eve_of_the_due_date }]`), and the creator resolves each under the same name: `id`, from the domain's values (D48: one loan, one eve, so the loan's id), unique per effect, since the key is the pair (effect, id); `payload`; `not_before`, an instant the creator computes with the clock as a parameter (the paper leaves the form of the delay open): "not before", since after a downtime the entry goes late, never skipped. A creator reads no state, so a payload carries what the effect and its return need; the delivery links what is not of the domain (a phone, a tool's name). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3)] When one value of the domain does not name the entry, its id comes from several, encoded by the domain with no ambiguity (a function of its `dsl`). Scenario: the drill goes to repair twice while Paolo's booking three weeks on stands; keyed by the reservation, the second notice would be the first's double, dropped by the network, so `send_the_repair_notice` is keyed by the stay in repair and the reservation (`the_notice_of_the_repair_to_the_reservation`), which an operator reads on a dead letter. Rejected: the id from the step's operation (`identity_of`), right but opaque outside and a use with no need; a composite id encoded by the library, a feature with one case (LIB-3), to weigh at the second. [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (5), A]
  - The return use case names the effect it comes back from (`command_use_case({ command, returns_from })`), not the entry: in Shed a command that queues an effect and a use case over that command would import each other (`no-circular`). One return per effect, checked by the generator. A return use case is not in its domain's door: its entry is the executor (D92.1), so that no persona sends an effect a payload of its choice. Divergence of form from D108.1, not of meaning. [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3)]
  - A deadline, or a reminder that depends on the state, is a wait and a guarded return (D68.3): `borrow_a_tool` queues `wait_until_the_eve_of_the_due_date`; its return, `remind_the_member_of_the_due_date`, reminds the member (`the_member_has_been_reminded_of_the_due_date`, queuing `send_the_due_date_reminder`) only if they still have the tool, and otherwise records `the_loan_was_returned_before_the_reminder` (D95): no entry is ever cancelled, and Marta, who brought the drill back early, gets no reminder. A wait has no world: it is an effect delivered by the library's `returns_the_payload` (the payload back, as the return's input), never transient nor exhausted; a timer is not a construct (A). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3)] The deadline is the same shape: `borrow_a_tool` queues, with the same fact, `wait_until_the_due_date` too, both entries keyed by the loan's id (the key is effect and id); its return, `declare_the_loan_overdue`, records `a_loan_has_become_overdue` if the member still has the tool, else `the_loan_was_returned_before_it_became_overdue`. Time is not a fact (D92.4): a loan becomes overdue only by this command, so the change can enter a stream. The two waits are queued by the loan, not one by the other's return: neither decides the other. [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3)] Since `loans` 2.0.0 the overdue's return reads the day too, and a wait that comes before the due day is over is queued again under a key of its own (DOM-22, the effects in flight). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - No order holds between two effects, nor any link: a link is a business rule, written with a process (D108, the note of 13 Oct). So a return guards against coming late, never against coming in some order. The executor is down from the 13th to the 16th; Marta's eve (the 14th) and due date (the 15th) are both due when it restarts, in two delivery units: whichever comes first, "bring it back tomorrow" would reach her on the 16th. A guard on the stamp (`term` still `running`) covers only the due date coming first. The guard reads the time instead: `remind_the_member_of_the_due_date` reminds only if the due date on the loan's card is still ahead at the executor's `now` (`the_due_date_of_the_loan_is_still_ahead`, the instant a parameter, GR-8); else it records `the_due_date_came_before_the_reminder` (D95), and the overdue stamp stays the due date's to give. The clock is the executor's, which also waits for `not_before`: a due date still ahead means its wait is not yet delivered, so the guard covers both orders. Time stays no fact (D92.4): the decision is recorded, and a redelivery finds it (DOM-11). [Q, 15 Nov 2026]
  - **A recurrence** (SC-43) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (4)]: a business that repeats on a calendar is a wait whose return queues the next, under a key of its own, with the constructs there are (D68.3): no timer, no schedule of the library (a timer is not a construct, A, 13 Nov 2026 (3)). A first command begins it and the domain keeps that it runs, so that a second beginning is a branch that queues nothing (D95), never a second chain; each wait's id is the domain's, from the values that name it once (D48: the member and the Monday), so that a return delivered twice is the duplicate branch (DOM-11, DOM-14). Its return counts the next period from the executor's `now`, never from the period it waited for: an entry goes late, never skipped, and a wait that comes after its period is over does what the period asked only if the business says so (D95), and awaits the current period. In Shed the desk sends a member the week's summary every Monday (`loans`' `begin_a_weekly_summary`, the member's `send_me_my_week_on_mondays`; `wait_until_monday`, its return `sum_up_the_week_for_the_member`, `send_the_weeks_summary`): the summary carries the week only, and the phone reads the loans and the reservations when the member opens it, since a creator reads no state and a content read when queued would be a week stale. Scenario: on Thursday 19 November Marta asks for her week (`a_weekly_summary_has_begun`, the wait for Monday the 23rd at the start of the shed's day, keyed by M-12 and the 23rd); asked again, `a_weekly_summary_has_begun_again`. On the 23rd: [`the_weeks_summary_has_been_sent` (the 23rd, next the 30th)], the summary queued and the wait for the 30th. The executor is down from 28 November to Wednesday 9 December: the wait of the 30th comes on the 9th, its week over, [`a_past_weeks_summary_has_been_skipped` (the 30th, awaiting the 7th)], no summary of a past week; the wait of the 7th, already past, goes at once: Marta reads her week on the 9th, and the next wait is the 14th's. After a downtime of three weeks she gets one summary, of the week she is in. It asks nothing new of the library. Rejected: the next period counted from the one awaited (after a downtime, a wait per period missed, each delivered at once); the content in the payload (a creator reads no state; read when queued, a week stale); the delivery reading the domain's state for the content (the business in an adapter, ADP-1). Left with its case: stopping a summary, and beginning it again in the same week, whose waits would need the summary's own id in their key (as a stay in repair's, DOM-12).
- **DOM-14** The effect executor is an internal entry, not a persona: a durable consumer that delivers at least once with the entry id as key. Position, order and dead letters are kept per effect; every rejection by the return use case goes to dead letter. D92, D108, D65
  - `zz.effect_executor({ registry, returns })(deliveries)({ outbox, returns, state, clock })`, one per domain: its deliveries by effect path, exhaustive, each typed by the input of its effect's return (`Delivery`); the outbox per effect, the return ports, its durable state (positions, dead letters), its clock. It waits for `not_before`. The return's operation has the effect's path as scope ("system · executor of effect E", D91.3) and the entry's id as id, so the key (effect, id) is the duplicate branch's (DOM-11): a double report is deduplicated, another outcome is `identity_already_used`; the fingerprint covers the entry and what the world said (D108.3). The adapter records the entries with the fact and the write, atomically (`PlannedBranch.entries`, DOM-8, D12). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3)]
  - "Deliver M on bond L" is executed by `post`, which deposits the envelope with the letter's identity as key; the receiver deduplicates (DOM-11). [Q]
  - **An entry's resources, and the executor's hold** (SC-53) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (5)]: the executor keeps the order per resource within each effect, and a dead letter holds its entry's resources for its effect, as a reaction's holds its part's (DOM-16, SC-42; D108, D67.3). An entry's resources are those of the part whose branch queued it (D108, the note of 13 Oct; D107, D113.3): the library writes them with the entry (`zz.StoredEntry.resources`), from the envelope, so that the executor reads them without the fact; a translation leaves them as queued. A later entry of the effect that touches one of them is not delivered and waits behind (`zz.WaitingEntry`), holding its own resources in turn; the position goes on and the other resources flow; another effect of the same resources is never held (the delivery unit is the effect, D108). A wait is never exhausted, but its return may refuse it: held the same way. The executor's state keeps what it holds, in the order of the outbox, named by the key (effect, id) (`zz.ExecutorState`: `held`, `hold`, `release`, `discarded`, in place of `dead_letter`); an operator resolves a letter through the running executor (`zz.EffectExecutor`: `redeliver`, the effect again for an exhausted letter, the return again with what the world said for a refused one, never the effect, D108.4; `discard`, with a trace), and the entries behind it then go on, each as met for the first time, after its `not_before`; `zz.Redelivered` and `zz.Discarded` serve both consumers. Scenario: on Tuesday the drill goes to the workshop (RP1), and the desk's round notifies Gianni (R8, booked for Thursday) and Paolo (R9, three weeks on). Gianni's carrier refuses messages for a day: his notice is retried, then exhausted, a dead letter of `send_the_repair_notice`, holding the resources of its part, `a_member_has_been_notified_of_the_repair` (R8, RP1); Paolo's notice touches RP1 and waits behind it, holding R9. On Friday the drill is back; on Saturday Marta returns it damaged again (RP2), and the round's notices of RP2 to Gianni (R8) and to Paolo (R9) wait too; Gianni's due-date reminder for the ladder, another effect, goes. On Monday the operator redelivers Gianni's letter: he reads the notice of RP1, then the outbox's order goes on, Paolo's of RP1, Gianni's of RP2, Paolo's of RP2. Without the hold Gianni would have read on Saturday the notice of the second repair, and on Monday that of the first, a stay already over told last. Had the operator discarded the letter (the stay was over), the notices behind it would have gone on as after a delivery. The cost of q-'s resources (DOM-10, every identity of the part): the round's stay is one, so one member's dead notice holds the round's other notices of that stay; with the paper's (D94.6: the instances mutated and the target) the part would hold its reservation alone, a second definition of a fact's resources, which the notice takes since SC-55 (DOM-10): its reservation alone, so Paolo's notices flow, and Gianni's of RP2 still waits behind his of RP1. Rejected: the identities of the entry's payload (D108 says the part's; and the notice's payload names the member and the tool, so a dead notice would hold every notice of the drill, of any stay); the entry's own id alone (no two entries share it, so no order is kept); holding by the effect whole (one broken phone would stop every notice of the shed). Left with its case: an effect that tolerates reordering (D67.3), which came with SC-56 (below).
  - **An effect that tolerates reordering** (SC-56) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (6); A for the shape]: an effect declares its order (`order`, D64.4, D67.3): `'per_resource'`, the default, omitted, as above; `'any'`, an effect whose entries may reach the world in any order, whose dead letters hold nothing (`zz.DeliveryOrder`, an ADT that leaves room for a third case without a second key). The letter stays, shown, for the operator to redeliver or discard (D67.3: nothing is lost); the entries of its resources after it go on as if it were delivered, and none waits behind it. Its deliveries are in flight together, none waiting for another's end (F7) [Q, A, 20 Nov 2026]. **The position behind a delivery in flight** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: the unit's position is advanced, as below ("The effect executor, at runtime"), to the last entry up to which every entry is handled, so it stays behind the oldest delivery in flight (a request waits up to its time: ping pong's 60 seconds, longer for a feature that asks it), and after a fall every entry handled above it is delivered again: at least once, as ever, over a wider window. Its world answers a delivery met again as it answered it: the kernel's port of requests keeps, by the request's identity, the answer it gave (F7), so the return is the exact duplicate; another effect `any` whose world answers otherwise the second time meets D92.2's cost, a dead letter of `identity_already_used`, the first outcome standing. Scenario on the oracle: Anna pings Bruno (P1, his phone off), then Carla (P2); Carla's pong comes and P2's return records it; Anna's phone is closed before P1's time, the position still behind P1. Started again: P1 meets its request again, still waiting, then `no_response` at its time; P2, delivered again, reads Carla's pong from the port's answer, Carla's phone now off or not, and its return is the duplicate: one pong, no dead letter. Rejected: the position advanced past a delivery in flight (a fall would lose it); the executor keeping the entries handled above its position (F7, rejected there). **At runtime** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.3): a due entry of an effect `any` is delivered without waiting for those in flight; each lands with its outcome, read as one delivered alone (a letter, its return invoked), and the position goes past it once every entry before it is handled; an operator's request is served between two landings, as between two deliveries. A stop abandons the deliveries in flight: an answer that comes once the executor is stopping is not read and its return not invoked, so that the executor started again meets the entry again (EXT-15: a request outlives a stop); a delivery that throws stops its effect, as before, the others in flight abandoned. The library's test on Shed's summary, the effect `any` of its test domains (SC-56, a network that hangs). Rejected: the stop waiting for the deliveries in flight (a request waits up to its time, so an extension's deactivation, EXT-15, would wait 60 seconds or more); the answer of an abandoned delivery read after the stop (the stopped executor would write its state while another, started again, runs over it). The declaration is the effect's, in the domain: whether an entry delivered late or out of order harms is the business's meaning of the effect, not a choice of the deployment. Scenario: Marta's carrier refuses her messages for a week; her summary of Monday 16 November (`send_the_weeks_summary`) is exhausted, a dead letter; the wait for Monday 23 goes on, another effect. On Monday 23 her next summary, which touches her (the part `the_weeks_summary_has_been_sent`), goes: each summary names its Monday, and the phone reads her loans and reservations live, so an older one read after a newer one tells nothing false. With the order per resource it would wait behind the letter until an operator acts, and Marta would read no summary for weeks. The repair notice keeps the default: with SC-55 Paolo's notices already pass Gianni's, and Gianni's second must not overtake his first (SC-53). Rejected: a boolean (`tolerates_reordering: true`, the paper's words, where the code wants cases); the order in the executor's wiring, per delivery (a business choice moved into the deployment, and a second executor of the same domain could choose otherwise). A reaction that tolerates reordering takes the same key with its case (D67.3 names any consumer); no reaction of Shed does.
  - **The effect executor, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.1; D108, D67, D91.3, D100, DOM-10, DOM-11, DOM-13, DOM-15, DOM-22, CFG-2): `effect_executor` runs one delivery unit per effect of its registry, each over the outbox of that effect from its position, inclusive, the entry at the position left out (it was handled, as a stream drops the fact at P, DOM-17). An entry goes when its `not_before` has come by the executor's clock; the due ones one at a time, in the order of the outbox. Its payload is read at the current shape (`Versions`, the one place, DOM-22), delivered, and what the delivery answers read as `Delivered` (one that is none of its cases is the delivery's fault, thrown): `transient`, the same entry tried again after a pause, the effect's later entries behind it; `exhausted`, a dead letter with its reason; `delivered`, for an effect with a return its port invoked with what the world said, under the operation (the effect's path, the entry's id), with the entry's correlation (below), the fingerprint over the return's path and what the world said as sent (LIB-11: the executor is the retrier, D108.3), and the context of the clock (`now` and its zone); applied, an exact duplicate too, the entry is handled; rejected, `identity_already_used` or `invalid_request`, a dead letter with what the world said (`EffectDeadLetter.cause`; `invalid_request`, DOM-21, a cause of its own, redelivered as a rejection; the failures by their paths as the trace names them, `TracedFailure`, a dead letter being of no domain's type). The position is advanced to the last entry up to which every entry is handled, delivered or held: an entry delivered while an earlier one is not yet due is delivered again after a fall (at least once, the key deduplicating, DOM-14). The executor takes, beside its clock, the time that passes (`zz.Elapsed`, required, the configurator's, as a tree's that waits, DOM-3): it reads the clock and asks to be woken when the next entry comes due, or a transient one is to be tried again; the domains have no clock that rings (GR-8). A technical abort, a delivery or a return's port that throws (the network fell, the adapter aborted), stops its effect where it is, unadvanced, and `stop` reports it: the entry comes again when the executor starts again, never taken for an answer. An entry carries the correlation of the fact that queued it (`StoredEntry.correlation`, written by the library from the envelope with its resources, left as queued by a translation), so that the return's fact is of the chain the queuing fact began (DOM-10, A, 16 Nov 2026 (5)): Marta's reminder is of her borrowing's chain. `redeliver` and `discard` act on a letter through the running executor (DOM-14); its holds since part 7.2 (below, "The executor's holds, at runtime"). At wiring (CFG-2), in one message per stage: an effect of the registry whose entries the store's versions do not read (DOM-22); a return use case not in the registry, of no effect of the registry (DOM-13), two returns of one effect; a delivery missing or of no effect (the types say so too); a return's port that is not the library's port of its use case. The pause before a transient delivery is tried again (a minute) is implementation freedom (D114). Scenarios on the oracle: SC-35 (on the eve of her due day Marta still has the drill and is reminded, her phone receives the reminder keyed by the effect and the loan; the drill back first, `the_loan_was_returned_before_the_reminder`, no reminder; no phone, a dead letter `exhausted`; the network down twice, the reminder tried again after a pause and delivered; the network falls, the executor stops, and started again sends it), SC-36 (the due date's wait stamps the loan `a_loan_has_become_overdue`, or records it came back first; every entry the outbox gives twice, and the executor falling before saving its position, one stamp: the duplicate branch, TST-4), SC-37 (down from the 14th to the 18th, the eve's return records `the_due_date_came_before_the_reminder`, the due date's the overdue, whichever comes first), SC-38 (Marta's waits of 1.x reach their returns at 2.0.0's shape; the overdue wait of 10:12 on her due day puts itself off to the 17th under a key of its own), SC-43 (the week's summary on the 23rd and the wait for the 30th; down from the 28th to 9 December, the past week skipped, the 7th's at once, the next the 14th's). Rejected: the executor waiting for an entry's `not_before` before meeting the next of its effect (one wait not yet due would hold every later one, below); the position advanced past an entry not yet due (a fall would lose it); the next attempt of a transient entry left to the clock alone (nothing would wake the executor); a technical abort taken as transient (a defect of an adapter tried again in silence, F6) or as exhausted (an operator's letter for a failure with no meaning to the world); the return invoked with the correlation of the executor's own (the chain of Marta's borrowing would stop at the eve); the fingerprint over the entry's payload beside what the world said (the operation names the entry already, and a wait's return reads the payload as what the world said).
  - **The order of an effect's entries that wait** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.1; D108, D64.4): an entry not yet due holds nothing: a later entry of its effect already due goes first, whatever its resources, and the due ones go in the order of the outbox. The order per resource is the outbox's among the entries due; an entry's `not_before` is when it may go, not a place in a queue. Scenario: on 5 November Gianni borrows the ladder, due on the 19th, and the loan queues its due date's wait for the 20th; on the 16th Marta's overdue wait of 1.x, put off, queues the next for the 17th (SC-38): later in the outbox, due first, it goes on the 17th. No two entries of one resource and one effect wait in the other order in Shed (an extension queues the eve of its new due day, later on both counts, SC-31; a correction the eve of the right tool, SC-13), so the order per resource is kept by the outbox there. Rejected: every entry of an effect behind the first not yet due (Marta's put-off wait behind Gianni's, three days late; the executor's own first design, "Rejected" above); the order of the outbox kept per resource among entries not yet due (no case in Shed, LIB-3; a shorter wait of one member behind a longer one would go late for nothing).
  - **The executor's holds, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.2; D108, D67.3, D64.4, DOM-10, SC-53, SC-55, SC-56): an effect's unit reads, before each delivery, what its state holds (`ExecutorState.held`, in the order of the outbox), the one place where a hold lives, so that a fall loses none.
