An entry that comes due is held when the effect holds it: in the order per resource, an entry the effect holds, a dead letter or one waiting, shares a resource with it, by path and value (`StoredEntry.resources`, its part's, declared or every identity, SC-55); then it is not delivered and waits (`WaitingEntry`, `hold`), holding its own in turn, and counts as handled for the position, which goes on, the other resources flowing. An effect whose order is `any` (`$Order`, the effect's own, SC-56) holds nothing: its dead letters stay, shown, and nothing waits behind them. An entry met again that the state holds, by its key (effect, id), is held once, never delivered again: a fall before the position was saved, or the outbox giving it twice (TST-4). What an operator asks of a letter (`redeliver`, `discard`) is served by the letter's effect's unit, between two deliveries, so that one effect delivers one entry at a time and the entries behind the letter go in its order; it acts on the letter as the state holds it now, by its key (a waiting entry is no letter, and a letter no longer held is `not_held`); a stopped or fallen executor serves no operator, the request refused with why. Redelivered: exhausted, the effect again; refused (`rejected`, `identity_already_used`, `invalid_request`), the return again with what the world said, never the effect (D108.4); delivered, the letter released; refused or exhausted again, held still with what was said this time (`hold` replaces it by its key). Discarded: released, with its trace (`discarded`: who, why, when by the executor's clock). Then, before the entries it meets, the unit delivers the entries waiting that go on, one at a time, in the order the state keeps them, each as met for the first time, after its `not_before`: one that no dead letter of the effect holds and that shares no resource with an entry waiting before it, gone on or not, so that the order per resource holds among them too; delivered, released; refused, a dead letter in its place, still holding those behind it. A waiting entry stays held in the state until it is delivered, so that an executor that falls between a redelivery and the entries behind it delivers them when it starts again. Scenarios on the oracle: SC-53 (on Tuesday Gianni's notice of RP1, tried again, then exhausted, holds R8 alone, and Paolo's of RP1 goes; on Saturday his notice of RP2 waits behind it, Paolo's of RP2 goes, and Gianni's reminder of the ladder, another effect, goes; on Monday the letter redelivered, RP1 then RP2; discarded instead, RP2 goes on; refused again, it holds still; the executor fallen and started again, what it held held once; fallen after the redelivery before RP2, started again, RP2 goes; every entry given twice, each held once; a stopped executor serves no operator; a wait whose return refuses it, `identity_already_used`, holds its part's resources, every identity of the borrowing: Marta's eve of the ladder waits behind her eve of the drill, the cost DOM-10 declares, and the letter redelivered meets the first outcome again, then discarded), SC-55 (Gianni with no phone: his notice holds R8 alone, Paolo's of the same stay goes), SC-56 (Marta's summary of the 16th exhausted, her summary of the 23rd goes past it; redelivered, the 16th's arrives after the 23rd's; every entry given twice and a fall, the letter held once and nothing behind it). Rejected: an operator's request run beside the unit (two deliveries of one effect at once, and the entries behind the letter racing the unit's next one); the waiting entries that go on put back among the entries the unit meets (the position, the low-water mark of the outbox, would go back); the letter redelivered as the operator passed it (a letter refused again since keeps its new cause in the state, and the operator's copy would be stale); a hold kept in the unit's memory beside the state (a fall would forget it, and an entry met again would be delivered past its letter).
  - **The holds follow the order of the entries due** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.2, from "The order of an effect's entries that wait"): a hold is decided when an entry comes due, against every entry its effect holds then, whatever their places in the outbox: an entry not yet due holds nothing and is not held. So the order per resource the executor keeps is the order in which the entries come due, the outbox's among those due at once: an earlier entry of the outbox not yet due, met after a later one of the same resource already in a dead letter, waits behind it, since the later one went first. No two entries of one resource and one effect come due in the other order in Shed (the rule above), so no scenario meets the difference. Rejected: a hold by the order of the outbox (only an entry after the letter in the outbox held): the executor would have to compare positions, which are the adapter's, opaque (DOM-17), or an entry already handled would have to be met again; and the earlier entry would reach the world before the letter's redelivery, after the later one was already tried.
- **DOM-15** Effects fail in three ways: transient (retried), definitive with a meaning (back as a business fact through the return use case), exhausted (dead letter, never a business fact by itself). D67
  - A delivery answers with an ADT (`Delivered`): `delivered`, with `returns` (the return's input, a definitive outcome with a meaning among them) only if the effect has a return; `transient`; `exhausted`, with its reason. A definitive failure of an effect with no return (a member with no phone) exhausts it: an operator sees it. A dead letter's cause is an ADT: `exhausted` (redelivered, the effect runs again), `rejected` by the return (what the world said kept with the failures; redelivered, the return runs again, never the effect), `identity_already_used` (the first outcome stands, D108.4). The oracle's world: `adapter/memory/reminders.ts`. [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3)]
  - On a phone the operator is the user: dead letters are shown, and one must not silently freeze a whole conversation. [Q, F6]
- **DOM-16** A reaction is "when fact E, command C": it reads only the fact, translates it purely, invokes at most one command per part of the fact, through a command use case. Its operation id is deterministic, from (event, reaction, part); a rejection goes to dead letter; each reaction is a delivery unit of its own. Its `Caller` is internal ("system · reaction R"), with no hat. D88, D89, D91, D113
  - A reaction is a component (`reaction({ when, invokes, translation })`, path `reaction/retirement/reaction/the_reservations_of_the_tool_are_cancelled`): `when`, the fact, from the source domain's door (a component that is no fact does not compile); `invokes`, a command use case, from the target's door; `translation`, pure, from the fact's payload to the invoked one's input (D89.3). It reads no state and has no context: the command's `now` is the entry's clock. Its path is its identity, stable across releases (D89.5): the scope of its operations and its delivery unit. A translation that gives nothing (D88.2) comes with its case. [Q, 15 Nov 2026] A reaction reacts to each part of its fact's path, at most one command per part, its operation from the fact's, its path and the part (DOM-10, D113.3). [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - A reaction cannot choose the instances it acts on, since it reads no state: when an upstream fact touches a set of downstream instances chosen by a condition (the reservations of a retired tool), it invokes one command that opens a process with a cursor in the downstream domain, one instance a step (DOM-20, D97, D112, D113.4); a set is never changed by one fact, whose size and transaction would have no bound. The process lives in the domain whose life cycle it walks (DOM-23): `loans`' `cancel_the_reservations_of_a_tool` opens the desk's work sheet (`cancellation_of_reservations`), keyed by the tool, since a retirement is final; if a repair cancels reservations too (step 9), the sheet takes an id of its own, its cause's. A second sheet for one tool is a rejection, to the dead letter: no expected occurrence (D89, D95). [Q, 15 Nov 2026]
  - The execution (D89.6, D65.6): `zz.reaction_consumers({ registry })(wirings)({ state, clock })`, one durable consumer per reaction, each its own delivery unit with its own position and dead letters, wired by path to the fact log of its fact's store and to the port it invokes (a reaction not wired does not compile). It reads from its position (from the first fact with none: in Shed every reaction is there from the start; where one added later starts is the deployment's, D64.3), skips the parts of other paths, invokes for each part of its path with its operation (DOM-10) and the clock's `now`; applied, the next part; rejected or identity already used, to its dead letter (with the part), then the next part; after the last part it advances; a technical abort does not advance, and the fact comes again, the duplicate branch covering what was applied. Two reactions run in parallel; one reaction may deliver facts of disjoint resources in parallel, partitioned by resource, one consumer active per partition: the order asked is per resource, and the partitions are the library's and the adapter's, not the domain's. [Q, 16 Nov 2026] Since SC-42 a dead letter holds its part's resources (below); at runtime since part 7.3 ("The consumers of the reactions, at runtime", below).
  - **A dead letter holds its resources** (SC-42) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (4)]: for its reaction, a dead letter holds the resources of its part, read in the envelope (D94.7, D67.3, D107; per part, D113.3): a later part of the reaction's path that touches one of them is not delivered and waits behind it (`zz.WaitingPart`), whatever its translation would give (`nothing` too); a waiting part holds its own resources in turn, since otherwise a later part of one of them would overtake it, and the order per resource is the contract (D64.4, LIB-8). The position goes on past a held part, and the other resources flow: a dead letter stops its resources, never its reaction nor the others (D89.6). The consumer's state keeps what it holds, in the order of the log, each part named by its fact's operation and its position in the fact, so that a part met again after a fall is held once (`zz.ReactionState`: `held`, `hold`, `release`, `discarded`; `dead_letter` before). An operator resolves a letter through the running consumers, which own its delivery and the order behind it (`zz.ReactionConsumers`): `redeliver`, the part again with the same operation, after a fix, delivered (the command applied, or nothing to invoke) or refused again and still holding; `discard`, with a trace (who, why, when), the part never delivered. Either way the consumer then delivers the parts that waited behind it and that nothing held before them still holds, in the order of the log, each as a part met for the first time: one refused becomes a dead letter in its place. Correcting a domain's data stays a command of a persona (D67.3). Scenario: on Tuesday Marta returns the drill damaged (F1, L7); a defect of Tuesday's release of the SQL adapter of `tools` reads the drill as neither in service, in the workshop nor retired, so no branch of `send_a_tool_to_repair` holds, and `reaction/damage` puts F1's part in its dead letter, holding L7, the drill and Marta; Wednesday's release fixes the defect, and Gianni borrows the drill, still in service in `tools` (a fact of another path, skipped); on Thursday he returns it damaged (F2, L9), which touches the drill: it waits behind F1, and holds L9 and Gianni. On Friday the operator redelivers F1: the drill goes to the workshop, its stay named by F1's operation and followed by Marta's correlation; then F2: `a_tool_has_been_sent_to_repair_again`. Without the hold, F2 would have opened the stay on Thursday, the shed's record saying the drill broke in Gianni's hands, and F1, redelivered, would have found it already in the workshop. Had the operator discarded F1, F2 would go on as after a delivery. The cost of q-'s resources, every identity of the payload (DOM-10): the member is one too, so the hold spreads by member; Gianni's sander, returned damaged on Thursday afternoon, waits behind his drill, and reaches the workshop on Friday. Rejected: holding only the dead letter's resources (D94.7 as written), which would let Gianni's sander overtake his drill; the hold by the fact rather than the part (D67.3, by part); the redelivery as a write to the state that the consumer picks up (two deliverers of one resource). Left with its case: a reaction that tolerates reordering (D67.3), with the key of the effects (`order: 'any'`, SC-56, DOM-14); the executor's hold, per effect, came with SC-53 (DOM-14). Corrected at part 7.3 [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: the cost by member does not hold in Shed, since `a_tool_has_been_returned` names the loan and the tool, not the member (a fact a reaction translates names what the reaction needs, below, A, 16 Nov 2026 (4)), so the part holds L7 and the drill, and Gianni's sander, returned damaged on Thursday afternoon, shares nothing with his drill and reaches the workshop that day; the cost stands for a fact that names its member, and Shed has none a reaction reads.
  - **The consumers of the reactions, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.3; D88, D89, D89.6, D65.6, D67.3, D91.3, D94.7, D100, D113.3, DOM-10, DOM-11, DOM-21, DOM-22, SC-40, SC-42, CFG-2): `reaction_consumers` runs one delivery unit per reaction of its registry, side by side, each over the fact log it is wired to from its position, inclusive, the fact at it left out (it was handled, as a stream drops the fact at P, DOM-17), from the first fact the log keeps with none. Of each fact, every part whose path is the reaction's fact's (found by the store's versions, `Versions.path`, the one place), in the order of the parts: one the state holds, by its fact's operation and its position in the fact, is held once, never delivered again; one that shares a resource, by path and value, with a part the reaction holds, a dead letter or one waiting, waits behind it (`WaitingPart`), whatever its translation would give; any other is read at the current shape (`Versions.part`), translated, and, unless the translation gives `nothing` (D88.2), its use case invoked under `reaction_operation` (LIB-10), with the fact's correlation (the chain the fact began: Marta's return and the workshop's fact are one chain, DOM-10), the fingerprint over the use case's path and the input as translated (LIB-11: the consumer is the retrier, D108.3), and the context of its clock (`now` and its zone): applied, an exact duplicate too, it is handled; rejected (its failures by their paths, as the trace names them, a step's read by its path: `TracedFailure`, `ReadFailure`, a dead letter being of no domain's type), `identity_already_used` or `invalid_request` (DOM-21, a cause of its own), a dead letter holding the part's resources. The position is advanced past each fact once its parts are handled. What an operator asks of a letter (`redeliver`, `discard`) is served by its reaction's unit, between two facts, on the letter as the state holds it now, by its key (a waiting part is no letter, and a letter no longer held is `not_held`; a stopped or fallen consumer serves no operator, the request refused with why): redelivered, the part again, translated again, with the same operation, after a fix; delivered (applied, or nothing to invoke), the letter released; refused again, held still with why this time. Then, before the facts it meets, the unit delivers the parts waiting that go on, one at a time, in the order of the log: one that no dead letter holds and that shares no resource with a part waiting before it; delivered, released; refused, a dead letter in its place. A technical abort (the port or the log throws, a version the store's versions do not know, a translation that throws) stops its reaction where it is, unadvanced, and `stop` reports it: the fact comes again when the consumers start again, the duplicate branch covering the parts already applied (D65.6). The log that keeps the reaction's position no longer (`position_unavailable`) is a fall too: the facts between are lost to the reaction, which no consumer can tell in silence (F6). What the consumers share with the executor, the holds per resource and the operator's lane, is one module of the library (`consumer.ts`), which knows resources, held items and requests, never a domain. **One partition per reaction**: the consumer keeps the log's order, so the order per resource (D64.4); partitions by resource, one consumer active per partition, its position the partition's, come with an adapter that partitions its log (the library's and the adapter's, A, 16 Nov 2026), and change neither a reaction nor its holds. At wiring (CFG-2), in one message per stage: a reaction to a fact of no domain whose majors the store's versions were given (DOM-22); a reaction not wired, a wiring of no reaction (the types say so too); a port that is not the library's port of the use case the reaction invokes. Scenarios on the oracle: SC-40 (on Monday Paolo returns the ladder whole, nothing; on Tuesday Marta returns the drill damaged, and it goes to the workshop under the reaction's operation, of her chain, its stay named by that operation, SC-7; Gianni cannot borrow it on Thursday; the consumer fallen before saving its position, every fact given twice, the adapter fallen and the consumer started again: one stay), SC-42 (above: on Tuesday a defect of the adapter of `tools` refuses Marta's return, a dead letter holding L7 and the drill; Gianni's return of the drill waits behind it, his sander goes; on Friday the letter redelivered, the drill to the workshop under F1's reaction, then Gianni's `a_tool_has_been_sent_to_repair_again`; discarded instead, his return opens the stay; redelivered with the defect back, held still; fallen and started again from the first fact, held once; every fact given twice, held once; fallen after the redelivery, started again, Gianni's return goes; stopped, no operator served), SC-7 (the stay of a damaged return named by the reaction's operation, derived from Marta's return), SC-27 end to end (the workshop's fact recorded by `reaction/damage/` with her return's correlation ends her flow). Rejected: the hold decided after the translation (a part translated to `nothing` would overtake a letter of its resource, DOM-16 above); an operator's request run beside the unit (two deliveries of one reaction at once, and the parts behind the letter racing the next fact); the parts that go on put back among the facts met (the position would go back); a hold kept in the unit's memory beside the state (a fall would forget it); `position_unavailable` skipped to the log's next fact (the facts between lost in silence); the reaction's path alone as the operation's id with the fact's in its scope (the scope is the reaction's, "system · reaction R", D91.3, the key the duplicate branch reads).
  - A reaction between two steps of one domain's process lives in that domain (`business/loans/reaction/…`, its scope the folder's facts and use cases): it moves no other office, and with no second domain there is no cycle to avoid. Between domains it lives above them (GR-9). So a process across domains keeps its state in the domain that owns its life cycle, or in a domain dedicated to processes when none does (DOM-23, step 9): the reactions that cross the domains live above them, those of the process to itself live with its state. In Shed the desk's work sheet moves itself on by two reactions of `loans`: one to its opening (`the_first_reservation_on_the_sheet_is_taken_up`), one to the step's part (`the_next_reservation_on_the_sheet_is_taken_up`), each translating to the tool; no one reacts to the closing. Who launches the first step is a gap of the paper (D97, D111-D113), filled by a reaction to the opening, with the constructs there are. Rejected: the opening composing the first step, which would read a sheet not yet written; a reaction with several `when`, a construct with no second case (LIB-3). [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (3)]
  - A translation may give `nothing` (D88.2), a value of the library: the consumer invokes no command and goes on to the next part. Scenario: Paolo returns the ladder whole, Marta the drill damaged; `reaction/damage/` sends only the drill to repair, its test a function of the procedure's own `dsl` (`the_tool_came_back_damaged`), since a reaction sees of a business domain its facts and use cases only. Rejected: two facts for a whole and a damaged return, since the branch would be chosen by a value of the input, not by the state, a predicate with no state that only the adapter could evaluate (ADP-1); for the desk it is one return, whole or damaged. A fact a reaction translates names what the reaction needs: `a_tool_has_been_returned` names the tool, which the member gives in the input and the desk checks against the loan's card (`the_loan_is_of_this_tool`): the member says which tool they hold, a claim of the world the desk verifies, not a copy of the state (A). Rejected for this case: the loan read by the command (`reads`), which the input makes needless; `reads` in a command waits for a case where the input cannot stand in (DOM-20). [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (4)]
  - The repair warns, it does not cancel (A, a choice of business left to C for what it asks of the library, 16 Nov 2026 (4)): when a tool goes to repair, the desk warns the members with a reservation ahead of it ("the drill you booked is in repair"), and the reservations stay; on the day, a tool still in repair is refused to the borrower by `the_tool_is_in_service`. Cancelling them all would lose Paolo's booking three weeks on for a two-day repair. The warnings are a process with a cursor in `loans` (DOM-20), opened by the repair, a fact of `tools`, through a reaction above (Luca's drill found broken on the shelf has no damaged return, A), and keyed by the stay in repair (`tools`' `repair_id`, DOM-12): the drill repaired twice opens two sheets, so the key is its cause's, as said for a repair on 15 Nov. Its step composes a transition that queues an effect, the notice. [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (4)] In the code (step 9, part 2), in business names (A): the desk's round of the notices of a repair (`repair_notice_round`), begun by `notify_the_members_of_a_repair` from `reaction/repair/`, walked by `continue_the_repair_notice_round` over the selection of the cancellation's sheet, which composes `notify_a_member_of_the_repair`: no change of state, a fact and the notice queued (D95). A reservation cancelled in the meantime by the retirement's sheet is no longer booked and leaves the round's selection. [16 Nov 2026 (5)]

### Reads and streams

- **DOM-17** A fact-stream read goes through the read port: a query and an opaque position; delivery at least once, deduplicated by event id, ordered per resource. Base events: entering and leaving the selection, caught up, "position no longer available" (re-read the state, then resume). Resume from a position is inclusive. D64, D72, D73
  - Collecting from a mailbox is this read; positions are compared only by order. [Q]
  - The events are a closed ADT (`zz.StreamEvent`): the fact, when it touches a candidate that the selection keeps after it, with the entry as it is after it (no "changed" event: a candidate that comes in through a fact that touches it comes this way); entered, for a candidate the fact did not touch that now satisfies the selection; changed, for a candidate the fact did not touch that stays and whose entry is no longer the same, with the entry (SC-19, below); left, touched or not, with the identity only; caught up, once; "position no longer available", terminal. Every event but the terminal one carries the position of the fact that caused it. D72, D72.5, D73.3 [Q, 11 Nov 2026]
  - An adapter's stream starts from a position only; the read use case composes the read at P and the stream from P (DOM-2), so that a live view has no gap and no double (D72). "From now" and "from the beginning" (D64.3) come when a case asks for them. A consumer deduplicates by position and identity: one fact per position, so the pair is the identity derived from fact and resource (D72); the envelope gets an id of its own with its case (DOM-10). [Q, 11 Nov 2026]
  - The adapter records the fact with its write, atomically (DOM-8), and the command's outcome carries its position: the plan gives the adapter the fact in its envelope. [Q, 11 Nov 2026]
  - An event names its entry by the query's identity (LIB-9). A fact touches an entry when the entry's identity is among its resources (DOM-10). The oracle defines the events of a fact (V10): the entries before and after it, compared by identity; an entry kept after it that the fact touches is the fact; one that appears untouched entered; one that disappears left, touched or not; an untouched entry that stays gives nothing if it is the same, `changed` if not (compared by value, since SC-19, below). Interpreter rule: the events of one fact come in the order of the entries after it, then those that left, in their order before it. Other adapters find the entries a fact may change as they can (re-reading, routing by resources), and are compared with the oracle (TST-2). [Q, 11 Nov 2026]
  - A read use case names the facts that a use case over it may name in its stream (`facts: [fact.a_loan_has_become_overdue]`, the facts themselves, as a command names its own; a component that is no fact does not compile): the notices that go with its register, the domain's choice, as a producer declares what it exports to its readers (D64.2, D101.4, D39). A fact is named when (A, 14 Nov 2026) it touches an entry and leaves it in the selection (one that takes it out comes as `left`, its identity only); it tells a reader something the entry does not, an occurrence and not a state ("has just fallen overdue", not "is overdue"); and a reader uses it (LIB-3). The first is mechanical, a check of the generator (the fact holds the query's identity), deferred with the others; the other two are the author's. A fact it does not name is no fault: it reaches the use cases over it as the entry it leaves. The door does not change: the facts travel in the type of the use case, which it exposes, and a use case over it names each by the name in its path (PER-18). [Q, 14 Nov 2026] A named fact may be a part of a fact (D111), and the three tests hold for the part: the step of the desk's round of notices records [the round advanced (RP1, its cursor), Gianni notified (R8, RP1)]; `the_reservations_of_a_member` names the notice, and `member/my_reservations` reads it on R8's row as `the_tool_you_booked_is_in_repair`. The projection takes the part named among those that touch the entry; the library finds it from the envelope and the query's identity (the touch is the rule above, per part), so the stream's event does not change and an adapter marks nothing more. A cancellation by the work sheet comes as `left`, unnamed. Two named parts touching one entry come with their case: a step's part touches its process, the transition's the instance it composes. Rejected: the step's part deciding (it does not touch R8); naming only facts of one part (the notice would be recorded alone, outside the step, a second shape, DOM-10); an event per part (it breaks the pair position and identity); the adapter marking the parts that touch, or giving them alone (a datum the library derives, and the fact no longer the log's). [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (6)]
  - A query's stream sees only the facts of its own domain, as if each domain had a log of its own (DOM-8, D64.3): the native stream by the adapter, the composed one by the library, which skips the facts whose paths are not in the domain's registry. A fact's resources stay every identity in its payload, another domain's too (DOM-10). Scenario: Marta borrows the drill; `a_tool_has_been_lent` holds the drill's id, of `tools`; in Shed's single log it would touch the drill in the visitor's catalogue, a `fact` of `loans` typed and parsed as `tools`', and an upstream domain would see a downstream one's fact (GR-1). A fact of another domain that changes what an entry reads, or whether a selection keeps it (a predicate over another domain's state, GR-3), gives no event: the note below. The log a leaf follows by correlation (DOM-3) is every domain's: another matter. Rejected: a stream seeing the facts of the domains its domain sees (GR-1), right for a selection reading upstream state, with no case in Shed (LIB-3), one of the ways of the note below; resources of one's own domain only (the round's `repair_id` would be none, D94.7); parts typed as any part. [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (6)] A stream still carries only its own domain's facts; since SC-19 (below) another domain's facts count for what they change of the entries. [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026]
  - An entry that reads another domain (`tool_name`, from `tools`) changes when a fact of that domain changes what it reads, and the stream says nothing: the consumer sees the old value until it reads again. The paper is silent (D71 reads another domain as a parameter, fixed). No case in Shed yet: when one comes, the choice is between an event "the entry changed" and re-reading the streams by crossing the fact's resources with the identities in the entry. [Q, 11 Nov 2026, paper gap] Step 9 brings none: the repair changes the drill's status, which no entry and no selection of `loans` reads (they read `tools`' name alone, which no command changes); Gianni hears of it by the notice named on his entry, an occurrence, not a field read from `tools`. Since a stream sees only its own domain's facts (above), the limit holds for a selection that reads another domain's state too (GR-3): an entry that should enter or leave on such a fact does not. A third way joins the two: a stream seeing the facts of the domains its domain sees (GR-1). The cases wait in the catalogue of scenarios: Luca renames the drill, and Gianni's row keeps the old name; "my reservations" shows whether the tool is in the workshop, a field read from `tools`. [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (6)]
  - **An entry that reads another domain** (SC-19, the note above): every fact of the node counts for a stream, of any domain, by what it changes of the entries; only a fact of the query's domain touches an entry and goes out as `fact`, with its envelope typed by the domain's registry (DOM-8, D64.3, GR-1). A fact that touches no entry gives `entered`, `left`, or `changed`, a new event of `StreamEvent`: an entry that stays and is no longer the same, compared by value, with the entry as it is after it. The oracle defines it, state before and after each fact of its cell; the composed stream reads again after each fact of the log, of any domain, and compares by identity and value; a native stream finds the entries a fact of another domain may change as it can, by crossing the fact's resources with the identities its entries hold (the drill's `tool_id`), compared with the oracle (TST-2). A read leaf projects `changed` as it projects an unnamed fact, `fact` with its row alone: no event comes to the persona's surface (PER-17), and Marta's client updates the row as for any fact. Scenario: Marta has the drill (L7) with "my loans" open; Luca renames it "cordless drill" (`rename_a_tool`, `a_tool_has_been_renamed`, a fact of `tools`); her row of L7 reads the new name, from `fact` (`changed` in `loans`' stream). The note's three ways join: the facts of the other domains reach the stream (the third), an event says the entry changed (the first), a native adapter routes them by resources (the second). Rejected: a fact touching an entry by any identity it holds (Gianni's booking of the drill for Thursday would touch Marta's L7, and carry his reservation to her row, PER-18); the stream following only the domains its domain sees by the map (`sight.ts` lets a business domain see every other's identities, and the facts of an unseen domain change no entry anyway: a filter for the composed stream's cost, with its case); `loans` keeping its own copy of the name, updated by a reaction (its fact would touch the tool, not the loan, and the row would still say nothing); a `changed` event at the persona's surface (a rename is one more fact to Marta's row). With a log per domain, a stream across two logs has a position of two, open (D64.3). [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026]
  - **The read port's stream** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 5.2; D72, D73.3, D69.3, DOM-22, TST-4): where the adapter streams a query (LIB-8, DOM-2), the read port gives after its snapshot at P the events from P, the stream's from P (inclusive) less those of the fact at P, which the snapshot holds: no gap and no double (D72); from a position, the stream alone, inclusive, a resume, which the consumer deduplicates by position and identity. A position is compared by equality only (the fact at P), never by order: it is the adapter's, opaque. An input not in its schema reads nothing, from a position too (DOM-21). A fact is the query's domain's when its parts' paths are in the domain's registry (a path is the same in every major, DOM-24); a stream carries it in its envelope as a reader sees it, its parts at the current shape through the store's versions, at the one place (DOM-22), with its operation and correlation, the branch and the fingerprint left in the store (PER-18). A native stream's events are parsed: the entry by the fields read and calculated, the identity by its field, the fact's parts; a fact of another domain in it is a fault of the adapter, thrown, as an entry its fields do not parse. The composed stream reads the fact log from P (the fact at P left out) and, for each fact after it, of any domain, reads the query again and compares its entries with the previous ones (the snapshot's first) by identity and by value, deeply, giving the events of DOM-17 in the interpreter's order: an entry a fact of the domain touches (its identity, by the plan's path, among a part's resources) and that the selection keeps is `fact`, an untouched new one `entered`, an untouched one no longer the same `changed`, a missing one `left`; `caught_up` and `position_unavailable` as the log gives them. A fact delivered again is read again: its touched entries come again as `fact` at its position, which the consumer drops (TST-4). The facts a read use case names change no event: the `fact` carries the whole envelope, and a use case over it finds the named part by the same touch (`touches`, part 6.3). **A composed stream does not resume**: from a position it gives `position_unavailable`, terminal, and the consumer reads again, then streams from that snapshot (D73.3), since the library compares each read with the one before it, and the state before a position it did not read at is gone. Scenario: Gianni follows "in the workshop" over a composed stream; R8 entered on Tuesday, when the drill went to the workshop; his phone drops, and on Wednesday the drill is back in service, a fact of `tools` that touches no reservation; resumed from Tuesday's position, the library could read only Wednesday's state, with nothing to tell that R8 was there, and his screen would keep R8. At wiring (CFG-2), in one message with the others: where the adapter streams a query, every fact of the domain's registry is of a domain whose majors the store's versions were given (DOM-22: the read ports' check); a fact a read use case names that is not its domain's. Scenarios on the oracle, each on the oracle's native stream (the reference, its events exact) and on the stream composed over its log, the snapshot at P with the events from P rebuilding the snapshot at the end, with no double (D69.3, `scenarios/oracle.ts`: `followed`, `view`): SC-15 (Marta's "my loans": `fact`, `left`, `caught_up`, live; a resume, inclusive; a position forgotten, `position_unavailable`, then read again; every fact delivered twice; the composed resume), SC-16 (the overdue, a named fact, `fact` with its part), SC-17 (the notice of the repair touches R8; the drill in the workshop changes nothing it reads; the round's composite fact with part 7.4), SC-18 (a loan of the drill, nothing in the catalogue; its rename, `fact`), SC-19 (`changed`; Gianni's booking of the drill touches nothing of Marta's), SC-20 (`entered`, `left`, by facts of `tools`); and SC-21's stream (a loan of `loans` touches its tool's entry by `tools`' id, its path from `identities`, DOM-18; a tool added `entered` at zero, renamed `changed`, retired `left`). The composed stream is less exact, as DOM-2 says: Marta's ladder, lent and returned before the stream read again, gives nothing there and `fact`, then `left`, on the native; the state rebuilt is the same. Rejected: a composed resume reading the query now and giving every entry again (an entry that left while the consumer was away cannot be told: R8 would stay); the read at the resume taken as the state before the position (the facts between would be missed, a gap); positions compared by order to drop the fact at P (the library knows no form of a position); the stream after the snapshot left inclusive, its double to the consumer (D72 asks none).
- **DOM-18** A query is data. A read sees a consistent snapshot, declares its freshness and may ask to be "at least up to P". Projections (audit, read models) are pure and record no facts. D41, D50, D53, D66
  - A query, in the module `query`, declares its input, its `fields` (the schema of an entry), its `identity` (the field that names an entry, LIB-9), its `selection` and its `order`, and a creator that binds the selection from the input (DOM-25); since SC-21 the fields it calculates beside them (`calculations`), and a creator that binds by part (below). The selection is a condition over selection predicates (module `selection`): predicates over the candidate, which they do not name; their parameters are the values of the filter. A command takes only predicates, a query only selection predicates, checked by type. Where the candidates are is the adapter's: one source per query (where it reads, how each field reads), the operators of the commands, a fragment per selection predicate (ADP-8). The query port returns the entries, typed by `fields`, and the position read at (D66); since SC-22 in pages, with the total (below). Freshness and "at least up to P" come when a case asks for them. D50.1, D50.3 [Q]
  - **The subject** (D50.1), decided on 10 Nov 2026: a selection speaks of one kind of thing, its subject, named by the business (a loan, a reservation, an entry of a member's agenda). A selection predicate declares its subject in its builder (`selection({ subject: 'loan', parameters })`), and the generator writes it after the kind, so the subject is a segment of the path (`business/loans/selection/loan/the_loan_is_ongoing`, DOM-24): always, even when a domain has one subject, so that a second subject moves no path. The library checks by type that the leaves of a selection are of one subject, where the selection is composed (DOM-5) and in `query`, and knows the subject only by that path: the subject is not an entity of state (LIB-14), it is a view, where it lives and how it is built is the adapter's (V12). A candidate is an instance of the subject that the selection looks at; an entry is what the query returns of a candidate it keeps, its `fields`. [Q, 11 Nov 2026: one file per subject always; 12 Nov 2026 (2): the subject declared in the builder, the file free]
    - A condition about another subject is a predicate of the candidate's subject that reads it (`the_tool_of_the_loan_is_booked_for_the_day`, in `selection/loan.ts`, whose fragment reads the reservations), as a predicate over another domain's state (GR-3). A query whose entries are of two kinds has a subject of its own (an agenda entry: a SQL `union all`, two maps in memory), or is two queries joined above: two reads, two snapshots, unless they are read as a group on one snapshot (D53), which comes when a case asks for it.
    - The adapter groups its selection fragments by subject, each group with its candidate typed (`(state) => (candidate: $loan) => boolean` in memory), and a query's source gives candidates of that type. A read adapter has three fragment types: a selection over a candidate, the source of the candidates, a field read from a candidate. In SQL they are one type, and one adapter serves the queries of every subject; the in-memory oracle builds its query ports one subject at a time, so that its reads are typed (`domains/test/shed/adapter/memory/`).
    - Subjects appear only where one looks: commands have none (conditions are predicates over values and ids, changes are facts), as the two sides of CQRS.
    - **A selection over another domain's state** (SC-20): a selection predicate of the downstream's subject, in its own words (`the_tool_of_the_reservation_is_in_the_workshop`, subject `reservation`, in `loans`), whose fragment reads the upstream's state through the state adapter, as a command's predicate over it does (GR-3, ADP-8): in the oracle a selection over the candidate that reads the state of `tools` (`Selection<Reads, reservation>`, typed by the import of `tools.ts`), in SQL a subquery on `tools`. The stream needs nothing more: a fact of the upstream that changes whether the selection keeps an entry gives `entered` or `left`, by SC-19's rule (DOM-17). Scenario: Gianni's "in the workshop" among his reservations (`member/my_reservations_in_the_workshop`, over `the_reservations_of_a_member_in_the_workshop`); on Tuesday the drill goes to the workshop and R8 enters, by a fact of `tools` that touches no reservation; on Friday it is back in service and R8 leaves. Rejected: a field of "my reservations" read from `tools` (the mechanism of SC-19, a row `changed`, with nothing of the selection); `loans` keeping the tool's status in its own state, updated by a reaction (a second state to keep in step with the first, where the adapter links them, V11). [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026]
  - **A calculation** (SC-21) [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026; A for the shape]: a value of the candidate that depends on parameters of the read, a concept of the business named in its vocabulary, whose meaning is the adapter's, as a selection predicate's: the paper's derived fields declared by the domain (D50.7; counting is the domain's vocabulary, not a construct of the language, D49). `calculation({ subject, parameters, value })`, in the module of its subject (`business/loans/calculation/tool/the_loans_of_the_tool_between_these_days`, DOM-25), declares its parameters and the schema of the value it gives; the library calculates nothing and knows no kind of aggregation (a count, a sum, the latest day are one construct to it). A query gives its calculations beside its fields, sisters (A): `fields` the fields read from the candidate, `calculations: { loans: tool_calculation.… }` the fields calculated, by name; its entry is both, which the library parses; a name both read and calculated, a calculation of another subject than the selection's (it reads the same candidate), or a component that is no calculation do not compile; the identity is a field read, the order runs over the whole entry (the most lent first). The creator binds by part, as a step's (DOM-20): `{ selection: …, calculations: { loans: { first_day, last_day } } }`, every query, `calculations` only where it calculates. The adapter gives a fragment per calculation by path, from its parameters, of the type of a field read (`CalculationFragments`: a SQL subquery `count(*)`, a function of the candidate and the state in the oracle), one translation reused by every query (ADP-8); bound, it is one more field of the planned query, so the read, the order and the streams meet fields alone: a fact of `loans` holding a tool's id touches its entry (`fact`, the count after it), a correction (SC-13) touches both tools, a tool added comes in at zero (`entered`), a renamed one is `changed`, a retired one `left`. Scenario: on 30 November Luca opens "the loans of the month" (`keeper/the_loans_of_the_month`, over `loans`' `the_loans_of_each_tool_this_month`): every tool still in the shed (`the_tool_is_still_in_the_shed`, subject `tool`, `loans`' view of the tools read from `tools`, V11), how many times it went out in November, the shed's month (SC-12: a loan's day as written; one lent at 23:30 on 31 October in Toronto counts for October), the ladder at zero; Marta's L7, lent on 2 November as the drill and corrected on the 4th, counts for the sander. The snapshot alone: Luca reads once, and the SQL adapter does not stream it (a count over the catalogue per fact is not cheap). Rejected: a builder per aggregation (`count`, A: a catalogue the library would chase case by case, an open set that is the domain's, LIB-1); a grouping in the query (the loans of the month, one entry per tool: the idle tools vanish, and the paper has none); a subject built by the adapter with the month in the candidate (the adapter would read the clock and decide "this month", TST-3); an adapter's field reading the query's input (a translation per query, and the adapter reading the business's contract with its callers); the whole entry in `fields` with `calculations` naming the fields calculated (the value's type written twice); the creator by part only in a query that calculates (two shapes of one key). Names set aside (A): `measure`, `derived_field`, "aggregate" (the paper's subject, D50.1, V12). A value over the whole selection (a total) and a calculation inside a selection (the tools idle this month) come with their cases.
  - **Pages** (SC-22) [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026; A for the shape]: a read of a query is in pages, the paper's page among the parts of a query (D50.1); by default one page, every entry the selection keeps, one snapshot: the read so far, and a get by identity (A). The node declares the page (D50.3, D72.8): a read leaf's creator binds by part, as a query's since SC-21, `{ query, page }`, the query's input and, if it reads in pages, their size, which may come from its own input, bounded by its schema (the visitor's `page_size`, 50 by default, at most 100); every read leaf binds `{ query }`. A read in pages is a stream of snapshots (A), one per page, read when the consumer asks for the next (pull), which ends after the last page or when the consumer stops. Each snapshot carries its position, the total (how many entries the selection keeps there, read in the same snapshot as the page: the paper's "pagination with cursor and total", D110) and, if another page follows, the cursor of the next (`next`, D72.4); a read whole is one page, its total the count of its entries. The cursor is the step's (`zz.cursor`, DOM-20): the order key of the page's last entry, closed by the identity (D109), encoded by the library, opaque to the persona and the client, which carry it (D57). The adapter gives the comparison with it, the same as a step's (`CursorAlgebra.after`), and the read of a page with its total (`ReadPage`: in SQL a `limit` and a `count(*)` in one serializable transaction, in the oracle one state); the business's port reads in pages beside the whole read (`pages`). The cursor serves to resume after a dropped connection, as a stream's position (D72.8: closing and reopening is the norm): a leaf's port takes it where a streamed leaf takes the position. One that does not decode to the query's order key is `invalid_request` (`cursor_not_in_schema`); one forged that decodes starts the page elsewhere, and the selection holds (D57): a feature, not a defense. The pages are not one snapshot (D72.8, D53): each page is read at its own position, so between two pages a tool added before the cursor is not met going on, and one renamed past it comes again; with an order by a calculation (SC-21) the counts move between pages. Each page says where it was read and how many entries there were: the software informs. A leaf that streams reads whole (its creator binds no page, checked by type): a stream from the position of a page would bring events of entries no page has delivered (D72); a live view in pages comes with its case. A cursor holds the values of the order key, opaque and not secret: a leaf in pages over an order on a field it does not project would carry that value to its client; none in Shed (the catalogue's order is shelf, name and id, all projected), with its case. Scenario: Paolo, not signed in, opens the catalogue on his phone (`visitor/the_catalogue`): 2,000 tools in service, by shelf and name, 50 at a time; the first snapshot reads 2,000 and the garden shelf's first 50, and his phone asks for the next as he scrolls. Two rakes fall across a page's boundary: the next page starts at the second, never repeating or skipping one (LIB-9). Between his first and second page Luca adds a bow saw to the garden shelf, before the cursor: Paolo does not meet it going on, and the second page's total reads 2,001. Rejected: an offset (every tool added or retired before a page shifts every page after it, and SQL reads and discards); one position for all the pages (the snapshot token, D53's backlog: in SQL a transaction held open per reader); a key `page` of the leaf beside the creator (A: the size is a value the leaf passes to the read it invokes, which is the creator's job); a bound on the pages (`max_pages`, A's first sketch: a pull stream ends when its consumer stops); pages of one by default (A's first sketch, as the get: a get by identity is one entry read whole, and every leaf read whole would have to say so, a streamed one the only value it may); the cursor in the leaf's input (the author would declare and thread a value of the library, which would reach the query's input, where the business could not read it, D57). A value over the whole selection other than the count of its entries (a sum: "37 loans in all") is a calculation over the selection, with its case.
  - **The read port's snapshot** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 5.1; D66, D109, DOM-21): `read_use_case_ports` builds a read use case's port over the read adapter, as `command_use_case_ports` over the state adapter: per invocation, the query's input parsed by its schema, built over the context where it is a function of it; the creator's binding, by part (SC-21), its selection bound as data and folded into the adapter's fragments, each calculation's parameters parsed by its schema and given to its fragment, one more field of the planned query; the order as written, closed by the entry's identity, ascending, unless the author ordered by it already (LIB-9); the adapter's read, in one snapshot; the entries parsed by the fields read and calculated (one they do not parse is a fault of the adapter, thrown); the snapshot, with its entries, the position read at (D66) and their count as its total (one page, SC-22). An input not in its schema reads nothing: its first event and its last is the base event `invalid_request` with its reason (`zz.InputNotInSchema`), which a leaf gives as it is, as the command port's `invalid_request` (DOM-21): a static rule of the query over its input (a window over `now`) is validation, never a technical abort. At wiring (CFG-2) the port stops, naming every fault in one message: a use case or a calculation not in the registry, an entry's identity no registry given names (below), a source, a calculation's fragment or a field of the order the adapter does not give. The snapshot reads the state, no stored part, so it checks no version: the stream does, for the facts of its domain (DOM-22), since part 5.2 (DOM-17, "The read port's stream"); the pages since part 5.3 (below); the actions since part 6.3 (PER-15, "The actions of a read, at runtime"). Scenarios on the oracle: SC-14, the business's catalogue (by shelf, then by name by code point, `Shovel` before `rake`, two rakes by their ids; the saw in the workshop and the old ladder retired out of it; the shelves chosen in the catalogue's order, not the choice's; no shelf chosen, or a field the query does not declare, `invalid_request`), the visitor's leaf with part 6.1; SC-21, the business's side (on 30 November the drill, the saw in the workshop and the sander, corrected from Marta's L7, once each, by name, the ladder at zero, the retired rake out; Gianni's loan of 23:30 on 31 October is October's in Toronto, November's in a shed whose calendar is UTC). Rejected: an input not in its schema thrown (a leaf would meet a technical error for a window its client crossed, DOM-21's reason); the order left as written when it does not name the identity (two rakes in the order the adapter's storage happens to give, which the oracle and SQL need not share).
  - **An entry's identity of another domain, and its path** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026] (part 5.1; GR-3, DOM-17): a stream touches an entry by its identity's path among a fact's resources (DOM-17), so the plan carries it (`PlannedQuery.identity.path`); an entry named by another domain's identity (`loans`' `the_loans_of_each_tool_this_month`, by `tools`' `tool_id`) finds it in the registries of the other domains the configurator gives the read ports beside their domain's (`identities: tools_registry`), while `registry` stays the domain's: the facts a stream carries are its domain's alone, by their paths in it (DOM-17), and the port's type carries the facts of `registry`. The command ports take another domain's identities by the same key (DOM-10), A's choice: one need, one key, and `registry` always the port's domain. Rejected: the key on the read ports alone, the command ports' registries merged (C's first shape: one need, two shapes); the registries merged on the read ports too: `loans`' streams would be typed with `tools`' facts, and the composed stream would take them for its own (Luca renames a tool, and its row of the month would be `fact` where SC-21 reads `changed`); the domain found by the nearest `version.ts` above the use case in a merged registry (right at runtime, the type still `tools`' facts).
  - **The read port's pages** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 5.3; SC-22, D50.1, D72.8, D109, D57): the port reads in pages beside its whole read (`pages(context, input, page)`): the input parsed as for the snapshot, then the page asked, its size a whole number, at least 1, and its cursor; a stream of snapshots, each read when the consumer asks for it (pull), by the adapter's `page`, one snapshot each, with its position and the total the adapter counts there; the page's selection is the query's, after the cursor's order key by the adapter's comparison (`CursorAlgebra.after`, the step's) from the second page on, and the adapter is asked one entry more than the size (`PlannedPage.limit`), to tell whether another page follows; then the snapshot carries `next`, the cursor of the page's last entry, and the next page starts after it. A page with no size is every entry, one page with no cursor, the adapter asked with no limit; a page as long as what is left is the last. An input not in its schema reads no page (`input_not_in_schema`), then a cursor that does not decode to the query's order key (`zz.CursorNotInSchema`, `cursor_not_in_schema`), each the first event and the last, which a leaf gives as it is (DOM-21). A size that is not a whole number at least 1 is a fault of whoever binds it, thrown: the leaf's schema bounds it (the visitor's `page_size`, SC-22). **The cursor's form**: the order key, each field of the closed order by its name with its value as the adapter gave it (an absent one with none, `[name]`), a list of pairs in the order's order, in canonical JSON (LIB-11's), its UTF-8 in base64url (`cursor_of`); decoded (`key_of`) only if it is a text the library would write, so that one key has one cursor, and the query's order key, its fields by name in order, each value parsed by its field's schema, read or calculated; the values go to the adapter as decoded. A stable function (LIB-10). Scenario on the oracle, SC-22: on Tuesday Paolo reads the catalogue's 2,000 tools 50 at a time (the visitor's leaf with part 6.1): the first page at position 2,000, total 2,000, the garden shelf's 49 hoes and the first of two rakes, the next page starting at the second; 40 pages, every tool once, in the order of the read whole. Between his first and second page Luca adds a bow saw to the garden shelf, before the cursor: Paolo does not meet it going on, and the second page reads 2,001 at position 2,001; had Luca renamed the first hoe a weeder, it would come again after the last spade. His connection drops after the first page: from its cursor the pages after it, as pulled. A cursor not as the library writes it, of a shelf the catalogue has not or with its fields in another order reads no page; one forged after a rake no one has starts the page at the first spade still in the shed. Rejected: the values alone, by position (a cursor of another order as long, after a release that swaps two fields of one type, would decode and start the page elsewhere unseen); an object by the fields' names (canonical JSON writes its members by name, and the order key's order is the cursor's meaning); the JSON as it is, not in base64url (a text a client escapes in an address, and that reads as an invitation to read it); a signed cursor (a defense the software does not promise: one forged starts the page elsewhere, the selection holds, D57, F6); the values parsed by the fields rather than as the adapter gave them (a field with a transformation would give the adapter's comparison a value of the other side); a size out of bounds as `invalid_request` (a base event more for a fault of the author, and a reason the client may not have caused); a page with no size read by `read` (from a cursor, its count would be the entries after it, not the query's total) or with the largest integer as its limit (a SQL `limit` of no meaning).

### Processes

- **DOM-19** Every command is a transition of a process; the simplest process is the life cycle of an entity. In one domain a step composes the target's transition and records one fact with one part per applied branch; across domains it invokes the transition through outbox and reaction. A failed step records a fact, not a rejection. D70, D68, D93, D111
  - A transition composed outside a step (SC-10) [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026; A for the shape]: a branch of a command may apply the transitions of other instances of its domain with no choice, each instance named by the input, since a creator reads no state and whoever invokes has read it: `[condition, fact, entries, composes({ the_reservation_is_taken_up: take_up_a_reservation })]`, the creator giving each transition its input under the same name (A: in the branch, after the effects, by name as the effects, `{}` for none; set aside, a key at the top by branch name, as the step's `composes`, with the input unnamed in the creator). Composition is per branch: the branch that lends composes, the one that records the reservation already taken up does not. The branch applies when its condition and a branch of each transition hold, the first in their order: the conditions of a transition are part of the branch, as in the choice of a step (D112.2); the library plans the branch once per branch of the transition, so the adapter meets plain branches. A rejection names the failed predicates of both, a transition's under its name. The fact has one part per applied branch, the branch's own then one per transition, in the order written (D111); a leaf projects the branch's own part, and the transition's reaches the reads by their streams. A composed transition is a command with no use case of its own, and composes nothing in turn (LIB-3). Scenario: Gianni booked the drill for Thursday (R8); that day, from R8's row, his phone sends `take_the_tool_you_booked` naming R8, and `lend_a_booked_tool` records [`a_tool_has_been_lent` (L5), `a_reservation_has_been_taken_up` (R8, `taken_up` by L5)]; his screen reads `you_have_the_tool`, and R8 leaves his "my reservations". On its day a reservation holds the tool for its member, so `borrow_a_tool` lends a tool booked for today to no one: Marta, that Thursday, reads `the_tool_is_not_available`. Set aside: the instance chosen by a selection (a choice, which only a step makes, with its cursor); the reservation named in the fact by its tool and day (a fact names its instances by identity, V9: the row of R8 would not be found).
  - **A composition, at the command port** (SC-10, SC-54) [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 4.3): the library plans a branch that composes once per combination of its single transitions' branches, the first transition's varying slowest, each plan's condition the path's, the branch's, then each transition's branch's, in AND, so that the adapter, applying the first plan that holds, applies each transition's first branch that holds, as a command's; a transition on a list (`each`) is planned per element (`PlannedBranchOverAList`), the adapter choosing each element's first branch that holds and `applies` writing from the choice (DOM-20, SC-54). A transition's input, which the composing creator gives under its name, is parsed by its schema, built over the context of the use case's point (DOM-21), and its creator gets the same context: an input not in its schema makes the request `invalid_request`, as the command's own, nothing planned (a static rule of the transition holds whoever composes it); a transition below a node the use case's path does not cross stops the port (with its case, DOM-5), as one that composes in turn (LIB-3) or a step. Every part is its fact's: its resources its fact's own (declared, SC-55, or every identity), an entry a transition queues holding that part's (DOM-14). A rejection names, after the branch's own failed predicates, for each transition on which no branch holds, every branch's failed predicates, under the transition's name and the branch's (`['the_reservation_is_taken_up', 'the_reservation_holds_the_tool_for_the_member_today_so_it_is_taken_up']`), then the components where the leaf stands: a transition's branches are judged as an OR's components (DOM-6), so a transition that holds names nothing; a list's elements likewise, under the transition's name, once each (the element itself with its case, D113.1). The outcome of a branch that composes carries, beside its fact, the parts it composed by the names of its composition, each `{ branch, fact }`, a list's one per element in its order (`composed`, `zz.ComposedParts`, which a leaf's projection reads, A's shape), and a branch that composes nothing has no such field; the stored envelope keeps the branch of each composed part (`StoredEnvelope.composed`), so that a retry reads them as the first request did. Scenario: on Thursday Gianni takes R8 [`a_tool_has_been_lent` (L5, the drill, Gianni), `a_reservation_has_been_taken_up` (R8, L5)], his retry the same, `composed` too; Marta names R8 that morning: `the_reservation_holds_the_tool_for_the_member_on_the_day`, under the transition's names, and nothing is recorded; once the drill is out, her branch's own `the_tool_is_on_loan` beside it. Rejected: a transition's failures under its name alone (two of its branches failing on one predicate would read as one failure); a transition's input out of schema thrown (a reaction would meet a technical abort for ever, DOM-21's reason); `composed` on every applied outcome, `{}` for a branch that composes none (a field with nothing to say on most branches); the composed branches found at a retry from the parts' paths (two branches of a transition may record one fact, as `retire_a_tool`'s). Not met in Shed, so built and not observed: a rejected transition with two branches, two single transitions on one branch.
- **DOM-23** A process lives in the business domain that owns its life cycle, or in a business domain dedicated to processes; its state is in the aggregate itself or in a process aggregate. Its steps in other domains are reactions (GR-9): never a direct call, never an outbox entry. The outbox is for effects toward the world, whose outcome comes back only to the domain that queued it. The domain that runs a step does not reject for a business outcome: it records a fact, which a reaction forwards to the process. D68, D70, D65, D92, D93, D111
  - In q- the ticket (preparing → ready → used or expired), the first face and the founding of a group are processes of `bonds`; the secrets are created by commands of `seals`, reached by reaction (PER-21). [Q, plan]
  - Who owns a process is who owns the life cycle it walks, not how simple it is (A, 16 Nov 2026 (4)). In Shed the repair after a damaged return is `tools`': the drill goes in service → under repair → in service, or retired beyond repair, the keeper's office; its state is the tool's (`tool_under_repair`, with the stay in repair, `repair { id, since }`). A domain dedicated to processes needs no construct of its own (D70.4: a business domain like the others; its steps to other domains are reactions above, its relaunches to itself reactions inside it, DOM-16), but there it would split the life cycle: with the repair open in a `workshop` and the tool's status in `tools`, reached by reaction, the drill would stay in service until the reaction came, and Gianni could borrow it in between; and `tools`' own commands (retiring a tool under repair) would read another domain to decide their life cycle, the alternative D70 rejects. Such a domain is right when the process is a case no office owns: a damage claim charging Marta in `loans` and costing the repair in `tools`. The damaged return reaches `tools` one way, by a reaction above (`reaction/damage/`): nothing comes back to `loans`, whose loan is closed. [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (4)]
- **DOM-20** A bulk operation chosen by a condition is a process with a cursor, one instance per step. An operation on a list of named instances stays atomic, with one branch per instance. D97, D112, D113
  - The process records its selection with its parameters when it opens (D97.1), and its cursor, the order key of the last instance handled (the order's fields and the identity, D109, D112.1). The cursor is the library's by default (`zz.cursor`), opaque, ordered and comparable, written by the library in the step's own mutation: the author declares where the process keeps it and never builds it. A business that must read it declares its own, the order key's fields, which the library derives from the order, checks by type and writes the same way (A), with its case. In Shed the desk's work sheet keeps the library's: `cancelling`, with the cursor (none before the first step), then `complete`; its selection, the tool's reservations still ahead (booked, from the day of the retirement on), is given by the tool and the start date it already holds. [Q, 16 Nov 2026]
  - The step is a construct of the library, a command for whoever invokes it (its path under `command/`): `cursor_step({ input, reads, fields, identity, selection, order, composes, outcomes, creator })` (A, 16 Nov 2026 (2), name and shape). It does two things. The choice (D112.2): the library adds to the selection the comparison with the cursor and the conditions of the composed transition, and takes the first instance in the order, then the identity (D109); only a process with a cursor needs it. The composition (D111): it applies the transition of another instance (`composes: cancel_a_reservation`, a command with no use case of its own) and records one fact with one part per applied branch, the step's part (the cursor the library writes) and the transition's (`a_reservation_has_been_cancelled`); or, none left, the closing (`complete`). `reads` is a declared read, instance form: the process's state by its key from the input, whose cursor the library reads and whose parameters the creator binds (`(context) => (input) => (sheet) => …`). One message: the adapter reads, chooses and writes in one transaction, the plan carrying pure functions of the read and of the choice; two messages would let the sheet change between them. Only in the step for now (LIB-3); whether `reads` and `composes` become parts of every command (A's hunch for `reads`) is weighed at step 9, with the repair. Rejected: the cursor carried by the fact, the reaction and the input (the state would no longer hold it, D112.1). [Q, 16 Nov 2026] `reads` and `composes` stay in the step (step 9, A: C's call, 16 Nov 2026 (5)). A creator reads no state, so a fact or an effect carries a value of the state only through the input, and in Shed whoever invokes a command has read what it needs: a client, whose claim the command guards (the tool Marta holds, `the_loan_is_of_this_tool`); a reaction, from the fact it translates (the stay in repair, from `a_tool_has_been_sent_to_repair`); the executor, from the payload the domain queued (the reminder's member and due date); a step, from the instance it read (the notified member and day, through `composes`). A command reads (`reads`) when a value of its own domain's state must go into its fact or effect, no invoker has read it (no client; the fact upstream does not carry it), and it is no claim of the world to check. A command composes (`composes`) outside a step when it applies the transition of another instance of its domain with no choice: Gianni borrows the drill on the day he booked it, and the loan takes up the reservation, one fact in two parts. Neither case is in Shed (LIB-3); the second waits in the catalogue of scenarios planned at Shed's close. [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (5)] [17 Nov 2026: the second is SC-10, under DOM-19.]
  - The shape, in the code (A, 16 Nov 2026 (2)). The instances the step walks are declared as a query declares its entries, `fields`, `identity`, `selection`, `order` (the order closed by the identity, D109): the order and the composed transition's input are typed on the instance, which the shape decided before had nowhere to declare. Its facts are named by the library, since every step has these two: `advances` (the step's part, beside the transition's) and `completes` (the closing); the author writes no condition. The creator, `(context) => (input) => (sheet) => …`, resolves the selection's parameters, `composes` (from the chosen instance, the transition's input) and the payload of each fact. The step's part keeps the cursor in a field of its own (`the_cancellation_has_advanced`: the tool and the cursor), which the library writes and the author omits: so the cursor is never built, and the creator has no function with a business literal inside (TypeScript widens a literal returned by a function nested in a creator whose facts are still being inferred). The process not found in the stage the step reads (none, or closed) is no expected occurrence: the step is rejected, naming the read (a redelivery is the duplicate branch, DOM-11). [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - The execution (A, 16 Nov 2026 (3)). The plan of a step is the read of its process and a pure function of the library over what was read (`choose`): not found in the stage, nothing writes; else the instances after the cursor, in the order closed by the identity, the branches of the composed transition for an instance, each with the step's part, the instances past one passed over, and the closing. The conditions of the transition are part of the choice (D112.2): an instance on which no branch of the transition holds is passed over, never a rejection, which would stall the process at it, every relaunch rejected again. Scenario: were the selection the ladder's reservations from the day on, without `booked`, and had Paolo cancelled his R4 himself, between Marta's R3 and Gianni's R5, the second step would cancel R5 and its cursor go past R4. In Shed the selection holds the transition's condition, so nothing is passed over. The adapter finds the process by the step's input (`StepSource.reads`: `cancellations.get(input.tool_id)`), as stored, and the library parses it with the read's schema, so the stage is the library's check: a read by the value's identities would need a rule for which one is the key, since a value may carry several. The adapter also gives where the instances are, as a query's, and the comparison with the cursor (`after`). A branch writes one write per part, in order. The oracle filters; SQL reads one instance at a time (`order by day, id limit 1`), and past one passed over, in the same transaction. [Q, 16 Nov 2026 (3)] An adapter gives one selection algebra for all its steps (`StepAdapter`): `loans`' two steps, the cancellation's and the round of notices', walk one subject, the reservation, and share it. A step over another subject would need, in the oracle, typed by subject, an algebra per subject: with its case (LIB-3). [16 Nov 2026 (5)]
  - **The steps of a process, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.4; D97, D109, D111, D112, DOM-11, DOM-19, DOM-21, DOM-22, CFG-2): the command ports build a step's port beside the commands' (`step_port.ts`), over what the state adapter gives for its steps (`StepAdapter`). Per invocation: the step's input parsed by its schema, built over the context (DOM-21), one it refuses `invalid_request`; then one message to the adapter (`PlannedStep`): the operation, which the adapter looks up first (the duplicate branch, DOM-11), the read of the process by its key from the input (`StepSource.reads`), and `choose`, a pure function of the library over what the adapter read: the process parsed by the read's schema, in the stage that steps; none, or in another stage, `not_found`, nothing written, and the step rejected naming the read by its path (`ReadFailure`); else the creator run over the context, the input and the process, and the walk: the instances of the selection it binds (folded into the adapter's selection algebra), after the cursor the process keeps (`CursorAlgebra.after`, from the order key the cursor encodes; every one before the first step), in the order as written closed by the identity (D109); for an instance as the adapter read it, parsed by the step's fields (one they do not parse is the adapter's fault, thrown), the branches of the composed transition on the input the creator gives it, each one fact, the step's part then the transition's (D111), the step's part with its cursor written by the library, the instance's order key (`cursor_of`, the page's form, LIB-10: one stable function for every cursor), the transition's entries queued with their part's resources (DOM-13); the instances after one passed over (D112.2), which SQL reads one at a time; the closing. The adapter applies, in one transaction, the first branch that holds on the first instance that has one, or the closing; the outcome is the walk of its last choice (it may choose again, whole, after a conflict, DOM-9): `advances` with its two parts, `completes` with its one, their position; from the duplicate branch the fact as recorded, or `identity_already_used`. The stored fact keeps the transition's branch under the step's key (`composed: { composes: … }`), as a command's composed parts (DOM-9), since two branches may record one fact. Where a process keeps its cursor is read from its schema: the field whose schema is the library's `cursor`, at any depth of the read's objects (the work sheet's in its stage, `progress.cursor`), exactly one; the step's part keeps it in one field of its own at its top, which the creator leaves out (`ResolvedStep`). At wiring (CFG-2), every fault in one message: a read not in the registry (a rejection names it), keeping no cursor or two; a part with no field of its own for it, or two; a transition that is no command of the registry, a step, or one that composes in turn (DOM-19); a step below a node that binds or requires; the adapter giving no steps, no source of a step, no field of its order; an identity of the instances no registry names; and, with the commands', a step's facts and its transition's of no domain the store's versions read (DOM-22). Scenarios on the oracle: SC-39 (Luca retires the ladder on Thursday; `reaction/retirement/` opens the sheet with no cursor; the desk's reactions take Marta's R3, then Gianni's R5, each step's cursor the reservation's day and id, then close it, every fact of the retirement's chain; a step on a closed sheet, or on none, rejected naming `cancellation_under_way`; the steps by hand, a retry the same fact, another request under its operation `identity_already_used`; the consumers fallen before saving their positions and started again, or every fact given twice: the duplicate branch, R5 cancelled once; a release of the adapter of `loans` that reads R3 as not booked for its commands, while the selection keeps it: the first step passes over R3 and cancels R5, its cursor at R5; a ladder with no reservation ahead, the first step closes the sheet), SC-41 (on Tuesday the drill to the workshop, RP1: the round notifies Gianni then Paolo, each step's notice touching its reservation alone, keyed by the stay and the reservation, delivered by the executor; the reservations stay; back on Friday and in the workshop again the next Tuesday, RP2: Paolo alone, under another key; the drill retired after the first step, the sheet cancels R8 and R9, and the round closes with no notice to Paolo; every fact twice and a fall: one notice each), SC-44 (Luca loads the library's 200 tools and his screen follows: 198 in the catalogue, lines 37 and 120 left, the sheet loaded, 202 facts; his retry the same events and no second sheet; a line its schema refuses refuses the sheet), SC-17 (the round's composite fact touches R8 by its second part, native and composed; Gianni's leaf reads it on R8's row). Rejected: the step's cursor in a form of its own (two stable functions for one order key); the cursor's field named by the author beside the schema (said twice, and the schema already holds the library's format); a cursor that encodes another order than the step's read from the first instance again or taken for the end (a release that changed a step's order while a process was under way: a fault, thrown, never a guess, as an unknown version, DOM-22); an input the transition's schema refuses for an instance passed over (silence, D112.2) or `invalid_request` (the adapter has chosen inside its transaction, and the contract would need a case of its own: thrown, with its case); the conditions of a business node's path composed into the transition's branches (a path that fails would pass every instance over and close the process: with its case); the walk planned again after the execution from the instance alone (the process the choice read is the adapter's, inside its transaction).
  - **A list loaded element by element** (SC-44) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (5)]: a list of named instances has two definitions, the author's choice (A, 18 Nov 2026 (5): one need has two or more definitions, the one that suits and a fallback): atomic (D113: a branch and a part per instance, all or nothing only where no branch holds; SC-54, below), or a process with a cursor over the list, when each element must stand on its own over a list as long as the world makes it, back to the process as D97 says for a partial outcome. The list is the process's parts (D94.1), each with an identity of its own, from the process's and its position (a function of the domain's `dsl`), kept beside the process and walked by its step as a query walks its candidates, a subject of the domain (`line`); the composed transition holds a branch for every expected case (D95), so that no element is passed over in silence (D112.2) and none stops the list. The process's id comes from the operation (D75.8) when the list has no name of its own. Its opening fact carries every element, so the list is bounded by its schema (D97); an element its schema refuses refuses the list (`invalid_request`, DOM-21) before any element goes in. The persona's leaf follows the process by correlation (D58.2), a step's part under a followed name too (`Follows`).
