The process lives in the domain that owns the life cycle of what it creates (DOM-23), its step in that domain's `StepAdapter`, over its own subject. Scenario: on Wednesday the town library gives the shed its 200 tools in a spreadsheet, a line a tool; Luca loads it (`keeper/load_tools_from_a_sheet`, `tools`' `load_the_tools_of_a_sheet`): [`the_loading_of_a_sheet_has_begun` (S1, its 200 lines)]; two reactions of `tools` walk it (`continue_the_loading_of_a_sheet`, composing `add_a_tool_from_a_line`): line 1, the library's rake, [advanced, `a_tool_has_been_added`]; line 37 names `drill-7`, the label of the shed's own drill, [advanced, `a_tool_has_been_added_again`]; line 120 repeats the id of line 12, whose step added it, and is left the same way; after line 200, `the_sheet_has_been_loaded`. Luca's screen follows: 198 tools in the catalogue, lines 37 and 120 left, the sheet loaded; Paolo's catalogue sees the tools come in one at a time. A retry of Luca's request is the duplicate branch, the same sheet; the same sheet sent again in a new request opens another, whose every line is left. Set aside for this sheet: the atomic definition, one fact, a part per line (D113), with a transaction and a fact as long as the sheet, the catalogue held while it runs, and a cap on the sheet for one transaction's sake (a short list's, SC-54); composing `add_a_tool` (a taken id would be passed over in silence, D112.2, and a branch for it would change Luca's own form, which refuses a taken id); the lines as a value inside the sheet (each step would read every line to read its cursor); a mark on the line left (no read asks for it: the fact says it, and Luca follows it; a read of the sheet comes with its case, LIB-3).
  - **A list loaded in one go** (SC-54) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (6); A for the shape]: the atomic definition of a list of named instances (D113; D97, the corollary on known ids). A branch composes a transition on each element of the list, `composes({ its_lines_are_loaded: each(add_a_tool_from_a_line) })`, the creator giving one input per element under the same name, in the order of the list (A: a marker inside `composes`, by name as SC-10's; set aside, the list said by an array literal, `[add_a_tool_from_a_line]`, one element saying many). Each element applies its own branch of the transition, the first that holds, on the state as it was before the command, with no combination between the elements' branches (D113.1); the branch applies when its condition holds and a branch holds on every element: all or nothing (D113.1). Its fact is the branch's own part, then one part per element, in the order of the list, each with the name, the payload and the resources of the branch it applied (D113.2, D107 per part). Since every element reads the state before, a list names each instance once, which the domain's schema checks (`invalid_request`, DOM-21; D113, a list of ids without repeats). The leaf reads the composed parts (A, the shape): the projection of a branch has a second parameter, the parts its branch composed by the names of its composition, each `{ branch, fact }` as a command's outcome, a list's one per element in its order (`zz.ComposedParts`); a projection that does not need it leaves it out (set aside: the leaf following its own fact's parts with `follows`, whose final event would come from the branch's part, the first of the fact, and `follows` is for the facts after the outcome, D58.2). The plan (C): planned once per branch of a list's transition, as SC-10's single transitions are, a branch would be as many as their combinations (2^N), and the elements choose on their own; so it is planned per element (`zz.PlannedBranchOverAList`): the adapter chooses, for each element, the first branch that holds, and a pure function of the library writes the fact from the choice (`applies`), as a step's `choose` (DOM-20); the execution gives the choice back (`Execution.elements`). Scenario: on Thursday Paolo gives the shed the twelve tools of his garage, on a short sheet; Luca loads it (`keeper/load_tools_from_a_short_sheet`, `tools`' `load_the_tools_of_a_short_sheet`): [`a_sheet_has_been_loaded_at_once` (S2, filed complete), line 1 `a_tool_has_been_added`, …, line 4, which names `drill-7`, `a_tool_has_been_added_again`, …]; his screen reads at once `the_sheet_is_loaded`, eleven tools added, line 4 left; Paolo's catalogue sees the eleven come in together. Luca's first try had line 9 repeating the id of line 3: refused before the desk read anything, and he corrected it. A short sheet (fifty lines at most), so that one transaction and one fact hold it and the catalogue is held for a moment only; a longer one goes line by line (SC-44). Rejected: the short sheet not filed (its condition would check an id nobody keeps; filed complete, the short and the long sheets are one register); planning the combinations. Left with its case: a rejection naming the element whose transition no branch holds on (D113.1, the outcome of each instance): in Shed every line has a branch, so a rejection names the predicates under the transition's name, as SC-10's (LIB-3). At the command port since part 4.3: DOM-19, "A composition, at the command port".

### Schemas and versions

- **DOM-21** Every signature (use case, port, command, query, fact) has one declarative zod schema: type, validation, JSON Schema export (the library's `json_schema_of`, since part 3.6, DOM-25). No parallel hand-written type or validator. An undeclared field never passes; the default is rejecting the request as "invalid request". D46, D75, D106
  - A static business rule over the input is validation, pure, in the domain: a limit (`max(80)`), a window ("a day after today, within 30 days"), even when it reads the context; it fails as "invalid request", not as a rejection. The input schema may be a function of the part of the context it needs (`(context: Now) => schema`), built by the library before parsing; the context is made of small interfaces, so the schema serves any context that has that part. A rule becomes a predicate, evaluated by the adapters, when its limit leaves the code for a configuration that can change. [Q]
  - **An input not in its schema, at the command port** (SC-6) [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: the command port parses its input, built over the context where the schema is a function of it, and the target the nodes of its path bind (D105); one that does not parse (an undeclared field, a value its schema refuses, a static rule over the input and `now`) is a fourth outcome of the port, `invalid_request` (`zz.InvalidInput`, in `CommandOutcome` and `StepOutcome`), beside applied, rejected and `identity_already_used`: nothing is planned nor executed, and no projection sees it; a leaf gives it as the base event `invalid_request` (`input_not_in_schema`), a reaction or the executor as a dead letter (their parts, 7.1 and 7.3; the executor's since part 7.1, cause `invalid_request`, DOM-14; a reaction's since part 7.3, likewise, DOM-16). Scenario: on Thursday 19 November Gianni books the ladder for that day, or for 20 December: `invalid_request`; for 19 December, booked; the same booking sent on 19 December, `invalid_request` (the window moves with `now`). Rejected: the port throwing (a reaction's translation or a return out of schema would be a technical abort, delivered again for ever, never a dead letter); the leaf parsing the command's schema before invoking (the port's other callers, the reactions and the executor, would reach it unparsed); the base event in the port's stream (an event of a leaf's union, not an outcome of the business). A read port parses its query's input the same way since part 5.1, an input not in its schema read as nothing (`zz.InputNotInSchema`, DOM-18). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
- **DOM-22** A fact's name is its meaning and never changes; its version is its shape, in the envelope. The log is never rewritten. The producing domain translates old facts at read time, one version to the next; a translation is pure, adds nothing, touches only the payload and never changes once published. A reader that meets an unknown version stops and retries, never guesses. Stored state is migrated by an adapter only through such a translation; values the business must decide are commands with their facts. D101
  - The shape of the facts (one fact with values, or distinct facts) follows the business language, which the business makes and which represents it; the technical staff may suggest, foreseeing future needs is the business's. Splitting a fact after a release is a new name (D75.6), so the choice is made before. [Q]
  - The export carries the state of every domain with its schema version; every release reads the letters and exports of the previous ones. [Q]
  - A domain has one version, semver, declared in its `version.ts` (`loans = version('2.0.0')`, a component of kind `version`, in the registry); a part of a fact and an effect queued carry the version of their domain when written (DOM-10). A minor is a change after which every shape it leaves still parses with the new schema (an optional field added): no translation, checked by CI. A major keeps the one that passed in a folder of the domain named by its number (`business/loans/1/`), in the tree and outside the path (DOM-24, DOM-25): every component of that major that is stored or published (for a business domain, its facts and effects), those that changed at the last minor of the major, written whole, with their step to the next major (`earlier(next.a_tool_has_been_lent)({ payload, to_next })`, `to_next` checked by type against the next shape), every other delegated (`delegate(next.a_tool_has_been_lent_again)`); the generator stops on a component forgotten. The shapes of a past major are branded with their path and major (`…/a_tool_has_been_lent@1`), so that no reader takes one for the current. A past major's scope gives the next major as `next` (a later major's folder, or the domain itself), so that a folder once released never changes; the current version knows no past (`.dependency-cruiser.mjs`, `shed-the-current-knows-no-past`): when a major leaves the window (D101: no fact kept has it), its folder is deleted and nothing else changes. A path names its component in every version; the version travels apart, in the envelope or the message (D75 rejects `v2` in the address). A translation uses the formats of the library, never the domain's `dsl`, which may change under it; it has no zone, so it takes a day the fact carries, never one of an instant (`day_of` needs a zone: A, 23 Nov 2026, SC-12). Scenario: on 2 Nov Marta borrows the drill at `loans` 1.x, due at an instant (`due_date`); on 4 Nov `loans` 2.0.0 makes a loan due on a day (`due_day`), the tool back by its end; her fact, read after, reaches every reader at 2.0.0, its due day the one 1.x wrote beside the instant, and the wait for the eve queued with it, at 1.x, likewise. Weighed and set aside (A): the history of a fact in its builder (no folder shows a version, 30 versions apart); a component of a kind of its own for a translation; every major in a folder, the current included (the imports of every domain that sees it change at each major); a folder per minor (it would hold only delegations). Who reads a past major, and the waits queued at 1.x that the new rule would meet early (Marta's overdue wait at 10:12 of her due day), are Shed's step 10, part 2. [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (7)]
  - Who translates (A, 17 Nov 2026): the library alone, at one place, where a stored part of a fact or a stored outbox entry becomes a parsed one, by its path and its version (each part by its own); every reader meets the current shapes only and never a version: the command ports (the duplicate branch gives back the fact recorded), the reads and their streams, native or composed, the trees (`follows`), the reaction consumers, the effect executor, the adapter's rebuild. The translation is the domain's, in its folders; the library runs it, as it runs the steps of a leaf of a past major (PER-17). The adapter keeps the version opaque, as a position. A version the library does not know (a 2.1.0 written by a newer instance during a rolling release, read by one at 2.0.0) stops the reader, which retries. Stored state is migrated by the adapter, when a release changes its tables, by a rebuild from the log through its own writes (`zz.replay`, the facts at the current shapes), never by a value rewritten by hand (`substr(due_date, 1, 10)` would be a translation written twice, and wrong: the day of a UTC instant is not the shed's). The log itself is never rewritten: a migration reaches one store, while the facts live in exports and in the stores of peers released independently (PER-17), and a writer at the old version may still write during the release. Scenario: Marta's loan (1.x) read after 2.0.0 by the stream of "my loans" resumed before it, by the rebuild of the SQL table `loans` (`due_date` become `due_day`), and her damaged return of 3 Nov (1.x, delegated) by the consumer of `damage`, down until 5 Nov. Weighed and set aside (A): a migration of the facts, in place (against D101) or by copy into a new log (the positions change, so every stream resumes from nothing, and exports and peers still need the translation at reading). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - Where the past majors enter (A, 17 Nov 2026): the generator lists the majors of a domain from its folders (`business/loans/_majors.ts`: `{ 2: registry, 1: the registry of 1/ }`), the one file of the current version that names a past major (`.dependency-cruiser.mjs`, an exception to `shed-the-current-knows-no-past`), rewritten when a folder is deleted; the configurator composes once the versions of a store from the majors of every domain whose data it keeps (`zz.versions([loans, tools])`) and gives them, a required part, to every builder that reads stored data; at wiring (CFG-2) the library stops on a builder that reads a domain absent from them (the consumer of `damage` reads a fact of `loans`) and on a domain whose current major is not its `version.ts`'s. Weighed and set aside (A): the past registries written by hand beside each builder's registry (one forgotten is found by the first fact of 1.x, at runtime); the majors inside each domain's registry (the configurator shows nothing, CI's comparison mixes current and past, `damage` still lacks `loans`). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - The effects in flight (A, 17 Nov 2026): an outbox entry carries the version of its domain when queued (`zz.StoredEntry.version`), and the executor reads its payload at the current shape before the delivery: Marta's eve, queued at 1.x with `due_date`, reaches `remind_the_member_of_the_due_date` with `due_day`. A translation touches the payload alone, never when an entry may go: a new rule that moves that instant is a value of the business, so the return's guard (D68.3). `declare_the_loan_overdue` reads the day at the executor's `now`: after the due day, overdue; tool back, nothing changes; the due day not over, a fact (`the_overdue_check_has_been_put_off`) and the wait queued again at the start of the day after, under a key of its own, the loan and the day (the first key is the operation of the return, DOM-11), so that "one loan, one wait of each" no longer holds for the loans of 1.x. Scenario: Marta's overdue wait, queued at 1.x for 10:12 of 16 Nov, comes on her due day; her drill becomes overdue on 17 Nov at 00:00, as Gianni's ladder after its due day. Weighed and set aside (A): a release procedure that queues the new waits (the guard is needed anyway, the wait of 10:12 still comes); the guard alone (Marta's loan never overdue); nothing (overdue on the due day, against 2.0.0). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - **The translation, at the one place** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 4.4; D101, SC-8, SC-9, SC-12): `zz.versions(majors)({ stored })` gives a store's readers its `Versions`: a part of a fact (`part`) and an outbox entry (`entry`, `zz.EntryFields`: its id, `not_before` and resources as queued, a translation touching the payload alone) at the current shape, and whether it reads a path's domain (`reads`). A domain is the folder of its current major's `version.ts`; a path is of the domain whose folder holds it, and its version's major picks the registry: at the current major, a minor up to the current one and any patch parse with the current schema (a minor leaves every shape it wrote parsable, CI's check); at a past major, any minor, through its folder (the last minor of the major, by construction). From the component its path names there the library follows the next majors' components as each folder names them (`earlier`: its own shape parsed, then its step to the next; `delegate`: the next's as it is) to the current major's, which parses the last, and whose path, by object identity in the current registry, the parsed part carries. A newer minor or major, a major whose folder is gone, a path a major has not, a domain not given, a payload its version does not parse: the reader stops, and retries, never a guess. At wiring (CFG-2) the library stops, naming every fault in one message: majors whose current registry is not under the major its `version.ts` declares, or that declare no version or two; a domain given twice; a path and version the store keeps (`StoredVersions`) that no major reads, so that a release that deleted `1/` while Marta's loan and her waits of 1.x are kept does not start (SC-9), as one that meets data a newer minor wrote. A builder that reads stored data checks with `reads`, at its wiring, every path it may read: the command ports the facts their commands and the transitions they compose record, which the duplicate branch reads back; `replay` the paths of its writes; the read ports, the trees, the executor and the consumers with their parts (steps 5–7), a step's facts and its transition's with the steps (since part 7.4, DOM-20, "The steps of a process, at runtime"); the read ports, where the adapter streams a query, every fact of their domain (part 5.2). `zz.replay({ versions, writes })(log)` gives an adapter's rebuild, from the log's first fact to the end of what it had recorded when the rebuild began (`caught_up`), each fact's position and the writes of its parts at the current shape; a part whose path the writes do not name is another store's to write and passes, untranslated (in Shed one log holds every domain's facts); a log that no longer keeps its first fact stops it. The oracle rebuilds its state the same way (`rebuilt`, `adapter/memory/`), and the scenarios' harness starts a shed from what its store kept before a release (`scenarios/kept.ts`: the drill at `tools` 1.0.0, Marta's loan of 2 November and its two waits at `loans` 1.4.0). Scenario: after 2.0.0 Marta extends her loan as due on the 16th, the rebuilt state's day of her due date; her phone's retry of the borrowing, under its operation of 2 November, reads its fact at 2.0.0 (`due_day`); her eve's wait reaches the executor with `due_day` and its `not_before` as queued (10:12 of the 15th); lent at 23:30 on the 2nd in Toronto, due at 23:30 on the 16th, already the 17th in UTC, the loan is due on the 16th, the day 1.x wrote beside the instant (`due_day`, SC-12, A, 23 Nov 2026). Rejected: the chain found by the majors' numbers (`majors[M + 1][path]`: the folder names its next, which the generator checks, and a second lookup could meet a component the folder does not lead to); the first fault only, at wiring (an operator would start the release once per fault); a builder checked by the domains of its registry rather than by what it reads (`loans`' ports take `tools`' registry for its identities, which are no stored data); `replay` stopping on a part it has no write for (an adapter rebuilds its own tables from a log of every domain), or translating it (a domain it does not rebuild would stop its rebuild).
  - A mistake corrected (SC-13) [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026]: a fact recorded by mistake stays in the log as recorded; the business corrects it by a command whose fact records the correction, and the state follows the new fact (D101; DOM-5: the fact carries the change). The effects queued with the wrong value are in flight: each return guards against the value its payload carries, with a branch that records the correction met (D95), and the correcting command queues what the right value needs, under a key of its own (DOM-13). A creator reads no state: the correcting command takes from whoever invokes it the values its effects need, a claim it checks. Scenario: Luca writes the drill on Marta's loan and hands her the sander; `correct_the_tool_of_a_loan` records `the_tool_of_a_loan_has_been_corrected` (the sander, the drill named before) and queues the eve of the sander; the eve of the drill, queued with the loan, records `the_tool_was_corrected_before_the_reminder`, and Marta is reminded of the sander only. The loan keeps its id and dates. It asks nothing new of the library. Set aside: the loan voided and a new one opened (its due day would move, and the voided loan's waits would meet no branch).
  - The day of an instant (SC-12) [Q, C, 17 Nov 2026; the instant [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]]: a node reads its days in one zone, its calendar, declared by its configuration (the clock's `time_zone`, a format of the library, an IANA name), never by a domain: one domain serves sheds in many towns, and q-'s nodes are persons in many zones. The library gives it in the root context (`RootContext.time_zone`). **An instant is absolute** [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]: ISO 8601 in UTC, `Z`, to the millisecond (`zz.date_time`, `z.iso.datetime({ precision: 3 })`: `2026-11-03T04:30:00.000Z`), as a clock writes it (`new Date().toISOString()`, the clocks of the PWA, of the oracles and of the relay as they are); the zone is explicit data where the business needs it, never a format's hidden part. A's reasons: explicitness (the zone was information hidden in a format); flaws and privacy (every `declared_at` of a letter told the peer its writer's zone: the software informs of what is needed, no more); order (an instant's text orders as its time, so LIB-9's text order by code point serves instants with no rule of its own, and an adapter that orders them as text, IndexedDB or SQLite, is right by construction: `adapter/memory/order.test.ts`, across the hour that repeats in autumn). Rejected (A): the instant with its writer's offset (`2026-11-02T23:30:00-05:00`, SC-12's form of 17 Nov 2026), whose reason was the day of a record as a property of the record, pure over the instant, for the translations of past majors: the fact carries that day now. A day is computed with the zone, `day_of(zone)(instant)`; from a day to an instant needs a zone with its rules, not an offset (a loan taken in Toronto on 20 Oct, at `-04:00` there, is due on 3 Nov, at `-05:00`): `start_of(zone)` and `end_of(zone)`, which give instants in UTC. All three only where a context gives the zone (a creator, for the `not_before` of a wait or a day a command decides; a leaf of a past major, PER-17), never in a translation of a fact, whose payload has no zone. **A day that matters where no zone is given is a field of the fact** [Q, C, 23 Nov 2026], decided by its command with the context's zone: a loan carries the day it started (`start_day`, beside `start_date`: the month's count of SC-21 reads it, in the oracle and in SQL) and the day it is due (`due_day`); `loans` 1.x wrote its days beside its instants (`start_day`, and `due_day` beside `due_date`, in the loan and in the eve's wait and reminder), and its translation takes them, never derives them. A day read where a context gives the zone is computed there: `dsl.today(context)`; a predicate's parameter `today`, a day, where it was `now` (`the_due_date_of_the_loan_is_still_ahead`, `the_week_of_the_summary_is_not_over`: the day of the clock of whoever asks, GR-8); the card valid today (`the_card_is_valid_today(context)`); the day a process's sheet started; a loan returned late, over the zone (`the_loan_was_returned_late(zone)`). Rejected (C): one field `day` on every fact whose day matters (a fact may have several days, and the office names each: lent on, due on); the zone a parameter of the calculation (each adapter computing the zone's days, in SQL `at time zone`: the zones' rules in every adapter); a day read in UTC. `in_zone` left the library (A, 23 Nov 2026): no port tells `now` in a zone, and a screen that shows a time formats it in the node's zone with `Intl` (the phone's texts), so nothing needs it. Days are counted on the calendar, in no zone. Scenario: at 23:30 on 2 Nov in Toronto, `2026-11-03T04:30:00.000Z`, Marta borrows the drill: the desk writes it lent on the 2nd and due on 16 Nov; the eve's wait goes at the start of 15 Nov there (`2026-11-15T05:00:00.000Z`), the overdue wait at the start of 17 Nov, and her phone at 1.4.0 reads 16 Nov, 23:59:59.999 there (`2026-11-17T04:59:59.999Z`); her loan of 1.x, due at 23:30 on the 16th, `2026-11-17T04:30:00.000Z`, the 17th in UTC, is due on the 16th at 2.0.0, the day 1.x wrote; in a shed whose calendar is UTC it would have been due on the 17th, its eve's wait would have gone at 19:00 on the 15th in Toronto, and her old phone would have read 18:59 on the 17th. At runtime (part 3.2, over the zones of the runtime, `Intl`, the name of a zone checked against them): a midnight that does not exist starts the day when the clock jumps into it (Santiago, 6 Sep 2026: 01:00 there, `2026-09-06T04:00:00.000Z`); in an hour that repeats, the earlier instant. [C, 19 Nov 2026] Set aside (17 Nov 2026): the zone a value of a domain, or a constant of its `dsl` (one town per domain, and a translation may not read the `dsl`); the instant with its zone's name (RFC 9557, `[America/Toronto]`), which would bring the zones' rules into the translations, and gives no zone to an event that carries only a day.
- **DOM-24** Commands, facts, predicates, use cases, and every other component of a domain are identified by their path: the folders from the root, the kind of the construct and the export's name (DOM-25). The path is rigid: it mirrors the folders, so a folder `retirement` adds `retirement` before the kind (`business/tools/retirement/fact/x`); the file is not in it, so moving a component between the files of one folder moves no path. A component is a named export. The path names the schema when a message is serialized for an adapter, and is the path of the event envelope (DOM-10). A path is an identity: like a fact's name (DOM-22), it never changes once released. [Q]
  - The domains' generator writes a path registry per domain, each path with its schema. It is compared in CI with the registry of `main`: a path that disappears, or whose schema changes without a new version of its domain (a major if a shape it leaves no longer parses), fails. By direction (PER-17, A, 17 Nov 2026): what the tree reads (an input, a fact, an effect) is a minor when every shape it leaves parses with the new schema; what its clients read (a leaf's events and entries) when every shape it now gives parses with the old one. Moving or renaming a component changes its path, so it is a new version: an explicit decision, with the old path deprecated and, if needed, kept with a translation for backward compatibility; never a side effect of a refactor. [Q]
  - The registry is also where the library finds a path at runtime (`_registry.ts`, each path with its component), and it is the map of the whole domain: identities, values, facts, predicates, named conditions, commands and their inputs, selection predicates, queries and their inputs, use cases [Q, 12 Nov 2026]: the configurator gives it the registries of the domains it composes, and the library reads them in reverse, from a component to its path, by object identity. One object, one path: the generator fails on a component declared under two names. The paper has no such registry; it asks that the exported surface be derived, not curated by hand (D75.4). [Q, 11 Nov 2026]
  - **A past major's registry is its whole map** (PER-17, DOM-22; Shed § Left by Shed): every path the major's clients were released with, the delegated ones included, and the input of every leaf, its own or delegated. The generator writes a delegated leaf's input as a delegation of the next major's (`return_a_tool_input = delegate(next.return_a_tool_input)` in `persona/member/1/_usecase.ts`, the type the next major's), wherever the next major's registry lists one; the author writes nothing more than `delegate(next.return_a_tool)`. The JSON Schema export of a major and CI's comparison by path and version read one registry per major, resolving a delegation to the next, as the port resolves a delegated leaf; at runtime nothing changes (the port parses with the leaf it finds, the next major's). Scenario: on 4 Nov `member` 2.0.0 is released and `1/` written; CI compares `1/`'s registry with the one 1.4.0 released, path by path: `return_a_tool_input` was there, and is still, delegated to 2.0.0's, the same schema; the export for the clients of 1.x lists every input of 1.x from one module. Without it the path would disappear from 1.x, a failure of CI or an exception written into it. Rejected: the input left out and derived by convention from the delegated leaf (`<leaf>_input` in the next registry: a map with holes by design, and every reader of the map following the convention); the next major's schema listed as it is (one object at one path in two majors, nothing telling it delegated, where a fact of `1/` says so). [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (7)]
  - Brands: a value is branded when exchanging it with another value of the same base type is a bug that compiles: identities (a value that names an instance: `tool_id`, `loan_id`) and formats (`date_time`, `day`); free text and enumerations are not. The brand's literal is the value's path (`tools/id/tool_id`), so two domains never share a brand by chance; the author never writes it. Formats carry no word of a domain and live in the library (LIB-2); a format's brand is its name (`day`), which no domain path can be. [Q]
  - A domain's identities live in its module `id`: the author declares their format with `id` (`tool_id = id({ format: z.string().min(1) })`), the generator writes in the twin each one branded with its path, under the same name, and the domain's files take them from there, as facts from theirs. The identities are all another business domain may import (GR-3): in Shed from `_id.ts`, checked by `.dependency-cruiser.mjs`. [Q, 11 Nov 2026]
  - Every generated file is committed; CI regenerates them and fails on any difference. [Q]
  - A component's path is not in its data. The generator brands every fact, predicate and selection predicate with its path in its generated module, as an identity (`fact.a_tool_has_been_added.brand<'tools/fact/a_tool_has_been_added'>()`): the same object as the author's at runtime, nominal in the types. Two facts of the same shape are never interchangeable, as schemas or as values; an unbranded schema is not a component; the builders check by type that a condition is made of predicates, a branch records a fact, a selection is made of selection predicates. At runtime the library finds the path in the registry; a fact travels in its event envelope, its path apart from its payload (DOM-10), and whoever consumes facts matches on the path, never on the payload, as in the paper (D56, D101.2, D107). No folder is named as a kind. [Q, 11 Nov 2026: until then the path was a field `$path` of the data]
  - The match on the path needs no function of the library: the envelope is a union discriminated by its path, so a `switch` on `path` narrows the payload and an exhaustive check (`never`) fails on a forgotten fact; an adapter matches with a record by path (`FactWrites`). The record generalizes when a second one comes (reactions, step 8). [Q, 11 Nov 2026]

### The domains of q-

| domain       | language and responsibility                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `seals`      | faces derived from seeds (flat index), seal for, open, sign, verify. Holds every secret; other domains hold opaque references and public keys. Primitives behind a port. Group keys come with groups, after stage 1.                                                                                                                                |
| `post`       | the protocol: envelope, letter (a request or a response, F7), version. Opens envelopes and verifies signatures; a request names the use case it invokes, the kernel's or an extension's (EXT-5). The kernel's port of requests (EXT-2): sends a request, again until its response or its time, and gives the response back as the request's return. |
| `mailbox`    | the relay's domain: deposit, collect from a position, keep 7 days, no deletion on collect.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `desk`       | the relay's personas: who deposits, who collects with R (PER-19).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `bonds`      | upstream business of the kernel: faces (one in stage 1, the model ready for more), bonds (my face, its key, its relays and D, my mailbox, name, annotations), tickets and the introduction. Groups and memberships, the kernel's, come in a later stage.                                                                                            |
| `extensions` | business of the kernel: the extensions installed, their activation by the user with the manifest accepted, the bonds each is offered to (EXT-7); the extensions in common with a bond (EXT-8).                                                                                                                                                      |
| `auth`       | data on the actor: which faces the local user has, which bond a remote face belongs to, which personas a caller may wear, which extensions are offered to a bond.                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `persona`    | the four personas and their use-case trees: the kernel's leaves and those the active extensions add (section 5, EXT-1).                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `user`       | activities and user concepts: drafts, active face, the extensions and their activation, "nothing happens in silence" (USR-1..USR-9).                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `ui`         | the language of the interface: screens, states, gestures (presentational state only; UI-1..UI-5).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

These are the **kernel** (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026): what every node of Cumino has. Every other feature is an **extension**, with domains of its own in a tree of its own (EXT-1); stage 1 has one, **ping pong** (`ping_pong`: a ping sent to a bond, its pong received; step 14). `conversations` (chats, messages, the news channel, receipts) is no longer a domain of the kernel: messaging will be the first extension after stage 1, and the news channel is to be placed (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]

Invariants of q- that the domains keep [Q]: every bond and every membership belongs to exactly one of my faces; no domain deduces a face's position from its key or its key from its position; the derivation of a face is invisible from outside. An extension never sees a face's position, and sees a key only if its manifest declares it (EXT-3); the scope of its operations is the bond's and the hat's, never the `Caller`'s key (EXT-2). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]

### The relay: `mailbox` and `desk`

The relay's domain and its personas (step 8; the words in the glossary, the stories to prove in [`packages/domains/src/mailbox/README.md`](../packages/domains/src/mailbox/README.md)). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]

- **MBX-1** A **deposit** is an envelope left on a deposit address D (`mailbox/deposits`, `deposit_an_envelope`), kept with the time the relay received it by its clock: the relay's measure, which a phone takes as the relay's claim (F2). Any D is a mailbox: none is opened or closed, and anyone may deposit on any (PER-19). The deposit's id is the operation's (`identity_of`, D75.8): a depositor names no deposit, and a retry is the duplicate branch (DOM-11), one envelope kept; the relay compares no envelopes. An envelope is opaque to the relay, base64url, at most 64 KiB decoded (87 382 characters): the relay's limit, its input's schema; D is base64url, at most 128 characters, its hash the interface's (PER-19). The depositor is told that the envelope is kept and when. Rejected: an id the depositor gives (D62), since the operation already names the deposit and a depositor is anyone; no limit on an envelope's size (a relay that keeps anything for anyone). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-2** **Seven days**: a deposit queues a wait of seven days of 24 hours from its deposit, by the relay's clock (`wait_seven_days_from_the_deposit`, DOM-13), whose return `discard_an_envelope` records `an_envelope_has_been_discarded`, collected or not: the deposit is discarded, its envelope out of the state, and it leaves the mailbox's selection (a live collect reads `left`). Time is not a fact (D92.4): an envelope leaves its mailbox only through a command. Rejected: a selection by `now` ("deposited in the last seven days"), where an envelope would leave the mailbox with no fact, no `left` on a stream and nothing the oracle defines; the retention as the adapter's alone (a time to live), since what the relay keeps is semantics a user observes (D114). What the fact log keeps of a discarded envelope, which its deposit fact carries (DOM-5), is the adapter's (step 19): it may drop it once discarded, and a position earlier than what it keeps is `position_unavailable` (DOM-17). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] The relay's storage forgets a deposit a day after its discard: MBX-8 (part 19). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-3** **Collecting** is the read `the_envelopes_in_a_mailbox` on its stream (F5, DOM-17), through the leaf `collect_the_envelopes` of `holder`: with no position, the envelopes kept, the oldest first, and a position, then those deposited after it; from a position, a resume. A collect changes nothing: no fact, no deletion. The holder's request names the mailbox's D, and the leaf is refused unless the collect secret shown is that mailbox's (the `Caller`'s id is the D named: `the_collect_secret_is_the_mailboxs`); who shows no secret is not found by the node of `holder` (PER-7, PER-14). A refusal tells nothing a depositor could not learn: every D is a mailbox. The phone compares positions only by order (DOM-17 [Q]); the library and the adapters by equality. Rejected: the node of `holder` giving D from the `Caller` (a node narrows the `Caller` only through a lookup's answer, and the desk has no data of auth, PER-19: a lookup that copies the `Caller` would be ceremony); R in the tree (V5: the interface turns it into D). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-4** **q-'s tree** (DOM-25): rooted at `packages/domains/src`, its map in `packages/domains/src/sight.ts`, written by the generator beside Shed's (`generator.config.ts`), its registries compared with `main`'s (DOM-24). Its tops are the kernel's domains of § 2, each as it is written (since part 8.1 `mailbox` and `desk`); a folder the map does not name (`library/`, a domain still to write) is outside the tree. Each top is the folder of its kind, as Shed's `business/` and `persona/`: a business top with its domains below (`mailbox/deposits`), a persona layer with its base node at the top and its personas below (`desk/node.ts`, `desk/depositor`, `desk/holder`). In the tree a domain has no `index.ts`: GR-8's "only exported surface" is its doors and scopes (DOM-25), and the cruiser's `only-through-index-inside` holds from no folder of the tree, whose imports its map's rules keep (`src-sight-of-desk`, …). What a package outside `domains` takes of the tree is MBX-5, since part 8.2. Rejected: a domain's components at its top (`mailbox/command.ts`), since the generator puts the doors to an audience in the folders below a top; an `index.ts` per domain re-exporting its doors, which the generator reads as a module of functions and which would be a door the map does not write. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-5** **The tree from outside** (part 8.2, its first consumer the oracle of MBX-7; then the relay's adapters, step 19, and the memory stack, step 17): a folder outside q-'s tree, of `domains` or of another package, reaches it through its **generated files** (`_registry.ts`, `_majors.ts`, `_fact.ts`, `_predicate.ts`, `_<subject>_selection.ts`, `_query.ts`, `_usecase.ts`, `_node.ts`, `_value.ts`, `_id.ts`, `_dsl.ts`, …), as Shed's adapters and configurator reach Shed's: never its scopes nor its doors (`_scope_*.ts`, `_door_*.ts`), which are the tree's inside, kept for its authored files (DOM-25); never an authored file, which its map's rule `src-authored-only-through-generated-others` already keeps. `@q-mino/domains` exports each top of the tree by its folders (`./mailbox/*`, `./desk/*`), and the cruiser's `only-through-index-inside` and `only-through-index-outside` let through, of the tree, only those files (`surface_of_q`, from the map's keys); the arrows between domains are unchanged. A top that enters the tree (`seals`, step 9) leaves its `index.ts` and its export by index for one by folder. Rejected: an exemption for the tests alone (the adapters and the stack are consumers too, and would ask for it again); a surface generated for the packages outside (a third kind of door, which the generator does not write, for what the generated files already are); an `index.ts` per domain (MBX-4). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-6** **The desk's interface** (PER-19, V5; part 8.2): what the interface of the desk gives the node `desk`, the `Caller`, from what a request shows: nothing (anyone, who may deposit), or a collect secret R, which it turns into its deposit address D through **a hash port** (`HashOfACollectSecret`: R → D = hash(R)), so that R never enters the tree and the domains compute no hash (GR-8); a hash out of D's form is a fault of the port. Which hash, and its encoding, are `seals`' (step 9: BLAKE2b-256 in base64url, `dsl.the_deposit_address_of`, SEA-4) and its primitives' (step 18). The **scope of a depositor's operation** is the persona alone, `depositor` (DOM-11): a depositor is anyone and has no id, so two deposits are told apart by their operations' ids, drawn at random by the phones (D62). A flaw turned feature: a phone that reuses another's operation id meets that deposit's fact, if it sent the same envelope on the same D (the same deposit, nothing lost), or `identity_already_used` (DOM-11): it learns that the id is taken, and nothing of the other envelope. In the `memory` stack the interface is a function (`the_desks_interface`, `packages/memory/src/relay/`); the HTTP interface of the relay (step 19) does the same over its requests (MBX-9). Rejected: the scope with an id of the depositor (a depositor has none, and one drawn per request would be the operation's id again); R hashed in the tree (V5, GR-8). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-7** **q-'s oracle** (TST-1, TST-2, V10; part 8.2): the in-memory state adapter of the kernel's domains, the reference interpreter, lives in the `memory` configurator (§ 2, CFG-3, PER-19: the stack of the oracles), in `packages/memory/src/oracle/`: a **cell** (the state of every domain of a node and its facts, each at its position with the state before and after it; the operations' facts; the outbox; the executors' state, and the reactions' consumers' (since part 11.3, BND-13); the trees' trace; the deviations of TST-4 injected: a position kept no longer, every fact and entry given twice), the **interpreter**, generic over the state (the serial writer, the reads, pages, the native stream, the fact log, the value order of LIB-9), and **one module per domain** with its fragments, sources and writes from its `dsl` (`mailbox.ts`, since part 8.2; `seals.ts`, 9.2; `bonds.ts`, 11.3; `post.ts`, 10.2). It is Shed's (`test/shed/adapter/memory/`) carried over, not shared: Shed's is a test tree's (LIB-2), q-'s is the product's. Over it, `packages/memory/src/relay/` wires **the relay** (the desk's trees, the executor of the deposits' waits, the interface of MBX-6), as Shed's configurator wires the shed, checked when it starts (CFG-2). The stories of a domain are a **suite parametrized by a factory** (TST-1: `packages/memory/test/relay/stories.ts`, over the harness `harness.ts`), run on the oracle streamed natively and composed by the library (`relay.test.ts`, D69.3); an adapter of `mailbox` runs the same suite with its own factory, as its conformance suite (step 19). A collect from a position is a resume only on a stream of the adapter's own: one the library composes answers `position_unavailable`, and the phone reads again (DOM-18, part 5.2), what the suite takes as a parameter (`Resume`); the relay's storage, step 19, says which it is: composed (MBX-8). Rejected: the oracle in `packages/domains/test/` as Shed's (the stack and the adapters' suites, in other packages, could not reach it); a cell per domain (the oracle keeps one log per node, DOM-8); the stories written once per adapter (TST-1). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-8** **The relay's storage** (part 19; ADP-6, ADP-8, DOM-9, TST-1): `mailbox/deposits` on SQLite (`node:sqlite`), in `adapters-node/src/sqlite/` (`the_relays_storage(location)`): one database for the state, the fact log, the outbox and the executor's state, reached only through the ports. A **serial writer** by construction: one process, and each command's step synchronous, one transaction from the duplicate branch to the commit (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`), so that the outcomes are a serial order's (TST-5) and a fall rolls the step back, fact and state together (DOM-8); WAL with `synchronous = FULL`, so that a deposit told kept is on disk. Positions are SQLite's `AUTOINCREMENT`, never given twice. Each predicate, selection, source, field and fact's write is a piece of SQL (ADP-8), conditions and selections under one algebra; LIB-9's value order is SQLite's binary order of UTF-8 (by code point), `NULLS LAST` ascending. Its read is **streamed by the library** over its log (DOM-2): a collect from a position is `position_unavailable`, and the phone reads its mailbox again (story 4, `by_reading_again`). Rejected: a native stream, which would keep a history of the rows to give the state before a position, for a mailbox of a week of envelopes: no case asks for it (LIB-3), and a measure of the phones' readings again would. **The relay forgets**: a discard takes the envelope out of its row; a day after its discard, at its next deposit or discard, by that fact's time, or when its configurator asks (`forget_what_is_past(now)`, which step 24's main calls), the relay forgets a deposit: its row, and the log's facts before the deposit fact of the oldest deposit it still remembers (the last fact always kept, for a reader waiting after it); an outbox entry is forgotten once the executor has gone past it. So the relay keeps nothing of a deposit past eight days, seven kept and one remembered, the retry window of its depositors (D100): a retry of a deposit forgotten is a new deposit, kept seven days more, with the first's id, its operation's (DOM-12): the one id TST-5 sees given again, to the same operation, so that a holder who collected the first drops it as the same; and a read of the log from a position it forgot is `position_unavailable` (TST-4), also for a reader it overtook. A flaw turned feature: the relay keeps no record of who deposited what where beyond what it must answer (CFG-3). With its clock set back, or a wait delivered late, discards come out of the order of the deposits, and a forgotten deposit's facts stay in the log until those before it are forgotten. Rejected: the log kept whole, as the oracle's (every envelope kept for ever in its deposit fact, and every D with its times: an access log by another name); forgetting at the discard itself (a wait delivered again after a fall, or a deposit retried within the hour, would meet nothing, and the wait's return would be a dead letter); the envelope taken out of its fact (a fact is not rewritten, and the duplicate branch projects it). Its conformance suite is the relay's ten stories through the relay's configurator on SQLite (`packages/memory/test/relay/on_sqlite.test.ts`), entries and facts given twice by decorators of its outbox and its log (ADP-5); its own tests, a restart and the forgetting, in `packages/relay/test/`. Both choices, the forgetting a day after the discard and the collect streamed by the library, confirmed by Alessandro (22 Nov 2026), to be measured at step 27: a week of mailbox on 8 bonds. [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]
- **MBX-9** **The relay's HTTP interface** (part 19; PER-19, MBX-6, ADP-1, ADP-4), on Hono, in `adapters-node/src/hono/` (`the_desks_http_interface`): a transport. `POST /depositor/deposit_an_envelope` with `{ version, operation, input }`; `POST /holder/collect_the_envelopes` with `{ version, input, from? }`, the collect secret as `Authorization: Bearer <R>`. What a request shows becomes the desk's `Caller` as in MBX-6 (nothing, or D = hash(R) through the hash port, so that R never enters the tree); the operation is in the depositor's scope, its correlation the operation's, since nothing of a phone's chain crosses the relay (F2); `now` is the relay's clock. The tree's events go back with 200 as NDJSON (`application/x-ndjson`), one a line, in their order, pulled as the phone reads them; a collect stays open until the phone lets it go, and its stream is let go then (one waiting for the next fact, at the next fact). A body not in this form, or an `Authorization` that is not a bearer credential, is 400 `{ fault }`, heard by no tree; a path the interface has not, 404. It reads nothing of an event. **No access logs** (CFG-3): it writes nothing about the requests it serves, and the trees' trace goes nowhere, since the relay has no user to show it to (F6). The wiring is the relay's configurator, `packages/relay/src/` (the trees, the executor and the interface over the storage), tested with `app.request`, no server started; its main, which opens the database, serves the interface and starts the executor, is step 24's. Rejected: paths by mailbox (`/mailboxes/:D`: D in a URL, which every proxy logs); R in the body (a bearer credential goes where HTTP carries one, V5); Server-Sent Events (an `EventSource` sends no `Authorization`, and over `fetch` its framing is no simpler than NDJSON); a status per event (the events are the contract's, the status the transport's). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] **CORS** [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]: the PWA and the relay are two origins, and a deposit or a collect sends `Authorization` and JSON, so the browser preflights it; without an answer the request fails as `unreachable` (WEB-6). The interface answers it with Hono's own `cors` (`hono/cors`, no new dependency): every origin (`*`), the method `POST`, the headers `Authorization` and `Content-Type`, the preflight kept a day (`maxAge` 86 400 seconds), no credentials (R is a bearer credential the phone sets, never a cookie); every answer carries `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (tested with `app.request`: the preflight's answer and headers, a POST's). A flaw turned feature: CORS is no gate. Only a browser enforces an origin, any other client ignores it, and the relay is open to every depositor anyway (every D a mailbox, MBX-6), so a page served anywhere, a fork's included, speaks to the relay as the PWA does. Rejected: a list of origins given by the relay's main (it protects nothing, and ties the relay to who serves the PWA: a fork served elsewhere would need the relay reconfigured).

### The secrets: `seals`

The domain of the node's secrets (step 9; the words in the glossary, the stories to prove in [`packages/domains/src/seals/README.md`](../packages/domains/src/seals/README.md)). Alessandro's choices of 6 Nov 2026 on the cryptography (his notes, "Crittografia" and "Volti: perché piatti") are its frame: libsodium behind the primitives port, Ed25519 to sign and its X25519 form to seal, one public key per face, seeds of 32 random bytes with a declared scheme, the face i at SLIP-0010's `m/i'`, its key and chain code kept in `seals`, D = BLAKE2b(R) with R random kept in `seals`, keys as bytes in storage. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]

- **SEA-1** **The safe** (`seals/safe`): one business domain under the top `seals`, which enters q-'s tree as a business top with its domain below (MBX-4) and is reached by folder from outside (MBX-5: `seals/index.ts` gone, `@q-mino/domains` exports `./seals/*`). It keeps the **seeds**, the **faces** and the **mailbox keys**, and sees no other domain (`sight.ts`: `seals: {}`; § 2, `seals` imports the library only). One domain, so that a face and the seed it takes its index from are written in one atomic write (DOM-8). Its values are bytes in base64url with no padding, each of its length (32 bytes, 43 characters; a signature 64 bytes, 86), so that a fact, a read and a request carry them as JSON and a value of another length is `invalid_request` (DOM-21). Rejected: three domains, `seeds`, `faces`, `mailbox_keys` (a face and its seed's next index would be two domains' writes, a reaction between them); `keys` as its name (a second word for the faces' keys and the mailboxes'). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-2** **A seed and its indices**: 32 random bytes, its scheme `slip10-flat` (the face at index i is SLIP-0010's hardened child `m/i'` over Ed25519: `dsl.the_face_at`, the one construction of q-'s own, over the port's HMAC-SHA512), and its **next index**, which only grows: an index is never taken twice, so that the seed alone gives back every face, trying the indices in turn. A face keeps its private key (SLIP-0010's key of the child, the seed of its Ed25519 key pair), its chain code (for the shareable branches to come, a face as a branch's root) and its public key. In stage 1 the node has one face (§ 4, `bonds`): a seed is drawn with its first face, at index 0, and keeps 1 as its next (`make_a_face_from_a_new_seed`). A face after the first, from a seed in use, is stage 2's ("more faces"): its fact must carry the seed's next index, a value of the safe's own state that no invoker has read, the case DOM-20 names for `reads`, which only a step has; it comes with its case (LIB-3), as `reads` on `command` (Alessandro's hunch, DOM-20) or another construct, a choice for Alessandro then. Rejected: the index from the digest of the operation (a random index: the seed alone could not give back its faces in turn); the index given by the asker (a face's position known outside the safe, against § 4's invariants); a process with a cursor over the faces asked for (a process with no case of its own, which would close when its queue empties). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-3** **A face**: asked for by a reaction only (PER-21; the first face is a process of `bonds`, DOM-23, step 11), which names it: `make_a_face_from_a_new_seed` records `a_face_has_been_asked_for` (the face **being made**) and queues `draw_a_seed_for_the_face`, whose return `keep_a_new_seed_and_its_face` records `a_face_has_been_made_from_a_new_seed`: the seed, its id the operation's (D75.8: a redelivery is the duplicate branch, one seed; a machine that draws again for the same entry says another seed, another request under the operation's identity, a dead letter `identity_already_used` that keeps the second seed, SEA-7, while the first seed and its face stand: [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026], part 9.2), and the face **made**, with its keys. The face's id is the asker's, the key of the link with the face as `bonds` keeps it (GR-3), so that the reaction back to `bonds` finds its face in the safe's fact; it is opaque all the same: it tells neither the seed, nor the scheme, nor the index, and the asker learns of the face its public key, from the fact. Alessandro's point (6 Nov 2026: the port is "a new face from this seed" and gives an opaque reference, so that the caller knows no scheme) holds: who asks names a face and gets its key, never its derivation. The letter of that decision is reversed (Alessandro, 21 Nov 2026, the final review): the port gives no reference back, who asks gives the id; his point holds, who asks knows neither the scheme, nor the seed, nor the index; the reason is the rejected form's, below. Rejected: the id chosen by the safe from the operation, as a deposit's (the reaction back would need a field of the asker carried through the safe's command and fact, a word of `bonds` in `seals`). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-4** **A mailbox key**: the collect secret R of one of the node's mailboxes, 32 random bytes, and its deposit address D = BLAKE2b-256(R) in base64url, 43 characters, inside the form of MBX-1's D (`dsl.the_deposit_address_of`). Asked for by a reaction (PER-21) under an id it gives, as a face: `draw_a_mailbox_key` records `a_mailbox_key_has_been_asked_for` and queues `draw_the_mailbox_key`, whose return `keep_a_mailbox_key` records `a_mailbox_key_has_been_drawn`. R leaves the safe only through the read `the_key_of_a_mailbox`, toward whoever collects from the relay, who shows it (V5: a bearer credential); D is public. `dsl.the_deposit_address_of` is the hash of MBX-6's port (`HashOfACollectSecret`): the relay's interface computes D from a shown R with it, over its own primitives (step 18). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-5** **The primitives port** (GR-5): `Primitives` in the safe's `dsl.ts`, the safe's machine, libsodium's ready constructions (step 18): HMAC-SHA512; the Ed25519 public key of a private key; sign; whether a signature holds; the sealed box of a content for the X25519 form of an Ed25519 public key, or none for bytes that are no such key (`Sealing`, an ADT, as `Opening`: libsodium refuses a value that is no point of Ed25519's group, or one of small order, and a peer's key is a claim, F2); its opening with the X25519 form of a private key's key pair (`Opening`, an ADT: opened with its content, or not); BLAKE2b-256; random bytes. It takes and gives `Uint8Array`; the safe's values tell bytes in base64url, with the dsl's codec (`base64url_of`, `bytes_of`: the domains have no `atob`, GR-8). Randomness enters only as the world's answer to an effect (GR-8): a creator is given none, so what is random (a seed, R) is drawn by the delivery of the safe's effect, and its return keeps it; the deliveries are the dsl's `a_new_seed_and_its_first_face` and `a_new_mailbox_key`, which an adapter only calls (ADP-1). The derivation is in the delivery too, since a creator is given no port. Sealing draws its own ephemeral key inside the port, as libsodium's sealed box does. Rejected: randomness and the port in a command's context (a new part of the library's root context for one domain); a keystore behind the port, the seed's bytes outside the state (Alessandro: key and chain code are kept in `seals`, as bytes in storage). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] Amended in part 18: `seal_for` answers `Sealing`, and `dsl.sealed_for` its told form, `sealed` with the sealed content or `not_sealed`, which `post` reads (step 10); found on the real machine, the port of 9.1 gave bytes for every key. Rejected: the adapter throwing on a key libsodium refuses (a pure function over a peer's claim would throw, a technical abort for what is an answer). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-6** **What leaves the safe**: ids, public keys, and what a face computes, never a private key, a chain code, a seed or an index (§ 4: "the derivation of a face is invisible from outside"). To sign and to open are **calculations** of reads (DOM-18, SC-21) over the face: `a_content_signed_by_a_face` (the face, its public key and its signature over a content) and `a_sealed_content_opened_by_a_face` (opened or not), whose values the adapters compute with the face's private key over the port (`dsl.the_signature_of`, `dsl.opened_with`); nothing is recorded. A face being made reads nothing. To seal for a face and to verify a signature need no secret: they are the dsl's pure functions over a public key (`dsl.sealed_for`, `dsl.the_signature_holds`), `post`'s to call (step 10, GR-5). Rejected: signing and opening as commands (they change no state, and a fact would record every letter's content); as effects (a letter would wait on its signature through the outbox, which is for the world, DOM-23); the private key read by `post` (§ 4: `seals` holds every secret). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-7** **The safe's facts carry its secrets**, as its state does, since they carry its change (DOM-5, V9): no more than the state, kept in the node as bytes in storage (Alessandro, 6 Nov 2026). No letter carries them, and no persona nor screen reaches them (PER-21); a reaction reads of a fact what it maps (step 11), the face's id and public key, a mailbox key's id and D. A dead letter of a return of the safe (DOM-15) keeps what the world said, a seed or an R, and is shown to the user of the node, whose secrets they are (F6: hiding is toward peers, never toward the node's user); a dead letter of a draw carries an id only. A flaw turned feature: whoever reads the node's storage reads its secrets, as with any key kept on a phone; the software says so. Rejected: a door that shows a folder's facts but some (a door gives a kind of a folder whole, DOM-25: a construct of the library with no case but this). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-8** **The safe's stories, one suite over any primitives port** (TST-1, TST-3): the stories of `seals` (its README) are one suite, `stories_of_the_safe(name, primitives)` in `packages/memory/test/seals/`, the core's (the dsl through `_dsl.ts`, MBX-5) and the safe's (q-'s oracle, MBX-7, module `oracle/seals.ts`) together; the harness wraps the port it is given, recording its calls and drawing the world's random bytes from a list the story gives, so that a story knows the seed and the R it meets over any machine. `safe.test.ts` runs it over a fake port whose answers are a rule the stories know (not cryptographic: a digest of its arguments; a sealed box that names its public key, with a tag); step 18 runs the same suite over libsodium's adapter, with the SLIP-0010 vectors. The deliveries of the safe's effects are the harness's in the stories, the dsl's over the port (ADP-1); in a stack, the configurator's. Rejected: the core's stories in `packages/domains/test/seals/` (the README's proposal of 9.1: a second fake, since a test of `domains` cannot import `memory`'s, and stories that run on the fake alone); a fake per story. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **SEA-9** **The primitives over libsodium** (step 18; ADP-1, TST-1, TST-6, SEA-5): `libsodium_primitives()` in `adapters-common/src/libsodium/`, the port once libsodium's WebAssembly is loaded (`sodium.ready`, its one asynchronous call: the port's calls are synchronous), the same code in the browser and in Node (§ 6). Each call is one of libsodium's constructions: HMAC-SHA512 in its multi-part form (`crypto_auth_hmacsha512_init`, `_update`, `_final`: the one-shot form takes only 32-byte keys, and SLIP-0010's master is under the 12 bytes of "ed25519 seed"); a private key as the seed of its Ed25519 key pair (`crypto_sign_seed_keypair`), signing and verifying detached; the sealed box (`crypto_box_seal`, `crypto_box_seal_open`) for the X25519 forms libsodium converts the Ed25519 keys to (`crypto_sign_ed25519_pk_to_curve25519`, `_sk_to_curve25519`); BLAKE2b-256 as `crypto_generichash` of 32 bytes, unkeyed; `randombytes_buf`. Where libsodium refuses what it is given (its -1, which the wrappers throw), the port answers by its contract, never by a throw: a signature or a public key of another length does not hold; bytes that are no key one can seal for seal nothing (`not_sealed`, SEA-5); a sealed content too short, broken or for another key is not opened. A private key of another length than 32 bytes is a fault of the caller: the safe derives every private key (SEA-2). Its own tests (TST-6: each call of the port), in `packages/adapters-common/test/libsodium/`: SLIP-0010's ed25519 vectors from the specification (`m` by the port's HMAC, `m/0H` by `dsl.the_face_at`, every row's public key by `public_key_of`), and the face at any index of any seed compared with Node's HMAC and Ed25519 (OpenSSL's); RFC 4231's HMAC-SHA512 and RFC 8032's Ed25519 known answers, signatures compared with Node's; BLAKE2b-256 of "" and "abc"; the sealed box opened by its key alone and whole, refused for what is no key. The safe's and the address book's suites run over it in `memory`'s tests (`safe.test.ts`, `address_book.test.ts`), which may import an adapter (CFG-4), as an adapter's tests may not import `memory`'s suites (`adapters-do-not-import-configurators`). The deliveries of the safe's effects stay the configurator's (SEA-8); the relay's hash of a collect secret (MBX-6) is `dsl.the_deposit_address_of` over this port, wired by the relay's configurator (step 19). Rejected: the suites in `adapters-common`'s tests (a second copy of the stories, or an arrow from an adapter to a configurator); HMAC-SHA512 composed over SHA-512 in the adapter (a construction of q-'s own outside the dsl, SEA-5); a test of the vectors among the stories (they hold on no fake). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - **The package** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: `libsodium-wrappers-sumo` 0.8.4 (ISC; libsodium.js, by libsodium's author; no install script; WebAssembly with its JavaScript, one build for the browser and Node), since the standard `libsodium-wrappers` has no HMAC-SHA512. Its weight, unpacked, with the libsodium it wraps: 2.18 MB (the wrappers 0.55 MB, `libsodium-sumo` 1.62 MB), against 1.78 MB for the standard build (the wrappers 0.55 MB, `libsodium` 1.23 MB), measured in `node_modules/.pnpm` (21 Nov 2026); § 6 names libsodium, not its package. Rejected: the standard build (no HMAC-SHA512: SLIP-0010 over another HMAC than libsodium's); `sodium-native` (a native addon, Node only); WebCrypto's HMAC (asynchronous, where the port is not); a library other than libsodium (against Alessandro's choice of 6 Nov 2026).

### The bonds: `bonds`

The domain of the node's user's faces and bonds (step 11; the words in the glossary, the stories to prove in [`packages/domains/src/bonds/README.md`](../packages/domains/src/bonds/README.md)). Part 11.1 writes the faces and the bonds as data and what the user keeps of them; the first face, the ticket and the introduction, which make them, are part 11.2's. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]

- **BND-1** **The address book** (`bonds/address_book`): one business domain under the top `bonds`, which enters q-'s tree as a business top with its domain below (MBX-4) and is reached by folder from outside (MBX-5: `bonds/index.ts` gone, `@q-mino/domains` exports `./bonds/*`); it sees no other domain (`sight.ts`: `bonds: {}`; § 2, `bonds` imports the library only). It keeps the **faces** of the node's user and her **bonds** (with part 11.2 the tickets and the introductions), as an address book keeps one's own cards and one's contacts. One domain, so that a bond and the face it belongs to are read and written in one atomic write (DOM-8): a bond is made only on a made face of mine (`the_face_is_made`, in the introduction's conditions, 11.2), and names **exactly one** face of mine (`my_face_id`, a field, never a list): § 4's invariant by construction. Rejected: two domains, `faces` and `bonds` (the face's condition would be another domain's, read non-atomically, GR-3); `contacts` as its name (a bond is the relation, not the person: glossary). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-2** **A face's id is its opaque reference**: the address book files a face under the id it gives the safe (SEA-3), the key of the link with the face the safe keeps (GR-3); my mailbox of a bond likewise, under the id of its key in the safe (`mailbox_id`, SEA-4); since part 11.2 the bond's own id (BND-7: every mailbox of mine is a ticket's, filed under its bond's id, so `mailbox_id` is gone). No other reference, and no secret: no private key, chain code, seed or index is in the address book; a face's public key and a mailbox's D are copied from the safe's facts by the reactions back (SEA-7, part 11.2). The two ids are the address book's own (`face_id`, `mailbox_id`): a reaction parses the safe's into them. Rejected: a reference beside the id (two names for one face, and a map between them to keep). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-3** **A face as data**: its **name**, the one it is introduced as; its **position** in its user's tree of faces, an ADT (`at_the_top`, or `under_a_face` with that face's id), data of hers and never of the keys (Alessandro, 6 Nov 2026: "l'albero è un dato"); its **public key**, once made. Being made (the safe draws its seed), then made (SEA-3). In stage 1 one face, at the top; the model is ready for more: faces are instances by id and the position a value, so that stage 2 (a face under another, _Sposta_, _Nuovo volto qui_) adds commands, not a migration (Alessandro: stage 2 adds faces and seeds "senza migrazioni"). No key tells a position, no position a key (§ 4). `my_faces` reads the made faces, by name. Rejected: no position in stage 1 (every face migrated when the tree comes); the position as a path of names (a rename would move faces); the seeds as nodes of the tree (they are not, Alessandro, 6 Nov 2026, and a seed is the safe's). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-4** **A bond's two mailboxes**: **their mailbox**, its D and its relays as they declared them at the introduction (part 11.2; their claim, F2), where my node deposits for them; **my mailbox**, its key in the safe under the bond's id (BND-7), its D and its relays as I declared them in my ticket, where they deposit for me: one for each bond, so one for each direction of each bond (Alessandro's notes, "Relay": the relay sees mailboxes it cannot link). The **relays** are a list of **relay addresses** (`https` URLs), of one relay in stage 1 (the schema says one; the preferred relays of a face, overridden per bond, widen it later). A D is in the relay's form (MBX-1: base64url, at most 128 characters), not the safe's 43 characters: theirs is their node's, which may hash otherwise, and the node is informed, not a judge. `the_bond_at_my_mailbox` gives, by the D a letter was collected from, the bond it came on, the face of mine that opens it (`post`, step 10) and the key it should be signed by (auth, step 13); at a D of no bond in the list, nothing. My D is one bond's only: the introduction keeps it so (part 11.2). Rejected: my relays at my face (stage 1 has one relay, and the preferred relays come later); the bond of a letter found by its signer's key alone (in stage 2 one key may be bonded to two faces of mine; the mailbox a letter came at is what my node measures, F2). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-5** **The node's annotations of a bond** (F6): the **handshake**, in person or at a distance, and when, by my clock; the **confirmation**, awaited until a letter of theirs comes on the bond, then received, with when (the screens' "lei ti ha? non ancora confermato"). They are my node's measures, written by the introduction (part 11.2) and by a letter's arrival (step 13); the user reads them and writes none: a note of hers would be her claim, another concept, with its case. "In person" is information, not a guarantee (Alessandro, 3 Nov 2026: the trust is in the person or the light, not in the gesture). Rejected: annotations the user writes (no case in stage 1); "in person" as a level of trust. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-6** **The user keeps her bonds**: `rename_a_bond`, the name she gives, hers only, beside the name they are introduced as, which stays (Alessandro: "il nome di presentazione originale resta"); `remove_a_bond`, the screens' "Elimina e basta": the bond leaves her list, its mailbox is collected no more (BND-4: no bond at its D), nothing goes to them, and removed again is nothing to do (DOM-6). A removed bond stays in the state, with when: a new handshake with the same face makes a new bond (part 11.2). A removed bond cannot be renamed: refused. A flaw turned feature: the removal is mine; they may go on depositing on my mailbox for seven days, which my node no longer collects, and their node is not told unless I say so. Rejected: the bond's data forgotten at its removal (its facts keep them, DOM-5: a removal that claimed to forget would be a guarantee the node cannot give). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - **"Elimina e avvisa"**, a removal that tells them, is a function of bonds, not of messaging (Alessandro, 22 Nov 2026): a request of the kernel between bonded faces, with no chat. **Open, after the block** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]; its design, as written now: the user's gesture is a case of removing a bond (`and_tell_them`, or the form the activities' ADT takes, USR-2); a request of the kernel on `known`, sent on the bond before it is removed; their node records that the face of the bond removed them, and their screen of that contact tells it. A flaw turned feature: a modified client may not tell, and the letter may not arrive within the seven days its mailbox keeps it.

- **BND-7** **A ticket is a bond's first stage** (part 11.2): one instance under one id, `bond_id`, from the ticket to the bond: **being prepared** (`ticket_being_prepared`: the safe draws my mailbox's key), **ready** (`ticket_ready`: it has its D, it can be shown), then **used**, the bond **kept**, or **expired** (`ticket_expired`); kept, then removed (BND-6). The ticket holds my side (my face, my mailbox, until when it holds); the introduction writes theirs (their face, their mailbox, the name I give, the annotations). The id names the ticket, the bond and my mailbox's key in the safe (SEA-4), so that a reaction from the safe finds the ticket by the key's id, and the two introductions of a crossed QR meet on one bond in either order (BND-10). In stage 1 every mailbox of mine is a ticket's. Rejected: the ticket and the bond as two instances (the command that makes the bond would have to write my face and my mailbox, which its creator cannot read, D105: they would be claims in its input); a `mailbox_id` beside the bond's id (two names of one mailbox, BND-2's reason). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-8** **The first face and the ticket, processes of `bonds`** (DOM-23), their steps toward `seals` reactions (GR-9, PER-21, BND-12). The **first face**: `make_my_first_face` (the phone's first start, a name, at the top) records `a_first_face_has_been_asked_for`; `reaction/first_face` invokes the safe's `make_a_face_from_a_new_seed` with the same id (SEA-3) and, from `a_face_has_been_made_from_a_new_seed`, `keep_the_key_of_a_face` with the face's id and public key only (SEA-7), which records `a_face_has_been_made`. One face in stage 1: a second first face is refused (`the_user_has_a_face`). The **ticket**: `prepare_a_ticket` (a bond's id, a made face of mine, the relays I declare, one in stage 1) records `a_ticket_has_been_asked_for` with its expiry, 48 hours after, by my clock, and queues the wait `wait_until_the_ticket_expires` (DOM-13); `reaction/ticket` invokes the safe's `draw_a_mailbox_key` under the bond's id and, from `a_mailbox_key_has_been_drawn`, `keep_the_mailbox_of_a_ticket` with the key's id and D only, which records `a_ticket_has_been_prepared`: ready. The wait's return, `let_a_ticket_expire`, records `a_ticket_has_expired` for a ticket still unused, ready or being prepared, and `the_ticket_was_used_before_it_expired` for one that made its bond (D95): no entry is cancelled. A key drawn after the ticket expired records `a_mailbox_has_come_for_an_expired_ticket` (DOM-23: a step's outcome is a fact). Rejected: the expiry as a selection by `now` (MBX-2's reason: time is not a fact, D92.4); 48 hours from the ticket ready (the user asked for it then; the safe's draw is the node's, of seconds). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-9** **The ticket's secret** is my face's signature over the ticket's mailbox address, prefixed (`q-ticket/` then D, `dsl.the_content_a_ticket_s_secret_signs`): the safe makes it when the ticket is shown (`a_content_signed_by_a_face`, SEA-6), and it is kept nowhere. At the introduction `the_secret_is_the_ticket_s` holds when the signature does over the ticket's D, for the public key of the ticket's face: the adapter verifies it with the safe's pure `dsl.the_signature_holds` (GR-3: the link is the state adapter's; SEA-6: a verification needs no secret). Only the face makes it, so a relay, which sees D, cannot forge an introduction; every secret stays in the safe (§ 4: the private key). A flaw turned feature: whoever has the ticket has its secret; a link forwarded is a ticket given, used once (BND-10). Alessandro's model (6 Nov 2026: the ticket issued keeps "segreto (riferimento)") read so: the reference is the face. Rejected: 32 random bytes kept in the safe under the ticket's id (a kind of secret of the safe's that leaves in every ticket shown, kept 48 hours in storage, and a ticket ready only once two draws are joined); the secret in the address book (a secret outside the safe, § 4). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
- **BND-10** **The introduction, from both sides, one path near and far** (Alessandro's notes, "Stretta di mano"; PER-11). To **open their ticket** (`open_their_ticket`, the user's, `self`) is to present a ready ticket of mine, the one I show (the crossed QR) or one prepared for their link: the bond is kept, their face and mailbox as their ticket declares them (F2), the name I give, the handshake at a distance by my clock, the confirmation awaited, and **my introduction** sent: the effect `send_my_introduction` (F4, F5), keyed by the bond, its payload their ticket, which `post` delivers (step 10) to their ticket's mailbox with its secret and my ticket, signed by my face, reading my side of the bond (`a_bond`, `my_faces`), since a creator reads no state. Its delivery, a request of the kernel's to their `unknown`, since step 17: STK-2.
