[Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] To **accept their introduction** (`accept_their_introduction`, the leaf of `unknown`, step 13, whose entry gives the bond by the mailbox it was collected from, `the_ticket_at_my_mailbox` or `the_bond_at_my_mailbox`, and their key as the signer, F3, and the time its letter declares, `declared_at`, a claim, PER-13): my ticket ready and the secret its (BND-9), the bond is kept, named as they are introduced, at a distance, the confirmation received (their introduction is a letter of theirs, BND-5), my introduction not sent. The **crossed QR** is the same flow done by both, in either order: the second of the two events, from the same face on the same bond, completes the first (my introduction sent, or the confirmation received), and **in person** when the two are within **the window of a crossing**, two minutes (`dsl.a_crossing_s_minutes`) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026], measured between the time their introduction declares (its letter's `declared_at`, their claim, PER-13, which the entry of `unknown` gives `accept_their_introduction` and the bond keeps, `their_introduction`) and my opening of their ticket (my introduction sent, by my clock), in either order and whenever my node takes the second event: with no push, a phone closed takes an introduction late, and one meeting reads one way (`i_opened_their_ticket_within_the_window_of_a_crossing_of`, `their_introduction_is_declared_within_the_window_of_a_crossing_of`; the branches `…_within_the_window_of_a_crossing_so_we_met_in_person` and `…_outside_the_window_of_a_crossing_so_…`) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]; a third is nothing to do (DOM-6). **Two flaws turned features**, told to the user: the time is their claim, so a letter declares the time it wants, and an introduction written later reads in person when it declares a time within the window; a crossing at a distance done within the window (two links opened together) reads in person. "In person" stays an annotation, never a guarantee (BND-5; Alessandro, 3 Nov 2026). Refused, no fact, to the trace (F6): an introduction to a ticket used by another face (a ticket used twice), expired, still being prepared, or a bond removed; a secret not the ticket's. A bond's relays are those its peer declared at the first event; a later one changes nothing. Rejected: "in person" from the screen's gesture (BND-5: the node measures, and a crossing at a distance is not in person); the window measured when my node takes the second event, my clock against my clock (with no push, a phone closed gave one meeting two annotations; Alessandro, 21 Nov 2026); a window of a few seconds (Alessandro's notes: phones that collect at intervals would miss it); no window (every crossing, however far apart, would read in person); one bond per key of theirs (two handshakes, two bonds, each one consistent, which the user may remove, BND-6); the opener checking the expiry (the issuer's, whose clock decides); the issuer introducing itself back (no case: the confirmation comes with their next letter, step 13). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-11** **The mailboxes I collect** (`the_mailboxes_i_collect`): my ready tickets', where introductions come, and my kept bonds', where their letters come; not an expired ticket's, nor a removed bond's (BND-6). `post` collects them (step 10). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-12** **The reactions' place** (GR-9, at the first step written): the top `reaction/` of q-'s tree, the kind folder of the reactions between the kernel's domains, as Shed's, its folders the **procedures** (`reaction/first_face/`, `reaction/ticket/`). Its map sees the facts and the use cases of `bonds` and `seals` (`sight.ts`), its arrow in the cruiser (`reaction: library, seals, bonds`, § 2) and its export by folder (`./reaction/*`, MBX-5) for the configurators that wire it. MBX-4's tops are the kernel's domains and, since part 11.2, the reactions between them. Rejected: the reactions inside `bonds` (`bonds/reaction/`: `bonds` would see `seals`, against § 2 and GR-9, no domain holds them); a top per procedure. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **BND-13** **The address book's stories, a phone on the oracle** (TST-1, TST-3, MBX-7, SEA-8; part 11.3): the stories of `bonds` (its README) are one suite, `stories_of_the_address_book(name, primitives)` in `packages/memory/test/bonds/`, run by `address_book.test.ts` over the safe's fake primitives port, by step 18 over libsodium's. A phone is **one cell** with `bonds` and `seals` (MBX-7: one log per node; the reactions read it), each domain's module over its part of the node's state: the address book's under its domain's name (`address_book`, since the safe has faces of its own), the cell seen by a module as its domain's (`seen_by`: a step recorded over the part keeps the rest). The cell keeps the **reactions' consumers' state** beside the executors' (DOM-16, as Shed's). The harness runs the reactions of `reaction/` and both executors until the phone is quiet; a wait comes back when the story's clock reaches it; the delivery of `send_my_introduction` hands the letter to the harness, which carries it as `post` would (step 10: my side read from `a_bond` and `my_faces`, their ticket's secret, at their ticket's D) and gives it to the other phone as the entry of `unknown` would (step 13: the ticket ready at that D, else the bond kept there; none, nothing invoked). The oracle's writes **parse the value they leave**, a face or a bond of the domain, so that a fact that leaves an instance in no state of its domain fails the story; the secret's predicate is verified over the primitives port the oracle is given (`bonds_ports(cell, primitives)`, BND-9). The crossed QR is proved in the three orders of its events (both open first; mine opened first and theirs taken first; the reverse). Rejected: a cell per domain on the phone (MBX-7's reason); both states flat in one (the two domains' faces would collide); the letter's halves written by the test (the harness carries what the queued letter and the phone's reads give). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]

### The protocol: `post`

The post speaks the protocol between two nodes (F2..F5, F7, GR-5, GR-6): the letter and its envelope, opened and verified, and the port of requests' record. Written in part 10.1 (Claude, 20 Nov 2026), on step 12's design as the gate left it, and proved in part 10.2 (PST-10..PST-13); the stories in [`post/README.md`](../packages/domains/src/post/README.md).

- **PST-1** **The letter and its envelope** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F2, F3, F7, PER-13): the top `post` of q-'s tree holds the protocol's values and functions (`post/value.ts`, `post/dsl.ts`), above its business `requests`. A **letter** is an ADT (`is`): a **request**, `{ schema, identity, from, to, declared_at, invokes, input }`, or a **response**, `{ schema, identity, from, to, declared_at, answers, event }`: `from` the sender's face key, `to` the deposit address D it is for, `declared_at` its declared time (a claim, PER-13), `invokes` what the request invokes (EXT-5), `input` and `event` JSON values, claims (F2) that the leaf's schema or the asker's delivery parses. Its **signed content** is `q-letter/` then the letter's canonical JSON (LIB-11), so the signature covers D, and no letter's signed content is a ticket's (`q-ticket/`, BND-9). The **signed letter**, `{ letter, signature }`, the signature optional (removing it is always possible, PER-2), is sealed for the face it goes to; the **envelope** is `{ format, sealed }`, told to the relay as the base64url of its canonical JSON in UTF-8, opaque, within MBX-1's limit. The letter's schema and the envelope's format are `1`; another is another value, a version of the protocol. Rejected: the envelope as bytes (a format byte, then the sealed box), a second encoding beside the JSON every other value of the post has; D outside the signature (a letter re-addressed by the face it was for would hold at another mailbox, PST-4).
- **PST-2** **Identities** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F7, DOM-11, PER-4): a request's identity is the BLAKE2b-256 of the canonical JSON of `['the_identity_of_a_request', whose]`, `whose` being F7's `{ of: 'an_extension', extension, effect, id }` or `{ of: 'the_kernel', effect, id }` (`dsl.the_identity_of_a_request`): 32 bytes, the primitives port's BLAKE2b (SEA-5), where step 10 of the plan said 16 bytes before F7 hashed it. A response's identity is the BLAKE2b-256 of `['the_identity_of_the_response_to', request]`: one response, sent again for each copy of its request, is one letter, the duplicate branch where it is taken (PST-7), never a "second response" in the trace; and it never meets its request's identity. The id of an `anonymous` letter is the BLAKE2b-256 of its envelope's text (PER-4). Rejected: a random identity per response (each copy of a request sent again would bring another response, each traced).
- **PST-3** **The post's machine** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (GR-5, GR-8): the post computes no cryptography; its functions take the **machine** (`dsl.Machine`), what the safe does for it with a face of the node's user the configurator binds: `sign` (the safe's `a_content_signed_by_a_face`), `seal_for` and `signature_holds` (the safe's `sealed_for`, `the_signature_holds`), `open` (`a_sealed_content_opened_by_a_face`), `hash` (BLAKE2b-256). `a_letter_in_an_envelope(machine)(letter, their_key)` signs and seals, or gives `not_sealed` when their key takes no seal (SEA-5: a peer's key is a claim, F2; the open point of step 9 closed): the deposit's delivery reads it as `exhausted` with its reason, `their_key_takes_no_seal`, a dead letter shown (F6, DOM-15), and the request closes with no response at its time. Rejected: the post importing the safe's functions (a dsl sees no other domain's functions, DOM-25: the machine is a port, the configurator's). Amended in part 10.2: `sign` and `open` give promises, and so do the two functions over them (PST-10).
- **PST-4** **Opening an envelope** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F3, PER-2, PER-4; the inbound path is step 13's): `the_letter_in_an_envelope(machine)(came_to, text)`, with the machine of the face whose mailbox the envelope came to, gives an ADT: `not_an_envelope` (no envelope of a format spoken), `not_opened` (not sealed for that face, or broken), `not_a_letter`; else, by the signature, `opened` with the letter and its `Caller`: no signature, `anonymous` (`signature: 'none'`); one that does not hold for the face the letter claims, `anonymous` with "invalid signature, claimed face K" (`does_not_hold`, `claimed_face`), PER-2; one that holds, a face (`a_face`, its key) if the letter is for the mailbox it came to, else `re_addressed`, which goes no further. Every outcome but `opened` goes to the kernel's trace (F6, EXT-6). D is checked under a signature that holds only: in an `anonymous` letter it is a claim like the rest. Rejected: `re_addressed` before the signature (an unsigned letter's D tells nothing, and removing the signature gives `anonymous` anyway).
- **PST-5** **The requests** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F7, EXT-2, DOM-13): `post/requests`, a business of the kernel, is the port of requests' record. `send_a_request` is the port's call (step 12.4 wires it): the identity the kernel composed, from my face (the safe's `face_id`, which signs, and its key), to their face (its key and their mailbox: D and relays, from the bond, `bonds`), what it invokes, its input, and the seconds it waits (the asker's, 1 to 7 days, the relay's keeping, MBX-2). Its creator writes the letter, its declared time the call's `now`, and the instant its time is over. Its operation is the identity, its scope the port's: called again after a fall, it is the recorded fact (DOM-11), the time the first call's. `a_request_has_been_sent` queues `deposit_a_letter`, the wait for the next sending, and `wait_for_the_response` at the end of its time. The map: `post` sees the safe's identities (`sight.ts`). Rejected: a request keyed by the bond's identity (the post imports no `bonds`, GR-6: a bond is, for it, the faces and the mailbox the port gives).
- **PST-6** **Sending again** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F7, ADP-4; the open point of phase A "how long `post` keeps a request waiting, its sendings"): a request is deposited at its declared time and again at each quarter of its time while it waits within it (ping pong's 60 seconds: 0, 15, 30, 45), the same letter every time (the deposit's payload carries it, `value.deposit`), so that the peer meets one operation (DOM-11: the fingerprint covers the declared fields) and answers again from its recorded fact; a response lost on its way, which the server sends once with no effect (F5), comes so. The wait's return, `send_a_request_again`, sends it again, or records `the_time_to_send_again_came_too_late` once it closed (answered, with no response, or called off, PST-14) or its time is over. `deposit_a_letter`, keyed by the identity and the attempt, has no return and its order is `any` (SC-56): copies of one letter. Its world is the **mailbox port** of the post (GR-6): deposit an envelope on D at the mailbox's relays, deposited, or refused by the relay (MBX-1's limits: `exhausted`, shown), or unreachable (`transient`); over the relay's HTTP (step 20) or the `mailbox` domain (the oracle, 10.2). The executor wakes for the waits by its clock (`zz.Elapsed`, DOM-14): nothing new in the library. Rejected: the executor's transient retry alone (a deposit that reached the relay is delivered; a response lost on the server's side is no failure of the asker's deposit); a pause that doubles (fewer copies, but a response lost late in the time would not be asked again); the schedule in the port's runtime (it must outlive a fall: the waits are durable).
- **PST-7** **A request closes** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F7, V18, EXT-6): `take_a_response`, invoked by the kernel's entry (step 13) with an opened response signed by a face, under the response's identity (PST-2), answers the request it names with its event (`a_request_has_been_answered`) if it waits and went to that face. Else refused, which the kernel's trace keeps, never a fact of the feature (F2, F6): late or to no request of the node's (`the_request_is_waiting`), from another face (`the_request_went_to_this_face`); a second response that differs meets the duplicate branch as `identity_already_used`. `close_a_request_with_no_response`, the return of the wait at the end of its time, records `a_request_had_no_response`, or `the_response_came_before_the_time` (answered), or `the_call_off_came_before_the_time` (called off, PST-14). An `anonymous` response is never taken: the entry annotates it (step 13).
- **PST-8** **The answer kept** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F7, EXT-2): `the_answer_to_a_request` gives the answer under an identity: `waiting`, `answered` with the event, `no_response`, `called_off` with why (PST-14); kept as long as the request, forever in the prototype (DOM-11). The port of requests (12.4) answers a call from it: a request that waits, it follows its facts to its close (DOM-3); closed, the answer kept, never sending again. The port's other answers are the kernel's, before `send_a_request`: `the_bond_is_gone`, read from `bonds` (a removed bond stays removed, BND-6, so the same answer every time, and the record keeps none), and `refused`, which sends nothing and keeps nothing (EXT-2). A bond removed while its request waits does not stop it: its letter is gone already.
- **PST-9** **A response, on the server's side** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F5, F7, GR-6; step 13 wires it): no fact and no effect. The kernel writes the response, its identity the one of PST-2, from the face of the bond, to their mailbox of the bond the `Caller`'s key belongs to (`bonds`), its declared time the kernel's `now`, the event of the leaf that served it; puts it in an envelope for their key (PST-3) and deposits it once through the mailbox port; a deposit lost is asked again by the asker's sending again (PST-6). A request whose `Caller` has no bond gets none (F7).
- **PST-10** **The post's machine and its mailbox port, at runtime** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 10.2; PST-3, PST-6, GR-6, ADP-1, ADP-4, DOM-14, DOM-15): the machine's `sign` and `open` give **promises**: they are the safe's reads (SEA-6), which answer when a read does, and the configurator binds them over the safe's read ports, a face the safe has not made a fault of the wiring; `seal_for`, `signature_holds` and `hash` stay the safe's pure functions over the primitives port. So `a_letter_in_an_envelope` and `the_letter_in_an_envelope` give promises. The post's **mailbox port** (`dsl.MailboxPort`, GR-6) deposits `{ operation, deposit_address, relays, envelope }` and answers, in the post's words (`dsl.Deposited`, an ADT): `kept`; `kept_already`, the relay's `identity_already_used`, an envelope of that operation kept from an earlier attempt (a sealed box is new every time); `refused`, with what the relay said (MBX-1's limits, or a relay of other limits: its software is its own, F2); `unreachable`, no answer. It is a transport: it translates and keeps nothing (ADP-4); a fault of the transport throws. The **delivery** of `deposit_a_letter` is the requests' dsl's `the_deposit_of_a_letter(machine_of, mailbox)`, which a configurator only wires (ADP-1): the letter put in an envelope by the machine of the deposit's face (`face_id`), for their key; not sealed, `exhausted` `their_key_takes_no_seal`; then deposited under the operation of PST-11: `kept` or `kept_already`, `delivered`; `refused`, `exhausted` `the_relay_refused_the_envelope: <what it said>`; `unreachable`, `transient`, the entry tried again after the executor's pause; a throw, the executor stops the effect unadvanced and, started again, delivers the entry again (DOM-14). A dead letter is the post's executor's, shown (F6), never a fact (DOM-15): the sendings again go on, and the request closes with no response at its time. Rejected: the machine synchronous over a private key the configurator holds (a secret out of the safe, SEA-6), which part 10.1's types asked for; the signature read by the delivery and given to the dsl as data (the delivery and the kernel's entry would each read the safe, then call the dsl, for what the machine names once); the port's answer in three cases, the relay's `identity_already_used` read as kept by each transport (the post's meaning of a relay's answer written twice, in the oracle's transport and in step 20's).
- **PST-11** **The operation of a deposit** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (MBX-1, MBX-6, DOM-11, DOM-13; part 10.1's open point "the deposit's operation at the relay"): `dsl.the_operation_of_a_deposit(machine)(what, D)`, the BLAKE2b-256 of the canonical JSON of `['the_operation_of_a_deposit', what, D]`, where `what` is a sending's key for a request (the identity and the attempt, `<identity>/<n>`, DOM-13) and the response's identity for a response (PST-9; step 13 wires it). A sending tried again (transient, after a fall, an entry given twice) is one operation, so the relay keeps its first envelope (MBX-1's duplicate branch: the same bytes, the recorded fact; another sealed box, `identity_already_used`, `kept_already`), while each sending again is a deposit of its own, so that the peer meets the letter again (PST-6). D is in it so that two nodes' letters of one identity (two nodes' effects keyed by one value, F7) on two mailboxes never meet at the relay, whose depositor's scope is the persona alone (MBX-6). The relay reads no identity in it: two sendings of one request, and the response to it, are three operations it cannot link. A response sent again for each copy of its request is one deposit at the relay, kept once; one lost before the relay is kept the next time. Rejected: an operation drawn at random per call (D62: a retry after an answer lost leaves two envelopes, harmless to the peer's duplicate branch, but against MBX-1's "a retry is the duplicate branch"); the request's identity alone (every sending one deposit at the relay: a response lost would never be asked again, PST-6); the identity itself (the relay would link the sendings of a request and its response, which F7 keeps from it) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026].
- **PST-12** **What a letter carries** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (MBX-1, SEA-1, DOM-21): the content of a request (`input`) and of a response (`event`) is at most 32 KiB of its canonical JSON in UTF-8 (`value.content`). A letter's other fields are under 1 KiB, so a letter signed, sealed (48 bytes more) and told twice in base64url, its envelope's JSON between, stays under the relay's 64 KiB (87 382 characters; about 60 000 at the most), and its signed content under the safe's limit (SEA-1). A request with more is `invalid_request` at the port's call, before any letter (DOM-21), which the ports of requests never meet: they check the input by this value before they call, sending nothing, the extensions' refusing the call, `too_large_for_a_letter` (EXT-2, step 12) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026], the kernel's answering `too_large_for_a_letter` (F7, "The kernel's own requests", step 13) [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]; an opened letter with more is `not_a_letter` (PST-4). **A response's event over it** [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]: the kernel's entry checks the leaf's event by this value before it seals (`answered_on_the_bond`, EXT-6); over it, it deposits nothing and traces `a_response_not_deposited`, `too_large_for_a_letter`: the asker reads `no_response` at its time, and the server shows why in its trace (F6). Written first, a response between 32 and about 60 KiB was sealed and deposited whatever its size: the relay kept it, the peer opened it as `not_a_letter`, then read `no_response`, and the server knew nothing. Kept as an alternative definition (form 2): the kernel deposits, in place of the event, a response of its own, "too large", a new case of the protocol, for when a real extension has the case. Rejected: no limit and the relay refusing (a dead letter at every sending of a request the port took; past 64 KiB the safe's read refuses to sign, a fault of the wiring that stops the effect); a limit on the envelope, measured by the delivery (the port would take a request it cannot send).
- **PST-13** **The post's stories, on the oracle** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (TST-1, TST-3, TST-4, MBX-7; part 10.2): the stories of `post` (its README) are one suite, `stories_of_the_post(name, primitives)` in `packages/memory/test/post/`, run by `post.test.ts` over the fake primitives port and over libsodium's (SEA-9). Stories 1–6 run on the dsl with a face known by its private key (`a_face`); stories 7–17 on phones of q-'s oracle, one cell each with the safe and the requests (`oracle/post.ts`: `PostState` under the domain's name, `post_ports(cell)(versions)`, the safe's registry given as `identities`, since a request's fact holds the face that signs it, a resource, DOM-10), the post's machine bound to the phone's face over the safe's reads, the requests' executor whose deposits go through a mailbox port over the relay's oracle, a transport on which a story injects a loss (before the relay, or of its answer), a duplication, an abort or a relay's refusal (TST-4). The port of requests and the kernel's entry are played as 12.4 and 13 will wire them: `send_a_request` under the request's identity in the port's scope, a response opened at the asker's mailbox taken under its own identity in the entry's scope; the peer's leaf answers from what it recorded under the request's identity (DOM-11), its response deposited once under PST-11's operation. Rejected: the machine over the safe and the mailbox port over the relay in `memory`'s `src` now (the stack wires them with the phones, step 17; here they are the harness's, as the safe's deliveries, SEA-8); the dsl's stories on the oracle (they need no state).
- **PST-14** **A request called off** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (F7, EXT-7, EXT-15; Alessandro's decision, 21 Nov 2026: who withdraws does not keep asking): `call_off_a_request` (`{ request_id, why }`, `why` in the port's words, `value.why_called_off`: `not_active`, `not_offered_to_the_bond`), invoked by the kernel under the operation of the request's identity in a scope of its own (step 12.4 wires it), records `a_request_has_been_called_off` for a request that waits; else it is refused (`the_request_is_waiting`), nothing to call off. The request is closed: its sendings again record `the_time_to_send_again_came_too_late` (PST-6), its time `the_call_off_came_before_the_time` (PST-7), its answer kept is `called_off` with why (PST-8), which the port gives its delivery as `refused` with that why (EXT-2); a response after it is a late one, refused (PST-7). The read `the_requests_waiting` gives the kernel each request that waits: what it invokes (an extension's, by its name), the face that signs it and the key it went to, by which the kernel knows its bond (the post knows no bond, PST-5). Rejected: one command for the requests of an extension on a bond (a fact over a set, which the post would select by the kernel's words); the branch of `close_a_request_with_no_response` for a closed request kept as `the_request_is_closed` (a request called off would record `the_response_came_before_the_time`, a false history, F6): it is `the_request_has_been_answered` now. Story 17.

### The kernel and its extensions

The paper has no extensions (G3): these rules are q-'s, from Alessandro's decisions of 19 Nov 2026 (the glossary, § Domain terms), designed in step 12: the boundary and the protocol (part 12.1, EXT-1..EXT-8, with F7 and F8), how an extension is built and loaded (part 12.2, EXT-9..EXT-16).

- **EXT-1** An **extension** is a feature that is not the kernel's: domains of its own (its business, the leaves it adds to the kernel's personas, its reactions and effects), in a tree of its own, built and loaded apart (EXT-9, EXT-13). Two nodes speak through an extension when one has the side that asks and the other the side that serves: the same extension, at versions whose windows meet (EXT-5). The kernel knows an extension by its manifest (EXT-3), never by its code; an extension knows the kernel only through the boundary (EXT-2). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - An extension adds no persona: its leaves wear the kernel's hats and hang below the kernel's node at the top of each persona, which verifies the hat and gives the context (PER-6, PER-7), so that they inherit the constraints of where they sit, as a mounted leaf does (D78.6). In stage 1 an extension adds leaves to `self` (the local user) and `known` only; `unknown` (the introduction) and `anonymous` (nothing) stay the kernel's. A persona of an extension's own, or leaves on `unknown`, come with their case.
- **EXT-2** The **boundary** between the kernel and an extension is made of messages, so that how an extension is loaded (with the app, in process, isolated: EXT-13) changes without changing what crosses it (C, 19 Nov 2026). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - **In**, an **invocation** of one of its leaves: the leaf (its persona and its path in the extension's surface, EXT-5), the version its client speaks, the context the kernel's node at the top of the persona gives, as far as the manifest lets the extension see it (EXT-3), the operation (for a command, its id, which for a peer's request is the request's identity, F7, and its scope, DOM-11), and the input. Nothing of the letter it came in: envelope, signature and key are the kernel's (F3).
    - **The scope given to an extension** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: the kernel composes it from the hat and, on `known`, the bond's identity (`known/<bond>`), never from the `Caller`; on `self`, `Caller` + hat as for the kernel's own leaves (the local user, no face's key). On `known` the `Caller` is the peer's face key (§ 5), which the extension sees only if its manifest declares it (EXT-3, § 4's invariants); "opaque" is the library's word (DOM-11), not the extension's, which may read what it is given. The bond's identity is what the context already gives, so the scope tells nothing new, and another peer still cannot reuse an identity (DOM-11). Rejected: `Caller` + hat, as for the kernel's leaves (every command of a peer would hand the extension the peer's key with no `sees`, a flaw undeclared); a hash of the key (a second identity of the peer beside the bond's).
  - **Out**, the events of that leaf's flow, in order, to its end (DOM-3): its own, its nodes' closings, the base events, typed by its surface.
  - **Calls**, from the extension to the kernel's ports its manifest declares, each answered by the kernel. In stage 1: the **port of requests** (F7), whose call carries the bond, what it invokes on the peer (EXT-5: the extension's own leaf, its version), the input, the effect's path and the entry's id, from which the kernel composes the request's identity (F7), and the time it waits; it is idempotent by the identity, so that a delivery after a restart meets its request again, or the answer the port keeps (F7). Its answer is a closed ADT [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: `responded`, with the peer's event; `no_response`, the time over; `the_bond_is_gone`; `refused`, with why, which sends nothing: `not_declared` (a call the manifest does not declare, EXT-4), `not_active` (EXT-7), `not_offered_to_the_bond` (the offer withdrawn from that bond: an offer goes both ways, EXT-7), these two also the answer, kept, of a request in flight the kernel called off at a deactivation or a withdrawal (F7) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026], `too_large_for_a_letter` (an input over what a letter carries, 32 KiB of its canonical JSON, `post`'s limit, step 10: a request the kernel cannot send, which the port checks before it sends) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]. Rejected: the response's event or `no_response` alone (the bond gone, whence F7's exhaustion, and a refusal would have no answer); a refusal thrown (an extension's fault taken for a technical abort, which stops its effect, DOM-14), an input too large included (the call unanswered, the executor's position held behind it, DOM-14). And the extension's storage, a partition the kernel gives (EXT-14).
  - **Its life** (EXT-15) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: out, `ready`, with its name, its version and its boundary version, and its fall; in, the kernel's start and stop of its consumers.
  - **Its frames' messages** (EXT-16) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: a frame is a part of its extension (EXT-13) and speaks through the kernel: in, its place's data (`{ bond }` on a bond) and the events of what it invoked or follows; out, its invocations of its extension's leaves on `self` and the reads it follows there, each answered as an invocation is. The kernel fences them (EXT-4).
  - **Its notices** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (F6, DOM-15): out, the extension's dead letters (its executor's and its reactions', with their causes, DOM-14, DOM-16) and its trees' trace entries (PER-14), which live in its partition, never read by the kernel (EXT-14); claims of the extension (F2), which the kernel shows in its mark (EXT-16), so that its dead letters are shown as the kernel's are. In, the user's request on one of its letters (`redeliver`, `discard`), handed to its executor or its consumers, the operator's lane (DOM-14; on a phone the operator is the user, DOM-15). Rejected: the kernel reading the partition for them (it carries, never reads, EXT-14); the extension's page showing them (a dead letter is shown whatever the extension draws, F6).
  - No other part of the kernel is reachable.
  - Every message is data a JSON value can carry (a frame passes it as it is, EXT-13); an invocation and its events, a call and its answer, are paired by an id of the kernel. The boundary carries, never translates (ADP-4); a call may last (a request waits for its response), so several are in flight together.
  - **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `boundary` (`messages.ts`): one ADT per direction, discriminated by `message`, every field a JSON value (`json`, zod's JSON value, which refuses `undefined`, a function, a date, `NaN`): `a_message_to_an_extension` (`invoke`: `id`, `persona` `self` or `known`, `use_case` its path in the surface, `version`, `context` a JSON object, `operation` `{ id, scope }` for a command, `correlation` if any, `input`; `answer`: the `id` of a call, the `port`, the port's answer ADT; `start`; `stop`; `redeliver` and `discard` by the letter's name, a discard with `by` and `reason` as the library traces it, DOM-14), `a_message_from_an_extension` (`event`: an invocation's `id` and an event, a JSON object named by `event`; `done`; `call`: `id`, `port`, its request, an ADT by port; `ready`: `name`, `version`, `boundary_version`; `fallen`: `why`; the notices `dead_letter` (the letter's name, `of` `an_effect` or `a_reaction` with its `path`, the `entry`, the `cause`), `released` (a letter let go: delivered at last after a redelivery, or discarded) and `traced` (an entry of its trees' trace)), `a_message_to_a_frame` (`placed`: the place and its data, an ADT by place; `event`; `done`) and `a_message_from_a_frame` (`invoke` and `follow`: `id`, `use_case` on `self`, `version`, `input`; no persona, no extension's name: the kernel adds it, EXT-4). The id that pairs an exchange is named by the side that opens it (the kernel an invocation, the extension a call, a frame its invocation or follow), unique among its exchanges in flight: the kernel pairs, and its fence checks, by it. A call's answer names its port, so that a message parses whole at the transport. `released` is added to the notices so that a letter redelivered and delivered leaves the kernel's mark (the kernel learns of a discard from its own request, of a delivery only from the extension); a follow ends with the frame, which the kernel stops serving once it is no longer shown (EXT-4). Rejected: a generic answer (`answer: <a JSON value>`) parsed by the side that called (a message the transport cannot check whole); a refusal message apart from the ports' answers (two forms of one refusal beside the port of requests'); the notices without `released` (a letter shown after its delivery, a false history, F6). Tests: `packages/boundary/test/messages.test.ts`.
  - **The extension's side, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `boundary` (`the_extensions_side.ts`): `the_extensions_side({ name, version, declaration, send, configurator })`, generic, zod only, the one place where an extension meets the messages. It gives its configurator the ports `touches` names (`PortsOf`), each a call sent under an id it names (`call-<n>`), whose promise settles with the `answer` that names it, parsed by the port's answer (one that does not parse rejects it: the kernel's fault), and its notices (`Notices`: `dead_letter`, `released`, `traced`). `receive` is given at once: answers settle while the configurator builds over its storage, and every other message waits for it; built, it sends `ready` (name, version, `boundary.version`), or `fallen` if the configurator throws. `invoke` gives the leaf's events as `event` messages, then `done`; an event that is no JSON object named by `event` is traced, not sent; an invocation that throws (a commit refused, a context its point does not parse) sends `fallen`. `start`, `stop`, `redeliver`, `discard` go to what the configurator built (`AnExtension`); a message that does not parse, or an answer to no call, is traced. What it sends is copied through JSON (a field `undefined` left out) and checked by the boundary's schema. Rejected: the pairing of calls written by each extension (EXT-2 is one protocol); the configurator awaited before `receive` exists (its first read of the partition would wait for an answer nothing could receive); a fall for a message that does not parse (the kernel's fault, which the extension cannot answer: traced).
  - **The kernel's side, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `adapters-common` (`extensions/hosting.ts`, GR-10: a kernel's adapter that hosts extensions imports `boundary`): `a_hosted_extension(wiring)`, from the extension composed (EXT-4), a `Transport` to its realm (EXT-13), `is_active` (EXT-7), its port of requests (F7) and its partition (EXT-14), `the_bond_on_known` (what the kernel's node on `known` gives of the bond, in the boundary's words: its identity, the peer's key, the bond's name), the kernel's trace (EXT-6), the port of its notices, the clock, and the while it waits at its unloading (EXT-15). It pairs by the id it names what it opens (`invocation-<n>`) and answers each call under the extension's id, if the realm that called is still loaded; a message that does not parse, or that pairs no exchange in flight, is traced (`a_message_not_in_the_boundary`); a realm unloaded is heard no more. `invokes(persona)` is the port `zz.mounted_leaf_ports` takes: a `zz.MountedInvocation` sent as `invoke` with the persona, the leaf's path, the version, the context the manifest lets it see (on `known` `{ bond }`, with `peer_key` and `bond_name` only if `sees` names them; on `self` `{}`), the operation with the scope the kernel composes (on `known` `known/<bond>`, in place of the entry's; on `self` the entry's, `Caller` + hat), the correlation, the input; the events come back as the library takes them (EXT-15). `outside_the_surface(persona)` traces what the library sets aside. Its storage is served only if `touches` names it (else `refused`, `not_declared`, traced) and while it is active (else `not_active`); its notices go to their port with its name (`ANotice`); `redeliver` and `discard` are handed to it while it runs (`handed`, else `not_running`). Rejected: the node's context forwarded and filtered by the boundary's names (the kernel's node, step 13's, names its context its own way: the translation is given at wiring, once); the scope composed by the extension (a peer's key in every command, the form rejected above). Tests: `packages/adapters-common/test/extensions/hosting.test.ts` (a made-up extension on the boundary's side of the messages), `packages/memory/test/extensions/greetings.test.ts` (greetings in process, over its partition).
- **EXT-3** The **manifest** says what an extension is to the kernel (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026), as data: its author's declaration and its tree's surface (EXT-10). It is one declaration, which the kernel reads at composition and at activation and from which the extension's scopes are derived (EXT-11), so that what the extension can name at compile time and what the kernel lets through at runtime cannot differ (the risk of step 12 in the plan). It declares: [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - its **name**, a business name in snake_case, unique among the extensions of a node (the composition refuses two with one name), and its **version**;
  - **the leaves it adds to which personas**: per persona, the paths of its leaves and the window of majors it serves (EXT-5), which are its tree's: the build derives them (EXT-10);
  - **what it sees of the kernel**: in the context of its leaves, the bond's identity for a leaf on `known` (opaque, the kernel's), the peer's face key or a bond's name only if it declares them; the kernel's read use cases it reads (none in stage 1);
  - **what it touches**: the kernel's ports it calls (in stage 1 the port of requests, toward its own leaves, and its storage);
  - **what it sees of the other extensions, and the use cases it offers them** (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026): declared on both sides, routed by the kernel only while both are active; none in stage 1, the mechanism with its case;
  - **its extension points** (pages, slots, actions: EXT-16).
  - **What its code may call** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (D104.3: what is required is derived from the code, never kept by hand): the context its leaves read is typed from `sees` (EXT-11), the calls from `touches`. The kernel gives the extension's configurator only the ports `touches` names, typed from the manifest (`boundary`'s ports by name, as a door gives only what its map opens: a port not declared cannot be named, `Property … does not exist`), and its deliveries and adapters reach them only through it; so what the code calls is derived by the compiler and cannot exceed the declaration, and the kernel still checks what it lets through at runtime (EXT-4), for an extension built otherwise (the code is free). Rejected: the calls checked at runtime only (an undeclared call compiles and is found when it runs: `touches` kept by hand); the manifest as the runtime form of the map (`sight.ts` stays the tree's own, EXT-11, and nothing of it enters the manifest, as the text said until the gate).
    - **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): `boundary.PortsOf<typeof manifest>` is `Pick<boundary.Ports, touches[number]>`, each port a function whose promise gives its call's answer, a closed ADT (`the_port_of_requests(request)`, `its_storage.read(read)`, `its_storage.commit(writes)`); a port the declaration does not touch is no property of it (`Property 'its_storage' does not exist`, a type test in `packages/boundary/test/ports.test.ts`). Its parameters are the boundary's output types (the bond's brand): the extension's adapter translates its business's bond into the boundary's (GR-10).
- **EXT-4** The kernel **enforces** the manifest (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - At composition (CFG-2, D104): an extension asks only for ports the kernel offers extensions, at versions the kernel speaks; its surface is the one its manifest declares; every leaf it adds to `known` answers in one event (F7), and its input holds no refinement its surface cannot tell, so that the kernel checks it whole before its node (EXT-6) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]. A mismatch is a composition fault (CFG-2).
  - At runtime: the kernel invokes only the leaves the manifest declares (a request naming another path of an offered extension is `invalid_request`, `not_a_leaf`, PER-5); serves only the calls it declares, and only while the extension is active (EXT-7); gives only the context it declares. An undeclared call is refused and goes to the kernel's trace (EXT-6), shown to the user (F6): the extension's fault, never a business fact.
  - **A frame's fence** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (EXT-2, EXT-16): the kernel serves a frame only its own extension's leaves on `self` and the reads it follows there, adding the extension's name itself, as for the port of requests (EXT-12: a frame cannot name another extension's leaf, by construction), and only while the extension is active and the frame is shown (placed in a screen the user has open); anything else a frame asks is refused and goes to the kernel's trace.
  - Isolated (the default, EXT-13), an extension has no way to the node but the boundary, so these checks fence the node's data; the network is closed only as far as the browser closes it (EXT-13), a limit declared to the user, never a promise [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]. In process it shares the kernel's memory and could pass them by: there the manifest informs and does not fence, a feature declared to the user, who accepts it informed (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026; the principle of the flaws).
  - **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `adapters-common` (`extensions/composition.ts`, `extensions/a_frames_fence.ts`). `compose_an_extension(offer)(manifest_as_data)`, `offer` what the kernel offers (`TheKernelsOffer`: the boundary majors it speaks, the ports, the names installed; `the_kernels_offer(installed)`, this kernel's), gives an ADT: `composed` (the manifest parsed by `boundary.the_manifest`, and per persona a `zz.MountedSurface`, every input and event read by zod's `fromJSONSchema`, EXT-6), or `a_fault`, every fault in one message, a line each, `<name>: <sentence> (<rules>)`: the manifest does not parse; a boundary major the kernel does not speak (EXT-9); a name installed already (EXT-3); a port not offered; a leaf on `known` that does not answer in one event (F7); a leaf on `known` whose input holds a mark (`x-refined`, `x-unrepresentable`, `x-lossy`, anywhere, named by JSON Pointer, `the_marks_in`; `an_input_on_known_the_kernel_cannot_check`); a surface with no leaves at its version's major (PER-17); a JSON Schema zod cannot read; an action whose `invokes` is no leaf of `self` at its version (EXT-10's check at composition); and, at a load, a `ready` that differs (EXT-15), one ADT (`ACompositionFault`). A frame's fence is `through_a_frames_fence({ frame: { extension, shown }, active, message, now })`, pure, for the frames of steps 16 and 22: a message that is no frame's (`boundary.a_message_from_a_frame`: one that names an extension or a persona does not parse), a frame of an extension not active, a frame not shown, refused with its entry of the kernel's trace (`a_frame_fenced`); else served, an invocation or a follow with `{ of: 'an_extension', extension: <the frame's>, persona: 'self', use_case, version }` and its input, its leaf checked by the mounted port on `self` (`not_a_leaf`). Rejected: the first fault only (each fixed fault would show the next at the next start, against CFG-2's one message); an action's leaf checked by the build only (an extension built otherwise: the code is free); the fence checking the leaf against the surface (the mounted port on `self` checks it, as for the user's invocation). Tests: `packages/adapters-common/test/extensions/composition.test.ts`, `route_and_fence.test.ts`.
- **EXT-5** A request names what it **invokes**, an ADT by whose use case it is: the kernel's, `{ of: 'the_kernel', persona, use_case, version }`, or an extension's, `{ of: 'an_extension', extension, persona, use_case, version }`, where `use_case` is the leaf's path in its surface from the persona's segment (DOM-24) and `version` the version of that surface the asker speaks. The persona is the hat, a claim the kernel's node verifies (PER-7). The surface an extension adds to a persona is a domain of its tree with a version of its own (PER-17, DOM-22, V14), not the kernel's persona's: the gate (`version_not_supported`) and the window are per surface, and the kernel's surfaces and each extension's change apart (V17). An extension invokes on a peer its own leaves only; a request toward another extension's leaves comes with its case. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - **The kernel's node above an extension's leaves** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (D105 corollary: a leaf between units released apart is under D75's rules; D75.6, PER-17): an extension's leaves inherit the context and the closings of the kernel's node at the top of their persona (EXT-1; on `known` the bond and `not_found`), which a response carries to the peer (F7). They are part of `boundary`'s contract, fixed within its major: a release of the kernel that adds a limit or a closing to that node, or changes what it gives, is a new major of `boundary`; outside the kernel's window of `boundary` majors an extension is a composition fault (EXT-9), inside it the kernel serves each extension that node as the major it was built with gives it (expand-contract, readers first). So the events a peer reads of an extension's leaf are fixed by the surface's version the request names and the boundary major the extension was built with; rebuilt with a major that changes them, its surface changes too, and the comparison with `main` asks a major of it (PER-17). Rejected: the request carrying the version of the kernel's persona too (a gate per pair of versions, and an asker would have to know each peer's kernel).
    - **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `boundary` (`the_kernels_node.ts`): `not_found` (no reason), and the base events `invalid_request` (`reason`: `not_a_leaf`, `input_not_in_schema`, the reasons of the library's mounted ports), `version_not_supported` (`current`, `oldest`), `identity_already_used`, each a strict schema as the library's base events are shaped (D106); `a_base_event` their ADT, `the_kernels_events_on_known` with `not_found`, by which and the leaf's own events an extension's delivery parses a peer's response (EXT-12). The context on `known` is `the_bond` and the schemas `sees.known` names (`mounted_of`, EXT-11); on `self`, the root's alone.
- **EXT-6** The **entry of a request** is the kernel's (PER-5 extended). After `post` gives the `Caller` (PER-5, 1), the kernel routes the request by what it invokes: one to the kernel goes to the kernel's persona; one to an extension goes on only if the extension is installed, active and offered to the bond of the `Caller`'s key (EXT-7), which auth tells (a lookup, `the_extensions_offered_to_the_bond_of_a_key`: data, compared by the kernel, PER-9), and otherwise the node stays silent (F8) and the request goes to the kernel's trace. This routing reads auth before the gate, so that no answer tells an extension that is there but not offered; it is the one check before the gate that reads auth (the hat stays the node's, V13). Then, as for any request, the gate (`version_not_supported`, the window of the surface invoked), the kernel's node at the top of the persona (the hat, the bond, its perimeters, PER-7), and the leaf: an extension's through the boundary (EXT-2), with the context the node gives. The events come back as the response (F7). A response is no request: it goes to the request it names (F7), never to a persona. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - **The leaf and its input, before the kernel's node** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (PER-5, PER-6, D106.1): after the gate, the kernel checks the leaf and its input against the extension's surface as the manifest carries it (EXT-10: per persona its leaves at each major, their inputs and events), so that `invalid_request` (`not_a_leaf`, `input_not_in_schema`) comes before the kernel's node, as for every leaf (PER-6: Marta without the keeper's hat reads `invalid_request`, never whether the keeper's tree knows her). The kernel's adapter reads the surface's inputs into schemas at composition (zod's `fromJSONSchema`, over the JSON Schema the extension's registries export, TST-7); the extension's tree ports parse again with its own schemas, as a port below does. A refinement JSON Schema cannot tell, and a form zod's reader reads back as another shape (the export's marks, `x-refined`, `x-unrepresentable`, `x-lossy`: "As measured" below), the kernel cannot check: an input of a leaf on `known` that holds one is a composition fault (EXT-4), so that a peer's request is checked whole before the node; on `self` the extension checks it after the node, the user of the node being hidden nothing (F6). How the kernel's tree ports serve the leaves mounted below its node: PER-6, "A mounted folder". Rejected: the input checked by the extension after the kernel's node (the order PER-6 rejects: a peer outside the node's perimeters would learn from `not_found` before `invalid_request` that its request was well formed); a first message to the extension that checks it before the node (a round trip per request, and an extension slow or fallen would hold the kernel's entry).
  - **The kernel's trace** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (F6, F8, D54): the kernel's entry keeps a trace of its own beside the trees' (PER-14), since the routing stands before every tree, with no leaf nor event read. It is a port the configurator gives the entry (served by the adapter of the trees' trace, in one store, eventual and without guarantees, D54: the fact counts, never the trace), required at wiring as a tree's; its entries are the kernel's type, never the library's (`zz.Traced` knows no extension, § 3). An entry tells the request as it came (what it invokes, the letter's identity, the `Caller`, `now`, the version) and why, a closed set: `the_extension_is_not_installed`, `the_extension_is_not_active`, `the_extension_is_not_offered_to_the_bond` (F8's three cases, the one place where they differ); `a_call_not_declared` (EXT-4); `an_event_not_in_the_surface` (EXT-15); `the_extension_has_fallen` (EXT-15); `a_response_not_awaited`, the responses F7 annotates (a second, a late, a malformed one, one to no request of the node's or from another face). It never leaves the node (PER-18); its reader is the user (F6), on the screens of step 15, what concerns an extension in its mark (EXT-16). Rejected: the trace of the kernel's persona tree, with a reason of the routing in `zz.TraceReason` (a word of q- in the library, § 3, for a check before any tree); the silence untraced (nothing happens in silence, F6).
  - **As measured** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), the gate's open point: zod's `fromJSONSchema` (zod 4.6.7, "semi-experimental" by its own word) as the kernel's check of a peer's input. Each shape of a table, its JSON Schema as the library exports it (`json_schema_of`), read back through a composed manifest and compared with the original on values the original accepts and refuses (`packages/adapters-common/test/extensions/composition.test.ts`, each verdict a test); greetings' surface read whole (`packages/memory/test/extensions/greetings.test.ts`).

    | shapes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | read back                                                                                |
    | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
    | strict and loose objects, optional fields, defaults; strings with min, max, a regex, an email, a uuid, `zz.date_time`; numbers with bounds, exclusive bounds, an integer, a multiple; literals, enums, discriminated unions, unions; arrays with bounds, tuples; nullable; records with string keys; branded strings; nested and recursive objects | holds: accepts and refuses as the original                                               |
    | a URL with a protocol (`format: uri`, the protocol lost); a record with enum keys (its keys not required); `.trim()` and the other overwrites; a pipe (its input's side only)                                                                                                                                                                      | lossy, accepts more: marked `x-lossy`; refused on `known` at composition (EXT-4)         |
    | a pattern with a Unicode class (`z.emoji()`, `\p{…}`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | lossy, refuses more (read without the `u` flag): marked `x-lossy`; refused on `known`    |
    | a refinement (`x-refined`), a date and the forms JSON Schema has no word for (`x-unrepresentable`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | marked: read as their base shape, or anything; refused on `known` at composition (EXT-4) |
    | `if`/`then`, `not` (but `{}`), `dependentSchemas`, `unevaluatedProperties`, an unknown `type`, a `$ref` not found                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | not read: a composition fault                                                            |

    It holds for the shapes q-'s and greetings' leaves use. A lossy shape that accepts more would let a malformed input reach the extension, which parses it with its own schemas: `invalid_request` would then come after the kernel's node, the order rejected above; one that refuses more would refuse a valid request. **The lossy forms are marked and refused on `known` at composition, like refinements and dates** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]: `json_schema_of` marks each `x-lossy`, with what the reading loses (`url`: a URL's protocol, hostname or normalization; `record_keys`: the keys a record with finite keys requires; `overwrite`: `.trim()`, `.toLowerCase()` and the others; `pipe`: a pipe, a transform included, its input's side only; `unicode_pattern`: a pattern with the `u` or `v` flag), the composition refuses an input on `known` that holds one (`the_marks_in`), and the generator's inclusion decides a marked shape by equality only (DOM-24). So the kernel accepts more forms whole and refuses more forms at composition; on `self` they stay, the extension checking them after the node (F6). Each verdict a test, and each shape that does not read back whole a mark (`composition.test.ts`: "marked exactly when its reading does not hold", "the lossy forms, marked and refused on known at composition"; `packages/domains/test/library/json_schema.test.ts`). Rejected: zod's reader kept with the limit declared (a peer's malformed input past the node, the order PER-6 rejects); a JSON Schema validator of its own (a dependency, or a reader to write, where zod reads what its own export writes).

  - **The kernel's trace, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `adapters-common` (`extensions/the_kernels_trace.ts`): `TheKernelsTraced`, an ADT by `why`, JSON throughout, and its port `TheKernelsTrace = (entry) => void`, required at wiring by the port of requests and the hosting: F8's three silences, with the request as it came (`ARequestAsItCame`: `invokes`, the letter's identity, the `Caller`, `now`, the version); `a_call_not_declared` (the extension, `now`, the call: its port and its request); `an_event_not_in_the_surface` (the persona, the leaf, the version, the event, and whether it was `not_typed` or `after_the_answer`, a second on `known`); `the_extension_has_fallen` (`the_first` or `the_second`, and why); `a_response_not_awaited` (the response's identity, the request it answers, the `Caller`, `now`, and what `post` answered: step 13's). The closed set gains three, which EXT-2, EXT-4 and F7 trace and it did not name: `a_call_not_answered` (a call the kernel could not serve: a fault of its storage, a request `post` does not send), `a_frame_fenced` (EXT-4's "anything else a frame asks", with the fence: `the_extension_is_not_active`, `the_frame_is_not_shown`, `not_a_frames_message`), `a_message_not_in_the_boundary` (a message of the extension's that does not parse, or pairs no exchange). The routing is `route({ invokes, installed, active, offered_to_the_bond_of_the_caller })`, pure, for the entry of step 13: `to_the_kernel`, `to_an_extension` with its name, or `to_silence` with F8's case, in the order not installed, not active, not offered. Rejected: a malformed message or an unanswered call under an existing reason (two causes under one word, which the user reads apart); a frame's refusals under F8's silences (a frame's message is no peer's request); the routing as an entry with its effects (step 13's: here the decision only, as data). Tests: `route_and_fence.test.ts`, `hosting.test.ts`. Since part 13.2 three more, which the entry traces (PER-25): `an_envelope_not_opened`, `not_a_leaf_of_the_extension`, `a_response_not_deposited`. Since part 13.3 one more, the entry's too: `a_request_not_answered` (PER-25, 4). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - **On the oracle** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4; the step's checks, `plan.md` step 12): the checks of F7, F8, EXT-2, EXT-6, EXT-7, EXT-12 and EXT-15 run on q-'s oracle with two nodes, greetings in place of ping pong (step 14), in `packages/memory/test/extensions/on_the_oracle.test.ts` over its `harness.ts` (each check with its test, those the earlier blocks prove and those deferred to their step, in that folder's README). Anna's, Bruno's and Carla's phones of the post's stories (PST-13), each with the office of the extensions beside its address book, its bonds the other phones' faces and mailboxes, greetings hosted in process over its partition (EXT-13, EXT-14); the asker's port of requests wired with the phone's post, its address book (`a_bond`, `my_faces`: the bond kept or gone) and the kernel's reads of its office (EXT-7). The kernel's entry is **played as step 13 will write it**: the letters collected and opened (PST-4); a request routed by `route`, over the office's reads and the bond of the `Caller`'s key, silent with its case in the kernel's trace, or through `zz.mounted_leaf_ports` on `known` below a **stand-in of the kernel's node** at the top of `known`, written in the test with the library's builders (`persona/known/node/known`: it takes the `Caller`, looks up the bond of its key, `auth/bonds/lookup/the_bond_of_a_key` over the address book's list, `not_found` when none, PER-14, and gives the bond, its identity and its name), the first event of the leaf the response, deposited once (PST-9); an invocation that throws (a fall, an extension not running) sends nothing back (EXT-15, F8), the hosting having traced the fall; a response taken under its identity (PST-7), else `a_response_not_awaited` with what `post` answered. **Step 13 replaces** the stand-in with q-'s tree of `known` and auth's lookups (`the_extensions_offered_to_the_bond_of_a_key` in place of the address book read by key and the office's `the_extensions_offered_to_a_bond`), and the played entry with its inbound path, whose tests these checks become. Rejected: the leaves on `known` invoked through the hosting alone, as the tests of the hosting do (the gate, the leaf, the input and the node would not be proven in this rule's order); the stand-in in `packages/domains/src/persona` (q-'s trees of the personas are step 13's design); one cell per phone (the phones of both harnesses rewritten for what the stack wires, step 17: here two cells side by side, the post's and the office's). `zod` is a dev dependency of `memory`, for the stand-in's schemas. **Since part 13.3** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] the stand-in and the played entry are gone: these checks run over the kernel's entry (PER-25), q-'s four trees and auth's adapters, on **the node of q-'s oracle** (`packages/memory/test/node/harness.ts`): one cell per phone with the states of `seals`, `post`, `bonds` and `extensions` and every executor and reaction they need, so that the face that signs a letter is the address book's own (a ticket's secret is its signature, BND-9); bonds made by tickets and introductions, one mailbox per bond; a collector over every mailbox the address book names (BND-11), a deposit whose entry throws left unseen (DOM-17); the relay's desk hashing R as the safe draws D. The 12.4 checks moved with it where the real entry differs from the played one: a node writes at the mailbox of its bond with the other, the `Caller` names it (PER-23), and a response lost on its way is traced `a_response_not_deposited` (PER-25, 5). It reverses, for these tests, the choice above of two cells side by side: two safes would give two faces, an introduction's secret signed by one and its letter by the other. Rejected: the node in `src` (the stack's, step 17, which moves it there).
- **EXT-7** **Installed, active, offered** are data of the kernel, in its business domain `extensions`. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - Installed: in stage 1, what the configurator composes (ping pong, preinstalled); installing from storage, and a fact of it, come after stage 1 (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026).
  - Active: the user **activates** an extension, the conscious act (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026), accepting its manifest as shown, at its version (`an_extension_has_been_activated`, which records what was accepted), and **deactivates** it (`an_extension_has_been_deactivated`). A preinstalled extension is off until activated. One whose installed manifest asks more than the one the user accepted (an update) is not active until the user accepts the new one: nothing happens in silence (F6). An extension not active runs nothing: no leaf invoked, no call served, its consumers stopped (EXT-15), its requests in flight called off (F7) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]. The notice of the activation shows, beside what the manifest declares, the flaws the kernel cannot fence: the network as far as the browser closes it (EXT-13), and a frame's invocations with no tap (EXT-16). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
  - Offered: an active extension is offered to every bond of the node, present and to come, except those the user **withdraws the offer** from (`the_offer_of_an_extension_has_been_withdrawn_from_a_bond`; offered again, `an_extension_has_been_offered_to_a_bond`: a change, so no `again`, which names the branch where nothing changes, DOM-6). The default, every bond: [Q, A, 20 Nov 2026].
  - **An offer goes both ways** (Alessandro, 20 Nov 2026): an extension offered to a bond is the one the node uses with it, asking and serving. Withdrawn from a bond, the node neither serves it there (F8) nor asks with it: the kernel's port of requests refuses a request of that extension on that bond (a case of its answer, EXT-2) and calls off those in flight, which `post` sends again no more (F7) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026], and `user` and `ui` draw none of its extension points on that bond's screen (EXT-8 answers without it, EXT-16). So a withdrawal reads from outside as the extension not active, never as a node that asks and does not answer (F8). Rejected: the offer one way, for the peer only (who withdraws and keeps asking tells the peer of the withdrawal, against F8; and EXT-8, whose input is what the asker offers, would hide the action EXT-16 kept). Rejected: offered to no bond until the user offers it, bond by bond (a second gesture after the conscious one, activation); both, as an ADT with the default suggested by the manifest (with its case, an extension to offer bond by bond).
  - Activation and offers are the user's: commands of `self`, never of a peer. `extensions` knows a bond by its identity only (GR-3); auth reads the offers through its ports (PER-9, GR-4); the manifests installed are the composition's, which the state adapter of `extensions` reads for its predicates (`the_extension_is_installed_with_this_manifest`).
  - Scenario: Anna's phone has ping pong preinstalled and off; a ping of Bruno's meets silence. Anna activates it, accepting what its manifest shows (its leaf `ping` on known faces, requests on her bonds, a page and an action on a bond); Bruno's next ping gets its pong. Anna withdraws the offer from her bond with Carla: Carla's pings meet silence, Bruno's still get their pong, and Anna's screen of her bond with Carla offers no ping; her ping to Carla still waiting is called off, her phone sends it again no more. An update of the app whose ping pong also asks for the bonds' names leaves it inactive until Anna accepts.
  - **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): the office is `extensions/activations` (`attivazioni`), where the user's choices are kept: what she did last of each extension (activated, with the manifest accepted and the instant; deactivated), the offers she withdrew (an extension from a bond), the questions of EXT-8. Its manifest is its own view (GR-3, GR-10), `value.manifest`: the name, the version, what it sees on `known`, what it touches, its extension points (each by kind, name and place), its leaves on `known` by path at any major, the majors of its surfaces per persona; every list a set. Not in it: the frames' files, the leaves' schemas and an action's leaf, which the kernel enforces (EXT-4) and the user is not asked about. The kernel's adapter translates the composed manifest into it (`the_manifest_to_accept`, `adapters-common/src/extensions/activations.ts`), parsed by the office's schema at composition. The manifests installed are given to the state adapter as data (the composition's), which reads them for `the_extension_is_installed` and `the_extension_is_installed_with_this_manifest`; the office records no fact of an installation (stage 1). Commands of `self`: `activate_an_extension` (input the manifest shown; condition: installed with it, field by field with the lists as sets, `dsl.the_same_manifest`, and not activated with it → `an_extension_has_been_activated`; activated with it already → `…_again`), so a manifest shown before an update, or not the node's, is refused; `deactivate_an_extension` (activated → `an_extension_has_been_deactivated`; else `…_again`, a never activated one too: nothing to do, DOM-6); `withdraw_the_offer_from_a_bond` and `offer_an_extension_to_a_bond` (condition: the extension installed and `the_bond_exists`, a bond kept in the address book, neither removed nor a ticket, BND-6; offered → withdrawn, withdrawn already → `…_again`; withdrawn → `an_extension_has_been_offered_to_a_bond`, offered already → `…_again`). A withdrawal stays across a deactivation: a reactivation does not offer again in silence what the user withdrew (F6). The reads: `an_extension` (by name: the manifest installed and `state`, an ADT, `active`, `not_active`, `waiting_for_the_new_manifest` with what it asks more, `dsl.the_state_of_an_extension`; no entry for an extension not installed), what the kernel's routing, its port of requests and its hosting read; `the_extensions_offered_to_a_bond` (by bond: the active ones whose offer is not withdrawn from it, each with the majors of its surface on `known`), what auth's lookup (PER-9, step 13) and the port's `is_offered` read; it does not check the bond, which the routing and the port read from `bonds` themselves. The oracle's module is `memory/src/oracle/extensions.ts` (`extensions_ports(cell, installed)`), over a phone's cell with the address book's state, so that `the_bond_exists` reads it in the same step (GR-3); the kernel reads the office through `the_kernels_reads({ clock, office })` (`is_active`, `is_offered`, `the_active_among`, `the_extensions_offered_to`: the lists `route` takes), over the office's read ports. Rejected: the name `register` (the generator's registry), `consents` or `offers` (half of what it keeps); the boundary's data form as the office's manifest (it would keep what the user is not shown, and `domains` would mirror a schema of `boundary`'s, GR-10); a hash of the manifest (no notice of what an update asks more); activation by the extension's name alone (the user would accept a manifest she was not shown); a deactivation of one not activated refused (a branch where nothing changes, DOM-6); the withdrawals cleared at a deactivation (above); the translation and the kernel's reads in `memory` (the phone needs the same, step 22). Tests: `packages/memory/test/extensions/activations/activations.test.ts` (the stories of the domain's README), `packages/adapters-common/test/extensions/activations.test.ts`.
- **EXT-8** The **extensions in common** (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026) are read through a read leaf of the kernel on `known`, `the_extensions_in_common`: its input, the extensions the asker offers to that bond (names and the majors it speaks); its snapshot, those of them the node offers back to the asker's bond, with the majors it serves. Each side tells only what it offers the other: an extension not offered stays silent there too (F8), and a list that claims more than the asker offers (a claim, F2) learns only what one request per extension would learn. The asker reads it as any request (F7): a command of `extensions` on `self` (`ask_a_bond_for_the_extensions_in_common`) queues it, and its return records what came back (`the_extensions_in_common_with_a_bond_have_been_learned`, or that no response came); `user` and `ui` show from it what can be used with a bond, and when to ask (at the introduction, on a bond's screen) is `user`'s (steps 15, 16). Scenario: Anna and Bruno have both activated ping pong; Anna's phone asks on their bond, Bruno's answers `ping_pong` at major 1, and Anna's screen of the bond offers the ping. Bruno withdraws the offer from Anna: the next answer is empty, and Anna's ping meets silence. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] **The delivery, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.3): the kernel's adapter of the office (`adapters-common/src/extensions/activations.ts`, `the_delivery_of_the_extensions_in_common(port)`) delivers `ask_for_the_extensions_in_common` over the kernel's own port of requests (F7, "The kernel's own requests"): a request of the kernel on `known`, `usecase/the_extensions_in_common`, at the version of `known`'s surface the node speaks (its `_version.ts`), its input what the node offers the bond (the office's `name` is the persona's `extension`), its time the question's. What came back, the office's closed set: the leaf's snapshot, each entry parsed by the leaf's own entry schema and the whole by the office's `offered_extensions`, `the_extensions_in_common_came_back`; any other event (a closing of the peer's node, a base event) or a snapshot that does not parse, `no_answer_came_back` with `the_peer_cannot_answer`; `no_response`, `no_answer_came_back` with `no_response`; a question too large for a letter (the kernel's port's `too_large_for_a_letter`, traced, PST-12), the same [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]; the bond gone, exhausted, a dead letter (DOM-15). Nothing the peer claims is filtered: a claim (F2), the software informs. Rejected: a strict schema for the whole event (a field more, the position or the total, would read as a peer that cannot answer); the claim cut to what the node offered (the office records what came back; a filter comes with its case). Tests: `packages/adapters-common/test/extensions/activations.test.ts` (each case), `packages/memory/test/persona/personas.test.ts` (story 5, end to end on the node of q-'s oracle).
  - **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), the business side, in `extensions/activations`: `ask_a_bond_for_the_extensions_in_common` (input: the bond and what the node offers it, names and the majors of `known` it speaks, as `the_extensions_offered_to_a_bond` reads it, since a creator reads no state; condition: the question new, named by the operation, `identity_of`; `the_bond_exists`; and `these_extensions_are_offered_to_the_bond`, each named offered to the bond with those majors, so the node tells only what it offers) → `a_bond_has_been_asked_for_the_extensions_in_common` and the effect `ask_for_the_extensions_in_common` (`order: 'any'`, keyed by the question; payload the bond, what it offers, 60 seconds). Its return `learn_the_extensions_in_common` takes the office's closed set (`the_extensions_in_common_came_back` with them; `no_answer_came_back`, `no_response` or `the_peer_cannot_answer`, D108) and records `the_extensions_in_common_with_a_bond_have_been_learned` or `a_question_about_the_extensions_in_common_had_no_answer`; a question that waits no more is refused, a dead letter. The server's read is `the_extensions_in_common` (input: the bond, which the kernel's node gives the leaf, never the asker's word, and what the asker says it offers, a claim, F2): those of the named the node offers to the bond, each with the majors its surface on `known` serves, the whole window, as a request's `version_not_supported` would tell it (PER-17). The kernel's leaf on `known` over it and the delivery of the effect over the kernel's port of requests (`{ of: 'the_kernel', … }`, F7) are step 13's. Rejected: the delivery reading what the node offers at its time (a payload that changes between attempts of one request); the majors in common, intersected (the asker learns no more by it, and could not tell an update to make). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] the two facts of the return, chosen by a predicate over the return's input alone (`the_extensions_in_common_came_back`, its meaning the dsl's), by DOM-6's criterion (one fact per meaning, or one fact with a closed case): the office speaks of two happenings, the extensions in common learned and a question that had no answer, so a fact for each; why no answer came (`no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer`) is a detail of the one happening, so a closed case of its fact (`why`), not a fact each. Rejected: one fact with the closed set kept as it is (as `greetings`' `a_greeting_has_been_closed`): one happening's name for two the office tells apart; a fact per reason of no answer (two names for one happening). Tests: as EXT-7's.
- **EXT-9** An extension is a **package** of its own, `packages/extensions/<name>/` (`@q-mino/<name>`), named as its manifest (`ping_pong`); the workspace lists `packages/extensions/*`. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - Its root is its tree, as Shed's folder is (DOM-25): the folders its map names, `business/` (its business domains), `persona/` (`self/` and `known/` only, EXT-1), `reaction/` when it has reactions. Beside them, outside the tree: `adapter/` (its state adapter over its partition, EXT-14, and its deliveries, EXT-12), `frame/` (the documents of its pages and slots, EXT-16), `texts/` (its Italian texts, its own), `manifest.ts` (EXT-10), `sight.ts`, `configurator.ts` (its composition root, CFG-1: its trees, its executor and its consumers wired to the boundary, not started, as Shed's), a `README.md` with its stories, its tests.
  - **Its copy of `zen0`**: it builds with a library of its own, the one it depends on (in this repository the workspace's, `@q-mino/domains/library`; a version of `zen0` of its own once the library is a package, LIB-13), bundled into its build. Nothing of the library crosses the boundary (no object, no brand, no symbol: the messages are JSON values, EXT-2), so the kernel and an extension built with two versions of the library meet only on the boundary's version. The paper is silent on units built with two versions of its library (D102).
  - **The boundary version**: `boundary` has its semver, and the kernel speaks a window of its majors, as a persona its surface's (PER-17); the manifest carries the version of `boundary` the extension was built with, taken from the package, never written by hand (EXT-10); outside the window it is a composition fault (EXT-4). Within a major the kernel reads what an older minor sends and sends what it reads: expand-contract, readers first.
  - Built apart: its build makes a bundle (its configurator and what it imports, its frames, its texts, its manifest as data); in stage 1 the PWA's build makes it too, for the extensions preinstalled.
  - **Its configurator, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), the test extension's `configurator.ts`: `configurator(realm)(given)`, from its realm the clock and the time that passes (`Realm`), from the boundary the ports `PortsOf<typeof manifest>` and the notices; it wires its partition, `versions` over its business's majors, the business's command ports, the tree ports of `self` and `known` with their mounted points as tops, the executor of `ask_for_a_greeting_back`, not started (CFG-2), and gives `AnExtension`: an invocation by its persona and path, the context parsed by the point's schema, `now` from the realm, the operation and the correlation (the operation's id when the kernel gives none, D72.7), the input given to the tree port as it came, which parses it (a cast with its reason); a path it does not serve, `invalid_request` (`not_a_leaf`); `start` and `stop` run its executor; a dead letter found by its name (`<effect>/<entry id>`) among the entries held, handed to the running executor. `greetings({ clock, elapsed, send })` composes it with the boundary's side: what the kernel's side imports (`@q-mino/greetings`), beside `@q-mino/greetings/manifest.json`. Fixed in the library [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: a mounted point whose context is empty (`self`'s) gives nothing beside the root's (`ContextOf`, `EntryOf`), since zod types an empty strict object's output as `Record<string, never>`, which no entry could satisfy.
