  - Discarded: the extension as a tree inside `packages/domains` (built and released with the kernel, its boundary crossed by imports the cruiser would forbid one by one); one package for every extension (released together, against EXT-1); the extension importing the kernel's domains for their types (the identities and contexts the kernel gives are `boundary`'s, translated at the kernel's adapters, GR-10).
- **EXT-10** The manifest the kernel reads (EXT-3) has two parts. Its **declaration** is authored, `manifest.ts`: the name, what it sees, what it touches, its extension points: what the user accepts, written once, by the author. Its version is its package's (`package.json`), which the build writes into the manifest as it writes the boundary version (EXT-9): never by hand, so that the package and the manifest cannot tell two versions. Its **surface** is its tree's: per persona its leaves (their paths, inputs and events, as its registries give them, DOM-24) and its majors (`_majors.ts`), and for each leaf on `known` whether it answers in one event (F7). The extension's build derives the surface and writes the manifest as data (`manifest.json`), the one form the kernel reads, for an extension built with the app as for one loaded from storage after stage 1. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026; the form: Q, A, 20 Nov 2026]
  - The form, a function of `boundary` whose keys read as the manifest's sentences (EXT-3): names from the closed sets of `boundary` in lists, as a fact's `resources` (SC-55) and the map's kinds; the extension points by name, as components:

    ```ts
    export const manifest = boundary.manifest({
      name: 'ping_pong',
      touches: ['the_port_of_requests', 'its_storage'],
      actions: { ping: { on: 'a_bond', invokes: 'usecase/send_a_ping' } },
      slots: { the_pings_of_a_bond: { on: 'a_bond', shows: 'frame/the_pings_of_a_bond.html' } },
    })
    ```

    Every leaf on `known` sees the bond's identity; `sees` lists what else, per persona (`sees: { known: ['the_peer_key'] }`), left out when nothing. `touches` names the kernel's ports for extensions (in stage 1 `the_port_of_requests`, `its_storage`); a leaf is named by its path in its surface on `self` (`usecase/send_a_ping`), typed by the paths of `self`'s registry that the generator writes (DOM-24), so that a path that is no leaf of `self` does not compile, as a field named by key is typed (SC-34's `operation.identity`), and checked again at composition (EXT-4); a frame (`shows`) is a file of its bundle, checked by the build. Rejected (Alessandro, 20 Nov 2026): the manifest written as JSON, with no function (no types for the author: a name misspelt found at composition only); records of booleans (`touches: { the_port_of_requests: true }`: a second form for a list of names); the version written in `manifest.ts` (a second declaration of the package's); a leaf's path checked at composition only.

  - The surface is derived, not declared, so that no list of paths or majors stands beside the folders (PER-17: the window is the folders); the declaration is authored, so that a use added in the code never widens by itself what the user accepted (EXT-3). This makes precise EXT-3's "one declaration": rejected, the surface declared by hand and checked against the tree.
  - What **asks more** (EXT-7), comparing two manifests: a name added to `sees` or `touches`, an extension point added, a leaf added on `known` (a new way for a bond to reach the node). Not more: a leaf on `self`, a new major of a surface (the same leaves, compatible inside the window), a text. **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): `dsl.what_it_asks_more(accepted, installed)` of `extensions/activations`, a pure function over the office's manifests (GR-10: never the boundary's), gives the list of what is asked more, an ADT by `asks` (`to_see_on_known`, `to_touch`, `an_extension_point`, `a_leaf_on_known`), in the order the notice shows it, empty when nothing; an extension point by kind, name and place, so one moved to another place is a new way to appear; a leaf on `known` by path at any major; a new version, a new major and a frame's file ask no more. Rejected: a boolean only (the notice of an update would not say what it asks, F6); an extension point by name only (a point moved from the extension's screen to a bond's would pass unseen).
  - **The declaration, as written** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): `boundary.manifest(...)` keeps the declaration's literal types (a `const` type parameter) and gives it back as data, after parsing it with `the_declaration` when its module loads (a name in snake_case, the names of `sees` and `touches` from the boundary's closed sets, each once, a leaf's path, a file's path; strict: an unknown key refused), so that a misspelt name fails at `pnpm generate`, which loads it. `invokes` is typed by `self`'s registry with `satisfies`: `export const manifest = boundary.manifest({ … }) satisfies boundary.Manifest<typeof registry>`, `registry` imported **as a type** from `persona/self/_registry.ts` (the generator's configuration imports the manifest at `pnpm generate`, before the registry is written again, so a value would be read stale). `Manifest<registry>` is the declaration whose `invokes` are `LeavesOnSelf<registry>`: the keys `persona/self/<path>` with a `usecase/` segment whose component has an input and is no schema (the input's schema the generator registers beside a leaf, `…_input`, is no leaf), from the persona's segment (`usecase/greet_a_bond`). `boundary.manifest` takes any string where `satisfies` is left out, and no compiler asks for it, so **the lint asks for it** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: in every extension's `manifest.ts`, the export `manifest` is `… satisfies boundary.Manifest<typeof registry>` (`eslint.config.js`, `no-restricted-syntax` with a selector over the declarator, `the_rule_of_a_manifest`), its case proved on the repository's configuration as it resolves for the test extension's `manifest.ts` (`greetings/test/manifest.test.ts`: the export with no `satisfies`, or another type, reported). Rejected: `boundary.manifest<typeof registry>()({ … })` (a curried call for a partial type argument: a second call where `satisfies` checks the same); the registry given as a value (`self: registry`: a runtime import that `pnpm generate` would read stale, and a value the manifest does not carry); a leaf as any key with a `usecase/` segment (a leaf's input schema would pass).
  - **The data form, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): `the_manifest` (`manifest.json`), strict: `name`, `version`, `boundary_version`, `sees: { known }`, `touches`, `actions`, `slots`, `pages`, every list and record present, maybe empty; `surfaces: { self?, known? }`, a surface `{ version, majors: { "<major>": { "<leaf>": { kind: 'command' | 'read', input, events: { <name>: <the whole event's JSON Schema> }, answers_in_one_event } } } }`, its majors holding its version's (refused otherwise, as the library's mounted ports refuse it at wiring), the JSON Schemas JSON values. `answers_in_one_event` on every leaf, `self`'s too (one shape of a leaf; EXT-4 reads it on `known`). `boundary.the_manifest_as_data({ declaration, version, surfaces })` makes it, the one place, with the boundary version the extension is built with (`boundary.version`), never given; the surfaces are derived by the extension's build (next part). Rejected: the boundary version a parameter (a second place where it could be written); the lists and records left out when empty in the data form (the kernel would read two forms of nothing). Tests: `packages/boundary/test/manifest.test.ts`.
  - **The surface, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): derived by two functions, written by the extension's build. `zz.the_surface_of({ majors, at })`, a function of the library of no kind (LIB-1, LIB-3; `library/surface.ts`), reads the majors the generator lists (`_majors.ts`): the version (one current registry, its `version.ts` under its major, as the gate reads it), and at each major the leaves below the folder by their path from it, each `command` or `read`, its input's JSON Schema (`json_schema_of`, the generator's export, TST-7, its marks kept; an input that is a function of the context marked `x-unrepresentable`), its events whole, as its tree port gives them (`{ event, payload }`; a read's `snapshot`; `the_wait_is_over` for a leaf that follows; the closings of the nodes below the folder and of the limits, `not_found` and `refused` with every limit's name), a past major's leaf with its own input and events and its current leaf's flow and limits, and `answers_in_one_event`. A read leaf that streams or lists actions throws: its surface comes with its case (a frame's follow, step 16). `boundary.below_the_kernels_node(persona)(surface)` adds to each leaf the events the kernel gives above it, as the boundary types them (`the_kernels_events_on_known` on `known`, `a_base_event` on `self`; no `identity_already_used` on a read), so that the surface types every event a peer's response may hold (EXT-12) and the kernel parses by it what the extension gives (EXT-15). The extension's `the_manifest.ts` composes them with `the_manifest_as_data`, with no Node; `scripts/manifest.ts` (`pnpm --filter @q-mino/greetings manifest`) writes `manifest.json`, formatted by Prettier, and a test fails when the committed file differs from the derivation, as `generate:check` does. Tests: `packages/domains/test/library/surface.test.ts` (on Shed: Giorgio's leaves against the export, Marta's borrowing at two majors), `packages/extensions/greetings/test/manifest.test.ts`. Rejected: the surface read from the generator's `_registry.json` (no kind of leaf, no `follows`, the events' payloads alone: a snapshot for the comparison with `main`, DOM-24, not a contract); the generator writing `manifest.json` (it would know the boundary, § 2); the kernel's events added by the library (it knows no kernel, § 3) or by each extension (a contract of the boundary's, EXT-5, written once).
- **EXT-11** An extension's tree is a tree of the generator (DOM-25), listed in `generator.config.ts` beside the kernel's: its root (`packages/extensions/ping_pong`), its map, its library, `compared_with_main: true` (its surfaces are given to peers, PER-17), and the folders **mounted** below the kernel's nodes: `mounted: { 'persona/self': …, 'persona/known': … }`, each with the context the kernel's node gives there, as the manifest lets the extension see it (EXT-3), which the configuration takes from the manifest. In stage 1, on `known` the bond's identity (`{ bond }`, opaque, the kernel's), with the peer's key or the bond's name if declared; on `self`, nothing (one face; the active face with stage 2, PER-3). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026; the key `mounted`: Q, A, 20 Nov 2026]

  - **What `mounted` writes** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: the configuration translates the manifest's `sees` into `mounted` by name: for each mounted folder, the names of `boundary`'s context schemas it composes (the bond's identity, and the peer's key or the bond's name if declared), never the manifest's type; the generator writes into the point's `_node.ts` the import of those schemas from `boundary` and the context they compose, as it writes a node's context at a point of the kernel's tree. The manifest's type depends on `self`'s registry (`invokes`, EXT-10), which depends on `self`'s leaves, whose point is that context: typed from the manifest, `persona/self` would be a cycle of types (manifest → registry → leaves → point → manifest). So `boundary` enters the tree in those generated files only (GR-10). Rejected: the context typed from the manifest (the cycle); `boundary`'s schemas imported by the authored files of the tree (an authored file imports only its scope, DOM-25). **The key's form, and what the generator writes** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (part 12.3): `mounted: { 'persona/known': { from: '@q-mino/boundary', context: ['the_bond'] }, 'persona/self': { from: '@q-mino/boundary', context: [] } }`, each folder with the module its schemas come from and their names (the word `boundary` only in the configuration: the generator writes the module it is told). The generator writes the point's `_node.ts`: `export const known = { mounted: 'persona/known', context: z.strictObject({ ...the_bond.shape }) } satisfies zz.Mounted` and `known_context = zz.ContextOf<typeof known>`, the point and the scopes below reading it as a node's (`builders_at<known_context>`); data, `satisfies` as for the generated data (A, 19 Nov 2026), no builder. It refuses, at `pnpm generate`: a mounted folder under no top its map names; a node in a mounted folder (its `_node.ts` is the point's; a node that adds goes in a folder below) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]; a node that takes below one (D105.2). Rejected: the module fixed by the generator (a word of q- in it, § 2); one schema per folder named by the configuration (a schema per combination of what `sees` lists); a node in the mounted folder with the point's context in a second generated file (two files where GR-10 names one, with no case: ping pong's folders hold none).   - The library's tree ports take, at a mounted folder, the context the entry gives (the invocation's, EXT-2), parsed by the schema bound there, with no node to take it: a mechanism of no kind, a tree whose top points are given their context from outside. Asked of the library by step 12.3.
  - **`mounted_of`** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): `boundary.mounted_of(manifest)` writes the key `mounted` of an extension's tree in `generator.config.ts`: `{ 'persona/known': { from: '@q-mino/boundary', context: ['the_bond', ...sees.known] }, 'persona/self': { from: '@q-mino/boundary', context: [] } }`, the module's name its package's (`boundary.name`), each name one of `boundary`'s context schemas (`the_bond`, `the_peer_key`, `the_name_of_the_bond`); its type, `Mount`, is a plain type of `boundary`'s, structurally the generator's (`boundary` imports zod only, and the generator no boundary). The two `Mount`s stay, one for each unit of release (GR-10, D37), and their meeting point is checked [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: `generator.config.ts`, where `mounted_of`'s value meets the generator's `Configuration`, is under `tsc -b`, with the root's other configuration files (`vitest.config.ts`), by a tsconfig of their own (`tsconfig.config.json`) that the root's references, so that a `Mount` of `boundary`'s that stops matching the generator's fails the type-check. Rejected: the generator's `Mount` imported by `boundary` (an arrow GR-10 forbids); `mounted_of` in the generator's configuration (the translation of `sees`, a word of the boundary, kept where `sees` is defined).
  - Its map is the architecture's for its kinds: a business sees another business's identities (GR-3), a persona the business's use cases, identities and facts (GR-1), a reaction its facts and use cases (GR-9); no `auth/` (auth is the kernel's, EXT-1). Its registries, majors, doors and scopes are as for every tree, its cruiser rules from `map_rules`, named by the root's last folder (`ping_pong-sight-of-persona`).
  - Discarded (Alessandro, 20 Nov 2026): a top node authored in the extension that `takes` the kernel's context (a second declaration of what the manifest declares, checked against it, EXT-3); a persona base above the hats (the bond is `known`'s, not every hat's); the generator reading the manifest itself (it would know a word of q-, and it imports the library only, § 2: the configuration translates the manifest into a key of no kind).
- **EXT-12** A **request, in an extension's code**, is an ordinary effect of its business (`effect({ payload, order: 'any' })`, F7, SC-56), named by what it asks of the peer (`ask_for_a_pong`), its payload what its delivery and its return need (the bond; the time it waits, a value of its business: ping pong's 60 seconds); its return an ordinary return use case (`returns_from`, DOM-13), whose input is the business's own closed set of meanings, in its words (`the_pong_came_back`, `the_ping_had_no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer`, `the_ping_was_called_off`), not the peer's events (D108: the closed set of meaningful outcomes). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - Its world is the peer's leaf, the delivery's (DOM-13: the delivery links what is not of the domain): a delivery in the extension's `adapter/` calls the port of requests with the bond, the leaf by its path in its own surface and the version it speaks (its current major's), the leaf's input, the effect's path, the entry's id and the time; it parses the response, a claim (F2), by the leaf's events as its tree types them, and maps every case of the port's answer (EXT-2) exhaustively: the response's events, `no_response`, and `refused` for `not_active` and `not_offered_to_the_bond` onto the return's input, each a meaning its return records as a fact; to `exhausted` with the reason only the faults, `refused` for `not_declared` and `too_large_for_a_letter`, and `the_bond_is_gone` (F7, DOM-15); an event added to the leaf fails to compile in the delivery until the business says what it means.
  - **A withdrawal is a meaning, not a fault** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (DOM-15: a dead letter is a failure the operator handles; D108: the closed set of meaningful outcomes): a request refused as not active or not offered to the bond, a new one or one called off in flight (F7), answers the user's own gesture, a deactivation or a withdrawal, which the business records as it records a silence (ping pong's `a_ping_has_been_called_off`, step 14); as a dead letter it would invite a redelivery that meets the same refusal. The faults stay dead letters: a call the manifest does not declare (EXT-4), an input too large for a letter, the bond gone (decided at the gate). Rejected: every refusal exhausted (a conscious gesture shown as a failure, its redelivery refused again).
  - **A response that does not parse** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (D108.2: the effect's adapter classifies every result; D67.1: an event that cannot be processed is exhaustion): one whose event the leaf's events do not parse is `exhausted`, with its reason (the peer's event kept), a dead letter shown in the extension's mark (EXT-2, EXT-16), which holds nothing, its effect being `any` (SC-56); never an exception. A delivery that throws stops its effect (DOM-14), and the port, giving the same answer by identity (F7), would stop it again at every start: one tampered peer (the code is free) would stop `ask_for_a_pong` toward every bond. Rejected: the kernel comparing the response with the surface and annotating it, the request left waiting to its `no_response` (a peer that answered would be recorded as silent, a false history, F6). The kernel adds the extension's name to what the request invokes (EXT-5): a call cannot name another extension's leaf, by construction.
  - Nothing new in the library. Discarded: a key on `effect` naming the peer's leaf, the return's input derived from its events (`asks: known.usecase.ping`: the business would name a persona's leaf, a layer above it, DOM-25, and its return would speak the peer's events, not its own meanings); a builder `request` (a new kind for what is an effect, LIB-3).
  - **The test extension's business, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): `greetings` (`saluti`), `business/greetings`, the office that greets the bonds and keeps what came back. `greet_a_bond` (`{ bond }`, an action's place data, EXT-16) records `a_bond_has_been_greeted` with the entry `ask_for_a_greeting_back` (`order: 'any'`; its payload the greeting, the bond, its words and `waits`, 60 seconds, `dsl.a_greeting_waits`), whose return `close_a_greeting` takes `{ greeting_id, outcome }`, `outcome` the closed set `the_greeting_came_back { words }`, `the_greeting_had_no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer`, `the_greeting_was_called_off` ([Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: a refusal of the user's gesture, EXT-12), and records one fact, `a_greeting_has_been_closed`, which keeps the outcome as it is, as Shed's returned loan keeps its condition, by DOM-6's criterion [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: the office speaks of one happening, a greeting closed, and how it closed is a detail of it (the other form, a fact per meaning, is the office of the extensions', EXT-8, and ping pong's, step 14: both stay, as alternative definitions); a greeting that waits no more is refused, a dead letter. The served side, `greet_a_bond_back` (`{ bond, their_words }`), records `a_bond_has_been_greeted_back` with the office's words. The identities of a greeting and of a greeting back are drawn by the business from the operation (`identity_of`, D75.8): an action gives the bond alone, and on `known` the operation is the request's identity (F7), so that a request sent again is the same greeting back. Its leaves: `self`'s `greet_a_bond` (`{ bond }`, `the_greeting_is_sent`), `known`'s `greet` (`{ words }`, its creator from `context.bond`, no `follows`: `greeted_back { words }`), each naming a rejection no branch reaches (`the_greeting_cannot_be_sent`, `not_greeted_back`). The words greeted and greeted back (`ciao`, `ciao anche a te`) are constants of the business's `dsl`, texts the peer's user reads [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]. Rejected for `greetings`: a fact per meaning, each branch chosen by a predicate over the input (four names for the one happening its office tells; and a command resolves every branch and the library parses each payload when it plans, so a branch that cannot hold would need a payload of its own: a greeting back's words where none came); the identities in the leaves' inputs (an action gives the bond alone; a peer would name the server's records).
  - **The delivery, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), `adapter/delivery.ts` of the test extension: the port of requests called with the bond (the business's, parsed into `boundary.bond`), `invokes: { persona: 'known', use_case: 'usecase/greet', version }` at `known`'s current version (its `version.ts`), `{ words }`, the effect's path, the entry's id and `waits`. `responded`: the event parsed by `the_kernels_events_on_known` (the peer's node closed it, or a base event: `the_peer_cannot_answer`), else by each of `greet`'s events, whole, from the leaf's own schemas (`greet[$UseCase].events`), through a record whose type is mapped over them (`greeted_back` → `the_greeting_came_back { words }`, `not_greeted_back` → `the_peer_cannot_answer`), so that an event added to `greet` does not compile until the record says what it means; neither, `exhausted` with the peer's event as JSON in its reason. `no_response` → `the_greeting_had_no_response`; `refused` for `not_active` and `not_offered_to_the_bond` → `the_greeting_was_called_off` (EXT-12) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]; the faults, `the_bond_is_gone` and `refused` for `not_declared` and `too_large_for_a_letter` (with its `why`) → `exhausted`, a dead letter shown, which holds nothing behind it (`order: 'any'`). Rejected: one union of the events' schemas (an event added would fail to parse at runtime, not to compile); the kernel's events mapped one by one (each means the same to the business).
- **EXT-13** **Isolated, in process.** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026; the parts: deferred to their case, Q, A, 20 Nov 2026]
  - Isolated, the default (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026), a part of an extension runs in a realm of its own that reaches nothing of the node but the boundary: not the kernel's memory, nor its storage, nor the DOM of its screens, nor another extension. The isolate is a configurator's, through an adapter: `phone` gives each part a frame sandboxed with an opaque origin (no storage, no service worker, no cookie of the app's origin), whose document the kernel gives from the extension's bundle, with a policy that forbids connections and every other source; `memory` runs the parts in process (the oracle), the boundary still messages, each a JSON value copied, so that the oracle and the phone speak one protocol.
  - **The network** is closed as far as the browser closes it: the policy closes `fetch` and its kin, the sandbox opens no window, no form, no top navigation; a frame that navigates itself, and WebRTC, stay open in today's browsers (checked on 20 Nov 2026, to be proven on the phone at step 22: the app's own `frame-src` may close the first; no policy closes the second). **Measured** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 22.2, WEB-23): the app's `frame-src 'none'` stops every navigation of a frame, so the phone's policy closes the first (step 25); WebRTC stays open, told the user. So isolation fences the node's data, not what the extension knows: what crossed the boundary to it (its invocations' inputs and contexts, its storage) it may send outside. A limit declared to the user at activation (F6, the principle of the flaws), never a promise.
  - In process, a choice of trust the user accepts informed (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026): imported in the kernel's realm, its boundary still messages (the same transport, with no copy), and the manifest informs, not fences (EXT-4).
  - **In stage 1 every part runs isolated on the phone**, and the manifest has no key for how its parts run. What runs in process, and what the user accepts **whole or in part**, comes with the first extension that asks for it (Alessandro, 20 Nov 2026: deferred to its case, the method of the core and the emergences), from his words of 19 Nov 2026: "L'utente è informato sui rischi e può accettare con l'estensione o magari parte delle funzionalità di quell'estensione"; "diversi tipi di estensioni con diversi livelli di accesso, visibilità e integrazione". Two readings, to weigh then: the parts that run in process are its domains and each of its pages and slots, declared by the manifest and accepted all or some (proposed at part 12.2, `runs`); or the user accepts the extension or only some of its functionalities (each extension point, each leaf on `known`), a functionality refused not there (no action drawn, its leaf silent). Rejected for now: whole only (all in process or all isolated).
  - **In process on the oracle, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4): the kernel reaches a realm through a `Transport` (`adapters-common`, EXT-2's kernel's side): given how the realm speaks to the kernel, it loads the extension there, and gives how the kernel speaks to it and how it is unloaded. `memory`'s `in_process(extension)` (`oracle/in_process.ts`), `extension` its side of the messages as its package gives it (`(send) => greetings({ clock, elapsed, send })`), copies every message as JSON both ways, turns an exception of its `receive` into its `fallen`, and unloaded neither hears nor speaks. A frame's transport is step 22's: written in part 22.2, WEB-23 [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]. Rejected: the copy in the hosting (the in-process choice of trust, with no copy, would copy all the same).
  - Discarded: a worker as the isolate (of the app's origin, its storage and network are the app's); the domains and the frames in one realm (a page and two slots are three documents; they meet through the kernel, EXT-16); in process as the default for the extensions the app ships (Alessandro: the tendency to isolate them always).
- **EXT-14** An extension's **storage** is a **partition** of the node's that the kernel gives it (EXT-2): its own, under its name, which no other extension reaches and the kernel never reads, only carries (its language is the extension's, GR-3). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - The call: **read** keys, or a range of keys in their order; **commit** a batch of sets and deletes, atomic, all or none; values JSON. Over it the extension's adapters implement its state ports: its command port (the fact, the state and the outbox entries in one commit, DOM-8), its reads and streams, its fact log, with the library's help for adapters (LIB-14). One writer: the extension commits one operation at a time (its command port in order), and only on the node's writer (DOM-9).
  - Its technology is an adapter's: on the phone an IndexedDB store of the extension's (step 21, rules in § 6, WEB-12), on the oracle a map. A full partition refuses the commit: a technical outcome (DOM-15), not a fact. It is exported with the node's data (G1, step 26) and removed with the extension (after stage 1).
  - **The call, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), in `boundary` (`ports.ts`): keys are non-empty strings, in the order of their code units (as IndexedDB orders strings), values JSON; `its_storage.read(read)`, `read` an ADT, `{ read: 'keys', keys }` (the entries present, in the order asked) or `{ read: 'range', from?, before? }` (from a key, inclusive, before another, exclusive, in key order; no bound left out), answered `{ answer: 'read', entries: [{ key, value }] }`; `its_storage.commit(writes)`, a list of `{ write: 'set', key, value }` and `{ write: 'delete', key }`, all or none, in order, answered `{ answer: 'committed' }` or `{ answer: 'refused', why }`. A call the kernel does not serve is answered too, `refused`, `why` `not_declared` (EXT-4) or `not_active` (EXT-7), as the port of requests answers it (EXT-2: each call answered); a commit also `the_partition_is_full`, a technical outcome (DOM-15). Rejected: a refusal thrown (EXT-2 rejects it for the port of requests); a call not served left unanswered (the extension's promise would never settle); a key prefix as the range (a range says it, `before` the prefix's successor).
  - **The partition-backed adapter, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), `adapter/partition.ts` of the test extension, modelled on q-'s oracle (its cell and its interpreter, out of reach by GR-10): commands, their fact log, the outbox and the executor's state over `its_storage`, nothing the extension does not use (no reads, streams, steps or branches over a list: each with its case). A fragment and a write declare the keys they read (`{ reads, holds }`, `{ reads, writes }`), so that a command reads in one call every key its branches name, decides on them, and commits in one batch its parts' writes (each seeing those before it, DOM-10), its fact, its operation, its outbox entries, the versions and the counts (DOM-8), one command at a time (a chain of promises, DOM-9). The keys: `meta/facts`, `meta/entries` (counts); `fact/<position>` (twelve digits: the order of the keys is the facts'); `operation/<[scope, id]>` (the pair as JSON, so that no scope and id meet); `entry/<effect>/<position>` (read as a range, `before` the prefix's successor); `executor/position/<effect>`, `executor/held/<effect>/<position>`; `version/<[path, version]>`; `state/…`, the business's records (`state/greeting/<id>`, `state/greeting_back/<id>`, `adapter/greetings.ts`, parsed by its values when read). The outbox and the log read from a key on and wait for the next commit, counted, so that one landing during a read is not missed. A read or a commit refused is a technical outcome, thrown (DOM-15): the invocation falls, nothing written. The executor's state tells a dead letter as it is held and as it is let go (EXT-2). What it reads back of its library's types is its own JSON, cast by the kind of its key with the reason on the line. Rejected: the partition read whole at load (a cost that grows with the history, as the in-memory state discarded above); fragments as functions of a state object (the keys to read unknown until evaluated); the trace kept in the partition (its entries go to the kernel as `traced`, eventual, D54).
  - **The partition on the oracle, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), `memory`'s `partitions({ characters })` (`oracle/partitions.ts`): a map per extension under its name, `of(name)` the storage the kernel gives it (`boundary.Ports['its_storage']`), `kept(name)` for a story to read; keys in the order of their code units; a commit applied to a copy, kept only if the partition stays within its size, the characters of its keys and of its values' JSON (`the_partition_is_full` otherwise, none of it kept). Rejected: a size in entries (a partition of few large values, unbounded); a size in bytes of UTF-8 (the oracle's measure need only be one; the quota is the phone's, step 21). Tests: `packages/memory/test/extensions/greetings.test.ts`.
  - Discarded: the extension's own IndexedDB database (an opaque origin has none, and the app's origin reaches the kernel's stores); the kernel running the extension's state adapter (its code in the kernel's realm); its state kept in memory, rebuilt from a log at every load (a cost that grows with its history).
- **EXT-15** **The life of an extension**: it runs only while it is active (EXT-7), and only on the node's writer (DOM-9). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
  - The kernel **loads** it at its activation, or when a node starts where it is active. Loaded, it says it is **ready**, with its name, its version and its boundary version, which the kernel compares with the manifest installed (a difference is a composition fault: it does not run, and the user is told). The kernel **starts** its consumers (its executor, its reactions: wired and not started by its configurator, CFG-2) and invokes its leaves; at its deactivation it **stops** them, waits a time for the invocations in flight, and unloads it.
  - Its requests outlive a stop while it stays active (a fall, the phone closed): a delivery in flight meets its request again when the extension runs again, by its identity, or the answer the port keeps (EXT-2, F7); what waits meanwhile is `post`'s (step 10). Not active, its requests in flight are called off, `refused` `not_active` (F7, EXT-7) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026].
  - An extension that falls (an error in its realm, a frame gone) is traced (F6); the local invocations in flight get a technical outcome (DOM-15: `user` retries them, step 15), the peers' meet silence (F8: the asker reads `no_response`). The kernel loads it again once; a second fall leaves it stopped, shown to the user.
  - An event its surface does not type, or a second event from a leaf on `known`: the kernel answers with the first event that parses, and traces the rest (EXT-4), the extension's fault.
  - **As written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.4), `a_hosted_extension` (`adapters-common`, EXT-2's kernel's side): `load()` only while it is active (`not_active`, nothing loaded, otherwise), its realm opened and its calls served while it loads (its storage read before it is ready), its `ready` compared with the manifest composed (name, version, boundary version: a difference is `a_composition_fault`, `a_ready_that_differs`, its realm unloaded, its life `stopped`), then `start`; `unload()`, its deactivation: `stop`, its invocations in flight awaited at most `a_while()`, its realm unloaded, those still in flight failed, a load still waiting for its `ready` ended (`unloaded`). A fall (its `fallen`, or an exception the transport turns into it): traced, its invocations in flight failed, a technical outcome thrown to the library's mounted port (`user` retries on `self`; on `known` the kernel's entry of step 13 sends nothing back, F8), its realm unloaded and loaded again once; a second fall leaves it `stopped` (`it_fell_twice`), shown by `life()`; the count starts again at the next `load()` (an activation, or the node's start). On `known` the first event its surface types at the request's major is the answer, every other traced (`not_typed`, `after_the_answer`); on `self` its events go to the library's port, which parses them and sets aside what its surface does not type (`outside_the_surface`). Rejected: invocations held while it is loaded again (a local invocation would wait with no end on a recovery: a technical outcome, which `user` retries); the falls counted for ever (a deactivation and an activation are the user's way to try again).
- **EXT-16** The **extension points** (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026), declared by the manifest, each in a **place** the kernel's screens offer: a closed set of `boundary`, versioned with it; in stage 1 `a_bond` (the screen of a bond) and `the_extension` (its own screen, from the list of the extensions). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026; the form: Q, A, 20 Nov 2026]
  - An **action** is data: a gesture the kernel draws in its place, with the extension's label, which invokes a leaf of the extension on `self`, its input the place's data (on a bond, `{ bond }`): an activity of `user` (step 15), as a gesture of the kernel's own. No code of the extension runs to draw it. The kernel draws it in the kernel's mark of the extension, as a frame (below): its label is the extension's text, and could read as a gesture of the kernel ("Revoca l'offerta"); the mark tells whose it is (Alessandro, 20 Nov 2026).
  - A **slot** and a **page** are **frames**: the kernel places in its place (a slot in a screen of its own, a page as a screen) the extension's document (`shows`, a file of its bundle), isolated (EXT-13). The frame is told its place's data and is a client of its extension's surface on `self` only, through the kernel: it invokes its leaves and follows its reads as `user` does, by the boundary's messages (EXT-2), fenced by the kernel (EXT-4), its invocations traced and shown in the kernel's mark of the extension (below). A frame always sits in the kernel's mark of the extension (its name, a border): a frame could draw a screen like the kernel's, a consent included, and the mark tells the user whose it is (F6: a declared feature, not a defense).
  - **A frame acts with no tap** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (the principle of the flaws): a frame may invoke its extension's leaves on `self` when it likes, with no gesture of the user the kernel can verify (ping pong's slot could ping Bruno as soon as Anna opens their bond's screen): the kernel does not see inside the frame, and a flag of the user's activation sent by the frame would be the frame's claim. So the kernel shows a frame's invocations as the extension's activities, in its mark of the extension (what the extension did, from which frame, when), never as the user's gestures; an action's tap, which the kernel draws and sees, is the user's. A declared feature, told at activation (EXT-7). Rejected: a frame's invocations shown as the user's gestures (Anna's history would say she pinged Bruno); a tap verified by the kernel (the frame's document is isolated); a frame that invokes only through gestures the kernel draws (a vocabulary of the kernel for every extension's screens, discarded below).
  - The texts of an action and of a frame are the extension's (its `texts/`); the kernel's are its own screens and the mark, which bears the extension's name from its words. Their form, the words of an extension, and how the kernel draws them: WEB-22 [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026].
  - A **hook**, a programmatic one (Alessandro: "presi con le pinze"), comes with a case that shows it is needed, and is justified there.
  - Scenario (ping pong, step 14, whose names only illustrate): on the screen of Anna's bond with Bruno, ping pong's action `ping` and its slot `the_pings_of_a_bond`. Anna taps "Ping", ping pong's label: `user` invokes `send_a_ping` with the bond. The slot's frame, told the bond, follows `the_pings_of_a_bond` and shows "pong in 0.8 s", later "no response". Anna withdraws the offer from Bruno: neither is drawn on that bond's screen any more, since an offer goes both ways (EXT-7), and Bruno's pings to her meet silence; they stay on her screen of the bond with Carla. Ping pong deactivated: neither is drawn on any bond.
  - Discarded: slots and pages drawn by the kernel from the extension's data, in a vocabulary of the kernel's (every extension would widen it: an open set, which by the rule of explicitness is the extension's, not a construct per case in the kernel); a frame for an action (a gesture of the kernel's screen, drawn by it); an action drawn outside the mark (its label alone could pass for a gesture of the kernel; one rule for every extension point); a frame with a way of its own to its domains (two entries to one surface, one the kernel's trace would miss).

### Ping pong: `ping_pong`

The first extension (EXT-1; glossary, "The words of ping pong"), written in part 14.1 through step 12's boundary, on the test extension's model (`greetings`, EXT-9..EXT-12): a ping sent to a bond, its pong received, no response after a time. Its stories are in [`packages/extensions/ping_pong/README.md`](../packages/extensions/ping_pong/README.md), each with the name of its test, proven in part 14.2: 1–9 through the boundary on a fake kernel (`test/stories.test.ts`), 10–14 between two nodes of q-'s oracle (`packages/memory/test/extensions/ping_pong.test.ts`), the step's checks.

- **PNG-1** **The extension and its office** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: the package `packages/extensions/ping_pong` (`@q-mino/ping_pong`, EXT-9), its tree `business/pings` (the office), `persona/self` and `persona/known`, mounted below the kernel's nodes with the context `mounted_of` takes from its manifest (EXT-11), in `generator.config.ts` with `compared_with_main: true` (its surfaces are given to peers, PER-17). The office keeps the pings it sent (the bond, when, how long it waits, where it stands: waiting, or closed with what came back and when) and the pongs it gave back (the bond, when): instants as the node's clock tells them (`value.instant`), never a duration, which is a reading of two instants (a screen's, a read's). Rejected: the office named `ping_pong` (the extension's name, not what its office keeps); the round trip kept as a duration (a value derived from two instants the office has).
- **PNG-2** **A ping and what came back** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F7, EXT-12, DOM-13): `send_a_ping` (`{ bond }`, an action's place data, EXT-16) records `a_ping_has_been_sent` with the entry `ask_for_a_pong` (`order: 'any'`, keyed by the ping; its payload the ping, the bond and `waits`, 60 seconds, `dsl.a_ping_waits`); the ping is named by the operation that sends it (`identity_of`, D75.8), so the user's tap again is the recorded fact. Its return `close_a_ping` takes `{ ping_id, what_came_back }`, the business's closed set `the_pong_came_back`, `the_ping_had_no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer`, `the_ping_was_called_off` (D108; the last [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: the user's own gesture, ping pong deactivated or withdrawn from the bond while the ping waited, a meaning, EXT-12), and records one fact per happening (DOM-6), `a_pong_has_been_received`, `a_ping_has_had_no_response`, `a_ping_could_not_be_answered`, `a_ping_has_been_called_off` ([Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: a happening, the ping called off, as DOM-6 names a fact; the branch `the_ping_was_called_off_so_it_is_closed`), each with the ping and the instant; the branch is chosen by what came back, a predicate over the return's input (`the_pong_came_back`, `the_ping_had_no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer`, `the_ping_was_called_off`, their meaning `dsl.what_came_back_is`), each with `the_ping_waits`. A ping that waits no more (closed, or never sent) is refused: a dead letter, shown. [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] the form of the return, a fact per meaning, by DOM-6's criterion (one fact per meaning, or one fact with a closed case): ping pong's office speaks of different happenings, "it answered", "it did not answer", "it could not answer", "it was called off", so a fact each, as the office of the extensions does for its two (EXT-8); the other form, one fact with a closed case, stays where the happening is one (`greetings`' `a_greeting_has_been_closed`, EXT-12; the user's activity, step 15), as alternative definitions. Ping pong's leaf on `self` (PNG-4) and the steps 15, 16, 22.2 build on it. Rejected: one fact `a_ping_has_been_closed` with what came back (the form of `greetings`): one happening's name for four the office tells apart, which the screen and the history would tell apart by a field; a fact per meaning only when it changes what follows (nothing follows otherwise here: it would bring ping pong to one fact, against a name that tells a happening).
- **PNG-3** **The served side** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F7, DOM-11, F6): the leaf `ping` on `known`, `{}` as its input (a ping says nothing but that it came; no refinement, EXT-4), its creator the bond from the kernel's node (`context.bond`, EXT-11), invokes `answer_a_ping`, which records `a_ping_has_been_answered` with the pong named by the operation (`identity_of`): on `known` the operation is the request's identity (F7), so a ping delivered again is the same operation, and the duplicate branch gives the recorded fact, one pong (the step's check). It answers in one event (F7, EXT-4), `pong`; `no_pong`, a rejection no branch reaches, is named as a leaf names its rejection. Rejected: `ping` as a read leaf that answers with no fact (the node would keep nothing of who pinged it: Bruno's user could not see that Anna pinged him, F6, and a ping delivered again would have no duplicate branch to meet); the pong carrying the server's instant (a claim of the peer's, F2, which the asker does not need: its own instants tell the round trip).
- **PNG-4** **Where the pong is shown** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (EXT-16, DOM-3, F7; what a screen shows): the action `ping` on `a_bond` invokes `usecase/send_a_ping` on `self` with the bond, and the leaf follows the ping's chain to its closing (`follows` the four facts of PNG-2): its events `the_ping_is_sent`, then `pong`, `no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer` or `called_off` ([Q, C, 21 Nov 2026], the ping called off, PNG-2), at most 70 seconds (the ping's 60 and the executor's delivery before them), then `the_wait_is_over`, which records nothing (F7: two times, both the feature's). So the pong is shown where the action is, in the kernel's mark of ping pong on the bond's screen, as `user` gives the action's events back as data (GR-7, steps 15 and 16). Steps 15, 16 and 22.2 build on it. **A flaw turned feature**, declared (the principle of the flaws): the leaf follows the ping at most 70 seconds from the tap (`at_most: 70_000`), while the ping's own 60 run from its delivery; an outcome that comes later (a slow delivery, the app closed or asleep: a PWA has no push) is a fact recorded, the ping closed in ping pong's office, but shown on no screen: her activity ended as no longer followed (USR-3), until the slot comes. **The remedy, deferred**: the slot `the_pings_of_a_bond`, a frame that follows a read of the bond's pings (EXT-16's illustration), which shows every ping with what came back, whenever it came: an extension point that asks more of the user at activation (EXT-10), its frame following a read. The library's open scenario SC-57 (`docs/scenarios.md`): a follow until what it follows closes, a `follows` bounded by the followed chain's own closing rather than by a fixed `at_most`. The label "Ping" is a text the user reads: ping pong's `texts/`, with step 16. **As rendered** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (part 22.2, WEB-22): the action in ping pong's mark on a bond's screen, labelled "Ping" by its words (`texts/it.json`; the label confirmed by Alessandro, 22 Nov 2026), off while its activity is in progress, its end told in its words ("pong!", "nessuna risposta", "non può rispondere"); after the leaf's 70 seconds its activity ends as no longer followed ("Non lo seguo più"), and a later outcome is told on no screen, the flaw declared above; the slot still deferred, its remedy (an extension point that asks more at activation, EXT-10).
- **PNG-5** **The manifest; preinstalled and off** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (EXT-3, EXT-7, EXT-10, EXT-15): `manifest.ts` declares the name `ping_pong`, `touches` the port of requests and its storage, the action `ping`; it sees on `known` the bond alone (no peer's key, no bond's name: a ping needs neither), and has no slot nor page (PNG-4). `manifest.json` is derived by `pnpm --filter @q-mino/ping_pong manifest`, kept equal to the derivation by `test/manifest.test.ts`. Preinstalled is the composition's: the configurators install it (the node of q-'s oracle at 14.2, `phone` at step 25), and it is off until the user activates it, accepting its manifest: the office of the extensions has no activation of it (`not_active`), its realm is not loaded and a peer's ping meets silence (EXT-7, EXT-15, F8). Nothing of it is the extension's to write.
- **PNG-6** **The delivery** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (EXT-12; `adapter/delivery.ts`, on `greetings`' model): the port of requests called with the bond, `usecase/ping` on `known` at its current version, `{}`, the effect's path, the entry's id and `waits`; `responded` with `pong` → `the_pong_came_back`; with `no_pong` or one of the kernel's events above the leaf (`not_found`, `invalid_request`, `version_not_supported`, `identity_already_used`, EXT-5) → `the_peer_cannot_answer`; with neither → `exhausted`, the peer's event kept (EXT-12); `no_response` → `the_ping_had_no_response`; `refused` for `not_active` and `not_offered_to_the_bond` → `the_ping_was_called_off`, a meaning, never a dead letter (EXT-12) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]; the faults, `the_bond_is_gone` and `refused` for `not_declared` and `too_large_for_a_letter` → `exhausted` with the reason, a dead letter shown in ping pong's mark, which the user may redeliver (EXT-2), holding nothing behind it (`order: 'any'`). Rejected, by Alessandro (21 Nov 2026): every refusal exhausted, as written first (a ping queued before a withdrawal became a dead letter whose redelivery meets the same refusal: a conscious gesture shown as a fault). The meanings are a record mapped over `ping`'s events, so that an event added to `ping` does not compile until the business says what it means. Its state adapter over its partition and its configurator, with the boundary's side, are part 14.2's, on `greetings`' (EXT-14, EXT-9) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: the partition's adapter is a copy of `greetings`', since an extension imports no other (GR-10; rejected: a package of the extensions' adapters in common, a dependency GR-10 does not name, with two extensions only); the three predicates over what came back read no key of the partition (they hold on the return's input, as the office of the extensions chooses its branch, EXT-8). On the node of q-'s oracle the app carries one extension, `greetings` or `ping_pong` (its `installed`), preinstalled there as PNG-5 says.

### The user: `user`

The user domain of the phone (D76, ADP-7, GR-7): what the user asks her node, in her words, and what she is told. Written in part 15.1 (Claude, 20 Nov 2026), on step 12's design as the gate left it; the stories in [`user/README.md`](../packages/domains/src/user/README.md), proved in part 15.2.

- **USR-1** **The user domain** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (D76, D40, D35, GR-2, GR-7): `user` is a top folder of q-'s tree with three offices, written with the constructs of every domain (D35): `activities` (_le attività_), what she asks her node, in progress until it ends, and how it ended (USR-2..USR-4); `drafts` (_le bozze_), the texts she started (USR-5); `choices` (_le scelte_), the face she acts as (USR-6). The top `user/value.ts` and `user/dsl.ts` hold her words shared by them: faces, bonds and extensions, what an extension asks (USR-7), held letters and what happened without a fact (USR-8). The map gives `user` nothing (`sight.ts`, `user: {}`): it names faces, bonds and extensions by the identities `self`'s reads give, as text, in its own schemas, and the **port of `user` toward `self`** is the configurator's, typed on `self`'s contract (GR-7): it parses her words with `self`'s schemas and links `self`'s to hers with exhaustive maps (GR-3's form), so that a new leaf or event of `self` fails to compile there until the link decides what it is to her. `user`'s state is the node's, its partition the IndexedDB's of step 21, its oracle a cell beside the kernel's (15.2). The placeholder `user/index.ts` is gone (`@q-mino/domains/user/*`, as the other domains of the tree). Rejected: `user` seeing `persona` in the map (the generator opens a door to every folder of `persona`, so `user`'s generated scopes would import `known`'s, which GR-7's lint refuses; a sight of one sub-folder is a new form of the map's key, with its case); `user` importing `bonds`' identities (GR-2); `user`'s own brand of a bond's id (a second brand of one key, GR-3's rejection, with nothing to check it: the port parses).
- **USR-2** **An activity** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (D76.1, D58, ADP-7, EXT-16, GR-7): the screens ask for an activity with `ask_for_an_activity`, its id drawn before the first attempt (DOM-12, WEB-5) and **what is asked**, an ADT by `activity`: one case per gesture of the kernel's screens, each carried out by one leaf of `self` of the same name with its input in her words (`make_my_first_face`, `prepare_a_ticket`, `open_their_ticket`, `rename_a_bond`, `remove_a_bond`, `activate_an_extension`, `deactivate_an_extension`, `withdraw_the_offer_from_a_bond`, `offer_an_extension_to_a_bond`, `ask_a_bond_for_the_extensions_in_common`); an action of an extension as data, `use_an_action` (the extension, the action's name, its place: on a bond with the bond, or the extension's own screen); the two gestures on a held letter (USR-8). Each case invokes at most one command (D58). The office records `an_activity_has_been_asked` with the effect `carry_out_an_activity` (USR-4); the same id again, `an_activity_has_been_asked_again`: nothing changes (DOM-6). Its reads: `an_activity` (what, when, its stage: in progress, or ended with how and when) and `the_activities_in_progress`, in the order she asked them. The reads of `self` (her faces, bonds, tickets, extensions) reach the screens through the same port, as `self` gives them (reads are free, D58): 15.2's. The relays of a ticket are the node's, the port's wiring (stage 1: one relay; the preferred relays of a face come later). Why one command with an ADT, from the office's lens: the office of the activities is one counter, where she asks "do this for me", and a request is one form, with a case for each gesture (make my face, open their ticket, use an action of an extension...): the counter takes every gesture the same way, keeps it under its id and follows it to its end (USR-3, USR-4); each case is still named and typed in her words. Rejected (the alternative for A): a command per gesture (a counter per gesture, eleven alike, the twelfth an extension's action as data anyway); the proof of the explosion as the reason (the kernel's gestures are a closed set, typed; the open set, an extension's actions, is already one case, `use_an_action`); the leaf's path and its input as JSON (the screens could not be typed: `ui` imports `user` only, GR-2). **An extension's action** (GR-7, amended): `user` names it by its extension, its name and its place; the leaf it invokes is the composed manifest's, which the office of the extensions does not keep (EXT-7), so the port reads it from the composition and invokes it on `self` through the hosting (`mounted_leaf_ports` at `persona/self`, as the user, EXT-2), its input the place's data (`{ bond }`, EXT-16). An action on a bond whose offer is withdrawn, or of an extension not active, is not drawn (EXT-7, step 16); asked meanwhile, it ends as USR-3 says.
- **USR-3** **How an activity ended** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (ADP-2, D74, D14, DOM-3, EXT-15): `end_an_activity`, the return of `carry_out_an_activity`, records `an_activity_has_ended` with **how it ended**, an ADT in her words, the meaning the contract's and the name hers (D74.1): `done`, `done_already`, `cannot_be_done`, each with what `self`'s leaf said (its event as data); `the_extension_told_how_it_ended`, an extension's own event (a pong, or no response: what the extension tells of the end, an answer or none), whose meaning its texts tell (EXT-16); `no_longer_followed`, the leaf's wait over before its end (`the_wait_is_over`, DOM-3); `the_extension_cannot_be_reached`, `it_is_not_active` or `it_has_stopped` after its second fall (EXT-15); `the_app_cannot_do_it`, a base event or a closing of `self`'s node (`invalid_request`, `version_not_supported`, `identity_already_used`, `not_found`, `refused`: a fault of the app's composition, never hers; every base event named, D74, `dsl.how_a_closing_ends`). The port follows the leaf's flow to its end and gives its last event (`send_a_ping`: `the_ping_is_sent`, then `pong`, `no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer` or `called_off`, the ping called off at a withdrawal or a deactivation, PNG-2, or the wait over at 70 seconds); a technical outcome thrown (an extension that falls, EXT-15; the node's storage) is no end: the queue tries again (USR-4). The link of `self`'s events, the port's exhaustive map: `done` for `your_face_is_being_made`, `your_ticket_is_being_prepared`, `you_are_bonded`, `you_met_in_person`, `your_introduction_is_sent`, `the_bond_is_renamed`, `the_bond_is_removed`, `the_extension_is_active`, `the_extension_is_deactivated`, `the_offer_is_withdrawn`, `the_extension_is_offered_again`, `the_bond_is_asked`; `done_already` for `you_have_a_face_already`, `you_opened_their_ticket_already`, `the_bond_was_removed_already`, `the_extension_was_active_already`, `the_extension_was_not_active`, `the_offer_was_withdrawn_already`, `the_extension_was_offered_already`; `cannot_be_done` for `the_ticket_cannot_be_prepared`, `their_ticket_cannot_be_opened`, `there_is_no_such_bond`, `the_manifest_is_not_the_installed_one`, `the_extension_cannot_be_deactivated`, `the_offer_cannot_be_withdrawn`, `the_extension_cannot_be_offered`, `the_bond_cannot_be_asked`. [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] one fact for the end, with its how, by DOM-6's criterion (one fact per meaning, or one fact with a closed case): the office speaks of one happening, "the activity has ended, so", and how it ended is a detail of it, her concepts, which the screens tell apart by data (the form of greetings' `a_greeting_has_been_closed`; ping pong and the office of the extensions keep a fact per meaning, where their offices speak of different happenings: both stay, as alternative definitions). The case of an extension's own event is named by what happened, `the_extension_told_how_it_ended` [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: the extension tells how its action ended, an answer or none (`no_response`, `the_peer_cannot_answer`). Rejected: a fact per how (seven names for one happening, each a return's branch chosen by a predicate on its input); `the_extension_answered` (it says the contrary of the happening where no answer came).
- **USR-4** **The queue of the activities** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (LIB-12, D76, D73.5, D73.6, D60, DOM-12, DOM-14, DOM-15): the queue policy of the user domain, which is the library's (LIB-12), is its effect executor: an activity is an entry of `carry_out_an_activity`, keyed by its id, which is the operation's id on `self` too (`{ id, scope: 'self/the_user' }`), so that every attempt is one operation and a fall in between meets the recorded fact again (DOM-11; the paper's uncertain outcome, resolved by sending again with the same identity, D73.6). Pending is in progress: an activity stays so until its end, across a restart of the app (the entry is durable; D74.2's _in corso_). A technical outcome is `transient`, tried again after the executor's pause with the same id (D60: the identity kept, the command rebuilt). Its order is `any`: no order between activities and none held behind another (the paper's first guarantee of a queue, D76 of 9 Oct; a ping that waits 70 seconds for its pong holds no rename), so a dead letter holds only itself. What cannot be carried out (the port fails for good) is exhausted, and an end the office refuses (an activity not in progress) a dead letter: both held letters of `user`, shown (USR-8, DOM-15). Rejected: a queue of the user domain's own in the library (a new construct for what the executor does already; the paper's example policy, the gestures' order and a chain that waits for an outcome, has no case in stage 1, where no activity depends on another's outcome: with its case, `per_resource` over the field that chains them, SC-55); the screen invoking `self` and a decorator retrying (D14's form: the retry would not outlive the app closed, and nothing would tell her what is in progress, F6). A gap of the paper: it gives the queue policy to the user domain's library (D76) and is silent on its form, here the outbox any domain may have (D42).
- **USR-5** **The drafts** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (D40, D73.5, D76.2): a draft is a text she started, kept by **what it is for** (an ADT: `the_name_of_my_first_face`, `the_name_i_give_a_bond` with the bond), one draft for each, its id derived from it (`dsl.the_draft_for`); `keep_a_draft` keeps its text (`a_draft_has_been_kept`; the same text again, `…_again`), `drop_a_draft` drops it when used or given up (`a_draft_has_been_dropped`; none kept, `…_again`); `a_draft` reads it. When the screens keep and drop it is `ui`'s (step 16). Stage 1 has no other text she types (messaging is an extension, after it). Rejected: drafts in `ui` (a phone that closes the app would lose them; a PM names them, D76.2); a draft id drawn at random (two drafts for one thing).
- **USR-6** **The active face** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (D78.3, D76.2; the glossary's "Cambia volto"): `choices` keeps the face she acts as (`change_face` → `the_active_face_has_been_changed`; the same face, `…_again`); `the_active_face` reads it, none before her first face. The office does not check the face is hers (it sees no other domain, GR-2): `self` refuses what she then asks with a face not hers, and the screens show the faces `self` reads. In stage 1 she has one face: the screens choose it once she makes it (16).
- **USR-7** **What an extension asks** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (EXT-7, EXT-13, EXT-16, F6): the notice of an activation is `dsl.what_an_extension_asks(manifest)`, her view of the manifest (`value.a_manifest`, as the office of the extensions keeps it) told in her words: what it sees (the peer's key, the bond's name), what it touches (requests on her bonds, a storage of its own), the bonds it answers (its leaves on known faces), its places on her screens; then the flaws the kernel cannot fence: the network as far as the browser leaves it open, always, and its frames acting with no tap of hers, when it has a slot or a page. `activate_an_extension` carries the manifest she was shown, which `self` accepts only if it is the one installed (EXT-7). In process, a choice of trust, is the composition's, not the manifest's: told with its case.
- **USR-8** **Nothing happens in silence** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (F6, DOM-15, D67, EXT-2, EXT-6, PER-14): she is told, in her words (`user/value.ts`): a **held letter** (a dead letter of the kernel's executors or reactions, of `user`'s own, or an extension's notice `dead_letter`, EXT-2: whose, which letter, what, and why: `it_could_not_be_delivered` with what the world said for `exhausted`, `what_came_back_was_refused` for `rejected`, `it_was_done_otherwise_before` for `identity_already_used`, `the_app_could_not_read_what_came_back` for `invalid_request`; the paper's word is never shown, D67), which she may send again or put aside, two activities (`send_a_held_letter_again`, `put_a_held_letter_aside`: the executor's `redeliver` and `discard` by the user, or the boundary's for an extension's; `done` when handed, `done_already` when no longer held); and **what happened without a fact**, a trace entry (the kernel's, EXT-6; a tree's, PER-14; an extension's): `a_request_met_silence` (the three cases, told her since nothing is hidden from her, F8), `a_letter_could_not_be_opened` (`an_envelope_not_opened`), `a_response_came_unasked` (`a_response_not_awaited`), `a_letter_was_refused` (a tree's trace, and `not_a_leaf_of_the_extension`), `a_response_could_not_be_sent` (`a_response_not_deposited`), `a_request_could_not_be_sent` (`a_request_not_sent`: a request of the kernel's over what a letter carries, PST-12) [Q, C, 23 Nov 2026], `an_extension_did_what_it_does_not_declare` (`a_call_not_declared`, `an_event_not_in_the_surface`, `a_frame_fenced`, `a_message_not_in_the_boundary`), `an_extension_fell`, `a_request_was_not_answered` (`a_call_not_answered`, `a_request_not_answered`). They are read through a port of `user` over the executors, the extensions' notices and the traces, which links them to her words with exhaustive maps (15.2); the trace is eventual and without guarantees (F6), so they are no facts of hers. An extension's are shown in its mark (EXT-16, step 16). The annotations of a bond (its handshake, its confirmation) come with `self`'s reads, unchanged; no response is an extension's event (`the_extension_told_how_it_ended`, ping pong's `no_response`) or the kernel's question had no answer. Rejected: the notices as facts of `user` (a second record of what the executors and the traces keep, and the trace promises nothing); the kernel's words shown as they are (D74: the name is hers).
- **USR-9** **The port of `user` and its news** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (GR-7, USR-1..USR-4, USR-8, DOM-14, DOM-15, EXT-2, EXT-15, F6; part 15.2): the port toward `self` is `adapters-common/src/user/`, `the_delivery_of_an_activity`, wired by the configurator with the node's clock, `self`'s tree by path (the kernel's, `zz.tree_ports_by_path`), the relays of a ticket, the extensions installed (each with its composition, whether active, its life, its leaves mounted on `self`, its letters held as its notices tell them, the boundary's `redeliver` and `discard`) and the kernel's held letters. A gesture of the kernel's builds the leaf's input from her words (`bond` → `bond_id`, `face` → `face_id`, the ticket's relays the wiring's), which `self`'s tree parses (a refusal is `invalid_request`: the app cannot do it); its link is a type over each leaf's own events (`OwnEvents` of `self`'s use cases, `satisfies`: a new leaf, a new event, or one more in the map, fails to compile). An action the composed manifest does not have at the place asked, or of an extension not installed, ends `the_app_cannot_do_it` with `not_found` (the screens drew what the composition does not have: the app's fault); the extension not active, then stopped, is checked before the invocation, which speaks the version of its surface on `self`. A flow that says nothing is exhausted (a held letter of `user`'s); a throw is `transient`. The gestures on a held letter: a kernel's by its name, `<effect>/<entry id>` (as an extension names its own, EXT-2), through its executor's `redeliver` or `discard` by `the_user` with her reason (`put aside by the user` when she gave none: the library's trace takes one); an extension's only while its notices tell it held, its boundary's message handed, `transient` when the extension does not run; `done` with `the_letter_is_handed`, `done_already` with `the_letter_is_no_longer_held`. Activating through an activity records the office's fact only: loading the extension on its activation, and unloading on its deactivation, are the configurator's (the stack, 17; the phone, 22). **The news** (`the_news.ts`): `the_held_letters` over the executors' dead letters (an entry waiting behind one is not a letter of its own), the reactions' and the extensions' notices (a `dead_letter` not `released` since; the cause it claims, F2, parsed, else `it_could_not_be_delivered` with no reason); `what_happened_in_the_kernel`, exhaustive over the kernel's trace; `what_happened_in_a_tree`, a letter refused with its leaf, none for `self`'s (her own request, told by her activity's end). A silence is told with its extension and its case; the bond is not named (the trace keeps the key, not the bond: the screens may join it, 16). Rejected: the screens following `self` and an extension's leaf themselves (GR-7: `ui` imports `user` only); the port loading the extension on activation (the life of an extension is the hosting's, wired by the configurator, EXT-15); a kernel's letter named by its position (an extension names its own by entry, one name for both); a tree's trace of `self` told her twice.

### The screens: `ui`

The screen model of the phone (V2, ADP-7, D76, GR-2): the screens of stage 1, their states and what happens on them, as a pure domain. Written in part 16.1 (Claude, 21 Nov 2026), from Alessandro's notes on screens and flows within stage 1's scope; the stories in [`ui/README.md`](../packages/domains/src/ui/README.md), proved in part 16.2.

- **UI-1** **The screen model** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (V2, ADP-7, D76, GR-8): `ui` is a top folder of q-'s tree, with a folder per screen; its top `value.ts` and `dsl.ts` hold the words the screens share: a screen (`a_screen`, with what it is about), **the way** (the screens she opened, the last shown), **where a gesture goes** (nowhere, to a screen, back, to the start), what a gesture asks (UI-3), the places and the mark (UI-4), and what the screens are given to show, in their words (a face, a contact in the list and its card, a ticket ready, an extension: GR-3's form, the rendering links `user`'s and `self`'s reads to them, USR-9); `the_start` (no face: the first face; a face and none active: her contacts, the face chosen, USR-6; a link: its invitation on top) and `the_way_after`. Each screen's folder: its **state** (presentational only), **what happens** on it (an ADT: her gestures, and what the screen comes to see: what it asked ended, a ticket ready, the camera), **a turn** (its state after, what it asks, where it goes), **what it is given** and **what it shows** (an ADT of its cases); and `at_the_start`, `after(state, what_happens)`, `what_it_shows(state, given)`. Values and pure functions only, no command, fact or query: the state of a screen is lost with the screen, as presentational state may be (what must outlive it is `user`'s: drafts, activities, the active face). The rendering keeps each screen's state while it is open and the way (Solid signals, step 22), draws the ids and the time at a gesture (UI-3), hands what a turn asks to `user`'s use cases, and tells the screen what it sees. Rejected: an office with commands and facts per screen (navigation recorded as facts, the published history the paper keeps for the business, D101; durable where presentational state may be lost; every tap through the duplicate branch); the state in the rendering alone (V2: the screen model is a domain, testable without the browser).
- **UI-2** **What the map gives `ui`** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (GR-2, GR-7, DOM-25, USR-1): `ui: { user: ['value', 'id'] }`, `user`'s words and identities, never its use cases (`ui` invokes nothing, ADP-7) and nothing of `persona`. `user` keeps the words its offices share at its top (`user/value.ts`: a face, a bond, a manifest, what an extension asks, a held letter, what happened without a fact), which the generator gave no door: a door was a folder's below its top. **The generator since part 16.1**: a domain's top has a door of its own twins only, when the map opens a kind it holds (`user/_door_ui.ts`, `value`), carried by the scopes under the domain's name before its folders' (`user`, `activities`, `choices`, `drafts`); its folders below keep their own doors; the cruiser allows it (`packages/generator/src/doors.ts`, `tree.ts`, `rules.ts`; `test/doors.test.ts`). No other tree changes: no other top seen by an audience holds a twin of a kind its map opens. Rejected: `ui`'s own copies of `user`'s words (two views of concepts a product manager names, which are `user`'s, ADP-7); `user`'s words moved into a folder below (its offices, siblings, would not see them); a door of the top that carries its folders too (every door of every scope would change its form: a folder's door named under its domain, `user.activities`, and so `bonds.address_book` elsewhere; with the top's own door, what is new enters at one point only, and the folders' doors keep the form every scope has today).
- **UI-3** **What a gesture asks, and its course** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (ADP-7, USR-2..USR-6, DOM-12, WEB-5, GR-8): a turn asks of her node `what_it_asks`, an ADT of `user`'s values: `an_activity` (its id and `what_is_asked`), `to_keep_a_draft`, `to_drop_a_draft`, `to_change_face`. The ids an activity needs (the activity's, a face's, a bond's to be) and the time are drawn by the rendering at the gesture and carried by it (`what_it_asked`): the functions are pure, and randomness and the clock enter as parameters (GR-8). A screen keeps what it asked, what for, and whether it ended; the rendering follows `an_activity` for each and tells the screen once it ended (`what_it_asked_ended`); `the_course_of` gives nothing asked, in progress, or ended with how (USR-3). **Once at a time**: a gesture whose activity for the same purpose is in progress asks nothing (a tap repeated; `user` would meet the same id as `…_again` only if the id were the same, and the rendering draws a new one per tap). **Drafts**: the name of her first face and the name she gives a bond while renaming it are kept as she types and dropped once used (`done`) or given up; the name at an invitation stays on the screen, his own by default. **The active face**: chosen by the start once she has a face (stage 1 has one). Rejected: the ids drawn by `ui` (no randomness in a pure domain, GR-8); the activity's end read by the screen's functions (`after` is given no reads: what it sees comes as what happens); a draft for the invitation's name, keyed by a bond drawn when the screen opens (a bond id drawn before she accepts, for a name whose default comes back).
- **UI-4** **The places and the kernel's mark** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (EXT-7, EXT-16, USR-2, USR-8): the places the kernel's screens offer are `a_bond`, on a contact, told `{ bond }`, and `the_extension`, on its own screen, told the extension (`a_place`); `the_marks_at(place, { extensions, offered })` gives, for each extension active and, on a bond, offered to it (an offer goes both ways), the points its manifest declares at that place in its order, as **a mark** (`a_mark`: the extension, the place told, its actions, slots, pages); an extension with none there has no mark there. An action of a mark tapped is `use_an_action` on the place's data (`an_action_tapped`), as a gesture of the kernel's own; a slot is a frame the rendering places in the mark, told the place; a page opens `a_page`, a screen whose content is the extension's frame in its mark, no longer there once its place draws it no more. An extension's letters held and what happened to it without a fact are shown in its mark, on its own screen and on `what_happened` (USR-8). The frames are clients of their extension's surface through the boundary (EXT-2), never of `ui`. Rejected: an action drawn for an extension not active or not offered to the bond (EXT-7: what the user would tap the kernel refuses); a page drawn inside another screen (EXT-16: a page is a screen).
- **UI-5** **The screens of stage 1** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (what a screen shows and the names she reads; Alessandro's notes on screens and flows, 3–4 Nov 2026): **the first face** (1a: her name, the button off with none; no colour, no import: step 26); once made, **the server** (1c, informative, no choice: the relay of the configuration her letters go through, what it declares, what it sees and what not; Cumino tells it, it cannot verify it), shown once after the first face and read again from the menu, going on to the start; **my contacts**, the home of stage 1 (there are no chats before messaging: the list of her bonds by the names she gives them, as introduced when it differs, whether they have her; none yet: the two handshakes; a word when something happened; the menu: the handshake in person, at a distance, the extensions, what happened, the server); with messaging too the list of contacts stays a view of its own, beside the chats (Alessandro, 22 Nov 2026); **a contact** (9: the name and as introduced, the handshake's way and time, "does she have you?", the relays of their mailbox; rename, remove once confirmed; the extensions active, each offered or not; the question of the extensions in common and, beside it, what came back of the last one asked (a request's return is shown where its gesture is, as PNG-4): waiting, the extensions in common by name, none in common, or no answer with why, from `self`'s read `the_questions_asked_of_a_bond`, over what the office of the activations keeps (EXT-8; each question with when it was asked, `asked_at`, the last first); the place `a_bond`); **the handshake in person** (5, revised: the crossed QR, the ticket prepared on opening, the first ticket scanned opened by its name, which she may change on the contact; bonded, with what her node said, told "in person" only when it said `you_met_in_person` (BND-10; Alessandro, 21 Nov 2026: an opening `done` on a bond at a distance does not say it); the camera's four states); **the handshake at a distance** (6, who invites: the flaw told first, a link made and shared); **an invitation** (6, who receives: his name by default, accept or not; offline the activities wait in progress); **the extensions** and **an extension** (its notice before activation, its new manifest to accept, its deactivation, its mark); **a page**; **what happened** (in progress, the kernel's news, each extension's in its mark); and over every screen **a strip** while the relay did not answer when her node last met it (`the_relay_met`, from the relay client's `unreachable`, step 20: her letters wait and leave as soon as it answers; telling no internet apart from a relay silent stays open). Out of stage 1 or with no leaf yet, so not drawn: the introduction's cards (after the prototype), the list of links waiting with _Annulla_ (no leaf cancels a ticket), _Elimina e avvisa_ (a request of the kernel between bonded faces, not messaging: its design written in BND-6, open, after the block). A missing read, given by the rendering until `self` has it: the extensions installed (the composition knows them). The Italian texts are 22.1's.

## 5. Persona and auth

### The four personas [Q, 6 Nov 2026]

| persona     | who                                                                        | `Caller`                                                         | can do (prototype)                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `self`      | the user of this node, from the app                                        | the local user                                                   | everything its tree exposes, the kernel's leaves and those of the active extensions (EXT-1); a node "as face X" binds the active face (PER-3)                                                |
| `known`     | valid signature, face known (a bond; groups and channels in a later stage) | the sender's face key                                            | the kernel's use cases (the extensions in common, EXT-8) and the leaves of the active extensions offered to its bond (EXT-6); no longer the chats, since messaging is an extension (step 12) |
| `unknown`   | valid signature, face never seen                                           | the sender's face key                                            | introduce itself with a ticket (PER-11)                                                                                                                                                      |
| `anonymous` | no signature, or a signature that does not verify                          | an id per letter, not a stable identity (as the paper's visitor) | nothing yet: every letter is rejected and annotated                                                                                                                                          |
