- **PER-1** Personas differ only when the trade differs; differences among wearers are values. Contact, member of group G, channel, the face of mine the bond belongs to and, later, permissions per known face are **values from auth**, not personas or trees. D33, D77 [Q]
- **PER-2** A signature that does not verify gives `anonymous`, with the value "invalid signature, claimed face K" as an annotation; never a `Caller` with the claimed identity. Not a persona of its own: removing the signature is always possible, so it could not be given less than `anonymous`. D106 [Q]
- **PER-3** My faces are not personas. Under `self`, a node "as face X" binds the face once per request; every constraint states whose value it reads. One face per request. D87 [Q]
- **PER-4** The `Caller` is produced by the interface from verified credentials and passed explicitly to the persona: id mandatory, other fields only if verified; absence is a value, never `null`. `anonymous` has an id per letter: with no stable sender, every letter is a new operation. D30, D78, D91
  - The id of an `anonymous` letter is the BLAKE2b hash of its envelope, computed by `post` when it opens it: the same envelope delivered again is the same operation, a different envelope a new one, and nothing declared inside the letter counts. [Q, plan]
  - In Shed the `Caller` is an ADT by what identifies it, `identified_by: 'account' | 'session'` (an anonymous session, D78.7), with its id; the entry gives it to every persona, whatever the hat, through the node of the persona base (`persona/node.ts`, D78.7), whose `takes` is the only one of the tree; it gives context, never leaves, and verifies no hat (D78). A persona reads the id; `visitor` reads it opaque, never its case, so that it gives no one more than anyone (PER-1, D78): for the scope of its operations, attribution (D56) and the owner of what it creates (D7, D98.4), when its first command comes. [Q, 13 Nov 2026]
  - **An actor from outside** (SC-34) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (3); A for the shape]: a system that calls the shed with credentials is a persona (D88.1, D91.1), with its hat, node, leaves, version and contract in its own words; neither a person nor an internal entry (PER-12). Rossi's workshop repairs the shed's tools, and its system closes a job when its van brings a tool back (`persona/workshop/`, `close_a_job`). Its `Caller` is a third case of the ADT, `identified_by: 'key'`, a key the shed gave it (`key_id` of `auth/accounts`), whose signature the interface verifies: not an account, which is someone who signs in, so that the `Caller` tells a system from a person and no person's lookup answers for it. The node of the persona base, in Shed the common top of every tree, which gives the `Caller` and holds no leaf (not the paper's persona base with "my hats", which an actor has not, D91.1), passes it down as any; `member` and `keeper` find no account and close with `not_found`; auth keeps the hats of the keys it gave (`the_hats_of_a_key`), and `workshop` verifies its hat as `keeper` does (PER-7). The operation (D91.2, DOM-11): the sender is outside and speaks its own language, so the id of its request is where it puts it, in what it sends; the leaf names the field of its input that holds it (`operation: { identity: 'notification_id' }`, as a read leaf's `stream.identity`, checked by type to hold a string, an optional field not), the library reads it after the schema (PER-5.3; for a past major, from the current leaf's input, after `to_next`), and the entry gives the scope alone (`zz.OperationScopeContext`, `workshop/K-ROSSI`), composed as for any leaf; the fingerprint covers it with the rest. Where the id is read is the contract's, not the interface's: one interface serves every tree, and a copy of the contract in it would drift. The wait is the outcome, with no `follows` (D74: an integration takes "accepted"). The business names what the system closes, the stay in repair (`close_a_repair` of `tools`, over `the_tool_is_under_this_repair`), so that a late retry is safe by the business too (D91.2): Luca's command by the tool alone stays his, who holds the tool. Scenario: on Friday Rossi's van brings the drill back and his system sends N-88 for RP1: [`a_tool_has_been_repaired`], Gianni's "in the workshop" sees R8 leave; the answer is lost and N-88 comes again: the duplicate branch, the same fact; N-88 with another job: `identity_already_used`. Had the system been down, Luca would have put the drill back by hand, Marta broken it again (RP2), and N-88 for RP1, on Monday, finds it in another stay: `there_is_no_such_open_job`. Rejected: the interface reads the id (the contract kept twice); a marker in the input's schema (the declaration hidden in it, two fields marked ambiguous, A); a function from the input (opaque; an id of several fields comes with its case, A); an account for the system; the tool alone in the job (a late notice would close the next stay). Left, with their cases: values from auth for an actor (several workshops, each closing only its own stays: a perimeter that reads state, D80.4); a sender with no stable id (every request a new operation, the business's idempotency alone, D91.2). At runtime [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 6.2): the entry gives such a leaf its operation's scope alone; one that gives an id beside it is the entry's fault, thrown (two ids for one request); the input is parsed by its schema, built over the root's context and the scope (no leaf's input reads an operation's id: a leaf that names it has none yet, so every command leaf's input reads the same, `OperationScopeContext`), then the id read from the field the leaf names, as an `operation_id`; a field that holds no id (an empty text a schema let through) is `invalid_request` (`input_not_in_schema`), as a field its schema refuses. The creator and the use case below get the whole operation, the scope and the id the input named. Scenarios on the oracle (SC-34): on Friday Rossi's system sends N-88 for RP1, [`a_tool_has_been_repaired`] under the operation N-88 in `workshop/K-ROSSI`, and Gianni's R8 leaves "in the workshop"; N-88 again, the same; N-88 with another job, `identity_already_used`; the late notice on Monday, `there_is_no_such_open_job`; Marta on the workshop's leaf, a key without the hat, Rossi on `member`'s, `not_found`. Rejected: the entry's id taken when it gives one (two sources of one identity, which could differ unseen); the id read before the schema (a value no schema checked, PER-5.3).
- **PER-5** Order of entry checks, fixed; the first that fails closes the request (D106):
  1. transport (relay or local mailbox) and interface establish the `Caller` (`post` opens and verifies);
  2. interface: contract version ("version not supported"), then hat ("hat not available");
  3. persona: node exists and is a leaf, input in the schema ("invalid request");
  4. persona: perimeter constraints on values and trusted-frame data only ("not found");
  5. persona: limit constraints on values, input and trusted-frame data only (rejection with all reasons);
  6. business, in the atomic operation: duplicate branch, then the composed condition.
     A check may answer before a perimeter only with what the caller already knows.
  - **A retry after the input's window over `now` moved** [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026; built 19 Nov 2026] (D61, D79, D100, D106; a gap of the paper): Gianni books the ladder on Thursday for Friday, the answer is lost, his phone retries on Friday morning; with the order above the schema ("from tomorrow") fails at step 3 and the retry reads `invalid_request` while the booking is there, the same for a limit that reads `now` (a card expired meanwhile). A command leaf looks its operation up in its scope (the caller and the hat) once the hat is claimed, before step 3: recorded with the same fingerprint, the recorded fact, projected by the leaf; with another, `identity_already_used`; none, on to step 3. The fingerprint is over the input as sent (LIB-11), so it needs no schema; the business's duplicate branch stays, for two tries racing. The outcome of the caller's own operation is what it already knows (step 6's rule), a hat revoked meanwhile included. A divergence from D106's order, for the guarantee the paper gives a retry (an operation's identity resolves the uncertainty). Rejected: the window kept, a declared feature, every client reconciling by reading; the schema split, the static shape before and the refinements over `now` after the duplicate branch (DOM-21's schema a function of the context, and the nodes' limits not covered).
    **Built** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (`tree_port.ts`, `recorded_before_the_schema`): after the gate and the leaf of the version's major (`not_a_leaf`: the lookup answers in that leaf's words), the leaf computes the fingerprint over its path and the input as sent (LIB-11) and asks the port below, which looks the operation up through the adapter's `recorded` (`zz.RecordedBy`, a read outside the step, DOM-9) and compares it as in the step (DOM-11): the recorded fact comes back as the outcome it was, projected and followed (D58.2) as an outcome is; `identity_already_used` is traced (F6). The lookup is the library's own, a member of the command and step ports by a symbol (`$Recorded`), not in the port's type: no one else asks it, and in the type every port would carry its outcomes twice, past what the compiler writes out for Shed's configurator. Where the input names its operation (SC-34), the id is its field as sent. Not looked up, so on to step 3 as before: an input that names no id; one that names it at a past major, whose id is read once the input has gone forward (with its case); an input that is no JSON value (a date, a map), which no request recorded (`zz.is_a_json_value`). At a past major (PER-17) the steps back read the context of the leaf's point (A, 17 Nov 2026, SC-12): a recorded fact is answered after the nodes, walked, and a walk that closes answers its closing, so there a hat revoked meanwhile reads `not_found`; `identity_already_used` crosses with no walk. Scenario on the oracle, `scenarios/retry.test.ts`: on Thursday Gianni books the ladder for Friday through a leaf over `reserve_a_tool` whose input keeps the business's window (a tree the test wires, as SC-29: no leaf of Shed books); on Friday his retry reads the booking, one fact recorded, while the same request under a new operation is `invalid_request`; another fingerprint under the operation, an undeclared field included, `identity_already_used`, traced; his card expired, or his hat of member revoked, meanwhile: a new request `refused` or `not_found`, his retry the booking; from his old phone at 1.2.0, the loan in the words of 1.x, `not_found` once the hat is revoked, the loan at 2.0.0. Rejected: the leaf reading the adapter's lookup through the wiring (the outcome as the port gives it, with the parts its branch composed, is the port's); the lookup in the port's type (above); a plan with no branches executed (a rejection standing for "nothing recorded", and a writer's step for a read). At the business's port too [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026 (the go: DOM-11 applied, no new scenario); Q, C, 19 Nov 2026 (the build)] (`command_port.ts`, `step_port.ts`, `executed`): found here, the port parsed its input before its duplicate branch, so a caller that is not a leaf, a reaction delivered again or the executor returning after its input's window over `now` moved, read `invalid_request` and went to a dead letter while its fact was there. Now the port looks its operation up first, as the leaf asks it, then parses (DOM-11, "Where the duplicate branch runs"); the branch in the adapter's step stays, for two tries racing. Scenario on the oracle, `retry.test.ts`: on Thursday the desk books the ladder for Gianni through the harness's entry, and on Friday its retry reads the booking, another input under the operation `identity_already_used`, a new operation `invalid_request`; a leaf's operation met at the port is another request (its fingerprint is over the leaf's path and input); a step's port reads its fact, and an undeclared field is `identity_already_used`, before its schema; a reaction the test wires over `reserve_a_tool` (no reaction of Shed books: who books the ladder gets the drill the same day), its consumer fallen before saving its position on Thursday, delivered again on Friday, reads the drill's booking, no dead letter. With the ports' lookup off, these three cases fail.
  - The events of a leaf are its own, how the nodes of its path may close it, and the base events, which no leaf declares, all terminal (D106, D75.1): `invalid_request`, with a formal reason (`not_a_leaf`, `input_not_in_schema`, D106.3), from the library at the entry of the tree; `identity_already_used`, command leaves only, from the duplicate branch of the command port (DOM-11, D79.4); `version_not_supported`, from the interface, with the contract version (deferred as an improvement, A, 13 Nov 2026; settled with its scenario on 17 Nov 2026, at the entry of the tree, and at runtime since part 6.4, PER-17). `not_found` and `refused` are the nodes' (PER-14), not base events (D72). "Attesa scaduta" (D74.2) and "non ancora aggiornato" (D72.4) come with their cases. [Q, 13 Nov 2026]
  - The hat (step 2) is verified by the node at the top of the persona, as a perimeter (PER-7): it fails with "not found", after "invalid request", which tells only what the contract, public, tells. The interface checks the contract version only (V13). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (A)]
  - **The kernel's routing, between 1 and 2** (EXT-6, F8) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: a request to an extension not installed, not active or not offered to the bond of the `Caller` is not answered at all, the node stays silent, before the gate, so that no event tells the extension is there; a request to the kernel from a bonded face is never silent (toward a face with no bond the node is, F8); a response is no request and enters no persona (F7). The numbers of the checks above stay, as the rules cite them. Then, for an extension's leaf, the gate of its surface and its leaf and input checked by the kernel on the manifest's surface, before the kernel's node (EXT-6), so that 2 and 3 keep their order; each silence goes to the kernel's trace with its case (EXT-6). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
- **PER-6** A request lands on the root of the persona and invokes a leaf; internal nodes group and accumulate, invoking one is "invalid request". Every wearer gets the same structure. Constraints only accumulate going down. D77, D105
  - A leaf is a use case of the persona, always in the full form, nothing derived from the business (`command_use_case_over({ use_case, input, creator, events, projection })` in Shed, a mechanism of no kind: the library does not know it is a persona's, LIB-1): the business use case it invokes, conformist (D36); the persona's own input, in its own values; the creator that turns it into the business's input; the persona's own events, a closed set (D72.1); the projection of the business's outcome, a function per branch and one for the rejection, exhaustive, so that a new branch in the business does not compile in the persona (D45). The surface is the persona's, as GR-3 isolates the business domains, even at the cost of redundancy: the business has one version and the versions coexist in the persona (PER-17, D75.6), which can keep its contract only if no part of it is the business's. A change in the business breaks the creator or the projection, not the surface. The projected outcome is the first event and a terminal one (D72.3). The builder is apart from the business's (`command_use_case`) because its parts are others. Sugar of the library (a scaffold of the full form written once by the generator) is deferred: evaluated later. [Q, 12 Nov 2026]
  - The tree is made of folders (A, 12 Nov 2026 (2); beyond the paper, which keeps one auth read before a pure descent, D77.3, and is silent on how the bound values reach a leaf). A folder may hold a node (`node`, from `_scope_node.ts`, at most one per folder, in any file), which the library meets as it walks a use case's path down, whatever the tree (persona or business, D105). A node declares the context it takes and the context it gives, typed: the entry gives the top node its context (for a persona the interface, with the `Caller`, PER-4; for a tree invoked by a leaf, the leaf gives what the invoked root asks, the `Caller`'s id, D7, the time, the operation id, never its own context: the business gets the persona's values as input, D33, D85, D105.1); each node extends the context, never rewrites it (D105.2: a child that rebinds the subject would leave its parent's perimeter); every construct below reads it. A node may run lookups (PER-9), its I/O, then its conditions (perimeter or limit, PER-14) and its bindings, pure, so that it can be mounted at several points (D78.6) and tested; never a query, never a read of business state, which goes into the composed condition, atomic (D32, D52, D80.4). A node above the personas (`persona/`) may give context and constraints, never leaves: a leaf above the hats would wear none (PER-7), and a node that offers use cases to its children is the tree of personas D78 discards (a role hierarchy; capabilities growing up mixed with constraints growing down; a child that cannot restrict what it inherits). Decided in principle; the first node is Marta's (`persona/member/`, the `member_id` from auth), with its case. [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - The context (A, 12 Nov 2026 (4)). The library's `RootContext` holds what its mechanisms read: `now`, one instant per request from a trusted frame (D99.4), read alike by the library (the envelope, the outbox, the window of DOM-11, when their cases come) and by the creators. The application extends it (declaration merging) with what every root has and every invocation carries over (the `Caller`'s id of D7, when a case asks: none in Shed); what only one tree's entry gives (the `Caller`, PER-4, its verified fields, the hat) goes in the context its root node takes: the library knows no `Caller`. One context per point of the tree, the same for every construct below it (commands, queries, use cases); a creator gets the context of its point from the builders bound there (`here`, DOM-25), with no annotation; an annotation narrower than the context is allowed, one that asks for more does not compile. The library's own data of a request go beside the context, by kind: a command gets `OperationContext` (its operation, id and scope, DOM-11: the entry composes the scope, `Caller` + hat, opaque to the library), a read its position (`from`). A lookup gets its key only, which the node computes: it is a component of another tree (auth's), which the node invokes as a leaf invokes the business, and the context does not cross an invocation (D105.1); one key, one value, so that any adapter serves it (a token on the client, a table on the server, D38, D43) and no decision moves into auth (PER-9). A leaf that invokes gives the invoked root the `RootContext`, copied, and the same operation. `CommandContext` and its placeholder `uid` are gone. [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (4)]
  - The node, Marta's (`persona/member/node.ts`). A folder with a node has two points: the one above it, whose builder of the node `_scope_node.ts` gives (`node`), and the context the node gives, `_scope.ts`, from which the scope of the leaves takes its builders (`_scope_usecase.ts`); so no import goes round (`node.ts` → `_scope_node.ts`; `_scope_usecase.ts` → `_scope.ts` → `_node.ts` → `node.ts`). The node is declared in one argument, in the order of its run, and reads as sentences with its name as the subject (A, 13 Nov 2026): `takes`, only at the top of a tree, what the entry gives (the `Caller`, verified, PER-4: in Shed the persona base's); what it `looks_up`, each lookup named by the value it finds (`the_member_card_of_the_caller`), run `of` a key from the context, an `Answer` (D30: `the_account_of_the_caller`, not known for a session, runs no lookup), typed as parsed so that another identity does not compile, with `when_not_known: 'not_found'`, a perimeter (D87.5), so that the value goes on known (the value "not known" with its case); its perimeters, as what it `is_found_only_if` (`the_caller_wears_the_hat_of_member`), failing with `not_found`, with no reason; its limits, as what it `is_refused_unless`, each named by what must hold, its name the reason, all evaluated; what it `gives`, its bindings. The keys say what happens, as LIB-1 names a mechanism by what it does; perimeter and limit, the paper's words, stay in the builder's JSDoc. Every part may be left out. [Q, 13 Nov 2026: until then `lookups` with `key`, `limits`, every part written] A node is a function of the context, not of the request: it sees no input, being above every leaf of its folder. A node closes a request with a base event of every leaf below it, terminal, which no leaf declares (`NodeEvent`, PER-14, D80): `not_found`, with no reason, or `refused`, with the name of every failed limit. The leaf reads what the node gives (`context.card`, the card whole) and gives its number to the business as input (D33). [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (4)]
  - A limit that holds for one leaf and not for every leaf of its folder is the leaf's (A, 13 Nov 2026 (2)): an expired card blocks borrowing, not reading one's loans nor returning a tool, so `the_member_card_is_valid_today` is `borrow_a_tool`'s, and `member` keeps its perimeters only (the hat, a card) and gives the card whole, its last day included. A leaf (`command_use_case_over`) declares what it `is_refused_unless`, as a node does: pure, over the context of its point and the input (PER-5.5), run after the nodes of its path and before the creator; every limit is evaluated, the failed ones close the request with `refused`, their names the reasons, in the leaf's union. A perimeter of the leaf, and a limit on a read leaf, come with their cases (a limit on a read leaf came with SC-32, PER-18). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - The ports of a tree (`tree_ports`, a mechanism of no kind): for each leaf, what the entry gives the node at the top (in Shed the persona base's: the `Caller`, with `now`) and, for a command, the operation, and the leaf's input in (for a read leaf that streams, a position to resume from, PER-18; a stream over a port that does not stream is a composition fault at wiring, CFG-2); its events out, typed with their union: its own, parsed; how the nodes of its path may close it; the base events (PER-5). A point of the tree carries how the nodes above it may close a request (`closes`), as it carries their context: the generator writes `<node>_closes` beside `<node>_context` in `_node.ts`, and the builders of `_scope.ts` take both (`builders_at<context, closes>`, `closes` left out when `never`, the default); a node adds `not_found` if it looks up or holds perimeters, `refused` with the names of its limits if it has some, nothing otherwise; a leaf adds `refused` with the names of its own limits. Marta's `borrow_a_tool` may close with `not_found` (from `member`) and `refused` (`the_member_card_is_valid_today`, its own), the keeper's `retire_a_tool` with `not_found` only; `visitor`'s leaves with neither, since the persona base cannot fail. The ports the tree invokes (use cases, lookups) the library finds by path; the configurator wires them (Shed, `configurator.ts`). [Q, 13 Nov 2026]
  - **The ports of a tree, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 6.1; PER-5, PER-6, PER-14, DOM-11, D100, D105.1, D106): per request, in the order of PER-5, the gate and the version's major pick the leaf (PER-17, "The versions of a surface, at runtime", since part 6.4); the leaf's input is checked against its schema before any node (`invalid_request`, `input_not_in_schema`), so that Marta, without the keeper's hat, sending a malformed request reads `invalid_request`, which the public contract tells, never whether the keeper's tree knows her (PER-7); so a leaf's input, declared as a function of the context, reads what the entry gives every tree (`RootContext`, and a command's operation's scope: its id since part 6.2 not, which a leaf like the workshop's reads from the input itself, SC-34), never what a node gives: the type of `command_use_case_over`'s and `read_use_case_over`'s input says so (DOM-21: a window over `now`). Then the nodes of the leaf's path, outer first, each in its order: the top's `takes`, parsed from what the entry gives (one it does not parse is the entry's fault, thrown: the interface verified it); its lookups, each `of` a key from the context, a key or a value not known closing with `not_found`, a perimeter (D87.5); its perimeters (`not_found`, no reason, the first that fails); its limits, all evaluated (`refused`, every failed one named, PER-14); what it gives, extending the context. Then the leaf's own limits (PER-5.5), likewise. Then the creator, over the context of the leaf's point and, for a command, its operation; the use case below gets the root's context alone, copied (`now`, `time_zone`, D105.1), and for a command the operation, the correlation the entry gives (the client's or its own, DOM-10) and the fingerprint the library computes here, over the leaf's path and the input as sent (LIB-11). The outcome comes back in the leaf's words: a branch by its projection, from its own part's payload and the parts it composed; the rejection by its projection; `identity_already_used`, and an input the use case below does not take, as base events, unprojected; each projected event parsed by its schema, one not in it the projection's fault, thrown. A read leaf that does not stream reads by the pages of the port below (SC-22), one page with every entry where its creator binds no size, each entry projected and parsed by its own entry, the leaf's cursor given back to the port below and `cursor_not_in_schema` passed through. The ports the tree invokes are found by the component a leaf or a node names: every port the library builds (a use case's, a lookup's) carries, out of sight, the component it serves and its path, so that the configurator gives them as their adapters give them, by the folder of what they serve (`Invoked`), and the tree needs no registry of the domains it invokes. At wiring (CFG-2), every fault in one message: a leaf or the top not in the registry; a leaf given under another name than its path's, or not below the top; a node below the top that takes, or one that binds or requires (its business's command port runs it, D105); a port the library did not build, or given in another folder than its own; a use case or a lookup no port given serves. A leaf's `operation` since part 6.2 (PER-4, "An actor from outside"); a leaf's `follows` (DOM-3, "A leaf's wait, at runtime"), a read leaf's `stream` (PER-18, "A read leaf's stream, at runtime") and its `actions` (PER-15, "The actions of a read, at runtime", past the checks of PER-5, so that a limit on a read refuses before them, SC-32) since part 6.3. Since part 6.2 every request answered with no fact leaves an entry in the tree's trace (PER-14, "The trace of a tree, at runtime"). Scenarios on the oracle: SC-23 (Luca retires a tool as keeper, every branch and the rejection in his words; the correlation he sent in his fact), SC-25 (the visitor's catalogue the same for a session, an account, a key; the keeper's hat a perimeter), SC-26 (`invalid_request` before the nodes; a retry with its fields in another order the first outcome again, the same operation for another tool `identity_already_used`; a page too long, a forged cursor), SC-14 (the visitor's entries without the keeper's notes, in pages, resumed from a cursor), SC-24's borrowing half (Marta's card from auth given down; an expired card `refused`, nothing recorded; no member's hat, or no card, `not_found`). Rejected: the input checked after the nodes, where a schema could read what they give (a caller without the hat would learn from `not_found` before `invalid_request` that the request was well formed, against PER-5's order); the business registries given to `tree_ports` to find the ports by path (the configurator would give each tree every registry it invokes, beside the ports that already know theirs); the ports looked up by the folder and the name under which the configurator gives them (a use case below a business node, `extend_the_loan`, is in `business/loans/an_ongoing_loan`, under the domain's ports); the whole context of the leaf given to the use case below (the `Caller` and what the nodes give would reach the business, D105.1, D33).
  - **A mounted folder** (EXT-11) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: the folders of an extension's personas hang below the kernel's node at the top of each persona (EXT-1); their point takes the context that node gives, as the manifest lets the extension see it, and no node of theirs takes (D78.6, D105.2: a mount inherits the constraints of where it sits, and binds nothing again). On the kernel's side [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (EXT-6, EXT-11): the kernel's tree ports serve, below its node at the top of a persona, the leaves of an extension mounted there, given at wiring as data from the manifest's surface (paths, majors, inputs read into schemas, events) with the port that invokes them through the boundary (EXT-2): the gate per surface (EXT-5), the leaf and its input checked on the surface, the kernel's nodes, then the invocation with the context the nodes give, its events parsed by the surface (EXT-15) and given in the leaf's words, the closings of the nodes and the base events as for any leaf. A leaf mounted from outside is in no registry of the kernel's, where a leaf not in the registry is a wiring fault ("The ports of a tree, at runtime"), so it is given beside the registry: a mechanism of no kind, no new builder, asked of the library by step 12.3. **At runtime** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 12.3). The mounted side: `tree_ports` takes as its top either the node at the top or a mounted folder's point, `zz.Mounted` (`{ mounted, context }`: the folder's path and the schema of its context), the data the generator writes into the point's `_node.ts` (EXT-11); the entry's context is parsed by that schema, as a top node's `takes`, with no node to take it, and the nodes below run as below any top; at wiring, a node of the registry that takes is a fault ("below the mounted folder …, whose context is given from outside"), and a leaf not below the folder. `zz.ContextOf` and `zz.EntryOf` read a `Mounted` as a node's. The side of the tree it hangs in: `zz.mounted_leaf_ports({ registry, top, at, name })({ invoked, trace, surface, invokes, outside_the_surface })`, one port for the leaves mounted below the folder `at` under a name (the trace's path `<at>/<name>/<leaf>`): the surface (`zz.MountedSurface`: the version it speaks, by major its leaves by path, each `command` or `read`, its input's schema, its events' schemas, whole events), the port that invokes a leaf (`zz.MountedInvocation`: the leaf, the version, the context the nodes give, a command's operation, the correlation, the input parsed), and the port that receives an event the surface does not type (`zz.OutsideTheSurface`, EXT-15: the unit's fault, the kernel's trace). Per request: the surface's gate (`version_not_supported` with its version and oldest major), `not_a_leaf`, a command's operation from the entry, the input (`input_not_in_schema`), then the nodes from the top down to `at` (`not_found`, `refused`), each closing in the tree's trace, then the invocation, its events given in order as the surface parses them. At wiring: the top not in the registry, `at` not below it, a node there that takes, binds or requires, a lookup unserved, a surface whose version's major has no leaves. Tests: `test/library/mounted.test.ts`, on Shed (Marta's tree mounted at `persona/member`, its card still looked up below; leaves of another unit below her node: her card in the invocation's context, Luca's `not_found` and his malformed input `invalid_request` before the node, the surface's gate, an event outside the surface set aside). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] the form of the second: a function of its own beside `tree_ports`, one port per mount, the leaf named in the request (`{ leaf, input }`); its port's type is `zz.PortByPath` (its events `zz.EventByPath`), one name for every port that takes the leaf by its path, not a type named for the mount (the kernel's own tree by path has the same shape). Rejected: a key `mounted` on `tree_ports`' wiring, its ports beside the leaves' (`TreePorts` is typed by the registry's leaves, and a name of a mount could meet a leaf's); a port per mounted leaf (the surface is data read at composition, its leaves unknown to the types).
- **PER-7** One hat per request. The hat is in the address of the use case — for a letter, the persona it invokes — and is a claim verified with auth, not a permission. D27, D78
  - The node at the top of each persona verifies its own hat, as a fixed datum, with no reading of the path: it looks up the hats of the caller's account (`the_hats_of_an_account`) and holds a perimeter (`the_caller_wears_the_hat_of_keeper`); a caller without the hat, or with no account, gets `not_found` (D87.5). A node above the personas that read the path would decide for its children, the tree of personas D78 discards. Who may wear a hat is its node: `visitor` has none, so anyone wears it, signed in or not (D78.7 names who has not signed in; the paper is silent on the others); its tree reads nothing of who wears it. Divergence V13. [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (A)]
- **PER-8** Access values never come from the peer; an undeclared field never enters. D33, D75
- **PER-9** Auth answers with data, never decisions. It may have commands; its read ports are implemented by adapters (in q-, by the read use cases of `bonds`, GR-4, and of `extensions`, which tell the extensions offered to a bond, EXT-6 [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]). D29, D43, D98
  - In Shed auth is a domain of kind auth, `auth/accounts/` (D35). Its read use cases are lookups (`lookup`: one key, one value or "not known", D30); its door exposes them with its identities; its data are its adapters' (D43), with no command until a case asks (D98); it imports no other domain. The hats of an account (`the_hats_of_an_account`), not "can it wear X?": a datum, which the node of a persona compares with its hat (PER-7), not a decision; and a lookup has one key and one value. One lookup per hat that has values (`the_member_card_of_an_account`), not a generic one. The account, the `Caller`'s id, and the member card are distinct identities: the member of a loan is a value of auth (PER-1), which the persona reads and gives the business as input (D33), as data; with one identity, `loans` would key its loans with auth's identities (PER-10). [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (4)]
  - Auth's questions are lookups (`lookup`, a construct of no kind, LIB-1): one key in (the `Caller`'s id, a hat), one value out or "not known" (absence is a value, D30), no list, no stream: "can the `Caller` wear X?", "which values?" (D43). A query, with its selection, order, identity and stream, is too much for a value by key. Where the value comes from is the adapter's: a token that carries the id and the role (no I/O), a store, an identity provider (D43); the node declares what it needs, so the tree does not change with the interface (D38, D76). Lookups are auth's only, a rule of the architecture: a stale access lets through one request made an instant before a revocation, legitimate when it was made; a stale state breaks an invariant for good (two loans of one drill). [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (2)]
- **PER-10** The interface uses auth to verify the hat; the persona uses auth for its values; the business never knows auth. D43, D77
- **PER-11** The introduction with a ticket is the paper's registration: a command of `unknown` that invokes the command of `bonds` creating the bond (in the paper, a command of auth: V8); its fact links the sender's key to the new bond. The sender is `known` from its next letter. D78, D98 [Q]
- **PER-12** Internal entries (reactions, effect executor) are not personas and wear no hat. D88, D91
- **PER-13** Request data lives in the frames of the envelope stack, written by each layer crossed; a datum is worth what its layer is worth. Only frames written by my node feed access constraints. The letter's declared time is a claim; the relay's arrival time is written by the relay and is annotated, not trusted; my node's collect time is trusted. D99 [Q]
- **PER-14** Perimeter or limit, declared by the node. A perimeter that fails gives "not found" without a reason (and filters reads); a limit that fails gives an explicit reason. This hides from the peer; the local user is informed (F6). D44, D80
  - **The trace of a tree, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 6.2; F6, D54, D56, D79.2, D106.3): every request a tree's ports answer with no fact leaves an entry in the tree's trace, a port the configurator gives `tree_ports` beside the fact log (`zz.Trace`), and the caller reads only its leaf's words: a perimeter hides from the caller, the trace keeps it. The entry (`zz.Traced`) is an event with the paper's envelope as far as the library holds it (D54, D56): the leaf by its path (the hat its segment); what the entry gave, the `Caller` and `now`, who asked and when; the version the message carried; a command's operation as far as the request named it (its scope alone when the input that names its id does not parse, SC-34) and its correlation; the input as sent, the letter as it came; the event the caller read; and why (`zz.TraceReason`), by the check of PER-5 that closed it: `the_gate` (the window the caller read, since part 6.4, PER-17), `not_a_leaf` (the major with no leaf at the path), `the_input` (each field and rule its schema refused, D106.3, at a past major the request's or a later major's it went forward to), `a_lookup` (every lookup of the node whose key or value was not known), `a_perimeter` (the first that fails, the order of the run), `limits` (every failed one, at the path of the node or of the leaf that holds them), `the_business` (a command's rejection with every predicate it names, the path's once with no branch, D105.4; `identity_already_used`; an input or a cursor the use case below does not take). The library hands the entry over as it answers and waits for nothing, so that no answer depends on it: where it is kept, for how long and whether it outlives a fall are the adapter's (eventual, without guarantees, D54: the fact counts, never the trace); one that throws is the adapter's fault, as any port's. Only what records no fact is traced: an applied outcome is its fact, and a read answered is no rejection (tracing reads is a deployment's choice, D66.1, with its case); an internal entry's rejection is a dead letter (DOM-14, DOM-16). A retry rejected again is another entry under the same operation (D79.2: the rejection stays in the trace). The trace never leaves the node (PER-18); its reader is the user of the node (F6), whose screens come with `user` (step 15 of the plan). Required at wiring: a tree with no trace would lose its rejections in silence. Scenarios on the oracle: Giorgio, a member card but only the keeper's hat, borrows the drill and reads `not_found`, the trace `a_perimeter` at `persona/member/node/member`, `the_caller_wears_the_hat_of_member` (SC-24); Gianni, a member with no card, `a_lookup`, the card's value not known; Luca's malformed request, the field and the rule (SC-26); Marta asks to keep the ladder Gianni booked and reads `someone_booked_the_tool`, Gianni asks to keep Marta's drill and reads `the_loan_cannot_be_extended`, the trace naming `the_loan_is_of_this_member` (SC-31); her shelf refused on 1 April, `limits` at the leaf (SC-32); Rossi's N-88 with another job, `identity_already_used`, and a person on the workshop's leaf, the key not known (SC-34). Rejected: the trace optional at wiring (a deployment that forgets it loses its rejections in silence, F6); the trace awaited before the answer (the answer would depend on it, and a slow store would hold every rejection back); successes traced too (a fact already tells them; reads are the deployment's, with their case); zod's error as it is (a library's type in a contract of the library, and the input held twice); every perimeter of a node evaluated for the trace (the run stops at the first, as the caller's answer does, and the order is the author's); the trace written by each persona's own code (the paper has the persona write it, D54; in q- the library walks the tree, a mechanism of no kind, LIB-1).
- **PER-15** Capabilities on three levels, each restricting the previous: the persona's tree, the values from auth, the applicability on one resource. In the UI they are only UX. D25, D47, D77
  - **An action of a read** (SC-33) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (2); A for the shape]: the applicability on one resource, per entry of a read (D47.2, D51.1). A read leaf lists, by names of its own, command leaves of its folder (`actions: { bring_it_back: return_a_tool, keep_it_longer: extend_your_loan }`, D47.4), whose nodes above are its own, already passed; its creator binds each by part, as the page (`{ query, actions: { keep_it_longer: (loan) => ({ loan_id: loan.id, … }) } }`), from an entry of the query to the leaf's input, the fields the caller gives marked `zz.given_by_the_caller`, explicit so that a field forgotten does not become not known unannounced (A). The applicability is a field of the entry under the action's name (`zz.Applicability`: the value composed, the branch when the branches tell it, D51.5, every leaf with whether it holds, as a failure names it, the leaf's limits), which the projection writes in the persona's words; an action named as a field of the entry does not compile, as a calculation. The library runs, per entry, the same pass as the write (D51.1): the leaf's limits, its creator, the nodes of the business's path (`binds`, `requires`), its command's creator; a leaf whose parameters hold the mark is not known (D51.3); a creator binds and does not read the mark (a choice on the input is a branch's, DOM-5), one that reads it leaves every leaf of the command not known (D51.4, prudent), a limit its own value; the library composes in strong Kleene logic, the limits in AND outside (D105), and N lives only here (D51.6). The adapter evaluates the leaves known, every one, in the snapshot of the read, with its commands' own fragments (`applicability`, ADP-8, an optional capability, D110, declared query by query as the streams, since whether an evaluation per entry is cheap depends on the technology and the data, LIB-8 [Q, A, 18 Nov 2026 (8)]), as one more field of the planned query (`PlannedLeaves`), so the pages and the streams meet fields alone: Gianni books the drill while Marta reads, and the row of L7, untouched by the fact, is `changed` (SC-19). A leaf that lists actions over a port with none is a fault at wiring (CFG-2). The business's port gives the same entries with `with_actions`. Scenario: on Friday 13 November Marta reads her loans: bring it back is possible on every row, not known before she scans the tool she brings (`the_requested_loan_is_of_the_tool_brought_back`); keep it longer is possible for the drill, not for the ladder (Gianni booked it for Thursday: told apart, as when refused, SC-31) nor for the sander (past its due date); with her card expired, no row (her limit, with its reason, D80). Rejected: the leaf and its binding together in `actions` (the context given twice, and the value the leaf passes to what it invokes outside its creator); omitting the caller's fields (implicit); deriving N from the creators (opaque functions); the events of the command leaf as the row's prediction (more library, and the leaves' values lost). Actions of another domain's commands, `applicabile`/`possibile` as selection filters (D51.2) and one statement for all the rows in SQL come with their cases.
  - **The actions of a read, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 6.3; D47.2, D51.1, D51.3-6, D105, D110, ADP-8, CFG-2): a read use case's port has `with_actions` where its adapter declares the query's `applicability` (the fragments of its domain's command predicates): the same port again, its entries with each action's applicability under its name, read whole, in pages and in its stream alike (the leaves are one more field of the planned query, so a fact that touches no entry and changes a leaf's value gives `changed`, SC-19). Per entry, the library runs the same pass as the write: the action's input from the context of the read and the entry, parsed as the read gives it; each node of the command's path, its target from that input (D105.2) and its requirement bound by its creator; the command's input, its fields given by the caller left out of its schema and given back as the mark; its creator, with no operation (the caller mints it later, DOM-12); each branch's condition bound, leaf by leaf, by the leaf's own schema, a leaf whose parameters hold the mark not known (D51.3). The leaves known, every one of every branch and of the path (no short-circuit, D51), go to the adapter as its fragments over their parameters (`PlannedLeaves`), evaluated on the state of the snapshot and written in order under a field no query can name (`@leaves`, taken out again before the entry is parsed). The library composes in strong Kleene logic (D51.4): AND false if one is, else not known if one is; OR true if one is, else not known; NOT keeps not known; the value is the path's conditions and the branches' OR in AND (D105); the branch the first that holds with every one before it false (D51.5); every leaf with whether it holds where it stands, a leaf under NOT holding when false, as a rejection names a failure (DOM-6), so that one function of the persona reads a row and a rejection (`only_a_booking_stands_in_the_way`, SC-31). A pass whose input holds a mark and fails on the way (a creator that read the mark and gave a value no schema parses, a target given by the caller) leaves the action not known with no leaves (D51.4, prudent); one with no mark that fails is a fault, thrown. A read leaf's actions go through it: the read's creator binds each action's leaf input from an entry (`actions`, by part), the command leaf's creator turns it into its business input over the read's point, which the leaves of one folder share; then each entry takes the command leaf's limits over that point and its input, each its own value, in AND outside the business's (D105), before the projection writes the row. A leaf that lists actions over a port with no `with_actions` stops at wiring (CFG-2). `with_actions` infers each action's use case by its name, so that its input is typed by its own command (the type given at step 5 inferred none). Not met in Shed, so built and not observed: an action whose command composes or is a step (thrown, with their cases, LIB-3). Scenario on the oracle (SC-33): on Friday 13 November Marta's "my loans": bring each back, not known before she scans the tool (`the_loan_is_of_this_tool` not known), keep the drill longer, the ladder `someone_booked_the_tool` (the booking the one leaf that does not hold), the sander, past its due day, `not_this_loan`; her card expired, `your_card_is_not_valid` on every row while she may still bring each back; Gianni books the drill while she reads, and the row of L7 comes as `fact` with keep it longer no more. With it, the persona's side of SC-15 ("my loans" live, a resume inclusive), SC-16 (`your_loan_is_overdue` with the row) and SC-24's reading half (her loans through her card, with no limit of the card). Rejected: a leaf's applicability computed by running the command port's planning with a fake operation (a creator that names an instance by its operation, `identity_of`, would give a value that looks known); every leaf with a mark parsed as not known by catching its schema's error (a value a creator computed from the mark would pass as a leaf known false); the limits given to the business port (a business read knows no leaf, PER-6).
- **PER-16** An opaque value grants no permission; whoever reads it validates it again. D57 (exception: the collect secret, V5)
- **PER-17** The contract version is the version of the persona's surface. A compatibility window is declared; an incompatible change is expand-contract inside the persona tree, readers first. This holds between peers released independently. D75, D101
  - The gate (`version_not_supported`, from the interface, with the version; per persona, two constants in its tree, the window declared by the tree, an integer) is deferred as an improvement (A, 13 Nov 2026, confirmed 13 Nov 2026 (2)): it stands in front of everything and nothing depends on it. [17 Nov 2026, A: settled with its scenario, below; the window is the tree's majors, the integer dropped] What may reshape the tree is how two versions of a leaf live in it (D72.7: inside the window the leaf is sought at the version the message carries): whether the version enters the path (immutable, DOM-24), a folder per version, or one leaf with two projections. Settled in Shed before the library steps of the plan, with its scenario (Shed's README, step 10). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - Two versions of a leaf (A, 16 Nov 2026 (7)): a persona is a domain with one version, semver, its contract (`persona/member/version.ts`, D75.6), and a past major is a folder of it outside the path (`persona/member/1/`, DOM-22, DOM-25), which the message's version selects (D72.7). A leaf of a past major is over the leaf of the next major, never over the business (`earlier(next.borrow_a_tool)({ input, to_next, events, from_next })`; for a read leaf `entry`, `from_next` from the next entry, and the stream's own events): its input goes forward, what the next leaf gives comes back in its words, one step per event, exhaustive, so that an event the next major adds does not compile there; the nodes' closings and the base events cross as they are; a leaf left unchanged is delegated (`delegate(next.return_a_tool)`). The limits, the creator and the projection of the business live in the current leaf alone, and the business, which has one version, changes under it alone: a folder once released never changes, as for the facts. Scenario: `member` 2.0.0 tells a loan due on a day (`due_day`); on 5 Nov Gianni's new phone borrows the ladder at 2.0.0 and reads `due_day` 19 Nov, Marta's old phone borrows the drill at 1.4.0 and reads `due_date` 19 Nov at 23:59:59.999Z, as its "my loans" does: the deadline told as 1.x tells it, the last instant of the day by whose end the tool comes back, the member's choice for its old clients, not an invention (D101.4 binds the facts' translations). Weighed and set aside (A): a leaf of the past major over the business (the limit and the creator twice, and every change of the business reopens the past major); one leaf with a projection per major (no folder shows the version). Left: the window and how the library finds the leaf at the message's version (the gate, points 4 and 6 of Shed's step 10), the identity field of a past entry checked by type (point 5). [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (7)]
  - The version in the message, and the leaf it picks (A, 17 Nov 2026): the client writes into every message the version of its persona it was built at, whole (`1.4.0`), beside the path, never in the input (the leaf's schema) nor in the path (D75); the entry gives it to the tree with the operation and the correlation (`zz.VersionContext`), and no creator or projection sees it: to them the version is the leaf. Its major picks the leaf, its minor serves the gate. A claim, not a defense: a client may name any version and chooses only the words it is answered in, since every major ends in the current leaf, with its limits and its creator. The generator lists a persona's majors as a business domain's (`persona/member/_majors.ts`, DOM-22); the configurator gives them to the tree's ports beside its current leaves, by name as before, and a leaf has one port (`zz.LeafPort`), where the major of the context's version picks the registry and the library finds the leaf by its path; a delegated leaf is the next major's, a path a major has not is `not_a_leaf` (D106.3), a major out of the window goes to the gate. The port is typed by the version: a test client at `'1.4.0'` reads the events of 1.x, the generic entry, with any version, the union of the majors. Scenario: on 5 Nov Marta's old phone sends `borrow_a_tool` at `1.4.0` and reads `due_date`, Gianni's new one the same path at `2.0.0` and reads `due_day`. Weighed and set aside (A): only the major in the message (the gate could not tell a client at 2.1 from a server at 2.0); the version as an argument of its own (it is the entry's, as the operation and the correlation); ports by major (`member[1].borrow_a_tool`: the version in the context would repeat the index, and a leaf of `1/` forgotten in the configurator would answer `not_a_leaf` at runtime); a `tree_ports` per major (the delegated port of 1 needs 2's anyway, the top and the registry wired twice). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - The gate (A, 17 Nov 2026): `version_not_supported` is a base event of the tree's ports (`zz.VersionNotSupported`), as `invalid_request` (D106.3), at the entry of the tree, before any node, so that no lookup runs. The window is the tree's majors, its folders as the generator lists them (`_majors.ts`): no number kept beside them, which would have to follow them by hand (it replaces the integer of 13 Nov). The gate stops a major the tree has not, and the current major at a higher minor; the patch does not count. It tells the version the tree speaks and its oldest major (`{ current: '2.0.0', oldest: 1 }`), so that the client knows whether to update, speak a lower minor or wait. Scenario: Marta at `1.4.0` meets the leaf of 1; Gianni's app at `2.1.0`, which adds an optional `note` to `borrow_a_tool`, meets a server still at 2.0.0 and is stopped, not answered `invalid_request`; on 1 Mar `1/` is deleted and Paolo's phone at `1.2.0` is stopped; a client at `3.0.0` meeting an instance at 2.0.0 during a release is stopped. Weighed and set aside (A): a higher minor served when its message parses with the current schema (Gianni with `note` would read `invalid_request`, false for 2.1); the gate in the interface with a window of its own (a copy of the folders to keep in step). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - Expand-contract, readers first (A, 17 Nov 2026): the reader of a shape accepts a change before its writer makes it, so the CI rule of a minor goes by direction (DOM-24). What the tree reads (inputs; for a business domain its facts and effects) may only widen in a minor: every old shape parses with the new schema. What its clients read (the events and entries of a leaf) may only narrow: every shape it now gives parses with the old schema. Every other change is a major, with its folder: the expansion is the folder that keeps the old contract beside the new one, the contraction its deletion. Reads stay strict on both sides (DOM-21): no client drops a field it does not know, nothing is lost in silence (F6). Scenario: `member` 2.1.0 would add to `you_have_the_tool` an optional `pick_up_by`; Gianni's phone at 2.0.0, strict, could not read it from a server at 2.1.0; so it is a major, 3.0.0, and the leaf of `2/` drops the field (`from_next`). Weighed and set aside (A): clients that drop the output fields they do not know (fewer majors, data lost in silence); a server that answers at the client's minor (a folder per minor, set aside in part 1). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - Contracting too early (A, 17 Nov 2026): the types cannot see it, by design (the current knows no past). For a business domain the store tells: the adapter gives the distinct paths and versions it keeps among its facts and outbox entries (`zz.StoredVersions`), and at wiring (CFG-2) the library stops on one that no major reads, naming it, so that a release that deleted a folder too early does not start (D101 made checkable). For a persona there is no store: when to delete its folder is the persona's decision, by its clients still about, and the gate makes those left out see it. Scenario: in February `business/loans/1/` is deleted while Paolo's loan of 3 Nov and a wait of 1.x for 20 Feb are kept; the release stops at its start, not a stream in a loop, a wait stuck on 20 Feb, a rebuild halfway. Weighed and set aside (A): nothing beyond DOM-22 (the first reader that meets the version stops, late and in pieces). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - The identity of a past read leaf (A, 17 Nov 2026): a read leaf of a past major whose next streams declares, as the current leaf does (PER-18), the field of its own entry that names a row (`earlier(next.my_loans)({ …, stream: { identity: 'loan_id', events, from_next } })`), checked by type to hold the identity the next leaf's stream gives; `stream` is required when the next streams and absent when it does not. The port is `Streamed` for every major, by the current leaf, so a past entry without the field would otherwise compile with a stream whose events are none. The value is the client's to trust: the brand keeps another identity out, not another loan's (a claim of `from_next`, not a defense). Scenario: Marta's old phone at `1.4.0` keeps "my loans" open, and on 17 Nov `your_loan_is_overdue` reaches the row of L1 by its `loan_id`; had 1.x named it `id` and 2.0.0 renamed it, the leaf of `1/` declares `id`, and a row of 1.x with neither does not compile. Weighed and set aside (A): the next leaf's field, by name, checked in the past entry (a renamed identity, a major the folder exists for, could not be written); an error type at the port instead of `never` (the fault far from where it is made, the rename still impossible). [Q, A, 17 Nov 2026]
  - The steps of a past leaf read the context (A, 17 Nov 2026, SC-12): `to_next` and `from_next` of a leaf of a past major take the context of its point first, once, as a creator (`from_next: (context) => ({ you_have_the_tool: (payload) => … })`, `(context) => (entry) => …`), since they run at the request: Marta's phone at 1.4.0 reads her due day as its last instant in the shed's zone (`end_of(context.time_zone)(due_day)`). The step of a fact or an effect stays over its payload alone, stored data that every reader translates alike (DOM-22). Set aside (A): the context in each mapping (`you_have_the_tool: (context) => (payload) => …`).
  - **The versions of a surface, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 6.4; PER-5, D72.7, D75.6, D106.3, CFG-2): the ports of a tree serve every major of its window, one port per current leaf (`LeafPort`). The window is the majors the configurator gives (`_majors.ts`), the version the tree speaks its current registry's `version.ts`'s, the oldest major the lowest folder: no number kept beside them. Per request, before any node: the version parsed (one that is no semver is the entry's fault, thrown); the gate, `version_not_supported` with `{ current, oldest }`, for a major the window has not or the current major at a higher minor, the patch not counted, a past major at any minor served (its folder is its last minor); the leaf at the leaf's path in the major's registry, `not_a_leaf` where it has none (`extend_your_loan`, the current major's alone), and the steps from it to the current leaf (`earlier` a step over its next major's leaf, `delegate` the next's itself, no step). Then the input in the schema of the major's leaf (PER-5.3, before any node, as at the current major); the nodes of the path, the same at every major (a past major's folder holds none, DOM-25); the input forward, step by step, each `to_next` over the context of the leaf's point, taken once, and the next major's schema, built over what the entry gives (DOM-21), an input a later major's schema refuses (a static rule over `now`) `invalid_request`, with each field and rule in the trace; for a leaf whose input names its operation (SC-34), its id read from the current leaf's input, once forward; the current leaf's limits, creator and projection, the use case below as at the current major. Back, each event of the current leaf's own, an entry, an event of its stream's own, step by step from the major nearest the current, each `from_next` over the point's context, taken once, and parsed by that major's schema (one not in it the past leaf's fault, thrown); a stream event keeps its position, identity and entry (the entry stepped back as every entry); the nodes' closings and the base events cross as they are, the events a command leaf follows (`follows`) step back as its own, and the actions of a read are the current leaf's, each past entry keeping of them what its `from_next` writes. The fingerprint is over the leaf's path and the input as sent, at the client's major (LIB-11): a retry from a phone updated between the send and the retry, the same input under the same operation, is the duplicate branch, in the words of its new version (SC-28); one that sends another shape meets `identity_already_used`, a declared limit. The gate and `not_a_leaf` answer with no fact and go to the trace (PER-14: `the_gate` with the window, `not_a_leaf` with the major), so that the user of the node sees a peer stopped (F6). At wiring (CFG-2), every fault in one message with the others: majors that do not hold one current registry declaring its version under its major; a leaf given that is not the current major's at its path (`my_loans` of `1/` in place of the current one, which the types let through, leaves being structural: only the identity of the object in the current registry tells; Shed's README § Left by Shed); a past major's leaf at the path that does not lead to the current leaf given. Scenarios on the oracle: SC-28 (on 5 Nov Gianni at `2.0.0` borrows the ladder and reads `due_day` 19 Nov, Marta's old phone at `1.4.0` the drill and reads `due_date` 19 Nov at 23:59:59.999 in Toronto, one business, one version, her loan due on a day; her "my loans" in each version's words; her return, delegated, as 2.0.0 tells it; her updated phone's retry under the same operation, the same fact told at 2.0.0; a rejection told in 1.x's words and traced, a node's `not_found` as it is, `extend_your_loan` at 1.x `not_a_leaf`), SC-29 (Gianni's app at `2.1.0` on 2.0.0 stopped, Luca with no card stopped by the gate too, before any lookup, both in the trace; `2.0.7` served; `3.0.0` during a release stopped; Paolo at `1.2.0` served the day before `1/` is deleted, stopped after with `oldest: 2`), SC-30 (Marta's old phone keeps "my loans" open, and on 17 Nov `your_loan_is_overdue` reaches the row of L1 by its `loan_id` with the row in 1.x's words, then `left`; the identity of each event of 1.x a loan's id, by type). Rejected: the input forward before the nodes, over what the entry gives (the steps read the point's context, A's shape, 17 Nov 2026); a forwarded input refused thrown, as the past leaf's fault (a static rule over `now` in the current schema would be a technical error for a request the client's contract accepted, DOM-21's reason); the fingerprint over the input gone forward (parsed by each schema, so with the defaults a release may change, LIB-11's reason); the gate left out of the trace (a peer stopped in silence, F6); the gate after the input's check (A's: Gianni's `note` at 2.1 would read `invalid_request`, PER-17 "The gate"); the window's version given beside the majors (a copy of `version.ts` to keep in step). Left with their cases: a leaf renamed at a major, kept under its old path over the new one (DOM-25: no port by the old name, since a tree's ports are its current leaves'); a past leaf that follows, over a leaf that follows (built, not observed: `return_a_tool` of 1.x is delegated).
  - The surface an extension adds to a persona has its own version and window (EXT-5): a persona's surface is made of the kernel's and of each active extension's, which change apart (V17). [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **PER-18** Answers are built field by field; attribution, frame stack, fact schema versions and fact resources never leave the node. Correlation may travel, and is a claim when it comes in. D45, D56, D72, D101, D107
  - A persona's read leaf is a read use case over the business's (`read_use_case_over({ use_case, input, creator, entry, projection })` in Shed, a mechanism of no kind, LIB-1): its own input and the creator that turns it into the query's; its own entry, in its own values; the projection, a function from an entry of the business to one of its own, field by field: a field it does not write never leaves (Luca sees no keeper's notes), and a value of the business that its own values do not hold does not compile (a new category in `tools` breaks the projection, not the surface, PER-17). It gives the snapshot alone: with no projection of the events of the stream its port is not `Streamed`, so that no live view takes it. The visitor keeps no stream open by exposure, not by a limit (D24, D77): a limit (D80) would serve a leaf that streams for some wearers and not for others, by their values (PER-1), with no case yet. The projection of the stream (`fact`, `entered`, `left`, in the persona's words) comes with its case, Marta's live "my loans". [Q, 12 Nov 2026 (4)] Its own values hold even where it adopts the business's word (`term` in `my_loans`, `running` or `overdue`): declared in the persona, a new value of the business stops the projection from compiling and the persona decides how its clients see it (PER-17); a business value re-exported by the door would reach the clients unannounced. Weighed and set aside (A): a door that re-exports values; between business domains GR-3 excludes it, inside a persona and outside its surface it waits for a case. [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (3)]
  - A read leaf streams when it declares how (`stream`, last of its parts), and then its port is `Streamed`: Marta's `my_loans`, over `the_ongoing_loans_of_a_member`, with an empty input and the card's number from the context (D33, D87, PER-8). Every entry of the stream is projected as in the snapshot, by the same `projection`. `fact` carries the entry the fact leaves, not the fact (A, 13 Nov 2026 (2)): projecting every fact of the domain under its name (D72.5) would bind the leaf to all the facts of `loans`, most of which never reach it or reach it with nothing to say. The facts by name (A, 14 Nov 2026): among the facts the read use case names (DOM-17), the leaf chooses those its clients read by a name of its own, declared as a command leaf's events, with their schemas (`stream.events`), and projected from the fact's payload (`stream.facts`, one function per fact, partial). The fact goes out as its event, the name at the top as in D72.6, with the position, the identity, the entry it leaves (so that the client updates the row without applying the payload) and the payload: Marta reads "your loan is overdue" (`your_loan_is_overdue`, from `a_loan_has_become_overdue`, its payload empty, the row says the rest). Every other fact (the loan, the reminder, any new fact of the business) goes out as `fact`, the entry alone: adding an event to the union is an incompatible change (D75.6), so a new fact of the business adds none, and the persona decides when one comes in (PER-17). The persona names a fact only through the type of the use case it invokes, as it names the branches of a command, so the door does not change; a fact the read use case does not name, a name that is no fact, an event the leaf does not declare, a name taken by the stream, the snapshot, a node that closes or a base event, do not compile. Whether a persona must cover every fact, and what becomes of those it does not name, the paper does not say (D72.5): a gap. [Q, 14 Nov 2026] `left` names the entry by the identity of the query's entry, as it is (`loans.loan_id`, the one the member sends back to return a tool): the leaf declares the field of its own entry that holds it (`stream: { identity: 'loan_id' }`), checked by type to hold that identity, as a query declares its own (LIB-9), and the port types every event's `identity` as that field of the persona. An identity of the persona's own (one per caller, so that two peers cannot link what they see; a row of two kinds, step 9) would then be an addition: a translation from the query's identity in `stream`, pure, over the context, the projection and the creator translating the rest; no leaf and no port change shape. Whether q- needs it is for step 2 (`known`, `unknown`). `caught_up` and `position_unavailable` go out as they are, the position opaque (D72.6); the envelope and its resources never (PER-18, D107.4). The events of a read leaf: the snapshot, those of its stream if it streams, how its path may close it, `invalid_request` (no operation, so no `identity_already_used`). [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (2)]
  - **A limit on a read** (SC-32) [Q, C, 18 Nov 2026 (3)]: a read leaf holds limits of its own, as a command leaf (`is_refused_unless`, PER-5.5, D80): pure, over the context of its point and the input, run after the nodes of its path and before the read; the failed ones close the request with `refused`, their names the reasons, before any snapshot. It serves a use case that some wearers reach and others do not, by their values (PER-1, D77: "express shipping only for premium" is a use case with a limit, not a persona): Marta follows the shelf live (`member`'s `the_shelf`, over `tools`' `the_catalogue`, `stream: { identity: 'tool_id' }`) while her card is valid (`the_member_card_is_valid_today`); on 1 April she is refused, told why, and reads the catalogue once, as the visitor's, which keeps no stream by exposure (D24). The limit refuses the request, not the stream after a snapshot: limits run before the business (PER-5), and the snapshot alone is the visitor's leaf. A limit never filters a row: a constraint that takes rows away is a perimeter (D80, on reads). Evaluated at the request: a stream opened on 31 March stays open past midnight, and its next request, a resume too, is refused. Rejected: the snapshot then `refused` in place of the stream (a new event after the read, and the limit run after the business); a persona for the active members (a trade that does not differ, D77). On the oracle since part 6.2 the refusal (`refused` before any snapshot, the trace keeping it at the leaf, PER-14); since part 6.3 the shelf live while the card is valid (a tool added comes in as `fact`, one sent to the workshop leaves), and a stream opened at 23:00 on 31 March that stays open past midnight while its resume at 00:30 on 1 April is refused before any read. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
  - **A read leaf's stream, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 6.3; D72, D72.5, D72.6, DOM-17, SC-19, CFG-2): a read leaf that declares `stream` reads whole and invokes the read use case's port below as a live view: with no position, its snapshot (every entry projected, at its position, with its total), then its stream's events; from a position, the stream alone, a resume, inclusive, the checks of PER-5 run first at every request (so SC-32's resume after midnight is refused before any read). Each event in the leaf's words: `fact`, `entered` with the entry projected as in the snapshot; a fact the leaf names, by the part of it that touches the entry, its own event with the position, the identity, the entry and the payload its projection gives, parsed by its schema; any other fact, and an entry `changed` by a fact that did not touch it, `fact` with the entry alone; `left` with the identity as the query gives it (the leaf's `stream.identity` holds it); `caught_up` and `position_unavailable` as they are; never the envelope nor its resources (PER-18). The part named is found as DOM-17's touch: among the parts whose path is a fact the read use case names and whose name the leaf projects, the first in the order of the parts whose resources hold the entry's identity by the path of the query's identity; so a read use case's port carries, out of sight beside the component it serves, the path of its entries' identity and the paths of the facts it names (`Served.read`), and the tree needs no registry of the business (as the ports of PER-6). Two named parts touching one entry come with their case. A leaf that streams over a port that does not stream is a fault at wiring, in one message with the others (CFG-2). The events of a stream are no rejection and leave nothing in the trace (PER-14); an input the query does not take does, as for a page. Scenarios on the oracle: SC-32's stream (above); SC-17's persona side, Gianni's "my reservations" reads `the_tool_you_booked_is_in_repair` on R8's row, from the notice's part, with the row and no envelope, natively and over the composed stream. Rejected: the named part found by its name alone, with no touch (the round's step and its notice in one fact would both be candidates, and a fact naming another member's reservation would reach Gianni's row); the business registries given to the tree to find the identity's path (the configurator gives them twice, PER-6's reason); the identity's path found among the parts' resources by value alone (a loan and a tool may share a text, `L7`).
- **PER-19** The relay has its own personas, by the trade of `mailbox`: whoever deposits on D (anyone), whoever collects with R. These are not the four personas of the phone, which hold only for `persona`: the relay has no bonds and no local user, and the envelope is sealed. They live in the domain `desk` (the counter of a post office, where one deposits and collects: the persona layer of `mailbox`; arrows: `library`, `mailbox`), which neither the phone nor other importers of the domains need. Its interface turns R into the `Caller` (id = D = hash(R)); its auth is trivial and gets a folder only when it has rules. The HTTP interface and the `memory` oracle both reach `mailbox` through them, never directly. D77, D88, D103, D75 [Q]
- **PER-20** A future carrier (a nearby device passing an envelope) wears a persona of the local mailbox's tree; whether known carriers differ is decided then, as a value or a persona. The carrier travels as a frame of the letter it carries, independent of the letter's own persona. D77, D99 [Q, not in the prototype]
- **PER-21** The command use cases of `seals` belong to no persona tree: only reactions reach them (internal entries, PER-12), wired by the configurator. No peer and no screen reaches a secret directly. D88 [Q, plan]
- **PER-22** **Auth's office, the acquaintances** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.1; PER-1, PER-9, GR-4): `auth/acquaintances` (_le conoscenze_) is the register of whom the node knows, at which of its mailboxes, and what it offers them. A domain of kind auth, as Shed's `auth/accounts`: identities and values of its own (`bond_id`, `face_id`, a face's key, a deposit address, an extension's name, a major), the same keys the address book and the office of the extensions give, linked by its adapters, which read them over `bonds`' and `extensions`' read use cases (GR-4); no command, no query; it sees no other domain (`sight.ts`, `auth: {}`). Its key is **a face at my mailbox**, `{ face, mailbox }`: the key whose signature holds and the mailbox of mine the letter came to, both verified by `post` (F2, F3, PER-13: a letter re-addressed to another mailbox does not open, PST-1). Its lookups, each one key and one value or not known (D30):
  - `the_bond_of_a_face_at_my_mailbox`: the bond kept at that mailbox whose face is that key, with the face of mine it belongs to (PER-1's value), its peer's key and the name the user gives it; not known when no bond is kept there, or the one kept there is another face's;
  - `the_ticket_a_face_presents_at_my_mailbox`: a ready ticket of mine at that mailbox, or the bond kept there with that face (the crossed QR, BND-11), what an introduction is for (PER-11), with the face of mine and the key asked with; not known otherwise;
  - `the_extensions_offered_to_the_bond_of_a_key` (EXT-6): the extensions the node offers to the bond of that face at that mailbox, each with the majors of its surface on `known`; not known when the face has no bond kept there, an empty list when nothing is offered. Read by the kernel's entry before the gate (EXT-6), never by a node.
    The goal's four questions are answered so: which faces I have, the face of mine each bond and ticket belongs to (PER-1); which bond a key belongs to, the bond of a face at my mailbox; which personas a caller may wear, from those values, each node comparing them with its hat (PER-7); which extensions are offered to a bond, the third. Rejected: the key alone as the lookup's key (a key may hold two bonds with the node, two tickets crossed at a distance, BND-12, and a key known on one bond writing to another bond's mailbox would be served as that bond's: the check "a known key outside its bond is not found"); the mailbox alone, with a value told by case (a ticket or a bond kept) that the node compares (a perimeter is a boolean and narrows no type: `gives` would hand the leaves and the kernel's adapter a value whose bond may be a ticket with no name); a lookup of the hats (`the_hats_of_a_face_at_my_mailbox`: a list derived from the same data, the rule of who wears which hat moved into auth's adapter, a decision, PER-9); `contacts`, `bonds` for the office (the address book's words: auth keeps its own language, GR-4). The ticket's value carries back the key it was asked with, so that the leaf of `unknown` gives the business the verified signer from auth's value, never from the input (PER-8). EXT-6's lookup is keyed by a face at my mailbox, for the reason above; its name stays.
- **PER-23** **The `Caller` of q-** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.1; PER-2, PER-4, PER-13): the persona's own value (`persona/value.ts`; `persona` does not see `post`), an ADT by `is`, which the entry builds from the opened envelope (part 13.2): `the_user`, the user of the node, from the app; `a_face`, its `key` the one whose signature holds, and `to_my_mailbox`, the mailbox of mine the letter came to; `anonymous`, its `id` the hash of its envelope (PER-4), its `annotation` (no signature, or one that does not hold with the face it claims, PER-2), and `to_my_mailbox`. The persona base (`persona/node.ts`, as Shed's) takes it, verifies no hat and gives it to every persona. Beside it the persona base takes **the time the request declares** (`declared_at`) [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (BND-10, A's decision of 21 Nov 2026: the window of a crossing reads the time their introduction declares): a letter's `declared_at`, its sender's claim (PER-13), which the entry gives from the letter; the user's request from the app, the instant of her gesture, by my clock. `unknown`'s leaf gives it to `accept_their_introduction`, as it gives the signer's key from its node (PER-8). Rejected: the mailbox beside the `Caller` in the context (a pair that can be impossible: the user by a letter); the time in the `Caller`'s letter variants (the `Caller` holds who asks, as verified, PER-4, and `unknown`'s leaf would narrow it to the face where its node found a ticket); the time in the introducer's input (a second declaration of what the letter declares); the post's `Caller` imported (an arrow `persona → post` the graph does not have, § 2).
- **PER-24** **The four trees, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.1; PER-6, PER-7, EXT-1, F7, F8). Each persona's node verifies its own hat as a perimeter (PER-7), each surface at `1.0.0` (PER-17):
  - `self`: its node holds `the_caller_is_the_user`, binds no face in stage 1 (one face; "as face X" comes with stage 2, PER-3) and gives nothing, so that the context of `self`'s mounted folders is the root's (EXT-11).
