Its leaves are the kernel's use cases the user invokes from the app, over `bonds` and `extensions` (`make_my_first_face`, the phone's first start, BND-8; `prepare_a_ticket`, `open_their_ticket`, `rename_a_bond`, `remove_a_bond`; `activate_an_extension`, `deactivate_an_extension`, `withdraw_the_offer_from_a_bond`, `offer_an_extension_to_a_bond`, `ask_a_bond_for_the_extensions_in_common`; the reads `my_faces`, `my_bonds`, `a_bond`, `my_ticket`, `an_extension`, `the_extensions_offered_to_a_bond`, `the_questions_asked_of_a_bond` (UI-5)), each in the full form with the user's events, every nested value of an entry rebuilt part by part (`persona/self/dsl.ts`), so that a field the business adds fails to compile in the persona instead of at the strict parse of its entry; not the ones only reactions, returns or deliveries invoke (PER-12), nor `seals`' (PER-21). An extension's leaves on `self` are mounted at `persona/self` at wiring (PER-6, "A mounted folder").
  - `known`: its node looks up `the_bond_of_a_face_at_my_mailbox` of the `Caller`'s face at the mailbox it wrote to, `not_found` when not known (PER-14: an anonymous letter, an unknown face, a known key outside its bond), and gives the bond whole; the kernel's adapter maps it to what `boundary` gives a mounted folder (`the_bond`, `the_peer_key`, `the_name_of_the_bond`, EXT-11). Its leaf is the kernel's `the_extensions_in_common` (EXT-8), a read over the office's, the bond from the node and never the input, one snapshot in one page, no stream (F7); the active extensions' leaves are mounted at `persona/known` at wiring.
  - `unknown`: its node looks up `the_ticket_a_face_presents_at_my_mailbox`, `not_found` when not known; its leaf `introduce_myself` (PER-11) over `bonds`' `accept_their_introduction`, the bond and the signer's key from the node's value, the secret, the name and the mailbox from the input. Who wears `unknown` is any face that presents a ticket of the node's, so a crossed QR's peer, already a bond's face, introduces itself there too; the business decides the rest (BND-11). The introduction is a request of F7, answered on the bond it made (PER-25).
  - `anonymous`: its node verifies nothing (anyone wears it, as Shed's `visitor`) and the tree has no leaf: every letter that claims it reads `invalid_request` (`not_a_leaf`) at the tree's ports, traced with its `Caller` and its annotation (F6, PER-14); one claiming another persona meets that persona's node, `not_found`. It has no node (as Shed's `visitor` and the desk's `depositor`) and its surface no leaf; that the tree's ports serve a persona with no leaf is first wired in part 13.2, by `zz.tree_ports_by_path` (PER-25).
    Rejected: a node above the hats that reads the `Caller`'s case and routes it (the tree of personas D78 discards: PER-7); the leaves of `self` named apart from the business's use case where its name says the user's gesture already (a second name for one gesture).
- **PER-25** **The kernel's entry, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.2; PER-5, PER-23, PER-24, F7, F8, EXT-4, EXT-6, PST-4, PST-7, PST-9, PST-11, GR-6; its stories in [`entry/README.md`](../packages/adapters-common/src/entry/README.md)): in `adapters-common` (`entry/the_kernels_entry.ts`, beside the kernel's side of the extensions), `the_kernels_entry(wiring)` takes one envelope collected at a mailbox of the node at a time (`{ came_to, envelope }`). Its wiring is functions, as the port of requests' (F7), so that the oracle and the phone wire it alike: the clock; the post's machine of the face of mine at a mailbox, or none; the kernel's four persona trees by path (`zz.tree_ports_by_path`, below); an extension's leaves mounted on a persona (`zz.mounted_leaf_ports`), or none where it has no surface; the routing's reads (the names installed, the active among them, and the names offered to the bond of a face at my mailbox, or not known, over auth's `the_extensions_offered_to_the_bond_of_a_key`, PER-22); the post's `take_a_response` and its mailbox port (PST-10); the bond a response goes on; the machine of a face of mine; the kernel's trace (EXT-6). A fault of a port, the kernel's trees included, throws: the collector does not advance its position (DOM-17), and the duplicate branches make a second pass the same (DOM-11, PST-7). In order:
  1. **An envelope came**: opened by the machine of the face of mine at the mailbox it came to (PST-4); none there, or every outcome of the post but `opened` (`not_an_envelope`, `not_opened`, `not_a_letter`, `re_addressed` with the letter's identity and its signer), goes to the kernel's trace as `an_envelope_not_opened`, with the post's outcome, and no further: no persona, nothing sent back.
  2. **The `Caller`** of the persona (PER-23), from the post's: a face, its key and the mailbox it came to; `anonymous`, its id, its annotation and the mailbox.
  3. **A response** goes to the request it names, never to a persona (F7, PST-7): taken under its identity in the entry's scope (`the_scope_of_the_kernels_entry`), its correlation the request it answers (DOM-10), the request's return if it waits and went to that face; else, and an anonymous one always, `a_response_not_awaited` in the kernel's trace with what the post answered: never a fact of an extension.
  4. **A request** is routed (`route`, EXT-6) over the names installed, the active among them and those offered to the bond of the `Caller`'s face at the mailbox it came to, read for every request, the kernel's too (`route` decides; an anonymous `Caller`, or a face with no bond kept there, has none offered: F8's third case). To silence: its case in the kernel's trace, nothing sent (F8). To the kernel: the persona tree the request names, by the leaf's path. To an extension: its leaves mounted on the persona the request names; where it has no surface (an extension adds to `self` and `known` only, EXT-1), `invalid_request` `not_a_leaf` (EXT-4) and `not_a_leaf_of_the_extension` in the kernel's trace. The context: the root's (`now` and the node's zone), the `Caller`, the time the letter declares (`declared_at`, PER-23), the version the request names, the operation (its id the request's identity, F7; its scope `<persona>/<the Caller's key, or an anonymous letter's id>`, `the_scope_of_a_letter`, `Caller` + hat, DOM-11, which the hosting replaces on `known` with the bond's, EXT-2) and the correlation, the request's identity. Then every check of PER-5 is the trees' ports', in its order: the gate, the leaf, the input, the nodes (the hat, the bond, the ticket: `not_found` hides from the peer and stays in the tree's trace for the user, PER-14), the leaf. The answer is the leaf's first event (one event, F7); an extension's invocation that throws (fallen, not running, EXT-15) answers nothing (F8), and the entry traces the request unanswered, `a_request_not_answered` with why, beside the fall the hosting traces [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.3, found by its story 9: a request that met an extension stopped after its second fall left no entry, against F6). Rejected: the hosting's trace alone (it tells the fall, never which request went unanswered); one of F8's silences (the extension is installed, active and offered: the routing let it through).
  5. **Answered on the bond** of the `Caller`'s key (F7, PST-9), for a face only: the wiring's `the_bond_to_answer_on({ face, mailbox })` gives the bond kept at the mailbox the request came to with that key, else one kept with that key (over `bonds`), else none, and the request gets nothing back. The response (PST-1, PST-2: its identity the one of the response to the request), from my face of the bond, to the deposit address its peer declared, the event, sealed for its key by the machine of my face, is deposited once through the post's mailbox port under PST-11's operation; not sealed, refused or unreachable, `a_response_not_deposited` in the kernel's trace (F6), and the asker's sending again asks again (PST-6). An event over what a letter carries is neither sealed nor deposited, `a_response_not_deposited` with `too_large_for_a_letter` (PST-12) [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026].
     So a known key outside its bond reads `not_found` on its own bond, and its user sees why in the tree's trace (step 13's check); a face with no bond, an unknown face or an anonymous letter gets nothing back; and the **introduction** (`introduce_myself` on `unknown`, PER-11) is a request of F7 to the kernel, answered on the bond it made or confirmed (`you_are_bonded`), while one refused, which made no bond, gets nothing back. The kernel's trace gains three reasons (EXT-6, "The kernel's trace, as written"): `an_envelope_not_opened`, `not_a_leaf_of_the_extension`, `a_response_not_deposited` (the response, the request it answers, the `Caller`, `now`, and why: `not_sealed`, or what the relay said, or unreachable). Rejected: the response on the bond kept at the mailbox the request came to only (a known key outside its bond would read nothing, no different from a node off, where step 13's check asks "not found to the peer"); the entry in `domains` (it opens, seals and deposits: I/O the configurator wires, GR-6); a node above the hats that reads the letter (the tree of personas D78 discards, PER-24); the entry mapping a path to a leaf's name itself, answering `not_a_leaf` and writing the tree's trace (`zz.Traced` built outside the library, which owns it); an extension's request on a persona it has no surface on sent to the kernel's tree there (its gate would answer in the kernel's version, another surface's words); the introduction answered at the mailbox its input names before any bond (a response to a face with no bond, against F7); the routing's reads skipped for a request to the kernel (a second copy of `route`'s first branch). **Open**: a leaf's event over a letter's 32 KiB (PST-12) is not refused by the entry: the peer reads `not_a_letter` and its port no response; a fault of a lookup inside an extension's mounted port is taken for a fall, untraced by the entry (as the 12.4 harness did); the delivery of `ask_for_the_extensions_in_common` over the kernel's port of requests (EXT-8, `{ of: 'the_kernel', … }`) came with part 13.3 (EXT-8, "The delivery, as written"), and the asker's side of the introduction comes with step 15.
  - **A tree's ports by path** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (part 13.2; PER-24's `anonymous`, EXT-5): a letter names its leaf by a path, and a path no leaf has must read `invalid_request` `not_a_leaf` in the tree's trace, which the named ports of `tree_ports` cannot answer (a path with no name has no port; `anonymous` has no leaf, so no port at all). `zz.tree_ports_by_path`, in the library, takes `tree_ports`' declaration and wiring and gives one port, a `zz.PortByPath<entry>` (the context, `{ leaf, input }`, the events `zz.EventByPath`), the one type of `mounted_leaf_ports`' port (A, 21 Nov 2026: one type for both, not a second name for one shape), the leaf's path from the persona's segment: the gate as the named ports', then the current leaf at that path, served by the named port's code, or `not_a_leaf` traced at `<folder>/<leaf>`; the surface's folder is its `version.ts`'s, so that a tree with no leaf is served (the gate, then `not_a_leaf`). Tests: `test/library/tree_port_by_path.test.ts`. Forms: (1, written) a function beside `tree_ports`, as `mounted_leaf_ports` is beside it, one port; (2) a key on `tree_ports`' declaration (`by_path: true`) adding the port to `TreePorts` (a name of the port could meet a leaf's, as for `mounted` in 12.3); (3) the entry finding the name and answering `not_a_leaf` itself (rejected above). Proposal: (1), the form of a construct that exists (the mounted port).
  - **Auth's adapters, as written** [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026] (part 13.2; GR-4, PER-9, PER-22): `adapters-common` (`auth/acquaintances.ts`), `the_acquaintances({ clock, address_book, office })` gives the three lookups as `zz.lookup_ports` takes them, over the address book's reads (`the_bond_at_my_mailbox`, `the_ticket_at_my_mailbox`, `a_bond` for the bond's name) and the office's `the_extensions_offered_to_a_bond` (names with majors; the kernel's reads give names only): data, compared by key, never a decision. Rejected: widening the kernel's reads of the office (`activations.ts`) for the majors. Their ports' types are declared by shape; part 13.3 wired them on the node of q-'s oracle, where they fit the address book's and the office's read ports as declared, unchanged.

## 6. Adapters

- **ADP-1** Adapters are mechanical translators: no business rule, no `if` on the domain's meaning. The logic is in the domains. §4
- **ADP-2** A technical event becomes a concept of the domain it enters. Technical outcomes map to user concepts: the meaning comes from the contract, the name from the user domain, the mapping is a pure function of the user core. D2, D14, D74
- **ADP-3** A port speaks the consumer's language. "Port" alone means a secondary port; primary ports are use cases. D41, D86
- **ADP-4** A transport adapter does not translate: it carries the message and the return stream, keeps order within a stream, signals abort; it may lose and duplicate. Reliability is end to end. D73
  - The HTTP client of the relay is a transport for the mailbox port of `post`. [Q] Since step 20, of the collect port too (WEB-6). [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]
- **ADP-5** Cache and retry are decorators of the same port, under its contract: a retry never repeats a domain outcome, and a decorator offers only what it preserves of the inner adapter. D2
- **ADP-6** Storage is reached only through the ports; its shape is private to the adapter. D102
- **ADP-8** Recommended for a state adapter, not required (LIB-14): one state adapter for every domain of a node, so that a predicate over another domain's state is one more fragment of it, read atomically with the command (GR-3, D71); an algebra of fragments in its own language (a SQL condition, a filter, a function over a transaction), one per predicate by path plus AND/OR/NOT, so that a predicate has one translation, reused by every command; for reads, an algebra over the selection predicates, grouped by subject, with the same operators, so that a selection predicate has one translation, reused by every query (V12), and a fragment per calculation, a field read with its parameters, likewise reused (DOM-18, SC-21); one write per fact by path; a command executed in one consistent step of its own (a transaction, a conditional write), reading the failed leaves in the same step when its query language allows it, in a second read otherwise. Searches the state technology cannot do (vectors, full text) are separate projections fed by the facts (DOM-18). Sketch: `domains/test/shed/adapter/sql.ts`. [Q]
- **ADP-7** The UI is an adapter of the user domain: it renders state, turns gestures into activities, listens to streams. Anything a product manager would name belongs to `user`. Activities are the use cases of `user`, at most one command each. D76, D58
  - In q- the screen model is the pure domain `ui`, and the Solid rendering is its adapter (V2). [Q]

Adapters of q- [Q, plan], by package; the order of writing is in the [development plan](plan.md):

| package           | adapters                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `adapters-common` | the libsodium primitives of `seals` (same wasm in browser and Node; SEA-9); the kernel's side of the extensions (EXT-2, EXT-4, EXT-6, EXT-15, F7; part 12.4), with the manifest in the words of the office of `extensions` and the kernel's reads of it (EXT-7)                                                                                                 |
| `adapters-node`   | the relay's storage (SQLite, `node:sqlite`) and its HTTP interface (Hono), through `desk`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `adapters-web`    | IndexedDB stores with the single writer (DOM-9) and `storage.persist()` (WEB-8..WEB-13); the HTTP client of the relay (WEB-6); the Solid rendering of `ui` (WEB-18..WEB-21); QR (drawn and scanned with the camera, WEB-2); the ticket link (after `#`, WEB-1); files (export and import, WEB-3); the device's clock and randomness (WEB-4); the service worker |

The browser's I/O (step 23; the words in the glossary; `adapters-web/src/`, one folder per adapter, its tests in `adapters-web/test/`):

- **WEB-1** **The ticket's link** (BND-7, BND-10, F2, ADP-4) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: the phone's page with a ready ticket after `#`: `ticket=`, then the JSON of the ticket as the domain shows it (`their_ticket` of `bonds/address_book`) in base64url, unpadded (`the_link_of_a_ticket(page)(ticket)`, `ticket_link/`). A browser sends no fragment to the server that serves the page, so the ticket reaches only the phone that opens it. The link carries the ticket without knowing its fields: a field it gains in a minor goes with it (DOM-24); `ticket=` names what the fragment carries, so that another kind of link is another mark. Read back from any text (`the_ticket_in_a_link`): `a_ticket`, parsed with the domain's schema as any data from outside; `no_ticket` (no link, or no `ticket=` after `#`); `a_ticket_out_of_its_form` (no base64url, UTF-8 or JSON, or no ticket as the schema says), so that the screen tells a broken link from another one. The page the link points to is not read: a link of another copy of the page carries a ticket as well (the code is free; who wrote it is a claim, F2). The page's address is the configuration's (step 25). Scenario: Anna sends Bruno `https://cumino.example/#ticket=eyJrZXkiOi…` in a chat; his phone opens the page, reads the ticket, asks "Come lo chiami? [Anna]" from its `introduced_as`, and opens it with a ticket of his prepared for it (BND-10). Rejected: the ticket's fields in a fixed order between dots (a third shorter, but the adapter would know every field and change with each minor of the ticket); the ticket in the query (it goes to the server); the JSON percent-encoded (three characters for each brace and quote); the JSON compressed (a few bytes on random keys, and asynchronous); the ticket read with no schema, `unknown` to the use case (the screen needs its name before opening it, and one schema gives one outcome).
- **WEB-2** **QRs drawn and read, the camera** (ADP-1, ADP-2) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: the QR of a ticket draws its link (WEB-1), so that a camera outside Cumino opens the page with it too, and the scanner reads it as any link. Every QR q- draws is of level M (15% of the symbol restored), in byte mode, with a quiet zone of four modules (ISO/IEC 18004): a ticket on a relay of a usual address is a version 16 at most (81 modules), a phone's screen read by another phone's camera. `the_qr_of(text)` (`qr/`): its modules, and the same in SVG (dark on light whatever the page's colours, one path of each row's dark runs; the page sizes it); or nothing when the text is longer than a QR holds (2331 bytes of UTF-8): a ticket whose relay address is that long has its link and no QR, which the screen says (F6). The scan (`camera/`): `scan_with_the_rear_camera({ media_devices, video, elapsed })` asks the browser for the camera facing away from the user where there is one (`facingMode: { ideal: 'environment' }`, 1280×720 ideal, no audio), plays it muted and inline in the page's video, and gives the texts of the QRs it sees, a look every 200 ms by the time that passes (`zz.Elapsed`), each when it differs from the last one given, until it is turned off (its tracks stop) or its consumer stops (`the_texts_seen`); or why it is not on: `refused` (`NotAllowedError`, `SecurityError`: by the user, or a page that is no secure context), `missing` (`NotFoundError`, `OverconstrainedError`), `busy` (any other: `NotReadableError`, another app has it). A frame with no QR the decoder reads gives nothing. The texts are any QR's: the screen reads each with WEB-1 and takes the first that carries a ticket. The construction is the `qr` package's, encoder and decoder: a dependency this document did not name, maintained, with no install script and no dependency of its own, MIT or Apache-2.0, a dependency of `adapters-web` pinned at 0.7.2 exactly [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]. Rejected: the browser's `BarcodeDetector` (Chrome on Android has it, Firefox and Chrome on Linux do not: two behaviours, one of them untested); a QR of the ticket's JSON alone (no camera outside Cumino would open it); level L (two versions smaller, less forgiving of a glare on the screen); a look at every frame (a decode on a phone takes longer than a frame).
- **WEB-3** **Files for export and import** (ADP-1) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: a file's bytes, name and media type only; what an export holds and how an import is read are step 26's (G1). `save_a_file(page)(file)` (`files/`): a blob, a link the page clicks with the file's name (`download`), the blob's address let go after a minute; the browser writes it where the user keeps downloads. `pick_a_file(page)(accept)`: a file input of those types, clicked; the pick is `a_file` (read), `nothing` (the picker closed, or no file chosen), or `a_file_that_cannot_be_read`. Both need a gesture of the user's on the way, as the browser asks. No server: the file stays on the device. Rejected: the File System Access pickers (not on Android); the Web Share API for an export (a choice of the export's screen, step 26).
- **WEB-4** **The device's clock and randomness** (GR-8, SC-12, DOM-12, D62, TST-3) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: the sources the configurator injects; nothing else of the phone reads `Date`, sets a timer or draws at random. `the_device_s_clock(time_zone)` (`clock/`), a `zz.Clock`: the instant the runtime reads, as it writes it, in UTC (`new Date().toISOString()`, A, 23 Nov 2026), and the zone the configuration gives, the node's calendar (`the_device_s_zone()`, the runtime's by `Intl`, or UTC, the node's choice, SC-12). `the_time_that_passes`, a `zz.Elapsed` over the runtime's timer. `ids_drawn_at_random(crypto)` (`randomness/`): the ids a node names before the first attempt (DOM-12: an operation, a bond, a face), 16 bytes of the runtime's cryptographic randomness (Web Crypto) in base64url, 22 characters. The secrets are the safe's, drawn by libsodium (SEA-5, SEA-9). The same code runs in Node (`Date`, `setTimeout`, `globalThis.crypto`): the relay's configurator (step 24) takes them from here, or the step moves them to `adapters-common`. Rejected: `crypto.randomUUID()` (36 characters, a second alphabet beside base64url's); ids ordered by time (an id would tell when it was named); a clock that reads the device's zone itself (the node's calendar is the configuration's choice).
- **WEB-5** **The browser's objects, given** (CFG-1, TST-1) [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]: an adapter of `adapters-web` takes the browser's objects it uses as parameters (`document`, `URL`, `crypto`, `navigator.mediaDevices`, the page's `<video>`), typed by the parts it uses, and reaches for no global: the configurator gives them as they are, with no wrapper and no cast (the compiler checks it, `test/browser.test.ts`), and the tests give fakes of those parts in Node, with no DOM. What only a browser does (a camera's stream played and looked through) is typed by the DOM and proved on the phones (step 27).

The relay's HTTP client (step 20; the words in the glossary; `adapters-web/src/relay_client/`, its tests in `adapters-web/test/relay_client/`, and against the relay served in `memory/test/relay/` and `memory/test/stack/`):

- **WEB-6** **The relay's HTTP client** (ADP-1, ADP-4, MBX-9, PST-10, STK-3, STK-5, WEB-4, WEB-5) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: `the_relay_s_http_transports({ fetch, elapsed })` (`relay_client/`) gives the two transports between a phone and the relay that the `memory` stack carries in process (STK-5), over the relay's HTTP interface (MBX-9), at the mailbox's relay (the first of its relays: one in stage 1, BND-4), whose address is the base of the interface's paths (`<relay>/depositor/deposit_an_envelope`, its own path kept). The **mailbox port** posts `{ version, operation, input: { deposit_address, envelope } }`, showing nothing, the version the one of the depositor's surface the phone was built at; it reads the NDJSON events to their end and tells the last in the post's words, as the stack's in process (PST-10): `the_envelope_is_kept` `kept`, `identity_already_used` `kept_already`, another `refused` with it (`invalid_request`, `version_not_supported`: what the relay said), none `refused` `no_event`, a line that is no event `refused` `an answer out of form`. A status other than 200 is the relay's answer too, `refused` `status <n>`, but those by which HTTP says that no answer was given and to try again (408, 429, 5xx: a proxy before a relay down, a relay busy), `unreachable`. **No answer** is `unreachable`: the request lost (`fetch` fails with the network's `TypeError`), the answer lost on its way (its body broken: the relay may have kept the envelope, and the post's sending tried again is the same operation, PST-11, `kept_already`), or nothing heard from the relay in **the longest wait**, 30 seconds for the answer to begin or for its next bytes, by the time that passes (WEB-4), after which the request is let go (its `AbortSignal`). An **abort** the client did not ask for (an `AbortError` of the page or the runtime, or any fault that is no network's) is signalled (ADP-4): the deposit throws, the post's executor stops its effect unadvanced and, started again, delivers the entry again (PST-10, DOM-14). The **collect port** posts `{ version, input: { deposit_address } }` with the mailbox's R as `Authorization: Bearer <R>` (V5), reads the first event, the mailbox's snapshot, its entries' deposit ids and envelopes parsed with the holder's schemas (data from outside), and lets the stream go (`cancel`), which the relay lets go too (MBX-9); anything else throws (`ACollect`): no answer, a status other than 200, a first event that is no snapshot (`not_found`, `refused`), an answer out of form, so that the collector leaves the mailbox for its next pass (STK-3). The client keeps nothing, tries nothing again and reads nothing of an event but its name and, of a snapshot, what the collector takes: reliability is end to end (ADP-4), the post's sending again (PST-6) and the collector's next pass. The page's `fetch` is given (WEB-5), typed by the parts the client uses, so that the browser's and Node's enter as they are (`test/browser.test.ts`). Rejected: every status other than 200 read as a refusal (a relay down behind a proxy would leave dead letters for what the next sending delivers); an abort told as `unreachable` (ADP-4 asks the transport to signal it, and PST-10 throws); a broken answer told as an abort (the commonest loss on a phone's network, which the executor's pause already tries again); no wait of the client's own (a collect held by a network that stalls holds the collector's pass, for the minutes a browser waits); a time for the whole answer (a long snapshot on a slow network never read to its end); a deposit at every relay of a list (one relay in stage 1: with the case of more, LIB-3). **The relay as met** (UI-5's strip; Alessandro, 22 Nov 2026) [Q, C, 22 Nov 2026]: the client keeps how it last met the relay, `the_relay` beside the two transports (`TheRelayAsMet`: `last()`, `not_met_yet`, `answered` or `did_not_answer`, and `when_it_changes(tell)`, told at each change): no answer (the request lost, the relay unheard in the longest wait) or a status of no answer is `did_not_answer`, any other answer `answered`, an abort nothing (the page's, not the relay's). No network and a relay silent are not told apart (open, UI-5). Rejected: the strip read from the post's outcomes (a collect that fails is no outcome of the post's, and the collector's pass is the phone's: one place where every request to the relay passes); a listener in the wiring (every caller would give one, the tests too, for what only the phone shows).
- **WEB-7** **The client proved against the relay** (TST-1, TST-4, STK-6, MBX-8, MBX-9) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: the relay's configurator over its storage on SQLite in memory (`packages/relay`, MBX-8), its HTTP interface served by Node's HTTP server on a port of 127.0.0.1, opened by each test and closed at its end with every connection (`memory/test/relay/served.ts`). The phones name the relay by the `https` address their tickets declare (BND-4): the test's `fetch` takes it to the server, as a name resolved on the machine, and injects the network's faults below the client (TST-4): the request lost, the answer lost after the relay, the request doubled on the way, an abort before or after the relay. `memory/test/relay/over_http.test.ts`: what the post and the collector are told, and what the relay keeps read back through the holder's collect, under each fault, with the relay down, and a collect let go (the relay's answers counted open until let go). `memory/test/stack/over_http.test.ts`: the seven scenarios of stage 1 (STK-6) under both fault plans, on the fake primitives and on libsodium's, each story's relay the one served, in the story's world (its clock and its time that passes: the relay's waits follow the story). The server is `@hono/node-server`'s request listener over `node:http`, the one step 24's main is foreseen to serve with, a dev dependency of `memory` pinned at 2.1.6 (MIT, no dependency of its own, no install script; the newest release older than two weeks) [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]. Rejected: the interface called with `app.request`, no server (the socket, the stream and its letting go untested: what the phones meet); a bridge from `node:http` written in the tests (a second server beside step 24's); the faults injected above the client only (the fault plans prove the stack's answers to the client's words, not that the client gives them).

The node's storage on IndexedDB (step 21; the words in the glossary; `adapters-web/src/indexeddb/`, its tests in `adapters-web/test/indexeddb/`; part 21.1 wrote these rules and the skeleton, part 21.2 the domains' ports over it and the suites):

- **WEB-8** **The node's database** (DOM-8, ADP-6, EXT-14, WEB-5) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: one IndexedDB database per node, opened with the stores the release needs (`open_the_node_s_database(indexed_db)(name, stores)`): one per business domain of the kernel, named by its path (`domain bonds/address_book`: the folder a command port is made for, so `user`'s three offices are three stores), one per extension's partition (`extension ping_pong`, WEB-12), and **the node's store** (`node`: the writer's term, WEB-10; the node's counters, the last fact's position and the last entry's; the consumers' positions and holds; the partitions' sizes). A domain's store keeps, under array keys in IndexedDB's order (numbers by value, texts by code units): its facts (`['fact', position]`), its operations (`['operation', scope, id]`, the fact's position), its outbox's entries (`['entry', effect, position]`), its versions (`['version', path, version]`, which `stored_versions` reads) and its business's records (`['state', …]`, the domain's adapter's, part 21.2). The stores missing are created by an upgrade, which waits for the other connections to close (WEB-10); none is deleted in stage 1 (an extension's, with it, after stage 1, EXT-14). The browser's `indexedDB` and `IDBKeyRange` are given (WEB-5). Rejected: one store for the whole node (a domain's boundary, DOM-8, would be a key prefix, not a transaction's scope); a store per kind of record (an upgrade for every record a domain adds); a database per domain (no transaction could read a predicate over another domain's state with the write, GR-3); IndexedDB's indexes for the queries (an upgrade for every query that changes: a query reads its source's records by range and selects them in memory, as the oracle does, until a source too large asks for an index, LIB-3).
- **WEB-9** **A command, one transaction; the node's log** (DOM-8, DOM-9, DOM-10, DOM-11, DOM-17, GR-3, ADP-8) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: a predicate's fragment and a fact's write declare the keys they read and decide over them synchronously, by type (`Fragment { reads, holds }`, `Write { reads, puts }`), as the partition-backed adapter of an extension does (EXT-14); the boolean operators join their keys (`the_operators`, LIB-7). A command (`execute_in(domain)`) is one `readwrite` transaction over its domain's store, the node's, and the stores its fragments read (another domain's, for GR-3's predicates: read, never written). It reads the writer's term (WEB-10), then its operation (the adapter's step of the duplicate branch, DOM-11: the recorded fact at its position), then every key its branches name; decides as the oracle does (the first branch that holds, with its elements; a rejection names every leaf and writes nothing, TST-5); and writes its parts' records, each seeing those before it (DOM-10), its fact at the node's next position, its operation, its entries at the outbox's next positions, its versions and the node's counters: all or none, in its own domain's store and the node's only (DOM-8). Inside it nothing is awaited but its own requests, since IndexedDB commits a transaction at the first task with none pending. So GR-3's atomicity is one transaction, and IndexedDB runs the writer's commands one after another, each holding the node's store: the serial writer of DOM-9, with no queue of the adapter's. The leaf's and the port's lookups of the duplicate branch (DOM-11, "Where the duplicate branch runs") are `recorded_in(domain)`, one `readonly` transaction each; what the two cost is part 21.2's measure (WEB-17), and A's decision: both kept, as built (22 Nov 2026), measured again at step 27. **The node's log** (`the_node_s_log`) reads the facts of every domain's store from a position, inclusive (DOM-17), in one transaction, in the node's order; then caught up, at the last position; then it follows, woken after each commit of the writer (`a_bell`), all in its tab (WEB-10), the bell asked before each read, so that a commit during the read is not missed. Rejected: a read and a write in two transactions, with a queue of the writer's commands between them (part 21.1's first form: one lookup of the operation more than DOM-11's three, and GR-3's atomicity kept by the queue, not by the transaction); the facts in a store of the node's log (against DOM-8's letter, a domain's store holding its history; the node's counter is written anyway); a log per domain, with positions of its own (the consumers keep one position of the node's one order, D109); a store's key generator for the positions (one per store, not the node's order).
- **WEB-10** **The single writer and "use here"** (DOM-9, DOM-15, EXT-15, F6, WEB-5) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: the tabs of a browser (an installed PWA and a tab of its page alike) share the node's database; one is its **writer**, the one that holds the browser's lock of the writer (`navigator.locks`, given, WEB-5; `ask_to_be_the_writer(locks, database)`). A tab that opens the node asks for it if no tab holds it (`ifAvailable`); without it, it composes nothing of the node, no port and no consumer, and its screen says that Cumino is open in another tab and offers **use here** (_usa qui_), which takes the lock from the tab that holds it (`steal`). A tab that takes the lock writes a new **writer's term** in the node's store; every commit, a command's, a consumer's or a partition's, reads it in its own transaction and is aborted unless it is its tab's (`NotTheWriter`, a technical outcome, DOM-15): so a tab whose lock was taken writes nothing more, not even a commit it had begun; the browser tells it (`lost`, `used_elsewhere`), and its node stops (its consumers, its extensions unloaded, EXT-15; its screen offers use here). A connection the browser asks to close for a newer release (`versionchange`) closes, and its node stops too (`closed`). Rejected: a second tab that reads (its streams would need the writer's commits from another tab, a channel of their own, for a case with no need, LIB-3); the lock alone, with no term (`steal` gives the lock at once: a commit of the old tab in flight, or begun before it learns, would land beside the new writer's); an election over a `BroadcastChannel` (no lock of the browser's: a tab frozen in the background keeps its claim); a "use here" that waits for the other tab to let go (a tab forgotten in the background, which a phone may not wake).
- **WEB-11** **The storage kept** (F6, WEB-5) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: the writer asks the browser at each start to keep the node's storage (`ask_to_keep_the_storage(navigator.storage)`: `persisted()`, then `persist()`): `persistent` (the browser clears it only if the user does), `best_effort` (it may clear it when the device runs short of room), or `not_told` (no storage manager, as outside a secure context, or no answer). The browser decides (Chrome grants it to an installed PWA, Firefox asks the user); the node informs (F6): the screen that shows the answer, and its words, are step 22's and 25's. Rejected: asking once in the node's life (a storage the user cleared, or a browser that changed its mind, would not be asked again); a denial as a fault (the node works on a storage the browser may clear: a declared feature, the export of step 26 its cover).
- **WEB-12** **An extension's partition on IndexedDB** (EXT-14, EXT-2, DOM-15, WEB-10) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: `the_partition_of(node)(name)`, the `its_storage` the kernel gives an extension, over its store (WEB-8), which only the kernel's adapter opens. A read is a `readonly` transaction (keys in the order asked, those present; a range through `IDBKeyRange`, `from` inclusive, `before` exclusive), its values parsed as JSON (`boundary.json`); a commit is one `readwrite` transaction: the writer's term (WEB-10), the partition's size, its writes in order, all or none. Its **size** is the oracle's measure (EXT-14: the characters of its keys and of its values' JSON), kept in the node's store with each commit, its largest the configuration's (step 25): a commit over it is refused `the_partition_is_full`, as is one the browser refuses for want of room (`QuotaExceededError`: the device is full), each with nothing written. Rejected: a size in bytes (a second measure beside the oracle's, on which the suites run alike, TST-1); the browser's estimate (`navigator.storage.estimate()`: the origin's, not a partition's, and approximate by its specification); one store for every partition, keyed by the extension's name and its key (no upgrade at an install, but EXT-14 gives each extension a store of its own, and in stage 1 the extensions come with the app's release, whose upgrade creates their stores, WEB-8; with the extensions loaded from storage, after stage 1, an install at runtime asks again).
- **WEB-13** **The storage proved in Node** (TST-1, TST-3, TST-5, WEB-5) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]: the tests give the adapter `fake-indexeddb`'s `IDBFactory` and `IDBKeyRange` (a new factory per test: a fresh browser) and Node's Web Locks (`navigator.locks`; two tabs are two connections over one factory); a type test checks that the browser's objects are what the adapter takes (`test/indexeddb/skeleton.test.ts`, with the writer, use here and a partition). The domains' suites parametrized on the adapter's factory, beside the oracle's (TST-1), and the step's checks are part 21.2's; a phone's browser proves the rest at step 27. `fake-indexeddb` is a dependency this document did not name: a dev dependency of `adapters-web`, pinned at 6.2.7 (Apache-2.0, no dependency of its own, no install script; the newest release older than two weeks) [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]. Rejected: the tests in a real browser (vitest's browser mode with Playwright: a browser downloaded by an install script, another runner for one package); a fake of IndexedDB written here (a second implementation of its semantics to keep right, beside one that runs the platform's own tests, web-platform-tests); a library over IndexedDB in the adapter (`idb`, Dexie: the requests as promises are twenty lines, `requests.ts`, and Dexie brings a schema and an upgrade model of its own).
- **WEB-14** **A domain's module on IndexedDB** (WEB-8, WEB-9, ADP-6, ADP-8, GR-3, TST-2, LIB-9) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 21.2): each business domain of the kernel has its module in `adapters-web/src/indexeddb/domains/` (`seals_ports_on_indexeddb`, `post_ports_on_indexeddb`, `bonds_ports_on_indexeddb`, `extensions_ports_on_indexeddb`, `user_ports_on_indexeddb`), the oracle's module (TST-2) over the node's database, giving the same ports, so that a composition root takes either (WEB-16). A domain's **records** are under `['state', <kind>, ...ids]` in its store, a **kind** for each set of values the oracle's state keeps (`seals/safe`: `seed`, `face`, `mailbox_key`; `post/requests`: `request`; `bonds/address_book`: `face`, `bond`; `extensions/activations`: `activation`, `withdrawn_offer` by extension and bond, `question`; `user/activities`: `activity`; `user/drafts`: `draft`; `user/choices`: `active_face`, one record), each value the domain's `_value.ts` gives, parsed when read; the meaning is the domain's `dsl`, as the oracle's. A fragment reads keys **and prefixes** (WEB-9 amended: `ARange`, every record of a kind, for "the user has a face"), one per predicate by path; a fact's write reads the records it changes, its earlier parts' writes laid over them (DOM-10), and puts only in its own store, by type (a put names no store, DOM-8); a value changed is parsed as the oracle parses it, and a change to a record that is not there writes nothing, as on the oracle. A query's source names the ranges its candidates are read from (a source does not see the plan's parameters, so it reads the kind whole, WEB-8); `read` and `page` are one `readonly` transaction (`reads_in`), the source's records and the node's position in one snapshot (D66), selected, ordered by LIB-9's value order (written in `reads.ts` as the oracle writes it: an adapter imports no configurator, CFG-4) and read in memory, a read's actions (SC-33) its command fragments read in the same transaction. The office's GR-3 predicate ("the bond exists") reads the address book's store by its layout (`the_address_book_s_records`), never writes it, in the command's one transaction. Rejected: one record per domain holding its whole state (every command would rewrite it, and a transaction's reads would not name what a command depends on); the oracle's state rebuilt in memory at each command (the oracle twice, with the cost of reading every record); a key per query (a source does not see the plan's parameters); the oracle's modules moved to `adapters-common` and shared (the differential test would compare the oracle with itself, TST-2).
- **WEB-15** **The node's consumers on IndexedDB** (DOM-14, DOM-16, DOM-17, DOM-22, WEB-9, WEB-10, USR-8) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 21.2; `consumers.ts`): the **outbox** of an effect reads the entries its domain's store keeps under `['entry', <effect>, position]`, from a position, inclusive, in the node's one order of entries, then follows the writer's commits (the bell, as the node's log); the **executors' state** and the **reactions' consumers' state** are in the node's store, a position per effect or reaction, a held entry by its effect and id, a held part by its reaction, its fact's operation and its part, a discarded letter's trace by its key, each commit with the writer's term (WEB-10), so a tab no longer the writer moves no consumer; the **versions kept** (DOM-22) are the union of every domain store's `['version', path, version]`; the **entries and parts held**, all of them, are what the user is told (USR-8). `the_node_s_storage` puts the database, the writer and these together: a tab that is not the writer gets nothing to compose (WEB-10). Rejected: the consumers' state in each domain's store (an executor of one domain's effects would write another's store; DOM-8 keeps a domain's store to its facts and records); a store of the outbox (the entry is written with its fact, in its domain's store, by the one transaction of WEB-9).
- **WEB-16** **A phone over its storage** (STK-1, CFG-1, TST-1, WEB-13, WEB-14, WEB-15) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 21.2): a phone's composition root takes **a phone's storage** (`PhoneStorage`, `memory/src/phone/`): each kernel domain's ports as a function of what they are given (the primitives, the manifests installed, the versions), the node's log, the outbox, the executors' and the reactions' state, the versions kept, the entries held, and the extensions' partitions of a size; `on_the_cell(cell)` gives the oracle's, `memory/test/indexeddb/storage.ts` the node's database on fake-indexeddb, as step 25's root will on the browser's (STK-1 amended: `phone(storage, release)(world)`; the stack's phone and the stack's stories take a storage, STK-5, STK-6 amended). What a story reads of a phone's storage (its position, the entries and parts held, a partition's records through `its_storage`) is asynchronous on both. The writer's lock is named by the database (WEB-10 amended: `cumino: the writer of <name>`), one writer per node: two nodes of a story, or two tests in one process, each have theirs. The tests of `memory` are typed with the DOM's lib, as `adapters-web`'s (the adapter's and fake-indexeddb's types are the browser's), and `fake-indexeddb` is a dev dependency of `memory` too, at WEB-13's version. Rejected: `phone` generic over a cell (the cell's shape is the oracle's: IndexedDB has no synchronous state); the IndexedDB storage put together in `adapters-web` (its shape is the configurator's, CFG-4); one lock for every node of an origin (in stage 1 a browser has one node, but the tests run many nodes in one process).
- **WEB-17** **The step's checks, and the duplicate branch's two lookups measured** (TST-5, DOM-9, DOM-11 "Where the duplicate branch runs", WEB-9, WEB-10) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 21.2; `memory/test/indexeddb/checks.test.ts`, `the_two_lookups.measure.test.ts`): on a phone over IndexedDB, requests sent at the same time give the outcomes of one serial order (each order on a fresh node: a bond renamed and removed twice through the address book's ports, a ticket asked for under two operations through `self`'s leaves, one sent twice); a rejection leaves every store, the position, the log, the versions kept and the outbox as they were; a second tab is `another_tab` and composes nothing, and after its "use here" the first tab's port commands, leaf commands and consumers throw `NotTheWriter` and write nothing, a command begun before the steal and committed after included. **Counted**: a request through a leaf opens two `readonly` transactions on its domain's store (the leaf's lookup and the port's, `recorded_in`) and one `readwrite` (the step, which reads the operation again inside it); a retry, one `readonly` (answered at the leaf); a caller that is no leaf, one of each. **Measured**, on fake-indexeddb in Node (12th Gen Intel Core i9-12900HK, Node 24.14.1; 200 requests of `rename_a_bond` through its leaf after 50 of warm-up, three runs, median / p90 per request): as built, a new request 0.38 / 0.44 ms, a retry 0.14 / 0.17 ms; with the leaf's lookup reading nothing (only the port's), a new request 0.33 / 0.39 ms, a retry 0.19 / 0.26 ms. So the second lookup costs a new request about 12% (0.045 ms here), and makes a retry about 30% cheaper, answered before the schema, the nodes and the port. fake-indexeddb is not a phone's browser: the numbers compare the two forms, the times are step 27's, where the same measure can run on Chrome for Android. **A's decision**, on the measure: the two lookups kept, as built [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026], to be measured again at step 27 on Chrome for Android.

The screens' rendering (step 22; the words in the glossary; `adapters-web/src/screens/` and `adapters-web/src/her_node/`, the texts in `phone/src/texts/screens/`, the stories in `phone/test/screens/` and `memory/test/screens/`; part 22.1 the kernel's screens, part 22.2 the extension points and ping pong):

- **WEB-18** **The screens, rendered** (V2, ADP-7, UI-1, UI-3, WEB-4, WEB-5; `CLAUDE.md`, "Solid") [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (part 22.1): `TheScreens({ node, device, texts, a_link })` (`screens/`), the adapter of `ui`, exported as `@q-mino/adapters-web/screens` and imported by the phone's composition root only, so that the node's other adapters, which `memory` imports in Node, load no DOM rendering. **The way** is a signal; each screen of the way is kept while it is open, only the last shown (`hidden`), so that back finds a screen as she left it (UI-1); `to_the_start` reads her faces and the face she acts as again and computes `the_start`, a link she opened the app with (WEB-1) waiting for her first face, then opened once. **A screen** (`a_screen`) keeps its state in a signal (its start may be read from her node: the draft of her first face), turns what happens with its `after`, hands what the turn asks to her node in the order asked (a draft kept as she types arrives in the order of her keys), and goes on the way; it follows each activity it asked, at each change of her node, and tells it `what_it_asked_ended` once (UI-3). **What a screen is given** is read from her node again at each change of its state or of her node (`what_it_is_given`, a resource) and shown through its `what_it_shows`, the rendering's only logic being where each value goes in the page (ADP-1). **The app** (her node, the device, the words every screen shares, the count of her node's changes, the two hands of a turn) is a Solid context: static wiring, read once at a screen's setup. The ids and the time of a gesture are the device's (`draws_an_id`, `clock`, WEB-4), the camera its `scans(video)` (WEB-2), given by the composition root (WEB-5). Rejected: the rendering in `phone` (the configurator composes; the plan gives the adapter of `ui` to `adapters-web`); the screens in the package's main export (`memory`'s tests would compile Solid's JSX); only the last screen mounted (back would lose its state); the app in each screen's props (a read of props at setup for a static wiring, which `solid/reactivity` warns of). **The link shared** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]: the handshake at a distance's link is shared by a button, with the phone's share (`navigator.share({ url })`); where the browser has none, «Copia il link» (the clipboard), then «Link copiato». The device gives the page's `navigator` by the parts used (`the_navigator`: `share`, missing where the browser has none, and `clipboard.writeText`, WEB-5); `ui` gives the gesture (`the_phone`: `shares` or `copies`; the link's `gesture`: `share`, `copy`, `copied`, after `the_link_is_copied`), the rendering hands the link to the phone. Rejected: the link as an anchor to share (on her own phone it opens the invitation she made). **Since the final review** (UI-5, Alessandro, 22 Nov 2026): the server's screen (`the_server`), shown once after the first face and from the menu; the strip over every screen, read from her node at each change (`ui.the_strip`); on a contact, what came back of the last question about the extensions in common, beside it.
- **WEB-19** **Her node, as the screens see it** (UI-1, USR-9, GR-3, ADP-7, DOM-17) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 22.1): `HerNode` (`her_node/`): the screens' reads in `ui`'s words (her faces, the face she acts as, her contacts, a contact's card, her ticket ready with its link, the extensions as the screens show them, the offers, what her node offers a bond, an activity, those in progress, a draft, the letters held, what happened without a fact), what a gesture asks (`asks`, one of `ui`'s `what_it_asks` handed to the use case of `user` of its name, resolved once `user` has it, rejected on a refusal so that the screens tell it), and `when_it_changes(tell)`, so that the screens read it again. **Over the phone's ports** (`her_node_over(wiring)`, a pure module exported by the package's main index; proved in `memory/test/screens/her_node.test.ts` over a phone of the stack): its wiring is what the composition root has (step 25): the clock; `draws_an_id`, the operation ids of `user`'s commands (`ids_drawn_at_random`); `user`'s ports, typed by its use cases (`zz.CommandUseCasePort`, `zz.ReadUseCasePort`); the reads of `self` (`the_reads_of_self`, USR-9); the ticket shown (`the_ticket_shown`, its link drawn by WEB-1 at `the_page`); the names of the extensions installed (the composition's), each read by `an_extension`; the news (the letters held, the kernel's and the trees' traces, the extensions' notices, and `when_written`); the node's log. `self`'s entries are parsed by each read leaf's own schema, then by `ui`'s, linked by exhaustive maps (`satisfies Record<…>` over the handshake's way, an extension's state, its points; a switch over the confirmation), so that a case `self` adds fails to compile here; the offers are built from what her node offers each contact. A command's answer is typed over its four outcomes: anything but `applied` is thrown, as a read that gives no snapshot, so that the screens tell it. A change is every fact after the log is caught up with, followed from its start as the extensions' follower does, and every news written. An operation's id is drawn, never the activity's: the port toward `self` uses the activity's id as `self`'s operation in the same scope (`self/the_user`, USR-4), and the two would meet. **Open for step 25**: `memory`'s phone keeps its traces, notices and letters held in arrays and tells no one when it writes one, so `when_written` is not proved; the phone's composition tells it (a hook where they are written), and gives the held parts, which `phone(...)` does not expose. Closed at step 25 (PWA-1): `when_written`, and the held parts from the node's storage. Rejected: the screens reading `user`'s and `self`'s ports themselves (every screen would speak the ports' words: the link is written once, exhaustive, GR-3's form); her node read again at a pace (a timer the screens would set, WEB-4, and a change seen late). **Since the final review** (UI-5, 22 Nov 2026): `the_extensions_in_common_with(bond)`, the first entry of `self`'s `the_questions_asked_of_a_bond` (the last one asked) in `ui`'s words, `not_asked` when none; `the_relay()`, the configuration's address and how the relay client last met it (`the_relay: { address, as_met }` in the wiring, `TheRelayAsMet`, WEB-6: not met yet is as if it answered), a change of it told as any change.
- **WEB-20** **The words of the screens** (UI-5; `CLAUDE.md`, "Language") [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (part 22.1): each screen declares the shape of its words (`<Screen>Texts`; the whole, `screens/texts.ts`), and the phone fills it in Italian, one file per screen (`phone/src/texts/screens/`), each `satisfies` its shape: a word missing fails to compile, another language is another set of files. A case of her words is a typed record over its ADT (how an activity ended, why a letter is held, what an extension asks, the camera...), so that a new case fails to compile until it has words; what a kernel's leaf said is told after how it ended when the phone has words for its event, else how it ended alone. The handshake in person's bond made says "di persona" only when her node said `you_met_in_person` (`the_words_of_a_bond_made`: `bonded_in_person`, else `bonded`, at a distance as her node measured it, BND-10; Alessandro, 21 Nov 2026). The words confirmed by Alessandro (22 Nov 2026), to be reviewed on the phones at step 27, with his changes: the invitation's «Se accetti, è tra i tuoi contatti; tu sarai tra i suoi quando gli arriva la tua presentazione.»; a bond made «Nuovo contatto: si presenta come …» (with «, di persona.», «, a distanza.»: the name is the one they introduce themselves as, never an identity claimed); «Elimina» for «Togli»; how an activity ended as the screens say it, without «L’estensione ha risposto» (an extension's end in its own words, WEB-22); the strip, the answer of the extensions in common, the server (UI-5); «Condividi il link», «Copia il link», «Link copiato» (WEB-18). Words written since, confirmed by Alessandro [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (`[Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]` in the texts), to be reviewed on the phones at step 27 as the others: the answer on a contact's card, waiting, in common («In comune: …», «Nessuna estensione in comune.») and with no answer (`the_answer`), and a bond made already («Era già tra i tuoi contatti: si presenta come …», `bonded_already`, as the bond made). - **WEB-21** **The screens proved in a DOM of test** (TST-1, WEB-5, WEB-18) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026; the dependency: Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (part 22.1): the stories of `ui`'s README rendered in `phone/test/screens/`, with the Italian words, over a node of test (what her node reads, kept in memory; what the screens hand it, recorded; its activities in progress until a story ends them) and a device of test (ids drawn in the order a story gives them; Saturday 21 November 2026, 10:00 in Rome; a camera a story feeds), in a vitest project of their own: Solid compiled for the browser (`vite-plugin-solid`, the phone's already, now the root's too) in a DOM of test, `happy-dom` 21.0.4 (MIT, maintained, no install script; a dev dependency of the root, where vitest runs, and of each package whose tests run in it, `phone` and `adapters-web` [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]; no happy-dom in the Node project: a page a test there gives is typed by the parts it uses, `ARealmsDocument`, never a cast, and the realm's frame test runs in the DOM project, PWA-6), real timers, every wait bounded (two seconds).
