- **WEB-22** **The extension points, rendered** (EXT-16, UI-4, USR-2, USR-3, PNG-4; `CLAUDE.md`, "Solid") [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (part 22.2): the kernel's mark and the points it holds are parts of the screens (`screens/parts.tsx`): `TheMark`, a section with the extension's name and a border around what it holds (her contacts' screen of a bond, the extension's own screen, a page, what happened: one mark, one look); `ThePoints`, for a mark `ui` gives (`the_marks_at`), each **action** a gesture the kernel draws with the extension's label, off while its activity is in progress (UI-3), its course told beside it, the end the extension answered in the extension's words for its event, any other end in the kernel's (USR-3); each **slot** a frame placed in the mark, told the place (WEB-23); each **page** a gesture that opens it (`a_page`, UI-4). So ping pong is rendered: on Anna's screen of her bond with Bruno, offered both ways, ping pong's mark, "Estensione «Ping pong»", with "Ping"; tapped, `use_an_action` on the bond, the gesture off until it ends; its pong told "pong!", no response "nessuna risposta", a ping called off "ping annullato" ([Q, A, 22 Nov 2026], PNG-2's `called_off`), in ping pong's words; the label "Ping" confirmed with them (A, 22 Nov 2026). Rejected: the points drawn by each screen (three copies of one rule, EXT-16's "one rule for every extension point"); the action's end told in the kernel's words alone ("L'estensione ha risposto": what the extension answered is its own, EXT-16).
  - **The words of an extension** [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (EXT-16; the form an extension's author writes): the texts the kernel draws for an extension, as data, so that no code of the extension runs to draw them: `boundary.the_words_of_an_extension`, `{ name, actions: { <action>: <label> }, slots: { <slot>: <label> }, pages: { <page>: <label> }, events: { <event>: <words> } }` (the extension's name the user reads; its points' labels by the manifest's keys; the words of its own events on `self`, the base events, the wait over and the closings being the kernel's, USR-3), one file per language in its `texts/` (`packages/extensions/ping_pong/texts/it.json`, exported by its package), which the phone parses with `boundary`'s schema and gives the screens by the extension's name (`texts.screens.extensions`). **Written as the manifest is** (A, 22 Nov 2026): a typed module of the extension, `texts/it.ts`, `export const words = { … } satisfies boundary.Words<typeof manifest, typeof registry>`, whose type asks the name, a label for every action, slot and page of the declaration and words for every own event of the leaves its actions invoke on `self`, by the registry the generator writes (read by its shape: `boundary` imports no library, GR-10), so that a word missing does not compile; the lint asks the `satisfies` of every module of an extension's `texts/`, as of its `manifest.ts` (`the_rule_of_a_manifest`, EXT-10); the build writes the JSON from it (`pnpm --filter @q-mino/ping_pong words`, `scripts/words.ts`, as `scripts/manifest.ts` writes `manifest.json`), which a test keeps equal to the module (`test/words.test.ts`). The kernel reads the JSON only: the phone and the screens import no module of the extension to draw. The screens draw the mark's title by the words' `name` ("Estensione «Ping pong»"), each point by its label, an action's end by its event's words; **a word missing** (an extension the app has no words for, a point or an event its JSON lacks) is drawn as a fault, by the manifest's name it is for, in place of the whole text that would hold it («ping»: manca la parola dell’estensione, `a_word_missing`, `in_its_words`; [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]), never silently that name; and an extension's end has no kernel's words (no «L’estensione ha risposto»): its own words, else that fault. The screens that showed a manifest's ids show the extension's words, the fault where they lack (Alessandro, 22 Nov 2026, the first round's finding): the extensions' list, the heading of an extension's screen, the notice of an activation («cosa chiede «Ping pong»»), the places it asks («Un’azione «Ping» …», a slot's, a page's), a contact's extensions. **An alternative definition**, kept: the JSON is the contract the kernel reads, so an author may also write it by hand, checked by `boundary`'s schema and a test (ping pong's form until 22 Nov 2026); the typed module is how ping pong writes it. Rejected: the module the kernel would import and run to read the words (EXT-16: no code of the extension runs to draw); the labels as a key of the manifest (`actions: { ping: { on, invokes, label } }`: a key that changes the manifest's form, and words of one language in what the user accepts at activation).
- **WEB-23** **A frame, placed** (EXT-13, EXT-16, EXT-4, EXT-2; the Checks of step 12 for step 22) [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026] (part 22.2): **its document** is the file of the extension's bundle its point `shows`, given by the composition root as text (`TheScreensWiring.documents`, by extension, point and name: the composition's, step 25), placed as `srcdoc` after **the kernel's policy** (`frames/`, `the_document_of_a_frame`: `<!doctype html><meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy" content="…">` and then the file as it is, so that whatever it holds is parsed below the policy, which a later one can only narrow): `default-src 'none'`, its inline scripts and styles, images, fonts and media as `data:`, no form, no base. **Its sandbox** is scripts only (`allow-scripts`): its origin is opaque. **Its transport** (`screens/a_frame.tsx`): told its place (`placed`, the boundary's form of `ui`'s place) once loaded; it hears only messages whose source is that frame's window, each through the frame's fence (`through_a_frames_fence`, EXT-4) with whether its extension is active, as the screen read it, and whether its screen is the one shown (`TheScreenIsShown`, the last of the way, UI-1); served, her node carries it out (`a_frame_asks`) and its events are posted back by the exchange's id, then `done` (a flow that fails ends with `done`: the frame learns nothing more); refused, the kernel's trace (`a_frame_is_fenced`, `a_frame_fenced`, shown in what happened, USR-8). A screen hidden stops the flows its frames follow; a frame removed hears nothing more. **What it did** is told under it, in the mark: the leaf it asked, when, "da sé" (EXT-16: an extension's activity, never her gesture). **Her node's door** (`her_node_over`, `the_frames`): a frame's leaf invoked on its extension's leaves mounted on `self` as the phone hosts it, as the user (`self` is her persona: the frame acts on her node with her hat, which is why the kernel shows its acts apart), a command under an operation the kernel draws in the scope `self/a_frame`, never the frame's id (it pairs its exchange only: a frame that sends an id again does not replay an operation); a read with none. Rejected: the frame's id as the operation (a frame could name an operation of another frame of its extension); a frame served on a screen hidden (EXT-4: only while shown); the frame's window reached through `window` (WEB-5: the window is the iframe's own document's view, never a global).
  - **Measured in a browser** (21 Nov 2026; the probe `packages/adapters-web/test/frames/in_a_browser/`, served by vite on the machine, opened in the desktop app's pane, Chromium 156 on Linux; to be measured again on the phones, step 27): a frame with the kernel's policy and sandbox reaches no storage of the app's origin (local and session storage, IndexedDB, cookies, the caches, the service worker: `SecurityError`; its origin `null`); no network (`fetch` refused; a socket, a beacon and an image blocked by the policy, each a violation); opens no window (`window.open` gives `null`) and does not navigate the top (`SecurityError`). **WebRTC stays open**: a peer connection is constructed in the frame (its traffic not measured: no address outside the machine was used); no policy closes it, as EXT-13 says, and the notice of an activation tells it (USR-7). **A frame navigating itself**: to a `data:` document it does, and that document keeps the kernel's policy (its `fetch` refused); to a page of the app's origin, or by a `<meta>` refresh, it did not reach the page, even with no policy of the app's (the browser's own protection of a local address, likely: not conclusive for a public address); **with the app's `frame-src 'none'` every navigation of a frame is stopped** (`data:`, the app's origin, a refresh, each a violation of the app's page), while the frames placed as `srcdoc` are placed and run. So the phone's policy (step 25) has `frame-src 'none'`, which closes a frame's navigation, and the flaw left is WebRTC. **And its `script-src` must let the frames' scripts run**: a `srcdoc` document inherits the policy of the app's page, so with `script-src 'self'` alone (measured: `default-src 'self'`) no frame's script runs, and a frame says nothing. Open for step 25: the hashes of the frames' inline scripts in the app's policy, computed at the phone's build from the bundles (proposed), against `'unsafe-inline'` (the app's own scripts would lose the policy's guard). Closed at step 25: the hashes (PWA-4).
- **WEB-24** **The points proved** (TST-1, WEB-21) [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 22.2): stories 12, 13, 21 (its points) and 22 of `ui`'s README, and the frame's transport, rendered in `phone/test/screens/the_points.test.tsx` over the node of test (which records what the frames asked and what the fence refused, and answers a frame as a story gives) and `happy-dom`, whose frame has a window the kernel posts to and that a story posts from, but runs no document's script: the frame's own document runs in the browser (WEB-23's probe, and the phones at step 27). The door over a phone of the stack in `memory/test/screens/her_node.test.ts`: ping pong active and loaded, a frame's `send_a_ping` gives `the_ping_is_sent`, a leaf not on its surface `invalid_request` (`not_a_leaf`), one refused traced. Ping pong's words in `extensions/ping_pong/test/words.test.ts`.

## 7. Configurators

- **CFG-1** A configurator is the composition root (the main): it picks the adapters and wires ports, the inbound interface to the persona, the effect executor to `post`, the reactions, the read port of auth to `bonds`. D88, D92, D104
- **CFG-2** Before the first request it checks what each component requires against what is offered: requirements derived from the code, offers declared by the adapters and proven by the suite; also the `Caller` fields auth reads against those the interface fills, and the frames needed against those written. An unmet requirement is a composition fault: the node does not start. Static wiring is checked by TypeScript; adapters picked by configuration at startup. D104
  - **The shed's configurator, at runtime** [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] (part 7.5; CFG-1, CFG-2, DOM-9, TST-2, TST-3): Shed's `configurator.ts` runs on the oracle, and every scenario runs through it. `shed(cell, release)(world)` composes the store's versions, checked against what it keeps (DOM-22), takes the oracle's adapter, and wires the four trees, the effect executor of `loans` and the consumers of every reaction, so that every check at wiring runs when the shed starts, before any request (CFG-2). It gives the trees' ports, the business's ports by folder (`invoked`: what the trees invoke, and where a test enters a use case no tree is above), the fact log and the versions; and the durable consumers wired, not started, each set to start (`loans_effects()`, `reactions['reaction/damage']()`, by the folder that holds its reactions): the writer runs them (DOM-9), a node that is not the writer does not, and a scenario starts those its story needs, when it says, again after a fall. The world is the runtime's: the members' phones, the clock, and one time that passes (`zz.Elapsed`) for the trees and the executor alike, a timeout of the runtime. The release is a parameter of the test configurator: the majors its code holds (SC-9 gives one that deleted `1/` too early) and the oracle's adapter as a scenario releases it (`MemoryRelease`: `loans`' fragments with a defect, SC-39; `tools`' with a defect or falling at an execution, SC-40, SC-42, for every command of `tools` while it lasts, since a release of an adapter is the node's; its queries streamed by composition, DOM-2). The harness (`scenarios/oracle.ts`) builds the store as a release finds it (its kept log, its state rebuilt, SC-8), fakes the world, and enters the business's use cases as an entry would where no tree is above them; its use case over the round's notice (`alone`, DOM-10) stays its own, never Shed's. Rejected: the consumers started by the configurator (a node that is not the writer would run them, and a scenario could not start the executor at its instant nor again after a fall); every reaction in one set of consumers (a scenario of the damaged returns would run the rounds of notices too, and one of the processes the damage procedure); the harness wiring the shed beside the configurator, as until part 7.4 (two wirings, the configurator's never run: running it found the keeper's tree with no time that passes for the loading its leaf follows, SC-44); a time that passes per consumer (one runtime, one timer).
  - **In the PWA** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (step 25): the start runs every check at wiring, and every manifest, before any consumer and before the screens (PWA-2).
- **CFG-3** Three configurators [Q]: `phone` (the PWA), `relay` (`mailbox`, HTTP interface, storage port; no access logs, declared: MBX-9), `memory` (phone and relay in one process with `mailbox` imported instead of HTTP, still through the relay's personas (PER-19): the stack of the oracles, and the start of the nearby network of the native version).
  - `phone` and `memory` compose the kernel with its preinstalled extensions (ping pong in stage 1), each from its package (EXT-9): `phone` hosts its parts isolated (EXT-13), `memory` in process; `relay` has none. [Q, C, 20 Nov 2026]
- **CFG-4** Adapters never import configurators; domains import no other package. [Q]

### The stack: `memory`

The phones and the relay in one process (CFG-3), the stack of the oracles and the oracle of the adapters (steps 20, 21, 25): step 17, Claude, 21 Nov 2026; the words in the glossary, the scenarios in [`packages/memory/test/stack/README.md`](../packages/memory/test/stack/README.md).

- **STK-1** **A phone in the stack** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (CFG-1, CFG-2, CFG-3, MBX-7): `phone(cell, release)(world)` in `packages/memory/src/phone/`, the composition root of a phone over q-'s oracle: one cell with the safe, the requests, the address book, the office of the extensions and the user's offices, each domain's ports over it (`seen_by`); the post's machine of a face (PST-3, PST-10); the executors of the safe, the post, the address book (STK-2), the office (EXT-8) and `user` (USR-9); the reactions of `reaction/`; the kernel's ports of requests and its reads (F7, EXT-2, EXT-7); auth's lookups (PER-22); the four persona trees by path and the kernel's entry with the bond to answer on (PER-25); the extensions the app carries composed (EXT-4), hosted in process (EXT-13) and mounted on `self` and `known`; the collector (STK-3), the ticket shown (STK-2) and the extensions following the office (STK-4). Everything is wired and checked when it starts (CFG-2): a composition fault of a manifest stops the start. The durable consumers are given wired, not started, each a function to start, as the shed's configurator gives them (CFG-2): the app (`user`'s executor), each office's executor, the reactions, the follower of the office; a phone **on** runs them and collects, a phone **off** runs nothing. The **world**: the clock, the time that passes, the primitives (the safe's machine and the world's random bytes, SEA-5), the post's mailbox port and the collect port (the transports to the relay, STK-5), the extensions the app carries, each its manifest as built and its realm (no extension package is imported in `src`, GR-10: the configurator composes what it is given), the relays its tickets declare. The **release** is the test configurator's, as Shed's `MemoryRelease`: the overrides the stories of the earlier parts need (an introduction kept for the story to carry, the realm's time, a partition's scripted commits, a node whose code changed, F2). The node of the kernel's tests (`test/node/harness.ts`) is built over it: one wiring, run by every story of the kernel. Rejected: the wiring kept in the test harness, as until part 16.2 (two wirings once the stack has its own: CFG-2's reason in Shed); the extensions imported by the phone (steps 21 and 25 vary them). **Amended** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (step 25, PWA-1): an extension of the app runs in process or isolated (an ADT, the configurator's); every extension's composition fault in one message; `when_written` and `trace`; the PWA runs this wiring.
- **STK-2** **The introduction through `post`** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (BND-10, F4, F7, EXT-8; the open point of part 15.1, the side of who introduces herself): the delivery of `send_my_introduction` is a request of the kernel's to the leaf `introduce_myself` of the peer's `unknown` (PER-11), through the kernel's own port of requests, as EXT-8's question (`the_delivery_of_my_introduction`, `adapters-common/src/bonds/`): its identity `{ of: 'the_kernel', effect: 'bonds/address_book/effect/send_my_introduction', id: <the bond's id> }` (PST-2); the bond the opening of their ticket kept (their face its key, their mailbox its D); its input `{ secret, introduced_as, mailbox }`, my side read at the delivery (my face of the bond and its name, my mailbox of it), since a creator reads no state; its time **48 hours**, a ticket's life (BND-8), sent again at each quarter while it waits (PST-6). The effect is `order: 'any'` (F7: a request's effect, its deliveries in flight together), and has no return (nothing comes back to the address book). What came back: `you_are_bonded`, `you_met_in_person`, `the_bond_is_confirmed`, `you_introduced_yourself_already` → delivered; another event or a closing → exhausted, `they answered <event>`; `no_response` → exhausted, `no response in 48 hours`; `too_large_for_a_letter` (PST-12, the kernel's port) → exhausted, `too large for a letter` [Q, C, 23 Nov 2026]; the bond gone, or my side not found → exhausted: each a dead letter, shown (F6, DOM-15). A flaw turned feature: an introduction refused (a ticket used by another face, expired, a secret not its) is answered with nothing, since the holder has no bond with that key to answer on (F7, PER-25): the opener learns it from the dead letter after 48 hours, never at once, and learns no more than that. **The ticket shown** (BND-8, BND-9; `the_ticket_shown`, beside it): `my_ticket` and the safe's signature over its D, in the address book's form `their_ticket`, what a QR and a link carry (WEB-1, WEB-2); none for a ticket not ready. Rejected: one deposit with no response awaited, F4's receipt form (no sending again: a deposit lost after the relay's answer, or a holder's response lost, would leave the opener with no dead letter and no bond); a time of minutes (the holder's phone may stay closed for hours); the delivery in the address book's domain (a call to the world is an adapter's, D108); the opener's confirmation recorded from the response (a return of the address book, a command of its own: open, with its case).
- **STK-3** **The collector** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (DOM-17, BND-11, PER-25; `the_collector`, `adapters-common/src/entry/`): one pass over the mailboxes the node collects, each with its R from the safe (SEA-4), read through the **collect port** (`ACollect`: a mailbox's D, R and relays → the deposits the relay keeps there, each its id and envelope, in the relay's order; through the desk in the stack, STK-5, over HTTP at step 20); each deposit not seen yet goes to the kernel's entry as `{ came_to, envelope }`. One the entry throws on stays unseen, and the mailbox's next deposits wait behind it; a collect that fails leaves its mailbox for the next pass and the others go on; the first fault is thrown once the pass is over. What it has seen is the runtime's: a phone started again collects again every deposit the relay keeps, and the duplicate branch meets them (DOM-11: a request answered from its recorded fact, its response deposited again under the same operation, PST-11, `kept_already`; a response taken again, the duplicate of `take_a_response`). Rejected: a position per mailbox (the relay's read is composed, MBX-8: a resume is `position_unavailable`); what it has seen kept in the node's storage (with the storage's step, 21, if a measure of the readings again asks for it).
- **STK-4** **The extensions follow the office** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (EXT-7, EXT-15, USR-9; `the_extensions_follow_the_office`, `adapters-common/src/extensions/`): a consumer of the node's fact log, at its start and after each fact of `extensions/activations`: each hosted extension active and not running is loaded, one running and no longer active unloaded; one stopped after its second fall is loaded again only on a new activation (EXT-15). Then, at each look, the calling off of the port of requests (F7, PST-14; Alessandro's decision, 21 Nov 2026), its wiring's `the_calling_off`: the requests in flight of an extension not active, or not offered to the bond they went on, called off in the phone's post, so that it sends them again no more and their deliveries read the refusal kept, at once if they wait, at the extension's next load if it was unloaded; a fall between a gesture and its look is made good at the next start [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026]. The phone of the stack wires it over its post and the address book's bonds, the bond of a request by my face and their key (`my_bonds`). Rejected: a consumer of its own for the calling off (a second follower of the office's facts, for one look). Test: `packages/memory/test/extensions/ping_pong.test.ts` (a ping in flight called off at a withdrawal, and at a deactivation). The port toward `self` records the office's facts only (USR-9). It reads the log from the first fact and follows it from where it is (DOM-17), never from one position twice. Rejected: the port toward `self` loading the extension itself (a second hand on the hosting, beside the start); a reaction of `reaction/` (the hosting is no domain's).
- **STK-5** **The stack and its transports** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (CFG-3, PER-19, MBX-6, ADP-4; `packages/memory/src/stack/`): `a_stack(relay_cell, release)({ clock, elapsed, hash })` gives one relay over its cell (MBX-7, its waits' executor to start) and makes phones over it (STK-1), each with its two transports, which a test decorates and steps 20, 21 and 25 replace. `the_relay_s_transports(desk, { clock, hash })`: the post's **mailbox port** deposits through the desk's `depositor`, the `Caller` from what it shows, nothing (MBX-6), its version the depositor's, the post's operation in the depositor's scope (`the_scope_of_a_deposit`), the relay's clock, the relay's answer told in the post's words (`kept`, `kept_already`, `refused` with its event, PST-10); the **collect port** collects through the desk's `holder`, the `Caller` from the collect secret through the desk's interface (R → D, V5), the first snapshot's deposits, the stream let go. `mailbox` is imported only through the desk (CFG-3). A transport carries and tells, nothing more (ADP-4). Rejected: the phones calling the relay's business ports (past the desk, which the relay's HTTP interface never is, MBX-9); a stack that starts its consumers itself (a story could not turn a phone off).
- **STK-6** **The scenarios of stage 1, the oracle of the adapters** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (TST-1, TST-3, TST-4; step 17's checks): `stories_of_the_stack(name, factory)` in `packages/memory/test/stack/`, one suite parametrized by a factory (the phones' transports and the primitives), run by `stack.test.ts` on the oracle with the fake primitives and over libsodium's (SEA-9); steps 20, 21 and 25 run it with their factories, as its conformance (TST-1). Seven scenarios, gestures asked as her screens will ask them, through `user`'s activities (USR-2): the first face; the crossed QR; the link; ping pong activated on both sides; a ping and its pong; a ping to a node that stays silent (F8); a node off told from a silence by the kernel's question (F8, "Another"; EXT-8). Each runs under two **fault plans** of the transports (TST-4, ADP-5: decorators): **none**, and **lossy**, a deterministic schedule per phone and transport, deposits lost (before the relay), doubled, their answer lost, aborted, collects doubled (each deposit twice) and lost; under lossy each scenario that uses the mailbox asserts the faults met, and the ping's scenario that the server's response was aborted, so that the duplicate branch answers its request sent again. The story's clock moves, every wait asked for then over; a phone settles by bounded rounds of collect and quiet, on real timers. Rejected: the scenarios written once per adapter (TST-1); faults drawn at random (a scenario that fails once in a hundred runs, with no seed to replay); the gestures through the business's commands (the screen's path is `user`'s, D76). **Amended** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (step 20): the factory gives the **relay of a story**, made in the story's world (its clock, the time that passes, the hash of R), with the phones' transports to it and its end at the story's (`ARelayOfTheStory`: on the oracle the stack's relay and its transports in process; at step 20 the relay served over HTTP, WEB-7); and a round waits, once the event loop is quiet, for every call of the transports to answer, within two seconds (a request over HTTP takes turns of the network); each test of the stack's two files has fifteen seconds, vitest's default five being near the longest scenario's time under the whole suite's load. Rejected: the factory's transports alone, as step 17 wrote them (a relay made outside the story would not follow its clock: its seven days and its waits on another time). **Amended** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (part 21.2): the factory gives each phone's **storage** too (`AStorageUnderTest`: a phone's storage, WEB-16, with what a round reads of it), a new oracle's cell unless given, so that `on_indexeddb.test.ts` runs the seven scenarios with every phone on IndexedDB; and the lossy schedule's **phase is set at a mark of a story** (`next_deposit_meets`): the ping's story sets Bruno's next deposit to abort before Anna pings, so that his response's deposit aborts whatever the rounds before took (on IndexedDB a round takes more turns of the event loop, the executors send again a different number of times, and the schedule, counted by deposits, met the abort on another letter). Rejected: the abort's assertion left to the count (a scenario whose fault depends on an adapter's timing proves nothing on another adapter); a schedule per letter (the transports carry envelopes they cannot read).

### The PWA: `phone`

The PWA's composition root (step 25, Claude, 21 Nov 2026; the words in the glossary): `packages/phone/src/node/` (the start), `src/realms/` (the extensions' realms), `build/` (the realms' bundles and the app's policy), `src/App.tsx` and `src/main.tsx`.

- **PWA-1** **One wiring of a phone** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (CFG-1, CFG-3, CFG-4, STK-1, WEB-16): the PWA composes the kernel with STK-1's `phone(storage, release)(world)` of `memory`, given the browser's storage (`a_phone_s_storage`, `phone/src/node/storage.ts`: WEB-14's modules over the node's database), the relay's HTTP transports (WEB-6), libsodium's primitives (SEA-9), the device's clock and time (WEB-4), the extensions it carries isolated (PWA-3), and no release (the kernel's adapters as they are). So the stack's stories and the app run one wiring, STK-1's reason. `phone` imports `@q-mino/memory`: a configurator importing another's composition, not an adapter a configurator (CFG-4 holds); the oracle's modules come into the app's bundle only as far as the wiring names them. Rejected: a second wiring in `phone` (the app's, never run by the stories); the wiring moved to a package of its own (a package for one function whose stories are `memory`'s: with the native version, if it asks); the phone's storage put together in `memory` (its source has no DOM, and the storage's types are the browser's, WEB-16), so the assembly is written twice, in `phone/src/node/` and `memory/test/indexeddb/`, 25 lines, a declared cost.
  - **An extension of the app, in process or isolated** (EXT-13; STK-1 amended): `AnExtensionOfTheApp` is an ADT, `{ runs: 'in_process', realm }` (its side, loaded in the kernel's realm: `memory`, the oracle) or `{ runs: 'isolated', transport }` (a transport to a realm of its own, which keeps its own clock and time: `phone`). Rejected: the transport in the release (how a realm is reached is the configurator's world, not a test's override).
  - **Every extension's fault at once** (CFG-2, EXT-4): the phone composes every extension the app carries and, with a fault, stops with every extension's message, a line each; until step 25 the first extension's fault stopped it alone, so a second's showed only at the next start, against CFG-2's one message. A fix of step 17.
  - **The news told** (WEB-19's open point): the phone tells whoever listens when it writes a trace of the kernel's or of a tree, or a notice (`when_written`), and writes a frame's fence's entry in the kernel's trace (`trace`, EXT-4, WEB-23); her node's held parts are the node's storage's (`held_parts`, WEB-15).
- **PWA-2** **The start in a tab** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (CFG-2, DOM-9, WEB-10, WEB-11, STK-3; `the_start(browser, configuration, time)(how)`): an ADT, each outcome a screen (F6). `another_tab`: another tab writes the node, nothing is composed and the database is closed; the screen offers "use here" (`use_here`). `it_does_not_start`, with why: the phone's composition threw (a mechanism's fault at its wiring, the versions kept, a manifest), the writer let go and the database closed; the message is shown whole. `running`: her node (WEB-19), what the browser said of the storage (WEB-11, told unless `persistent`), `stops`, and `stopped`, which settles when the node stops by itself: another tab took it (`used_elsewhere`), or a newer release asked to close the database (`closed_for_a_new_release`); the screen says which, and offers use here or to open the page again.
  - **The composition check is the library's, at each mechanism's wiring** (CFG-2: each gives every fault of its wiring in one message), and the manifests' (EXT-4): the start runs them all before any consumer starts and before the screens are mounted, so before the first request. No new piece of the library gathers them: a mechanism is wired over what those before it gave (the versions before the ports, the ports before the trees), so a fault stops what depends on it, and each tells its own whole (LIB-3; the plan's "a library piece, at its trigger" is met by the pieces there since steps 3–7). Rejected: a function of the library that runs the wirings and gathers their faults (there is nothing to gather past the first that fails, the rest being wired over it); a fault typed by the library at every wiring (an ADT where the start shows only the message).
  - **Running**: every executor and every reaction started, the app (`user`'s executor, USR-9), the extensions following the office (STK-4), and the collector passing every **5 seconds** while the app is open (no push in stage 1: a pong comes well within its 60 seconds); the post's executor started again at the pass after a deposit's abort (PST-10), which a decorator of its mailbox port tells (ADP-5); the pause cut short when the node stops. Stopping: the consumers stopped, the extensions unloaded, the writer let go, the database closed.
  - **The configuration**: the database's name (`cumino`); the relay (`Q_RELAY` at the build; locally `https://relay.cumino.test`, an address of no relay, RFC 2606: the node starts and collects nothing, step 24 being A's); the page of the ticket's links (the app's base URL, WEB-1); the extensions carried; a partition's size (1,000,000 characters, the oracle's, WEB-12). Rejected: the collector driven by the screens (her node does not know the relay); a pace per mailbox (one relay in stage 1).
- **PWA-3** **An extension's realm in a frame** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (EXT-9, EXT-13, EXT-15, GR-10, WEB-23): `a_realm_in_a_frame(page)(script)` (`adapters-web/src/frames/`) is the transport to a hidden frame the kernel adds to the page, sandboxed with scripts only (an opaque origin), its document the kernel's policy and then the realm's one inline script (`the_document_of_a_realm`; a `</script` in it written `<\/script`); it hears only its frame's window and hands what it hears as it came (the hosting parses it, EXT-2), holds what the kernel sends until the frame has loaded, and unloaded removes the frame. **The realm's script** is the extension's configurator wired to the frame's window (`phone/src/realms/`: `a_realm_in_this_frame`, its fall told at an error, an unhandled rejection or a message its side throws on, EXT-15; ping pong's with the frame's own clock and time, written there, so that its bundle holds the extension and its library only), bundled by the phone's build into one IIFE (`build/the_realms.ts`, Vite's library mode, `virtual:q-realms`): the bundle EXT-9 asks the PWA's build to make for the extensions preinstalled. Measured in the desktop app's browser pane (Chromium, the preview, 21 Nov 2026): ping pong activated, its frame placed, hidden, `allow-scripts`, its script running (a probe frame with the same document called its storage), the frame removed at a "use here" from another tab and placed again at the next start. Rejected: a worker as the realm (of the app's origin, EXT-13); the script as a file of the app's origin loaded by the frame (an opaque origin's `script-src` is still the app's policy, and a file's URL would open it to every file of the app); the kernel's adapters in the realm's bundle (a second copy of the node's code in the extension's realm).
- **PWA-4** **The app's policy** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (EXT-13, WEB-23; part 22.2's open point): a `<meta>` Content-Security-Policy first in the page's head, written by the build (the server of development, which injects scripts of its own, has none): `default-src 'self'`; `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` and each realm's script by its hash (SHA-256 of its text as the frame holds it, computed at the build from the bundle); `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`; `img-src 'self' data: blob:`; `connect-src 'self'` and the relay's origin; `frame-src 'none'`; `object-src 'none'`; `base-uri 'none'`; `form-action 'none'`. A frame's document inherits it (`srcdoc`, WEB-23) beside its own (EXT-13), so a frame runs only the scripts the build hashed. Measured (the preview): the realm's script runs, the same document with one statement more does not (another hash); libsodium's WebAssembly compiles only with `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` (without it the node did not start, and said why, PWA-2); `frame-src 'none'` stops a frame's navigation (WEB-23). Rejected: `'unsafe-inline'` for scripts (the app's own scripts would lose the policy's guard, WEB-23); `'unsafe-eval'` (WebAssembly needs `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` only); the policy as an HTTP header (the page is static files on a host not chosen yet, step 24); hashes for styles (the frames' styles are inline by EXT-13's policy, and the screens' styles are set through the CSSOM, which no policy guards). The documents of slots and pages, and their scripts' hashes, come with the first extension that has one: ping pong has none (`documents` gives none).
- **PWA-5** **The service worker and the update with notice** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (F6, EXT-10): `vite-plugin-pwa`, `injectManifest`, `registerType: 'prompt'` (the scaffold): the new worker downloads and waits, she is told and chooses; "Aggiorna" stops the node first (PWA-2: consumers, extensions, writer, database), then the new worker takes over and the page reloads; "Più tardi" leaves the old code running. The precache holds the app's files, the realms' scripts inside its bundle. The words of the start, of the storage and of the update are Alessandro's (`phone/src/texts/it.ts`) [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026], to be reviewed on the phones at step 27: the update's notice «Se aggiorni, Cumino si chiude e si riapre con la versione nuova. Un’estensione con un manifesto nuovo si ferma finché non lo accetti.» (no promise on the storage, which the browser keeps at best, WEB-11); a composition fault «Cumino non parte: un’estensione non si compone.», then a sentence for each fault, by its extension's name and its case (`ACompositionFault`, its sentences written by Claude, confirmed by Alessandro [Q, A, 22 Nov 2026]), the English message under «Perché». So the phone's composition throws its faults typed (`TheExtensionsDoNotCompose`, `memory`'s phone: each extension with its faults and the message), and the start tells why it does not start as an ADT (`WhyItDoesNotStart`: `an_extension_does_not_compose` with each fault, or `a_fault`, each with the message). The server's screen (UI-5) shows the configuration's relay, given to her node with the relay client's meetings (`the_relay`, WEB-19); the device gives the page's `navigator` to share a link (WEB-18). Not measured in a browser here: the desktop app's pane registers no service worker (an unknown error fetching the script, a page with no policy as well): the phones at step 27. Rejected: an update applied at the next start with no notice (an update may change the manifests, what she accepted, EXT-10).
- **PWA-6** **The PWA proved** [Q, C, 21 Nov 2026] (TST-1; the step's "done"): `phone/test/node/the_start.test.ts` (Node: fake-indexeddb, Node's Web Locks, a happy-dom page): a node composes with ping pong preinstalled and off, and runs; a second tab composes nothing until it takes the node, the first then stopped, used elsewhere; two extensions with wrong manifests (a boundary major the kernel does not speak, a port it does not offer): the node does not start, one message with both, the writer let go, and the release that composes starts after on the same node. `adapters-web/test/frames/a_realm_in_a_frame.test.ts` (the frame, its document, its messages, its unload). `phone/test/build.test.ts` (the policy's form). By hand in the preview (`vite preview` within 15 minutes, closed): the first face; ping pong activated, its realm loaded; "use here"; the database deleted under the node (`closed_for_a_new_release`). Left to the phones (step 27): the service worker and the update, a ping between two phones over a relay (step 24; its CORS answered by the relay, MBX-9 [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026]), the camera's stream.

## 8. Tests and conformance

- **TST-1** Domain tests run on the in-memory implementation. Run again against each adapter they are its conformance tests: suites parametrized by an adapter factory. A domain writes no separate conformance tests. D102
- **TST-2** The oracle is an in-memory state adapter that knows every domain, one module per domain, built from the domains' `dsl` functions over their own values. It is the reference interpreter (V10): what a command and a read mean, with the interpreter rules (LIB-9); the other adapters of the port are compared with it, never adapter with adapter. Recommended, not required (V10): with the business domains isolated (GR-3) it is where the links between domains are typed (a fragment of `loans` over the state of `tools` imports the types of `tools`), and its imports show who reads whom (GR-1). The tests of the fragments that cross domains become one conformance suite run on the oracle and on each adapter. The `memory` configurator is the stack where the oracles run. D103, D102, D38 [Q]
- **TST-3** Compare only what is observable: state through the read port, never storage contents; positions only by order ("at least up to X"). Clock and ids are injected. D102, D48, D57
- **TST-4** Allowed deviations are injected, not hoped absent: loss, duplication and abort on transports (mandatory), resume and "position no longer available"; redeliveries on consumers. D103, D73, D69, D89
  - Since part 5.2 [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: the scenarios' harness injects, into the oracle's streams and its fact log, a position the cell keeps no longer (`forget`) and every fact delivered twice (`redelivered`); a resume is a read from a position. The consumers' redeliveries come with them (7.1, 7.3), the transports' loss and abort with the transports. Since part 7.1 the executor's: every entry the cell's outbox gives twice (`redelivered`), an executor that falls before saving its position (`saves_its_position: false`) and is started again, a network down (`transient`) or that falls (a technical abort, thrown), each scenario's world as the oracle fakes it (`adapter/memory/reminders.ts`, DOM-14, "The effect executor, at runtime"). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] Since part 7.2 the holds' (DOM-14, "The executor's holds, at runtime"): an executor that falls with letters and entries waiting, started again over a position it did not save, holds each once; one that falls after a redelivery, before the entries behind the letter (`network('delivers', 'falls')`), delivers them when started again; every entry given twice, each held once. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] Since part 7.3 the consumers of the reactions' (DOM-16, "The consumers of the reactions, at runtime"): every fact the cell's log gives twice (`redelivered`), consumers that fall before saving their position (`saves_its_position: false`) and start again from the first fact, the adapter they invoke falling at an execution (a technical abort, thrown), and released with a defect (SC-42); one stay, each part held once. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026] Since part 7.4 the processes' (DOM-20, "The steps of a process, at runtime"): the consumers of the reactions that move a process on fallen before saving their positions and started again, and every fact given twice: each step the duplicate branch, R5 cancelled once, one notice per member; an adapter released with a defect that reads an instance's transition as holding nowhere, the instance passed over. [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
- **TST-5** Every command adapter passes: scenarios, differential (against the oracle, when there is one), concurrency (the result equals some serial order, ranges included); fact atomic with its mutation; a rejection leaves no fact; a duplicate returns the recorded fact at the same position, a different fingerprint is rejected; ids are never reassigned. The mid-write failure test is optional. D21, D79, D95, D96, D48
  - Since part 4.2 [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]: requests sent at the same time are run on a fresh shed, and every serial order of them on another each, and the outcomes at the same time must be those of one order (`scenarios/serial.ts`, a request written as a function of a shed, its operation named so that it is the same in each); SC-11 and a property over arbitrary batches (`scenarios/routing.test.ts`). On the oracle, a serial writer, it holds by construction; it is the conformance check another adapter will run (TST-1).
- **TST-6** An adapter is tested exactly on the capabilities it declares. D110
- **TST-7** Stability: the stable functions of the library are re-read against a corpus at every release; the JSON Schema export of every exported signature and fact is snapshotted and diffed in CI. D100, D101, D75 Since part 3.6 the snapshot is each major's registry as JSON Schema, `_registry.json`, written by the generator and diffed by `pnpm generate:check`; a tree released to clients is compared with `main`'s by direction (DOM-24, DOM-25). [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]
- **TST-8** Business correctness (cores, branches, predicates) belongs to the domain tests; the suite checks only what is mechanical. D102
- **TST-9** When the suite runs and how inputs are generated are left to q-: on every pull request (`pnpm check`). In stage 1 the fast-check arbitraries are written by hand; generation from the zod schemas comes when hand-written arbitraries start duplicating them. D102, D104 [Q, plan]
  - The routing's properties (part 4.2, [Q, C, 19 Nov 2026]): over `tools`' life cycle on the oracle, arbitrary sequences of Luca's and Anna's requests under a few operations, so that they repeat: one operation records one fact; a retry of it is that fact, at its position, another request under it `identity_already_used`; a rejection records nothing and names every branch of its command (none held); positions are never given twice. The fingerprint's own properties beside its corpus (LIB-11). What a branch decides is the business's, tested by its scenarios (TST-8).

Checks:

| automatic                                                                                                    | review                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| dependency-cruiser: arrows, no cycles, only `index.ts`, no Node or other packages in domains, npm only `zod` | an adapter with an `if` on a business rule (ADP-1)                                                                              |
| TypeScript: domains without DOM and Node                                                                     | a semantic rule without its test cases (LIB-4)                                                                                  |
| JSON Schema snapshots of signatures and facts (TST-7)                                                        | a retry that mints a new operation id (DOM-12)                                                                                  |
| transport and consumer suites contain the injected deviations (TST-4)                                        | a fact translation that reads state or clock, or invents a value (DOM-22)                                                       |
| conformance tests import no storage API (TST-3)                                                              | a reaction that reads changing state or invokes more than one command (DOM-16); `user` using a persona other than `self` (GR-7) |

The cruiser's rules on npm imports [Q, A, 23 Nov 2026] (`domains-npm-allowlist`, the npm half of `generator-core-no-node`; `boundary-imports-zod-only` since part 12.3, GR-10): the cruiser sees the npm modules and does not follow them (`doNotFollow`, not `exclude`, which hid them from every rule, so that these never fired). A test proves they fire: `packages/domains/test/the_cruiser.test.ts` runs the repository's configuration over a fixture laid out as the repository (`test/cruiser/packages/...`), one import refused per rule and one allowed (`zod` in a domain), where no rule of the normal run reaches it. [Q, C, 23 Nov 2026] Rejected: a script of `pnpm check` reading the cruiser's output (a second runner beside the tests); the fixture at the real paths, excluded from the normal run (an exclusion that hides it from the rules it proves).

- **TST-10** **The lint's heap** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (TST-9; CLAUDE.md, TypeScript and the graph): `pnpm lint` runs ESLint's bin under Node with a heap of 8 GiB (`node --max-old-space-size=8192 node_modules/eslint/bin/eslint.js .`), one value from step 16, where it first rose, to the top of the stack. The lint is type-aware (`strictTypeChecked`, ESLint's project service over every tree of the repository), and holds their types at once: its peak, V8's heap measured with `--trace-gc` on 21 Nov 2026 with the final review's changes, 4 928 MiB on step 16 (5.1 GiB resident) and 6 139 MiB on step 25 (6.4 GiB resident), past Node's default (about 4 GiB: an abort out of memory, with no finding) and at the edge of the 6 GiB (6 144 MiB) step 16 had until then; the whole lint was about 4.4 GB on step 16 before the final review (PR #33). Rejected: the lint split per package (one program per run, more configuration, for a peak no measure asked to lower); fewer type-aware rules (the strict rules keep casts and `any` out of the trees, CLAUDE.md). Measured again when a step adds a tree or a package to the lint.

## 9. Divergences of q- from the paper

- **V1** No trusted server: every node is the server of itself and the trusted level is my node (F1). The paper reasons on a trusted server side. [Q]
- **V2** `ui` is a pure domain (the screen model) inside `domains`, and only its rendering is an adapter; in the paper the UI is the adapter of the user domain (D76). The paper's rule still holds: `ui` holds only presentational state.
- **V3** `seals`, `post` and `mailbox` are technical domains with their own language and use cases, outside the paper's types (user, persona, auth, business). In the paper the interface speaks the protocol and is deliberately not a domain with rules (D5), and technical concerns become user concepts, not a service domain (D2); q- gives the protocol and the cryptography a domain each. [Q]
- **V4** "Every domain is a package" (D39) is read as a module unit: one package `domains`, a folder per domain, boundaries enforced by tools. [Q] An extension's package is a release unit, not a domain's package: its domains, its adapters and its configurator together (EXT-9), where D39 wants a domain's package without adapters, the adapters and the composition root elsewhere; the purity of its tree is kept by the cruiser, as Shed's and `domains`' (GR-10). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] Rejected: D39 dropped from GR-10's sources (its reason, a pure domain shippable apart, is what the package keeps, by the cruiser).
- **V5** The collect secret R of a mailbox is a bearer credential: whoever holds it collects. It is not an opaque value (D57): it is a transport credential that yields the relay's `Caller`, never a permission carried around.
- **V6** The paper's visitor has an anonymous session; `anonymous` has only an id per letter, and registration (D98) is done by `unknown`, whose key is verified but not known.
- **V7** q- is the pilot of the paper's library, `zen0`, which grows by q-'s needs (section 3).
- **V8** The actor's data (faces, bonds, memberships) live in the business domain `bonds`, not in auth: auth reads them through its ports (GR-4), and registration creates a bond, not a user (PER-11). In the paper auth's sources are external (token, database, identity provider, D43) and registration is a command of auth (D98). The same for the offers of the extensions to the bonds (GR-4, EXT-7) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: access data per bond, which leaves a bond reaches, live in the business domain `extensions`, written by commands of `self` (activate, withdraw the offer, offer again); in the paper an assignment of access is auth's data (D29: roles and memberships) and written by a command of auth (D98, D43: "assign a role"). Auth reads them through its ports (PER-9), as it reads the bonds: they are the user's choices about the node's business, kept and shown with its history, and `extensions` keeps the invariant they need (the bond exists, GR-3). Rejected: the offers in a domain of kind auth with its commands (D98), a third place for what `extensions` decides with the activation, and auth would hold a rule (an offer to every bond but those withdrawn) where it gives data only (PER-9).

- **V9** The change of state is carried by the fact, not by the branch of the command (DOM-5). In the paper a branch carries its own mutation with the primitives create, modify, delete (D16, D47, D94) and the fact records it with the resolved values (D54). The semantics is unchanged (state primary, fact atomic with it); what moves is where an author writes the change: in the content of the fact, read by one rule of the library, so that the business language names no primitive and no instance kind. So the resources of a fact, which the paper derives from the primitives and the target and never from the payload (D107, D94.6), come in q- from the identities in the payload (DOM-10): a fact also counts as resources the instances it only names (the tool and the member of a loan).
- **V10** The library has no semantics of state (LIB-14), and the reference interpreter is not in it. In the paper the library ships a reference interpreter, generic over every domain, that is the semantics of conditions and of the mutation primitives, the in-memory adapter and the oracle at once (D50, D102); every predicate declares what it reads, and a command's read set, which isolation covers, is derived from those declarations (D96); every adapter is compared with the oracle (D103). In q- the domain gives commands, conditions and facts as data in the library's format, and its adapters materialize the state and evaluate the predicates; isolation is the adapters' (one writer per node gives it by construction, DOM-9). The reference interpreter is the in-memory oracle (TST-2), an artifact written with the domains, one module per domain, from their `dsl` (DOM-5): it says what a command and a read mean and implements the interpreter rules (LIB-9); the other adapters and the UI must agree with it. Recommended, not required. Besides being the oracle of the tests, it can run on the client to predict the state after a command or a use case (an optimistic preview). What stays as in the paper: the condition language and the rejection that names the failed predicates (LIB-7, DOM-6), the duplicate branch, the envelope, the total order. [Q, 11 Nov 2026: the oracle is the reference interpreter]

- **V11** The business domains are isolated: none imports another, except the other's identities (GR-3). Each models its own view of the others' concepts, in its own language; the state adapter links them, implementing the downstream's predicates and queries over the upstream's state; the domains meet only above them (persona, user, reactions, adapters, configurators). In the paper two business domains that can import each other are conformist: the downstream consumes the upstream's use cases as its own ports without translating and composes its exported, versioned predicates; a translating adapter between importable domains is a violation (D36, D37, D41, D50.4, D71, D75.7). q- gives up the compiler's check of the link and the single way of consuming a domain; it gains domains that evolve without touching each other, and a concept that can have another shape in each. The check moves to the in-memory oracle, where the links are typed (TST-2), and to the tests of the fragments that cross domains (GR-3); the value of a predicate was already the adapters' (V10). Atomicity across domains needs one state adapter for both (ADP-8). [Q]
- **V12** A query's selection uses selection predicates, a vocabulary apart from the predicates of the conditions, over the candidate of a subject that they do not name (DOM-18). In the paper commands, selections and applicability share one vocabulary of predicates (D44, D47.1), and the downstream composes the upstream's exported predicates in its selections (D50.4). The condition language (AND, OR, NOT, two-valued, LIB-7) is the same. q- pays one translation more per concept in the adapters (a predicate and a selection predicate for "in service") and gains selections that read as plain filters over their subject. The subject differs too: in the paper it is an aggregate (D50.1, D64.1), a kind of entity with state, the unit of identity, life cycle, vocabulary and order (D94), present on the side of the commands as well (D11, D52.2, D94.1); in q- it is a view named by the business, which the adapter builds and which may read more than one domain (an entry with the name of a tool, an agenda of loans and reservations), present only on the side of the reads (DOM-18), as the library knows no entities (LIB-14, V9, V10). Whether q- reintroduces the aggregate is open, to be seen in the next steps: the identity of an entry is declared by each query (LIB-9), not by the subject; when every query of a subject declares the same identity, the subject with its identity is the paper's aggregate, the sign to reintroduce it. [Q]

- **V13** The hat is verified by the node at the top of each persona, as a perimeter: a caller without it gets "not found" (D87.5), after "invalid request" (PER-5, PER-7). In the paper the interface verifies it with a read of auth and answers "hat not available", a base event, before the persona (D78.2, D106.1(b)). q- moves the check into the tree, where a node already has the means (a lookup of auth and a pure condition): the interface keeps the transport, the version and the operation, and gives every persona the same `Caller`; it loses an answer that the client, which knows the user's hats, does not need. [Q, 13 Nov 2026 (A)] Since step 12, before the version, the kernel's routing reads auth's offers for a request to an extension, and answers it with silence or lets it on (EXT-6, V16). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
- **V14** The version is the domain's, not the fact's: one semver per domain, which every part of a fact and every effect queued carries, a folder per past major with the shapes that changed and their steps (DOM-22). In the paper each fact, and each part, has its own version, derived from its schema (D101.3, D111.3). The pair path and version still names one schema; the version is declared and checked by CI, not derived. [Q, A, 16 Nov 2026 (7)]
- **V15** A command leaf looks its operation up in its scope before its schema, and answers a retry with the recorded fact, or `identity_already_used`, before the input's checks and the nodes (PER-5, "A retry after the input's window over `now` moved"; built 19 Nov 2026). In the paper (D106) the duplicate branch is the business's, at step 6, so a retry after the input's window over `now` moved, or a limit that reads it, would read `invalid_request` or a refusal while its operation's fact is there. The business's port looking its operation up before it parses its input is no divergence: it is DOM-11 ("runs first") applied, for a caller that is not a leaf (built 19 Nov 2026). [Q, A, 19 Nov 2026]
- **V16** The silence (F8): a node may answer nothing to a request for an extension it does not offer to the bond of the `Caller`. In the paper every stream closes with a declared terminal event, a server that answers nothing is a fault its client handles with its local wait and a resend (D72.2, D58, D73.6), and the surface of a persona is public: a node it lacks is "invalid request", a hat not worn "hat not available", and "not found" for a missing node was rejected (D78.2, D106.3, D75.1). q- keeps the paper's rule for the kernel's surfaces toward its bonded faces, which every node has, as V13 leaves it (a node it lacks `invalid_request`, a hat not worn `not_found`); an extension's surface is each node's choice, bond by bond, and stays its own (Alessandro, 19 Nov 2026). The asker handles a silence as q- handles every missing answer, with a `no_response` at its time, definitive (F7, V18), where the paper leaves a command with no answer uncertain, resolvable while its identity is kept (D73.6). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] **The routing reads auth before the gate** (EXT-6, PER-5) [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]: a change of D106.1's order, which rejects the hat read before the version ("a read of auth for a message that might not be interpretable"). In q- the routing reads of the message only what it invokes (`{ of, extension }`, EXT-5), which every version of the protocol carries in the letter `post` opened (F3), so it reads nothing the gate would have to make interpretable; and with the gate first, `version_not_supported` would tell an extension that is there but not offered (F8). Declared, as V15 declares its change of the order. **Toward a face with no bond the node is silent** [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] (F7, F8): the kernel too, a refused introduction included (PER-25), where the paper answers a request it refuses with an explicit refusal and its reason, generic when the value must not be revealed (D80.2, D80.5), and closes every stream with a declared terminal event (D72.2, D78.2). Alessandro's reason (21 Nov 2026): the paper was thought with a server in the background; Cumino's phones are personal devices, not servers, and keeping silent, which the paper does not foresee, is there not only possible but necessary, for security and privacy.
- **V17** A persona's surface is made of parts, the kernel's and each extension's, each with its own version and window (EXT-5), where the paper gives a persona's surface one version (D75.6, D75.4); and, after stage 1, parts loaded from storage at runtime, from a trusted host, where a surface grows only by a reviewed change of its nodes, mounted leaves included (D75.4, D78.6). The activation and the offers are the paper's runtime permission, a constraint whose value comes from auth (D78.8: "this administrator may refund"), here the offers auth reads (PER-9) and the routing compares (EXT-6), no divergence as data; not so their answer: where the paper refuses a permission missing explicitly, with its reason, generic if its value must not be revealed (D80.2, D80.5), q- answers with silence, V16's divergence [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]; and in stage 1 the structure is code the configurator composes (EXT-7, installed), as D78.8 keeps it the persona's code. [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026] Rewritten at the gate: until then V17 called the surface composed at runtime, and the paper's runtime only the assignment of personas, which D78.8 does not say.
- **V18** The time a request waits is the port's, and `no_response` is a definitive outcome with a meaning (F7, EXT-12): the kernel's port of requests stops sending at the time the entry's payload declares and answers `no_response`, which the return records. In the paper a timeout is transient, retried by the infrastructure, out of the domain (D67.1); exhaustion never becomes a business fact by itself, and a business that cares about time models a deadline of its own, a delayed effect and a guarded command that evaluates the condition in place of the infrastructure (D67 corollary, D68.3, as DOM-13 does for Shed's due dates); a command with no answer stays uncertain, resolvable while its identity is kept (D73.6). q- departs because a silence is a normal answer there (F8): a node that does not offer an extension answers nothing by design, so "no response within 60 seconds" is the feature's question, not a failure of the infrastructure; read as exhaustion, every silence would be a dead letter for the user. And the request's delivery needs an end anyway: with no time it would be sent again for a response that may never come. Rejected: the paper's form, a wait of the business beside the request (`wait_for_the_pong`, 60 seconds, whose guarded return records `the_ping_had_no_response` if no pong is recorded) and the request's delivery with no time of its own: two entries for one question, and the delivery in flight with no end (the port sending again as long as the relay keeps, 7 days), or with the same time declared twice. A late response is annotated, not a fact (F7): the outcome stays as recorded. [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]
- **V19** The response is the return of a call in flight (F7): the executor's delivery of a request waits for it, up to the request's time, several together (`order: 'any'`, DOM-14), and the port gives it to the delivery as what the world said (D108.2). In the paper a world that answers late over an asynchronous channel is an actor from outside that invokes the command with an identity of its own (D92.3, D91), the form F4 keeps for a receipt, and a long flow is no long connection (D72.8). q- takes the return for the response because it is Alessandro's concept (19 Nov 2026, the glossary): the remote form of the leaf's projection, paired with the request it names. As an actor's request it would be a use case of the asker's node that any bonded peer could invoke with a pairing of its choice, where a return use case is in no door, its entry the executor (DOM-13), so that no persona sends an effect a payload of its choice; and the asker's time (V18) is the call's. The cost: a delivery in flight up to its time, the executor's position behind it (DOM-14), and a call resumed after a restart (G4). A feature that answers by a request of its own keeps F4's form. [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]

## 10. Gaps of the paper met by q-

Reported to the paper; until it answers, a point that depends on them is decided case by case and recorded in q-'s decisions, not invented in the code.

- **G1** Restoring an export on a new device: positions, operation ids, outbox entries not yet executed, duplicates still in the mailbox.
- **G2** Between business domains that can import each other the paper offers only the conformist relation: no own view, no example of one concept with different models in two domains, no rule on how a domain types the ids of another, no reason given for the acyclic graph, nothing on a value over the upstream's data that the upstream does not have. q- chose isolation (V11).
- **G3** Extensions: the paper has none. No boundary between a kernel and packages released apart that add leaves to its personas, no manifest, no isolation; the nearest are one package per domain, a translating adapter between release units (D39, D37), a mounted leaf (D78.6); the composition check covers capabilities and the `Caller`'s fields, not use cases nor what a part may touch (D104, D110). Nor does it say how units built with two versions of its library meet (D102), how a subtree served by another release unit hangs below a node (D78.6 and D105 mount within one unit), or what a third party's interface may draw (D76). What a node offers to a bond is no gap: D78.8's permission from auth (V17). q-'s answer: EXT-1..EXT-16.
- **G4** A call in flight resumed after a restart: the request of F7 is a delivery in flight until its response or its time (V19, a divergence, since the paper's late answer is an actor of its own, D92.3), and a node that restarts meets it again by its identity, sent again with it (D73.6), or answered from what the port keeps (F7). The paper's executor has one delivery unit per effect (D108), and says nothing of a delivery that outlives its executor's run. q-'s answer: F7 (`order: 'any'` in flight together, A, 20 Nov 2026), DOM-14 (the position behind the oldest delivery in flight). [Q, A, 21 Nov 2026]

Outside the paper, to be decided by q- when it comes: the life cycle of keys (rotating or revoking a face, keeping faces unlinked in shared outputs), which is business or credentials (D98).
